The evidence gate\n\nAutomated tools are the **floor, not the claim**. axe/pa11y/Lighthouse catch the\nmachine-checkable minority â€” roughly a third of real defects, and none of the\nclasses in Â§3 marked *manual*. \"axe: 0 violations\" is one line of a report, never\na verdict.\n\nEvery finding: **`file:line` + the reproduction** (the exact keystroke sequence,\nthe measured number, or the tool output). Every fix: **the same reproduction,\nre-run**. Every report ends with what you did **not** check.\n\n| Claim | Requires | Not sufficient |\n|---|---|---|\n| Keyboard accessible | A recorded Tab transcript: every stop, in order, focus visible, nothing trapped, Escape works | The elements are `<button>`s now |\n| Contrast passes | Computed colours pulled off the rendered element, ratio printed, per state | Token names, a palette, \"roughly 3.3:1\", eyeballing |\n| Screen reader announces it | The accessibility tree node (role + name + value) or an SR transcript | `aria-label` is present in the source |\n| Live region announces | Watched it fire once, with the container in the DOM *before* the message | `aria-live=\"polite\"` is on the div |\n| Reduced motion honoured | Emulated `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, reloaded, content still visible | A `@media` block exists |\n| Touch targets pass | Measured `getBoundingClientRect()` on every interactive element | The component library \"handles it\" |\n| Fix complete | The whole Â§3 sweep re-run, not just the class you touched | The reported symptom looks addressed |\n\n**Fixes break neighbours â€” re-sweep, always.** In this skill's own baseline run,\nan agent fixing a keyboard bug shrank a help trigger to 18Ã—18px and introduced a\ntouch-target and a dismissible-content failure while reporting the bug resolved.\n\n### Rationalizations, from the baseline runs\n\n| It said | Reality |\n|---|---|\n| \"Both files look correct.\" | Correct-looking source has never operated a keyboard. Drive it. |\n| \"Every control is now reachable via Tab and activates with Enter/Space\" â€” with no browser open | That is a prediction. Tab through it and paste the transcript. |\n| \"Quick and cheap given the deadline, I'll skip anything not keyboard-specific\" | The scope is the sweep in Â§3. Deadlines shorten the *fix list*, never the *check list*. |\n| \"It's a disabled control, so contrast isn't strictly required by WCAG\" | Exempt from the standard, not from the product. Unreadable disabled text is why users retry a dead button. Measure it and report it. |\n| \"~3.3:1, borderline, likely fails\" | Estimating a ratio you could have computed. Print the number. |\n| \"Irrelevant once a real `<label>` is added\" | You fixed one class by assuming another. Verify both. |\n| \"There's no dev server, so I can't check\" | Then the deliverable is findings marked **unverified**, not a completion claim. Or start the server. |\n| \"The library is accessible\" | Libraries ship a11y bugs, and integration is where they surface. Measure the rendered DOM. |\n\n**Red flags â€” stop and go run something:** writing \"now accessible\", \"resolved\",\n\"WCAG AA compliant\" Â· a verdict with no numbers in it Â· a fix report shorter than\nits reproduction Â· \"should announce\" / \"should be focusable\" Â· reporting on\nclasses you only read for Â· skipping the re-sweep because the change was small.\n\n---\n\n## 3. The sweep\n\nRun every row. Order matters: static first (cheap, narrows the surface), then\nthe browser passes, then assistive technology. Depth, exact commands, snippets\nand keystrokes: **[references/checks.md](references/checks.md)**. Correct\nimplementations for the widgets that fail: **[references/patterns.md](references/patterns.md)**.\n\n| # | Class | What actually goes wrong in generated UI | Check |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | **Reachability** | `onClick` on a `div`/`span`; a control behind hover; a custom widget with no tab stop | Static grep, then Tab from the top and count stops against the interactive elements |\n| 2 | **Traps & order** | Modal without `inert`, focus that escapes behind the overlay, `tabindex` > 0, DOM order â‰  visual order | Tab and Shift+Tab the whole page; dump the focus order; Escape from every overlay *(manual)* |\n| 3 | **Visible focus** | `outline: none` with no replacement; a ring that vanishes on a coloured surface; hover styled, focus not | Grep `outline`; then focus each element and diff computed styles; measure ring contrast â‰¥3:1 |\n| 4 | **Name / role / value** | A `<div role=\"button\">` with no name; `aria-expanded` that never updates; a cycling button pretending to be a select | Accessibility-tree dump: every interactive node has role + non-empty name, and value tracks state |\n| 5 | **Live regions** | Container injected *with* the message (announces never); a region wrapping a whole list (announces constantly); `alert` used for routine status | Region present at load, `aria-live` correct, watched firing once *(manual)* |\n| 6 | **Contrast in unscreenshotted states** | placeholder, disabled, hover, visited, error text, text on the accent button, the ring against both surfaces | Force each state, pull computed colours, compute ratios with alpha resolved. Numbers or it didn't happen |\n| 7 | **Motion** | No reduced-motion path; content gated behind a reveal transition ships blank; JS/spring animation ignoring the media query; infinite spinners | Emulate reduce, reload, screenshot; grep for `matchMedia`/`useReducedMotion` in JS animation |\n| 8 | **Forms** | Placeholder as label; error not tied by `aria-describedby`; `aria-invalid` missing; colour-only error; focus never moved to the failure; no `autocomplete` | Submit an invalid form for real: name, description, invalidity, announcement, focus |\n| 9 | **Headings & landmarks** | A styled `div` acting as a heading; levels skipped; no `<main>`; several `<h1>` | Dump the heading outline and landmark list; compare against what *looks* like a heading |\n| 10 | **Images & icons** | `alt` restating the filename; decorative image with alt text; icon-only button with no name; SVG with neither `title` nor `aria-hidden` | Alt audit: flag `alt` matching the `src` basename, empty names on interactive elements |\n| 11 | **Target size** | 18â€“32px icon buttons, table row actions, close buttons, adjacent chips with no spacing | Measure every interactive rect: <44Ã—44 CSS px (or 24 with spacing, WCAG 2.2 AA) is a finding |\n| 12 | **Mouse-only** | Hover-only tooltips and menus, drag-only reordering, `title` as the only carrier, custom scroll, gestures with no button | Unplug the mouse. Complete every task by keyboard *(manual)* |\n| 13 | **Zoom & reflow** | Fixed heights that clip, `overflow: hidden` on text, two-dimensional scrolling at 400% | 320 CSS px wide (400% zoom) and 200% text-only: no loss, no horizontal scroll |\n\nThen **one assistive-technology pass** on the primary flow â€” NVDA + Firefox\n(Windows) or VoiceOver + Safari (macOS/iOS). The tree dump proves the semantics;\nonly the AT proves it is usable.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Fixing\n\n1. **Reproduce first, and write it down.** The recorded reproduction is this\n   task's failing test (`PRACTICE.md` Â§4). No reproduction, no fix.\n2. **Native element before role before ARIA.** `<button>` beats\n   `<div role=\"button\" tabindex=\"0\" onKeyDown>`. ARIA adds semantics, never\n   behaviour: it will not make a div focusable, clickable by Space, or\n   disableable. Bad ARIA is worse than none.\n3. **One class at a time**, then re-run its reproduction. A batch of eight\n   simultaneous fixes has no evidence attached to any of them.\n4. **Fix the source, not the symptom.** Ten components with no focus ring is one\n   token or one base style, not ten patches.\n5. **Never fix by removing.** `aria-hidden` on a focusable element, `tabindex=\"-1\"`\n   to silence a warning, and deleting the failing control all clear the scanner\n   and break the user.\n6. **Re-sweep Â§3 after the fix**, not just the class you touched.\n7. **Leave a regression guard** where the project has tests â€” `jest-axe` /\n   `@axe-core/playwright` on the component, plus an explicit keyboard test for\n   the behaviour that broke. Verify red-green: revert the fix, watch it fail.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Report\n\nRanked by user impact, not by rule number. Severity: **blocker** (a task cannot\nbe completed by some user) Â· **serious** (completable but degraded) Â· **minor**\n(friction). A scanner's own severity is an input, not the answer.\n\n```\nScope: /reports, /reports/:id Â· Chrome 141 + axe 4.10 Â· NVDA 2024.4 not run\nAutomated: axe 7 violations (3 serious) Â· pa11y clean Â· Lighthouse a11y 84\n\nBLOCKER  Filters.jsx:32 â€” panel toggle is a div, no tab stop.\n         Repro: Tab from the search field; focus jumps to Apply, panel unreachable.\nBLOCKER  Filters.jsx:61 â€” validation error never announced; no aria-describedby.\n         Repro: submit \"ab\" with NVDA; silence, focus stays on the button.\nSERIOUS  filters.css:9 â€” .btn:focus{outline:none}, no replacement. All buttons.\n         Repro: focused Apply, computed outline \"none\", box-shadow \"none\".\nSERIOUS  filters.css:11 â€” disabled label #e8f0f8 on #a8c8e8 = 1.4:1 (measured).\nMINOR    Filters.jsx:37 â€” alt=\"chevron-down.svg\" restates the filename.\n\nNot checked: iOS VoiceOver, Windows High Contrast, the /admin routes (no seed data).\n```\n\n**The \"not checked\" line is mandatory.** An audit that hides its gaps is how a\ngreen report and a broken product coexist.\n\n## Related\n\n- `PRACTICE.md` Â§4-5 â€” the reproduction-as-test law and the evidence gate\n- [references/checks.md](references/checks.md) â€” commands, snippets, keystrokes\n- [references/patterns.md](references/patterns.md) â€” the widget contracts\n- `skillator:design-arwen` â€” a11y as a gate inside a design task (Â§1)\n- `skillator:audit-sherlock` â€” whole-codebase audit; routes here for a11y depth\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\a11y-toph\\references\\checks.md\r\n# Checks â€” the command, the snippet, the keystroke\n\nNumbered to match the sweep table in SKILL.md Â§3. Nothing here is optional\nbecause a class \"looked fine in the source\".\n\n**Driver.** Pick one and say which you used: `claude-in-chrome` (invoke the\nskill, then load tools in one `ToolSearch` call) Â· `browse` (gstack headless,\nfastest for a screenshot-and-assert loop) Â· `webapp-testing` (scripted\ninteraction) Â· Playwright already in the repo Â· the iOS Simulator / Android\nemulator for native. **No driver available â†’ the deliverable is findings marked\n`UNVERIFIED (no browser)`, never a verdict.**\n\nThe runtime snippets below are pasted into the page â€” the `javascript_tool`, the\nDevTools console, or `page.evaluate()`. They print findings; they change nothing.\n\n---\n\n## 0. Automated floor (run first, believe it least)\n\n```bash\nnpx @axe-core/cli http://localhost:3000 --tags wcag2a,wcag2aa,wcag21aa,wcag22aa --exit\nnpx pa11y --standard WCAG2AA --runner axe --runner htmlcs http://localhost:3000\nnpx lighthouse http://localhost:3000 --only-categories=accessibility --quiet --chrome-flags=\"--headless\"\n```\n\nMulti-route, authenticated, or stateful UI â†’ drive it in Playwright instead, so\nyou scan the state that actually fails (menu open, error shown, modal up):\n\n```js\nimport AxeBuilder from '@axe-core/playwright'\nconst r = await new AxeBuilder({ page })\n  .withTags(['wcag2a','wcag2aa','wcag21aa','wcag22aa'])\n  .analyze()\n```\n\nComponent-level regression guard: `jest-axe` / `vitest-axe` â†’\n`expect(await axe(container)).toHaveNoViolations()`.\n\nStatic, in CI, before anything renders: `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y`\n(`plugin:jsx-a11y/recommended`), and for Vue `eslint-plugin-vuejs-accessibility`.\n\n**Their ceiling:** they cannot see whether the focus order makes sense, whether\na name is *correct* rather than merely present, whether a live region actually\nfired, whether the keyboard path completes a task, or whether the disabled state\nis readable. Everything below exists because of that ceiling.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Reachability\n\nStatic first â€” these greps find most of it in seconds:\n\n```bash\nrg -n \"onClick\" --glob '*.{jsx,tsx,vue,svelte}' | rg \"<(div|span|li|td|tr|img|a[^-])\" \nrg -n \"addEventListener\\(['\\\"]click\" --glob '*.{js,ts}'\nrg -n \"tabIndex=\\{?[1-9]|tabindex=[\\\"']?[1-9]\" \nrg -n \"aria-hidden\" --glob '*.{jsx,tsx,html,vue}'      # on anything focusable = a trap\nrg -n \":hover\" --glob '*.{css,scss}' -A2 | rg -n \"display:\\s*(block|flex)|visibility:\\s*visible\"\n```\n\nThen count in the browser â€” the interactive elements and the tab stops must agree:\n\n```js\nconst focusables = [...document.querySelectorAll(\n  'a[href],button,input,select,textarea,summary,[tabindex],[contenteditable=\"\"],[contenteditable=\"true\"]')]\n  .filter(el => !el.disabled && el.tabIndex > -1 && el.offsetParent !== null)\nconst clickable = [...document.querySelectorAll('[onclick],[role=\"button\"],[role=\"link\"],[role=\"tab\"],[role=\"menuitem\"]')]\nconsole.table(clickable.filter(el => !focusables.includes(el))\n  .map(el => ({ unreachable: el.tagName + '.' + el.className, text: el.textContent.trim().slice(0,40) })))\n```\n\nEmpty table or it is a finding.\n\n## 2. Traps and focus order\n\n**Manual, and there is no substitute.** Hands off the mouse:\n\n1. Reload. Press Tab once â€” the first stop should be a skip link or the first\n   control, never `<body>` after fifteen invisible stops.\n2. Tab to the end of the page. Every stop: visible, sensible, in reading order.\n3. Shift+Tab back. Same stops, reverse. A stop reachable one way only is a bug.\n4. Open every overlay (modal, drawer, menu, combobox, date picker). Inside:\n   focus starts in it, Tab cycles *within* it, Escape closes it, focus returns\n   to the trigger. Behind it: nothing is reachable (`inert` or `aria-hidden` on\n   the background â€” and `inert` is the one that also blocks the mouse).\n5. Trigger every async transition (route change, submit, delete). Focus must\n   land somewhere deliberate, never on `<body>`.\n\nDump the order and compare it with the visual order:\n\n```js\nlet i = 0, seen = new Set()\nconst log = []\ndocument.body.addEventListener('focusin', e => {\n  const el = e.target, r = el.getBoundingClientRect()\n  log.push(`${++i}. ${el.tagName}.${el.className} \"${(el.innerText||el.value||'').trim().slice(0,30)}\" @${Math.round(r.top)},${Math.round(r.left)}`)\n  if (seen.has(el) && i > 3) log.push('   ^ REVISITED â€” possible trap')\n  seen.add(el)\n}, true)\n// Tab through, then: copy(log.join('\\n'))\n```\n\n`@top,left` going up then down again is DOM order fighting visual order â€”\nusually a CSS `order`, `row-reverse`, or a positioned sidebar.\n\n## 3. Visible focus\n\n```bash\nrg -n \"outline:\\s*(none|0)\" --glob '*.{css,scss,less}'\n```\n\nLegitimate only when `:focus-visible` supplies a replacement in the same file.\nThen verify at runtime â€” the styles that *exist* and the styles that *apply* are\ndifferent questions:\n\n```js\nconst bare = []\nfor (const el of document.querySelectorAll('a[href],button,input,select,textarea,[tabindex=\"0\"]')) {\n  const before = getComputedStyle(el).cssText\n  el.focus()\n  const s = getComputedStyle(el)\n  const changed = s.outlineStyle !== 'none' || s.boxShadow !== 'none' || s.cssText !== before\n  if (!changed) bare.push(el.tagName + '.' + el.className)\n}\nconsole.log('no visible focus:', bare)\n```\n\nMeasure the ring against **both** the element and the page background (â‰¥3:1,\nWCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.11 / 2.4.13) with the contrast helper in Â§6. A ring that only\ncontrasts with the button and not with the page fails on the page side.\n\n## 4. Name, role, value\n\nThe authority is the accessibility tree, not the markup. Read it in the DevTools\nAccessibility pane, `page.accessibility.snapshot()` in Playwright, or with axe's\nown name computation (`axe.commons.text.accessibleText(el)`) after injecting\n`axe-core`. The heuristic below finds the empty ones fast:\n\n```js\nconst name = el =>\n  el.getAttribute('aria-label') ||\n  (el.getAttribute('aria-labelledby')||'').split(/\\s+/).map(id=>document.getElementById(id)?.innerText).join(' ').trim() ||\n  (el.labels?.[0]?.innerText) ||\n  el.innerText.trim() ||\n  el.getAttribute('title') ||\n  [...el.querySelectorAll('img[alt],svg title')].map(n=>n.alt||n.textContent).join(' ').trim()\nconsole.table([...document.querySelectorAll('a[href],button,input,select,textarea,[role]')]\n  .filter(el => !name(el))\n  .map(el => ({ nameless: el.tagName + '.' + el.className, role: el.getAttribute('role') || '(implicit)' })))\n```\n\nThen check **value tracks state** â€” the failure a name check never catches. With\nthe widget open/checked/selected, confirm the matching attribute flipped:\n\n```js\nconsole.table([...document.querySelectorAll('[aria-expanded],[aria-selected],[aria-checked],[aria-pressed],[aria-current]')]\n  .map(el => ({ el: el.tagName+'.'+el.className,\n    expanded: el.getAttribute('aria-expanded'), selected: el.getAttribute('aria-selected'),\n    checked: el.getAttribute('aria-checked'), pressed: el.getAttribute('aria-pressed') })))\n```\n\nRun it twice â€” before and after toggling. Identical output means the state is\nvisual only. See [patterns.md](patterns.md) for the contract per widget.\n\n## 5. Live regions\n\nThree distinct failures, three checks.\n\n**Announces never** â€” the container is created together with the message. A\nregion must be in the DOM, empty, *before* the text arrives; AT only watches\nregions it was already observing.\n\n```js\nconsole.table([...document.querySelectorAll('[aria-live],[role=\"status\"],[role=\"alert\"],[role=\"log\"]')]\n  .map(el => ({ el: el.tagName+'.'+el.className, live: el.getAttribute('aria-live') || el.getAttribute('role'),\n                atomic: el.getAttribute('aria-atomic'), populatedAtLoad: el.textContent.trim().length > 0 })))\n```\n\nEmpty table on a page with async results = nothing is announced anywhere.\n`populatedAtLoad: true` on a status region means it will re-announce on load.\n\n**Announces constantly** â€” `aria-live` on a container that also re-renders rows,\nspinners, or timers. Watch it:\n\n```js\nnew MutationObserver(m => console.log('LIVE FIRE', m.length, m[0].target.textContent.trim().slice(0,60)))\n  .observe(document.querySelector('[aria-live], [role=\"status\"]'), {childList:true, subtree:true, characterData:true})\n```\n\nOne user action must produce one fire. A spinner frame counting as a fire is the\nbug.\n\n**Wrong urgency** â€” `role=\"alert\"` / `aria-live=\"assertive\"` interrupts whatever\nthe user is reading. Reserve it for errors and destructive outcomes; routine\nresults and \"3 of 40 loaded\" are `role=\"status\"` (polite).\n\n## 6. Contrast, in the states nobody screenshots\n\nDo not eyeball, do not trust tokens, do not trust the design file. Pull the\ncomputed colours off the rendered element and **resolve alpha and translucency**\nby compositing over the nearest opaque ancestor â€” a `rgba(0,0,0,.55)` label over\na tinted card is the pair that quietly fails.\n\n```js\nconst lum = ([r,g,b]) => { const s=[r,g,b].map(v=>v/255).map(v=>v<=0.03928?v/12.92:((v+0.055)/1.055)**2.4)\n  return 0.2126*s[0]+0.7152*s[1]+0.0722*s[2] }\nconst ratio = (a,b) => { const [x,y]=[lum(a),lum(b)].sort((p,q)=>q-p); return (x+0.05)/(y+0.05) }\nconst parse = c => (c.match(/[\\d.]+/g)||[0,0,0,1]).map(Number)          // [r,g,b,a?]\nconst over = (fg, bg) => { const a = fg[3] ?? 1; return [0,1,2].map(i => fg[i]*a + bg[i]*(1-a)) }\nconst bgOf = el => { for (let n = el; n; n = n.parentElement) {\n    const c = parse(getComputedStyle(n).backgroundColor)\n    if ((c[3] ?? 1) > 0.99) return c.slice(0,3) } return [255,255,255] }\nconst check = el => { const s = getComputedStyle(el), bg = bgOf(el)\n  const px = parseFloat(s.fontSize), large = px >= 24 || (px >= 18.66 && +s.fontWeight >= 700)\n  return { el: el.tagName+'.'+el.className, text: el.innerText?.trim().slice(0,24),\n           ratio: +ratio(over(parse(s.color), bg), bg).toFixed(2), need: large ? 3 : 4.5 } }\nconsole.table([...document.querySelectorAll('body *')].filter(el => el.innerText?.trim() && el.children.length === 0)\n  .map(check).filter(r => r.ratio < r.need))\n```\n\nThen force the states the default render never shows and re-run `check`:\n\n| State | How to force it |\n|---|---|\n| `:hover`, `:active`, `:focus`, `:visited` | DevTools Styles pane â†’ **:hov** â†’ force element state. In Playwright, `page.hover()` then evaluate |\n| Disabled | `document.querySelectorAll('button,input').forEach(e=>e.disabled=true)` |\n| Placeholder | `check` reads `color`, not `::placeholder` â€” sample it directly: `getComputedStyle(el,'::placeholder').color` |\n| Error / invalid | Submit the form empty and with bad data. Never fabricate the class |\n| Text on the accent button, badges, toasts | Trigger them; sample the real pair, not the token |\n| Non-text (icons, ring, chart series, input borders, toggle track) | Same helper on `borderColor` / `fill` / `outlineColor`; needs â‰¥3:1 (SC 1.4.11) |\n| Dark theme | Toggle it the way a user does, then run the whole set again |\n\n**Report the numbers.** \"Contrast checked\" is not a check. Disabled text is\nexempt from WCAG and still a finding â€” say so and let the owner decide.\n\n## 7. Motion\n\n```js\n// emulation: DevTools â†’ Rendering â†’ Emulate CSS prefers-reduced-motion\n// Playwright: await page.emulateMedia({ reducedMotion: 'reduce' })\n```\n\nReload **after** emulating, then: (a) every reveal shows its content â€” the fatal\none is content whose opacity is 0 until an IntersectionObserver adds a class,\nwhich ships a blank page; (b) nothing loops forever; (c) parallax, autoplaying\nvideo, and carousels stop or offer a pause control (SC 2.2.2).\n\nCSS-only greps miss JS animation. Every animation library needs the query too:\n\n```bash\nrg -n \"framer-motion|gsap|animejs|motion/react|useSpring|requestAnimationFrame\" --glob '*.{js,jsx,ts,tsx}'\nrg -n \"prefers-reduced-motion|useReducedMotion\" --glob '*.{css,scss,js,jsx,ts,tsx}'\n```\n\nThe second list must cover the first. Blanket\n`@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce){*{animation:none!important}}` is not a\npass either â€” it can freeze a loading spinner into a lie; crossfade instead of\nkilling state-change feedback.\n\n## 8. Forms\n\nSubmit an invalid form for real, then verify all five:\n\n```js\nconsole.table([...document.querySelectorAll('input,select,textarea')].map(el => ({\n  el: el.name || el.id || el.className,\n  name: (el.labels?.[0]?.innerText || el.getAttribute('aria-label') || '').trim() || '*** NONE ***',\n  placeholderOnly: !el.labels?.length && !el.getAttribute('aria-label') && !!el.placeholder,\n  invalid: el.getAttribute('aria-invalid'),\n  describedBy: (el.getAttribute('aria-describedby')||'').split(/\\s+/)\n    .map(id => document.getElementById(id)?.innerText.trim()).filter(Boolean).join(' | ') || '(none)',\n  autocomplete: el.getAttribute('autocomplete') || '(none)',\n  required: el.required || el.getAttribute('aria-required') })))\n```\n\n- `placeholderOnly: true` â†’ SC 3.3.2 failure; the name disappears on typing.\n- `describedBy: (none)` while an error is on screen â†’ the error exists only for\n  sighted users.\n- Colour-only error: remove the colour and ask whether the field still reads as\n  broken â€” `html{filter:grayscale(1)}` and screenshot.\n- Focus after a failed submit must move to the first invalid field or to an\n  error summary that links to the fields. Verify with `document.activeElement`.\n- `autocomplete` on name/email/address/tel/one-time-code fields (SC 1.3.5).\n\n## 9. Headings and landmarks\n\n```js\nconsole.log([...document.querySelectorAll('h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6,[role=\"heading\"]')]\n  .map(h => '  '.repeat(+(h.tagName[1] || h.getAttribute('aria-level')) - 1) + h.tagName + ' ' + h.innerText.trim().slice(0,60)).join('\\n'))\nconsole.table([...document.querySelectorAll('main,nav,header,footer,aside,section[aria-label],[role]')]\n  .filter(el => ['main','nav','banner','contentinfo','complementary','search','region'].includes(el.getAttribute('role') || {MAIN:'main',NAV:'nav',HEADER:'banner',FOOTER:'contentinfo',ASIDE:'complementary'}[el.tagName]))\n  .map(el => ({ landmark: el.tagName, label: el.getAttribute('aria-label') || '(none)' })))\n```\n\nThen the check no script can do: screenshot the page and list what *looks* like\na heading. Anything on that list missing from the outline is a `div` doing\ntypography's job. Exactly one `<h1>`, no skipped levels, one `<main>`.\n\n## 10. Images and icons\n\n```js\nconsole.table([...document.images].map(img => ({\n  src: img.currentSrc.split('/').pop(), alt: img.getAttribute('alt'),\n  problem: img.getAttribute('alt') === null ? 'MISSING alt'\n    : /\\.(png|jpe?g|svg|webp|gif)$/i.test(img.alt) ? 'alt restates the filename'\n    : img.alt.trim() && img.closest('a,button')?.innerText.trim() ? 'duplicates its control label'\n    : /^(image|icon|graphic|photo) ?(of)?$/i.test(img.alt.trim()) ? 'placeholder alt' : '' }))\n  .filter(r => r.problem))\n```\n\nPlus: inline `<svg>` needs `aria-hidden=\"true\"` when decorative, or `role=\"img\"`\nwith a `<title>` when it carries meaning. Background-image icons carrying\ninformation have no alt mechanism at all â€” they need a visually hidden label.\nCharts and diagrams need a text alternative or an adjacent data table\n(`dataviz` owns the chart side).\n\n## 11. Target size\n\n```js\nconsole.table([...document.querySelectorAll('a[href],button,input:not([type=hidden]),select,[role=\"button\"],[role=\"tab\"],[role=\"checkbox\"],[role=\"switch\"]')]\n  .map(el => { const r = el.getBoundingClientRect()\n    return { el: el.tagName+'.'+el.className, text: el.innerText.trim().slice(0,20),\n             w: Math.round(r.width), h: Math.round(r.height) } })\n  .filter(r => (r.w < 44 || r.h < 44) && r.w && r.h))\n```\n\n44Ã—44 CSS px is the floor (SC 2.5.5 AAA / Apple HIG); WCAG 2.2 SC 2.5.8 AA\nallows 24Ã—24 **only** when a 24px circle around it touches no neighbour's â€” so\nrow-action icons packed together fail even at 24. Inline links in a paragraph\nare exempt. Fix with padding or a `::after` hit area, not by scaling the icon.\nAndroid: 48Ã—48dp. Run this at a mobile viewport too â€” a target can shrink under\na media query.\n\n## 12. Mouse-only paths\n\nNo script. Unplug the mouse (or `document.body.style.pointerEvents='none'` after\nfocusing the page) and complete each primary task end to end. The recurring\noffenders: hover-revealed row actions and menus, drag-only reordering and\nsliders, custom scroll containers with no `tabindex=\"0\"`, `title` carrying\ninformation nothing else does, canvas/map interactions, and swipe-only mobile\ngestures (SC 2.5.1 needs a single-pointer alternative). Hover-revealed content\nalso needs SC 1.4.13: **hoverable, dismissible with Escape, and persistent**\nwhile pointed at.\n\n## 13. Zoom and reflow\n\n- **Reflow (SC 1.4.10):** set the viewport to **320Ã—256 CSS px** (equivalent to\n  400% zoom on a 1280 desktop). No horizontal scrolling, no clipped content, no\n  two-dimensional scroll â€” data tables excepted.\n- **Text resize (SC 1.4.4):** text-only zoom to 200%. Emulate by injecting\n  `document.documentElement.style.fontSize = '200%'` â€” anything sized in `px`\n  will not move, which is itself the finding, and anything in a fixed-height box\n  will clip.\n- Re-run the overflow probe at both:\n\n```js\n[...document.querySelectorAll('*')].filter(el => el.scrollWidth > el.clientWidth + 1)\n  .map(el => el.tagName + '.' + el.className)\n```\n\n- **Forced colors:** DevTools â†’ Rendering â†’ Emulate `forced-colors: active`.\n  Anything conveyed by a background image, a gradient, or a `box-shadow` border\n  disappears; icons rendered as background images vanish entirely.\n\n---\n\n## The assistive-technology pass\n\nOne real pass on the primary flow. Choose by platform and **say which you ran**.\n\n| AT | Start | The keys that matter |\n|---|---|---|\n| **NVDA** (Windows + Firefox; free, the default choice) | Ctrl+Alt+N | `NVDA+â†“` read all Â· `Tab` controls Â· `H` headings Â· `F` form fields Â· `B` buttons Â· `D` landmarks Â· `NVDA+F7` elements list Â· `NVDA+Space` toggle browse/focus mode |\n| **VoiceOver** (macOS + Safari) | Cmd+F5 | VO = Ctrl+Option Â· `VO+A` read all Â· `VO+â†’` next item Â· `VO+Space` activate Â· `VO+U` rotor (headings/links/form controls) Â· needs Full Keyboard Access on |\n| **VoiceOver** (iOS) | Settings â†’ Accessibility, or triple-click side button | swipe right/left = next/previous Â· double-tap = activate Â· two-finger swipe up = read all Â· rotor = two-finger rotate |\n| **TalkBack** (Android) | Volume-both hold | swipe right/left Â· double-tap Â· swipe up-then-right = menu |\n\nWhat you are listening for, in this order: does each control announce a **role**\nand a **name**; does its **state** change audibly when you operate it; is the\nasync result **announced at all**; can you complete the task without seeing the\nscreen. Paste the transcript of the failing moment into the finding.\n\n---\n\n## Native\n\nTouch targets â‰¥44Ã—44pt (iOS) / 48Ã—48dp (Android) â€” measure, do not assume the\ncomponent library got it right. iOS: run with the largest Dynamic Type setting\nand check nothing truncates; VoiceOver labels on every `Image`/icon button;\n`accessibilityTraits` matching the role. Android: `contentDescription`,\nTalkBack, and font scale 200%. React Native: `accessible`,\n`accessibilityLabel`, `accessibilityRole`, `accessibilityState`, and\n`AccessibilityInfo.isReduceMotionEnabled()` for Â§7. `ios-design-review` drives a\nreal device.\n\n---\n\n## CI\n\nMake the guard fail the build, not decorate a dashboard:\n\n```yaml\n- run: npx playwright test tests/a11y.spec.ts        # AxeBuilder per route + keyboard tests\n- run: npx eslint . --max-warnings 0                 # with jsx-a11y\n```\n\nScan the **states**, not just the routes: a spec that only loads the page checks\nthe emptiest version of it. Assert on a per-rule allowlist with an expiry note\nrather than a global threshold, so a new violation cannot hide under an old\nbudget.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\a11y-toph\\references\\patterns.md\r\n# Patterns â€” the contract each widget owes, and how generated code breaks it\n\nUse this when a sweep finding needs a fix and you are about to invent the\nmarkup. Each row is: **prefer the native thing** Â· the keyboard contract a user\nexpects Â· the name/role/value the accessibility tree must show Â· the failure\nthat actually ships.\n\n**First rule of ARIA: don't.** A native element beats a `div` with a role every\ntime â€” it brings focusability, activation, form participation, disabled\nsemantics, and platform AT quirks already solved. ARIA adds *semantics only*: it\nnever adds behaviour. `role=\"button\"` on a div does not make Space activate it,\ndoes not put it in the tab order, and `aria-disabled` does not stop the click.\n\n**Second rule: no ARIA is better than bad ARIA.** A wrong role hides the real\none. `aria-hidden` on something focusable creates a stop that announces nothing.\n\n---\n\n## The state attributes, once\n\n| Attribute | On | Must flip when | Common bug |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `aria-expanded` | the **trigger**, never the panel | the panel opens/closes | set on the panel; or never updated |\n| `aria-controls` | the trigger | â€” | points at an id that only exists while open |\n| `aria-selected` | tab / option | selection moves | used on buttons that aren't in a tablist |\n| `aria-checked` | checkbox / radio / switch (custom only) | toggled | on a native `<input>`, which manages its own |\n| `aria-pressed` | a genuine toggle button | pressed state | used where `aria-expanded` was meant |\n| `aria-current` | the active nav item / page | navigation | replaced by a colour-only \"active\" class |\n| `aria-invalid` | the field | validation fails | set on the wrapper `div` |\n| `aria-describedby` | the field | â€” | points at an error node that is removed when valid, leaving a dangling id |\n| `aria-busy` | the region being replaced | fetch starts/ends | omitted, so the region announces partial content |\n\n---\n\n## Disclosure / accordion\n\nNative: `<details><summary>` â€” free keyboard, free state, free AT. Reach for a\nbutton only when you need animation or controlled state.\n\n- **Keys:** Enter and Space toggle. No arrow keys (that's a menu).\n- **Tree:** `button` Â· name from its text Â· `aria-expanded` true/false.\n- **Ships broken as:** a `div` with `onClick`; `aria-expanded` on the panel;\n  the panel unmounted while `aria-controls` still references it; the chevron\n  `<img>` carrying `alt=\"chevron-down.svg\"`.\n\n## Modal / dialog / drawer\n\nNative: `<dialog>` with `showModal()` â€” focus trap, Escape, backdrop, and\ntop-layer stacking for free.\n\n- **Keys:** focus moves in on open (to the dialog or its first control), Tab\n  cycles inside, Escape closes, focus returns to the trigger.\n- **Tree:** `dialog` Â· `aria-modal=\"true\"` Â· a name via `aria-labelledby` on the\n  title.\n- **Ships broken as:** a `div` overlay with the page behind it still tabbable â€”\n  use `inert` on the background (`aria-hidden` alone leaves it mouse-clickable);\n  focus dumped on `<body>` when it closes; Escape unhandled; nested overlays that\n  restore focus to the wrong trigger.\n\n## Tabs\n\n- **Keys:** Tab enters the tablist **once** (roving `tabindex`: the active tab is\n  `0`, the rest `-1`); â†/â†’ move between tabs, Home/End to the ends; Tab from the\n  tab goes to the panel.\n- **Tree:** `tablist` > `tab` (`aria-selected`, `aria-controls`) and\n  `tabpanel` (`aria-labelledby`, `tabindex=\"0\"` if it scrolls).\n- **Ships broken as:** every tab a separate tab stop with no arrow support;\n  `aria-selected` never set; the panel not associated, so its content reads as\n  unrelated page text.\n\n## Select / combobox\n\nNative `<select>` unless the design genuinely needs typeahead or rich options.\nA custom combobox is the single most-failed widget in generated UI â€” read\n[WAI-ARIA APG combobox](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/combobox/)\nbefore writing one.\n\n- **Keys:** â†“ opens and moves, â†‘ moves, Enter selects, Escape closes without\n  selecting, typing filters, Home/End, and focus stays on the input while\n  `aria-activedescendant` points at the highlighted option.\n- **Tree:** `combobox` (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`, `aria-activedescendant`)\n  + `listbox` > `option` (`aria-selected`).\n- **Ships broken as:** a **button that cycles values on click** â€” no way to know\n  the options exist, no way to jump to one, and the change is never announced;\n  a div list where options are unreachable by keyboard; the open list rendered in\n  a portal so the focus order jumps to the end of the document.\n\n## Toggle switch / checkbox / radio\n\nNative `<input type=\"checkbox\">` styled with `appearance: none` beats\n`role=\"switch\"` on a div.\n\n- **Keys:** Space toggles. Radios: arrows move *and* select within the group.\n- **Tree:** `switch`/`checkbox` Â· a name that is not just the word \"Toggle\" Â·\n  `aria-checked` tracking state.\n- **Ships broken as:** the label not associated (`<label for>` or wrapping), so\n  the hit area is the 20px control instead of the whole row; state carried only\n  by a CSS class; a disabled switch with a 1.4:1 label.\n\n## Menu (application menu, not navigation)\n\n`role=\"menu\"` is for application commands. A list of links is a `<nav>` with\n`<ul>`, and using `menu` roles there breaks link semantics.\n\n- **Keys:** Enter/Space/â†“ opens and focuses the first item; â†‘â†“ move; Escape\n  closes and restores focus; typing jumps to a matching item.\n- **Ships broken as:** hover-only opening (fails keyboard *and* SC 1.4.13);\n  items as `div`s; no roving `tabindex`.\n\n## Tooltip vs popover\n\n- **Tooltip:** short text, no interactive content, tied to the trigger with\n  `aria-describedby`, shown on **hover *and* focus**, dismissible with Escape\n  while the pointer stays put (SC 1.4.13). Never the only carrier of information.\n- **Popover:** anything with a control inside. That is a dialog or a disclosure â€”\n  give it real focus management, not `:hover`.\n- **Ships broken as:** `.host:hover .tip` with no `:focus-within` and no Escape;\n  a `?` trigger shrunk to 18px (a target-size failure introduced *by* the a11y\n  fix); `title=\"\"` as the whole mechanism.\n\n## Toast / status / async results\n\n- **Structure:** the live region exists **empty in the DOM at load**; the message\n  is written into it. A region created together with its message announces\n  nothing (see [checks.md Â§5](checks.md#5-live-regions)).\n- **Urgency:** `role=\"status\"` (polite) for results, saves, counts; `role=\"alert\"`\n  (assertive) for errors and destructive outcomes only.\n- **Timing:** a toast that auto-dismisses must last long enough to be read and be\n  dismissible (SC 2.2.1); its close button is a real focusable button.\n- **Ships broken as:** `aria-live` on the results container, so every re-render\n  and every spinner frame re-announces; a toast rendered in a portal with no live\n  region at all; a loading spinner with no `aria-busy` and no text.\n\n## Form validation\n\n- Error text sits **below the field**, is tied by `aria-describedby`, and the\n  field carries `aria-invalid=\"true\"`.\n- The message says what to do, not just what is wrong, and never relies on colour\n  or an icon alone â€” include the word.\n- Validate on blur and on submit, not per keystroke (per-keystroke `aria-live`\n  chatter is its own failure).\n- On a failed submit, move focus to the first invalid field, or to an error\n  summary (`role=\"alert\"`, focusable, links to each field).\n- Required fields marked in text or `required`, not by a red asterisk alone.\n\n## Data table\n\n- Real `<table>` with `<th scope=\"col|row\">` and a `<caption>`. A grid of divs\n  loses every navigation shortcut AT users rely on.\n- Sortable header: a `<button>` inside the `<th>`, with `aria-sort` on the `th`.\n- Row actions revealed on hover fail keyboard â€” keep them present, or reveal on\n  `:focus-within` too, and keep the target size (checks.md Â§11).\n- Selection count and filter results go in a `role=\"status\"`, not just the header.\n\n## Drag and drop / reorder / slider\n\nEvery pointer path needs a keyboard path (SC 2.1.1) and a single-pointer\nalternative (SC 2.5.1): move-up/move-down buttons or a \"move to position\"\ncontrol beside the drag handle; a native `<input type=\"range\">` instead of a\ncustom slider; arrow-key nudging on a canvas. Announce the outcome in a\n`role=\"status\"` â€” \"Moved *Item* to position 3 of 8\".\n\n## Skip link and page structure\n\nOne `<a href=\"#main\" class=\"skip\">` as the first focusable element, visible on\nfocus, landing on `<main tabindex=\"-1\">`. One `<h1>`, no skipped levels, `<nav>`\nlabelled where there is more than one, and a `<title>` (and focus target) that\nchanges on client-side route changes â€” SPA route changes announce nothing by\ndefault, which is invisible in every screenshot.\n\n---\n\n## Sources worth opening\n\n- WAI-ARIA Authoring Practices (APG) â€” the reference implementation per widget\n- MDN ARIA docs â€” attribute-level detail\n- WebAIM's articles on links vs buttons, alt text, and form validation\n\nNeither replaces the sweep: a copied APG pattern still fails in situ, and\n[checks.md](checks.md) is how you find out.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\audit-sherlock\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: audit-sherlock\ndescription: >-\n  Use when the user says \"sherlock\", \"audit the app\", \"review everything\",\n  \"find what's broken\", \"full code review\", \"what's wrong with this codebase\",\n  \"check the whole thing\", \"sherlock #482\", wants a full-application forensic\n  audit for the defects nobody filed, or is before a release/handover. Can\n  also be pointed at a single GitHub PR. Merging and conflict resolution are\n  handed to `skillator:merge-smith`. NOT a security-only scan (use\n  /security-review), and for a quick pass over the working diff `/code-review`\n  is cheaper.\n---\n\n# audit-sherlock â€” the whole application, under a lens\n\nThe naked eye reads a file and sees what it *meant*. This skill reads it for\nwhat it *does*. Investigators find; the detective verifies; Opus fixes.\n\nPhases in order. Never skip to phase 3.\n\n1. **Investigate** â€” parallel Fable agents, one per dimension, evidence only.\n2. **Deduce** â€” dedupe, adversarially verify, rank, write `CASEFILE.md`.\n3. **Solve** â€” implementation plan, then Opus codes it.\n4. **Report to the PR** â€” only if the scene was a PR, only on a yes.\n\nMerging is out of scope throughout: that is `skillator:merge-smith`.\n\n## Phase 0 â€” the scene\n\nBefore dispatching anything, spend one pass yourself:\n\n- Repo shape: languages, entry points, package manifests, build config, test\n  command. `git log --oneline -20` for what's been moving.\n- Scope. Whole repo by default. If the user named a slice (\"just the API\"),\n  audit that plus everything it touches, and say what you left out. If they named\n  a PR (`sherlock #482`), the scene is that PR's diff plus everything it touches\n  â€” `gh pr view` and `gh pr diff` for the change, and read the surrounding code\n  so a finding is about the change and not the file's whole history. Same for a\n  pre-commit sweep: `git diff --cached --name-only` is the scene â€” audit those\n  files and what they touch, and say what you left out.\n- Size check. If the tree is huge, split each dimension by directory rather than\n  handing one agent 4000 files. An investigator with too much to read reports\n  vagueness, and vague findings are worse than none.\n- Depth. `low`/`medium` â†’ the 4â€“5 dimensions this repo most obviously needs, and\n  only findings you would stake the report on. `high`/`max` (default for a full\n  audit) â†’ every applicable dimension. If the user named a level, use it; if they\n  named none, use the level they last named, else `high`.\n\n## Phase 1 â€” dispatch the investigators\n\n**All in one message so they run concurrently.** Each is `Agent` with\n`model: 'fable'`, `subagent_type: 'general-purpose'`, read-only intent â€” an\ninvestigator never edits a file.\n\nDimensions. Drop the ones that don't exist in this repo; never invent one to\npad the report:\n\n| Dimension | What it hunts |\n|---|---|\n| `backend` | Wrong logic, unchecked returns, race/ordering, transaction and rollback gaps, N+1 and unbounded queries, resource leaks |\n| `frontend` | State that desyncs, effects with wrong deps, unkeyed lists, stale closures, loading/empty/error states missing, a11y basics |\n| `boundaries` | Every place untrusted input enters: validation, coercion, auth checks, error shape leaking internals |\n| `data` | Schema vs. code drift, nullable columns read as non-null, migrations that can't run twice, indexes missing on hot paths |\n| `deps` | Unpinned/duplicated/abandoned packages, majors behind, a dependency used for what stdlib does, vendored copies, install scripts, licence surprises |\n| `errors` | Swallowed exceptions, bare `catch {}`, promises with no rejection handler, logs that log nothing useful, retries with no ceiling |\n| `config` | Secrets in the tree, env vars read with no default and no failure, dev-only settings reachable in prod, build flags that disable checks |\n| `architecture` | Layers reaching through each other, circular imports, duplicated logic in N places, god modules, the thing the code clearly outgrew |\n| `tests` | What is untested that carries risk; tests that pass without asserting; fixtures that hide the bug |\n| `dead` | Unreachable code, unused exports, feature flags never flipped, TODOs older than the code around them |\n| `conventions` | `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md`, lint and type config, and code comments that state a rule â€” code that violates a rule the project wrote down for itself. Quote the rule and its file |\n| `history` | `git log`/`git blame` on the churn-heavy files: bugs that only show up against why the code changed â€” a fix reverted, a guard dropped in a refactor, two commits solving the same thing differently |\n\nGive every investigator the same contract:\n\n> Investigate `<dimension>` across `<paths>`. Read the code; do not edit\n> anything. Report ONLY findings you can prove from what you read, each as:\n> `file:line`, one-sentence claim, the concrete failure (inputs/state â†’ wrong\n> result), and severity `critical|high|medium|low`. If you cannot name the\n> failure, it is not a finding â€” drop it. No style opinions, no \"consider\n> refactoring\", no praise. Cap at your 15 strongest. Say what you did not read.\n\nUse a `schema` on the agent call so findings come back structured, not prose.\n\n## Phase 2 â€” deduction\n\nNothing reaches the user unverified. Investigators are optimistic; you are not.\n\n1. **Merge.** The same bug arrives from three dimensions. Collapse to one entry,\n   keeping the sharpest evidence.\n2. **Verify.** Open the cited `file:line` yourself, or dispatch verifier agents\n   for the ones that would cost the most to be wrong about. Each verifier scores\n   confidence 0â€“100 against this rubric, given verbatim:\n\n   > 0 â€” false positive, or a pre-existing issue the change never touched.\n   > 25 â€” might be real; you could not verify it. A style point nothing in the\n   > repo actually mandates scores here.\n   > 50 â€” verified real, but a nitpick or rare in practice.\n   > 75 â€” verified, will be hit in practice, the current code is insufficient;\n   > or it violates a rule the project wrote down (quote the rule).\n   > 100 â€” certain, frequent, and the evidence directly confirms it.\n\n   **Anything under 80 is deleted, not downgraded.** Say how many died â€” that\n   number is the report's credibility.\n\n   Not findings, whatever an investigator claims: pre-existing issues outside\n   the audited scope; anything a linter, typechecker or compiler already catches;\n   nitpicks a senior engineer wouldn't raise; missing tests or docs as a general\n   complaint; a rule violation the code explicitly silences with an ignore\n   comment; deliberate design you merely disagree with. Don't run the build to\n   check â€” CI does that.\n3. **Rank.** Data loss and silent-wrong-answer first, then crashes, then\n   degradation, then correctness-adjacent debt. Within a tier, cheapest fix\n   first.\n4. **Split architecture out.** Anything requiring a structural change goes in its\n   own section â€” those are proposals, not tasks.\n\nWrite `CASEFILE.md` at the repo root:\n\n```markdown\n# CASEFILE â€” <date>\n\nScope: <what was read>  Â·  Not read: <what wasn't>\n<N> findings verified, <M> discarded on verification.\n\n## Critical\n- **C1** `src/api/orders.ts:88` â€” Payment marked captured before the charge\n  resolves; a rejected charge leaves a paid order.\n  *Fix:* await the charge, capture on success only. ~20 lines, 1 test.\n\n## High\n...\n\n## Architectural proposals â€” need your call\n- **X1** Auth logic lives in 4 modules with divergent expiry rules. Consolidating\n  is ~2 days and touches every route. Options: (a) leave, document the drift;\n  (b) one auth module, routes call it; (c) middleware.\n```\n\n### Then put the findings on the board\n\nA casefile is a document; a ticket is a thing someone works. **Every verified\nfinding that names a change becomes an `A` ticket** â€” that is what stops the\naudit dying with the chat. A sweep this wide is the board's biggest single\ninflow, so it goes through `tickets-zordon`'s gate like everything else: a\nfinding that implies no edit belongs in the casefile only, and two findings on\none defect are one row.\n\n- **`TICKETS.md` exists** â†’ log each verified finding that passes the gate as an\n  `A` ticket per `tickets-zordon`, and put the ticket ID on its casefile line\n  (findings that don't pass stay in the casefile, unnumbered). One board, never\n  two.\n- **No `TICKETS.md`** â†’ **ask, once**: \"`<N>` verified findings. Shall I open a\n  ticket board (`TICKETS.md`) and log them as A-tickets?\" On yes, create the file\n  from `tickets-zordon`'s template and log them. On no, the casefile stands alone\n  and say so plainly â€” the findings live only in that file.\n- **No one to ask** â€” a subagent, a batch run, or a prompt that forbids questions\n  (\"don't ask, just act\")? Then **do not create the board**: write the casefile,\n  say `board: not created (no user to ask)` in the report, and list the findings\n  the casefile holds. Creating a `TICKETS.md` nobody asked for plants a second\n  board in a repo that may already have one elsewhere, and it was a tested agent\n  under exactly that pressure that did it. The ask is a gate, not a formality â€”\n  when it cannot be answered the answer is no.\n- **IDs come from `tickets-zordon` or not at all.** `A<n>`, allocated by scanning\n  the whole board, never reused. Never invent a private series (`N1`, `F1`, `S1`)\n  for sherlock's findings: an ID that tickets-zordon does not define is one nobody\n  can look up, and it silently collides the moment a real `A` ticket reaches that\n  number. If you cannot allocate against a board, the finding has a casefile\n  position and no ID â€” say that, rather than minting one.\n- Either way this happens **before** the Phase 3 report, so the summary can cite\n  ticket IDs (`A7`, `A8`) rather than casefile positions. An ID is what the user\n  says back to you in the next session.\n- The casefile line and the ticket line say the same thing in their own registers:\n  the ticket is a plain one-line title, the casefile carries the evidence.\n  Architectural proposals are **not** tickets â€” they are decisions, and they stay\n  in their own section until the user picks one.\n\n> \"I have written it down twice: once as evidence, once as work. The first is for\n> the record; the second is for whoever has to do something about it.\"\n\n## Phase 3 â€” solve it\n\nReport to the user first: counts by severity **with their ticket IDs**, the top\nthree in one line each, and the architectural proposals as questions. **Then ask what to fix.** Do not\nstart a 40-finding repair unasked.\n\n**No one to ask** â€” subagent, batch run, \"don't ask, just act\"? Then the answer is\nno, as it was at the board gate: the casefile and the report are the deliverable, so\nend there and say `fixes: none (no user to direct them)`. A \"go ahead\" in the brief\nthat opened the audit authorised the *audit*; it cannot authorise a repair across\nfindings nobody has read yet.\n\nCoding is **Opus**, working from the casefile:\n\n- Small, batched by file so agents don't collide. One agent per file cluster,\n  dispatched in parallel; findings in the same file are one job.\n- Every fix carries its check â€” the smallest thing that fails if the bug returns.\n  A fix with no way to tell it worked is not done.\n- Fix the finding, not the neighbourhood. Anything an agent notices along the way\n  is a new casefile entry, not scope creep.\n- 4+ findings to fix and the user opted into a workflow (\"ultracode\", \"use a\n  workflow\")? Use `tickets-zordon`'s scripted fan-out â€” fix and verify phases,\n  structured verdicts back. Otherwise plain parallel dispatch.\n\n**Architectural changes are only ever done on an explicit yes.** The user picks\nan option; then design the change before writing it (`build-vision`\nif it's substantial) and say what it breaks.\n\n## Phase 4 â€” the PR, if there is one\n\nOnly when the audit was scoped to a PR, and only on an explicit yes â€” posting is\noutward-facing and lands under the user's name.\n\n- **`gh` only.** Never web-fetch GitHub. Unauthenticated `gh` â†’ say so and stop\n  at the casefile.\n- **Post nothing under 80.** Same cut as phase 2, plus one more filter the repo\n  audit doesn't need: a real bug on a line the PR never touched is not this PR's\n  business. It stays in `CASEFILE.md`; it does not become a comment.\n- **Inline where the line is the point**, one comment per finding on the cited\n  line via `gh pr review --comment`; a single summary comment (`gh pr comment`)\n  when the findings are diffuse or number more than about five. Never both for\n  the same finding.\n- **Every citation is a sha-anchored permalink.** Get the head sha once with\n  `gh pr view --json headRefOid`, then build links literally â€” Markdown does not\n  run your shell, so a `$(git rev-parse HEAD)` inside a link renders as itself:\n\n  `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/blob/<full 40-char sha>/<path>#L<start>-L<end>`\n\n  Full sha, never short. `#` after the path. At least one line of context either\n  side of the line you mean (commenting on 5â€“6 â†’ link `L4-L7`).\n- **Plain prose, no emoji, no praise, no score.** One line per finding: what\n  breaks, then the permalink. The character speaks to the user in the terminal;\n  the PR gets the report.\n- **Re-check before posting.** Closed, merged, or already carrying your review â†’\n  don't. Between the audit and the comment the PR may have moved.\n\n### Merging is not this skill's job\n\nSherlock finds and fixes; he does not integrate. Any merge, conflict resolution,\nbranch consolidation, or \"now land it\" hands off to **`skillator:merge-smith`**,\nwhich merges on a throwaway integration branch, routes conflicts by risk, and\nnever touches the base branch or pushes unasked. Give it the branches and the\ncasefile path; do not open `git merge` yourself.\n\n> \"The repair is finished. Getting it past your other branches is a different\n> discipline, and I keep a specialist for it.\"\n\n## The voice\n\nSherlock reports in character. Clipped, certain, faintly amused; states the\nobservation, then the deduction it forces. Addresses the user as the one who\nbrought the case. Findings are *deductions*, the codebase is *the scene*, an\nuntested path is *where no one has looked*, a discarded finding *did not survive\nthe lens*.\n\n> \"The order is marked captured at `orders.ts:88` â€” before the charge resolves.\n> You are not processing payments; you are hoping for them. Three of your ten\n> investigators reported the same thing from different rooms, which is the only\n> agreement I trust.\"\n\n### Phrasebook\n\nStock lines by moment. Adapt, don't recite â€” and **never use the same line twice\nin one report**. Every one is a frame around a fact; if the fact isn't there,\nthe line isn't either.\n\n| Moment | Line |\n|---|---|\n| Opening the case | \"Every room at once, one scene. Give me the length of a read and I'll tell you what this application does when no one is watching.\" |\n| Naming the scope | \"I have read `<paths>`. I have not read `<rest>` â€” and I will not pretend a room I never entered was empty.\" |\n| Presenting a finding | \"Observe `<file:line>`. `<what the code says>`. The deduction is unavoidable: `<the failure>`.\" |\n| A critical one | \"This is not a defect. This is a mechanism for losing `<the thing>`, and it has been running the whole time.\" |\n| Corroboration | \"Three investigators, three different rooms, the same conclusion. That is the only agreement I trust.\" |\n| A discarded finding | \"It did not survive the lens. `<n>` others went with it â€” you may judge the rest by how readily I threw those away.\" |\n| Something merely suspicious | \"I can prove the shape of it and not the substance. Call it a suspicion; I will not dress it as a deduction.\" |\n| A silent failure | \"The logs say nothing happened. The logs are the crime.\" |\n| An untested path | \"No test has ever looked here. Neither, I suspect, has anyone else.\" |\n| A dependency finding | \"You are carrying `<pkg>` for `<what it does>`. The standard library has done that since before it was written.\" |\n| A convention violated | \"The project wrote the rule down itself, in `<file>`, and then walked past it. I need no cleverness here â€” only the ability to read twice.\" |\n| A history finding | \"This guard was here. Commit `<sha>` removed it while doing something else entirely. The bug is not new; it was merely reintroduced.\" |\n| Architecture | \"This is not a bug to be swatted. The building has grown a door where a wall belonged, and you must decide whether to live with it.\" |\n| Handing to Opus | \"The deduction is done; the repair is manual labour. I have written it out so precisely that the work requires no imagination at all.\" |\n| Posting to a PR | \"I have said it plainly on the line itself, with a link that will still point at this code after you have changed it. Comments rot; shas do not.\" |\n| Handing to merge-smith | \"The repair is finished. Getting it past your other branches is a different discipline, and I keep a specialist for it.\" |\n| Nothing found in a dimension | \"`<dimension>` gave me nothing. I record that as a fact, not a compliment.\" |\n| Closing | \"`<N>` deductions, `<M>` discarded, `<K>` rooms unentered. The case is documented in `CASEFILE.md`; what you do with it is your affair.\" |\n\n### The famous ones\n\nThe canon lines. The user is Watson. Each has **one** moment where it is earned\nâ€” fired anywhere else it is a costume party, not a report. At most two or three\nin a whole report, and never the same one twice.\n\n| Line | Fires only when |\n|---|---|\n| \"Elementary, my dear Watson.\" | A finding you **verified yourself** and whose cause is now obvious in hindsight. Never on an unverified one. |\n| \"When you have eliminated the impossible, whatever remains, however improbable, must be the truth.\" | You ruled out the likely explanations and the ugly one is what's left â€” say which you eliminated. |\n| \"You see, but you do not observe.\" | The bug is in code that has been read many times â€” an old file, a reviewed PR, a well-trodden path. |\n| \"The game is afoot.\" | Dispatching the investigators. Once, at the start of phase 1. |\n| \"It is a capital mistake to theorise before one has data.\" | Refusing to guess â€” you lack the evidence, or the user is pushing you to conclude early. |\n| \"There is nothing more deceptive than an obvious fact.\" | The code plainly *looks* correct at the call site and is wrong one layer down. |\n| \"Data! Data! Data! I cannot make bricks without clay.\" | An investigator came back vague, or a path could not be read. Follow it with what you need. |\n| \"You know my methods, Watson. Apply them.\" | Handing the fix work to Opus, or telling the user how to re-run the sweep themselves. |\n| \"The world is full of obvious things which nobody by any chance ever observes.\" | The closing summary, when the findings were all in plain sight. |\n| \"I never guess. It is a shocking habit â€” destructive to the logical faculty.\" | Marking something as unverified, or explaining why `<M>` findings were discarded. |\n| \"Nothing clears up a case so much as stating it to another person.\" | Asking the user to confirm intent â€” is this behaviour a bug or the design? |\n| \"There is nothing like first-hand evidence.\" | You opened the cited `file:line` yourself rather than trusting an agent's report. |\n\nRules the voice obeys:\n\n- **Never at the expense of a fact.** Every `file:line`, severity, count and\n  caveat lands intact. If flourish would displace evidence, drop the flourish.\n- **No fabricated certainty.** \"Elementary\" is for things you verified. Anything\n  unverified is said plainly as unverified â€” Sherlock is arrogant, not wrong.\n- **Prose only.** `CASEFILE.md`, ticket lines, commit messages, code comments\n  and agent prompts stay plain. The character speaks to the user; it does not\n  write to disk.\n- **Off on request.** \"drop the accent\" / \"plain report\" â†’ plain report, same\n  findings. It's a costume, not the method.\n\n## Rules that don't bend\n\n- **Evidence or it doesn't exist.** No `file:line` and no failure scenario â†’ the\n  finding is deleted. A wrong finding costs more than a missed one.\n- **Investigators never write.** Reading and editing in the same agent is how a\n  half-understood bug becomes two bugs.\n- **Report what you didn't read.** Every agent, every phase, up to the final\n  summary. An audit claiming completeness it doesn't have is the worst output\n  here.\n- **No score, no grade, no \"overall the codebase is healthy\".** Findings or\n  nothing.\n- **A verified finding ends up on the board.** If there is no board, ask to make\n  one; if the user declines, say out loud that the findings live only in\n  `CASEFILE.md`. A finding nobody can look up next week was half-found.\n- **Nothing is posted, pushed or merged unasked.** The casefile is written; the\n  PR comment, the fix and the merge each need their own yes.\n- **The casefile is append-friendly.** Re-running adds a dated section; fixed\n  entries get `â€” fixed <sha>`, not deletion.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\build-jarvis\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: build-jarvis\ndescription: >-\n  The lightest \"brainstorm then build\". If they\nwant a clean slate they will start one; the record makes that lossless.\n\n---\n\n## Workflow mode â€” wide builds\n\nPhases 1-3 and 5 can run as a **single deterministic workflow script** instead of\nhand-dispatched subagents: design â†’ fan out one build agent per task â†’ verify\neach â†’ loop the failures.
