Stay with plain dispatch\nfor 1-3 sequential tasks.\n\nThe ceremony does not move into the script: write the design file **before** the\nworkflow and pass its path in `args`; run `handoff-cortana` and Checkpoint A in this\nsession, around the call.\n\n## Rules\n\n- **Design â†’ `deep`-tier agent, build â†’ `build`-tier agent(s).** Don't design or\n  code in the orchestrator session (except writing the session record).\n- **The design/record file is the source of truth** â€” pass its path to build\n  agents; don't rely on chat context outliving a compact/trim.\n- **Handoff before any context loss.** Run `handoff-cortana` before Checkpoint A â€”\n  never shed context without a verified handoff on disk.\n- **Never invoke slash commands.** You cannot run `/compact` or `/clear`; don't\n  claim to. Context is shed by delegating with the design file path, and by the\n  host's own auto-compaction. Ask the user to `/compact` only if context is\n  genuinely tight, once. Never clear or reset the session at all.\n- **Use host-native dispatch.** Agent tool, Task tool, background subagents,\n  `rlm(...)` â€” per platforms.md, never a tool that isn't available. Where the\n  host has no delegation at all, run the phases sequentially in-session with the\n  design file as the handover, and say so.\n- **Autonomous within phases, honest across them.** No confirmation gate between\n  design and build, but if a phase fails or an agent returns nothing, say so and\n  stop.\n- **This skill is the process â€” don't stack another in front of it.**\n  Everything `superpowers` would contribute here is merged into `PRACTICE.md` â€”\n  brainstorming (Â§1), writing/executing-plans (Â§2-3), TDD and\n  subagent/parallel dispatch (Â§4), verification-before-completion (Â§5),\n  requesting- and receiving-code-review (Â§6), systematic-debugging (Â§7),\n  worktrees and finishing-a-branch (Â§8). Running the originals first re-runs the\n  same process and spends the budget the build needs. PRACTICE.md's closing\n  table lists what is deliberately *not* in it.\n- Want all-build-tier with no ceremony? Use build-ultron. Want\n  to offload simple build tasks to a fast tier? Use build-jarvis.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\build-vision\\references\\platforms.md\r\n# Platform adapters â€” build-vision\n\n**Read the root `PLATFORMS.md` first â€” it is the authority and this file does not\nrepeat it.** It owns host detection, skill paths, how you delegate and switch\ntier on each host, the tier vocabulary (`deep` / `build` / `cheap` /\n`orchestrator`), the per-host model slug for each tier, context-checkpoint\nmechanics, hooks, frontmatter portability, and the non-negotiables. Anything you\nneed about *a host* is there; anything you need about *this skill's run* is here.\n\nWhere it is: **the repo/plugin root**, two levels above this skill's directory\nonce installed (`../../PLATFORMS.md` from this file â€” skills land at\n`<dest>/<name>/`, the docs at `<dest>/`), or `../../../PLATFORMS.md` inside the\nskillator repo, where skills live under `skills/`.\n\n## This skill's three seats, in root tier terms\n\n| Seat in this skill | Root tier | Job in this run | Must not |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **Design pass** | `deep` | Approaches, tradeoffs, contracts, the task breakdown, written to the design file | Write production code or edit the repo |\n| **Build pass** | `build` | Implement exactly what the design file says | Redesign, or expand scope beyond the design file |\n| **You, the session** | `orchestrator` | Dispatch, write the session `.md`, run handoff and Checkpoint A | Design or code directly, bar tiny edits to the session record |\n\nTake the slug for `deep` and for `build` off the root Role tiers table, on your\nhost's column. Use two *different* models where the host has them; where the\ndesign pass and the build pass end up on the same model, record that in the\nsession file.\n\n## Checkpoint A â€” shedding context\n\nThe one checkpoint this skill has, and it is not a host feature â€” it is a rule\nabout ordering:\n\n1. It runs **only after `handoff-cortana` has written a verified handoff to disk.** Never\n   shed context without one.\n2. It is **never `/compact`** â€” a skill cannot invoke a slash command. The move is\n   always the same: carry the design forward **as a file path**, not as chat\n   history, and let each subagent hold its own context.\n3. Only if the orchestrator's context is still genuinely tight do you reach for\n   the host's own context checkpoint (root `PLATFORMS.md`, *Context checkpoint*\n   row) â€” and on hosts where that is something the *user* must type, say so once\n   and continue from the session file rather than waiting.\n\n**There is no Checkpoint B.** Do not clear, reset or start a new session at the\nend of a run â€” a skill can't, and the session `.md` plus the handoff already make\na fresh start lossless whenever the *user* wants one.\n\n## Parallel builds\n\nFan the build pass out wherever the host delegates in parallel. Two tasks of\n*this* design that would touch the same tree get a git worktree first\n(`PRACTICE.md` Â§8) â€” on every host, including the ones with no sandbox of their\nown.\n\n## User overrides\n\nA platform or model the user names wins over everything above. Record it in the\nsession file header:\n\n```\nPLATFORM: <host>\nDESIGN_MODEL: <slug>\nBUILD_MODEL: <slug>\n```\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\deploy-merlin\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: deploy-merlin\ndescription: >-\n  Use when the user says \"prep the deployment\", \"set up deploy\", \"deployment\n  wizard\", \"get this ready to ship to my server\", \"scaffold the deploy\", or is\n  about to stand up a new app on a Linux VPS with the standard single-VPS\n  pattern (Docker Compose + nginx + GitHub Actions, one-push deploy,\n  maintenance gate, TLS via Let's Encrypt). NOT for non-VPS / managed-PaaS\n  deploys, or for the actual server administration.\n---\n\n# deploy-merlin â€” prep the standard VPS deployment\n\nRuns as a **wizard**: one step at a time, confirm before moving on. It gathers the\ndeployment-specific values, scaffolds the filled-in config/scripts, and hands the\nuser an ordered checklist of the manual steps only a human should do.\n\n**Target pattern:** a single Linux VPS where **nginx** is the only public-facing\nservice (static frontend + `/api/` proxy + maintenance gate + TLS), **Docker\nCompose** owns the app stack (api, workers, postgres, redis, object storage), and\n**GitHub Actions** SSHes in on `push main` to run an idempotent `deploy.sh`.\n\n**Public-safe & secure by construction:**\n- **Prompt for every specific** â€” nothing about a real deployment is baked in.\n- **Never collect secret *values*.** DB/storage passwords, SSH private keys, API\n  tokens are *never* typed into chat or written into repo files. The wizard records\n  their **names** and tells the user to place them in a server-side `.env` (mode 600)\n  or a GitHub secret â€” done by the user, not the wizard.\n- **Never touch remote/DNS/credentials.** The wizard prepares; the human executes\n  every step that reaches a server, DNS provider, or secret store.\n\n## Step 0 â€” Orient\n\n1. Detect the project: is there a frontend (build step), a backend (health route),\n   a `docker-compose.yml`, existing `deploy/` or `.github/workflows/`? Note what\n   already exists so the wizard fills gaps, not overwrites blindly.\n2. **Authoritative templates:** if the project root (or the user) has a\n   `DEPLOYMENT_PLAYBOOK.md`, treat it as the source of truth for artifact *contents*\n   and only substitute the wizard's answers into it. Otherwise generate artifacts\n   from the structure in Step 2. (Never copy private values out of any playbook.)\n3. Confirm the platform is this VPS pattern; if it's a managed PaaS (Vercel, Fly,\n   Render, etc.), stop â€” this wizard is for the self-hosted VPS pattern.\n\n## Step 1 â€” Interview (the wizard Q&A)\n\nAsk in small, grounded batches; use multiple-choice / sensible defaults where you\ncan; confirm each batch. Collect these values (names only for secrets):\n\n- **Identity:** `APP_SLUG` (used in `/opt/<slug>`, filenames, keys), `GITHUB_ORG`,\n  `GITHUB_REPO`, `APP_DIR` (default `/opt/<APP_SLUG>`).\n- **Server / SSH:** `DEPLOY_HOST` (IP/host), `DEPLOY_USER`, `DEPLOY_PORT` (default 22).\n- **Domain / TLS:** `SUBDOMAIN` (public hostname), `CERTBOT_EMAIL` (Let's Encrypt reg).\n- **nginx / ports:** `API_PORT` (host port nginx proxies to, e.g. `127.0.0.1:18000`),\n  `CONTAINER_API_PORT` (in-container app port, e.g. 8000), `HEALTH_PATH`\n  (default `/api/health`), `MAX_BODY_SIZE` (nginx `client_max_body_size`, e.g. `2G`).\n- **Stack:** which compose services (`STACK_SERVICES`: api / workers / postgres /\n  redis / object-storage). For each present: `DB_NAME`, `DB_USER`; object-storage\n  `bucket`. **DB_PASSWORD and storage access/secret keys are SECRET â†’ record the\n  variable name only, never the value.**\n- **App-specific:** `CHILD_TABLES` (tables for restore-append backups), any per-app\n  localStorage keys.\n\nState back the collected config (secrets shown as `<set on server>`) and get a\nconfirm before scaffolding.\n\n**Nobody to interview?** A subagent, a batch run, or \"just set it up, I'm out\" â€”\na deployment's specifics cannot be inferred, and a scaffold built on invented ones\nis worse than no scaffold: it looks configured and points at a host that isn't\ntheirs. Don't scaffold. Write Step 1 out as an answer sheet, filled in where the\nrepo actually tells you (`APP_SLUG`, ports, `STACK_SERVICES`, `HEALTH_PATH`) and\nblank where only the user can (`DEPLOY_HOST`, `SUBDOMAIN`, `CERTBOT_EMAIL`, secret\nnames), print the Step 3 checklist beneath it, and stop there. The wizard prepares\nand the human executes â€” with no human in the loop, preparing is the whole job.\n\n## Step 2 â€” Scaffold the artifacts (prep)\n\nGenerate these into the project, substituting the answers (secrets stay as\nplaceholders / env references â€” never literal values). List every file you write.\nIf a `DEPLOYMENT_PLAYBOOK.md` is present, take contents from it; otherwise follow\nthis structure:\n\n- `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` â€” on `push main`: SSH to `DEPLOY_HOST:DEPLOY_PORT`\n  as `DEPLOY_USER` using secret `DEPLOY_SSH_KEY`, run `APP_DIR/deploy/deploy.sh`.\n- `deploy/deploy.sh` â€” idempotent first-run + update: install base pkgs, `ufw` allow\n  22/80/443, clone-or-`git reset --hard origin/main`, **maintenance gate ON** (trap\n  clears it on EXIT/ERR), `docker system prune -af` then frontend `npm ci && build`,\n  pre-deploy DB snapshot, `docker compose up -d --build`, poll `HEALTH_PATH` (â‰¤45Ã—2s,\n  dump logs + fail on timeout), **gate OFF**, `envsubst` nginx template â†’ reload.\n- `deploy/nginx.conf.template` â€” server for `SUBDOMAIN`: static root, `/api/` â†’\n  `127.0.0.1:API_PORT`, maintenance-flag rewrite, `client_max_body_size MAX_BODY_SIZE`.\n- `deploy/maintenance.html` â€” static page, auto-refresh ~10s, served with 200. Wears\n  the app's own brand where one exists; inventing a look falls under the shared design\n  floor `references/anti-slop.md` beside the installed skills â€” try\n  `../references/anti-slop.md` first (the `install.sh` layout), then\n  `../../references/anti-slop.md` (git checkout, plugin cache); neither resolves â†’ say\n  so in one line and stay on the system font stack.\n- `docker-compose.yml` + `deploy/docker-compose.prod.yml` â€” base + prod overlay for\n  the chosen `STACK_SERVICES`; secrets referenced from `.env`, never inlined.\n- Backend **health endpoint** (`HEALTH_PATH` â†’ `{ok:true}`, no auth/DB) if missing.\n- If a frontend: `src/lib/maintenance.ts` (sticky content-type poller) +\n  `MaintenanceModal.tsx` (overlay, OK reloads).\n- Optional `.githooks/pre-push` â€” best-effort DB snapshot on push (never blocks).\n\nThen **offer to run only the safe LOCAL steps** with per-step confirmation: write the\nfiles, `chmod +x deploy/deploy.sh`, stage them. **Do NOT** `git push`, run anything\nagainst the server, or write any secret. Confirm before each write if overwriting.\n\n## Step 3 â€” Manual handoff checklist (the human-only steps)\n\nPrint an ordered, copy-pasteable checklist with the user's non-secret values filled\nin (secrets as placeholders). These are the steps the wizard will NOT do:\n\n1. **Deploy key** â€” `ssh-keygen` a deploy keypair; add the public key to\n   `DEPLOY_USER@DEPLOY_HOST:~/.ssh/authorized_keys`.\n2. **GitHub secrets** â€” add `DEPLOY_HOST`, `DEPLOY_USER`, `DEPLOY_PORT`,\n   `DEPLOY_SSH_KEY` (the private key) in the repo's Actions secrets.\n3. **DNS** â€” create an A record `SUBDOMAIN â†’ DEPLOY_HOST`; wait for propagation.\n4. **First bootstrap** â€” ensure `DEPLOY_USER` has sudo and `APP_DIR` is writable\n   (or let the first `deploy.sh` create it).\n5. **Server `.env`** â€” place `.env` (mode 600) in `APP_DIR` with the app secrets +\n   `DB_PASSWORD` / storage keys. The runner holds no app secrets.\n6. **Firewall** â€” confirm the provider firewall also allows 22/80/443.\n7. **TLS** â€” first issuance: `certbot --nginx -d SUBDOMAIN -m CERTBOT_EMAIL`\n   (needs live DNS + port 80 reachable); auto-renews after.\n8. **Go live** â€” `git push main`, watch the Actions run, confirm the health check\n   and `https://SUBDOMAIN` load.\n\n## Step 4 â€” Verify first deploy\n\nWalk the user through the first `push main` â†’ Actions â†’ SSH â†’ `deploy.sh`. The deploy\nis proven only when: the Action is green, `HEALTH_PATH` returns ok on the box, the\nmaintenance flag is cleared, and `https://SUBDOMAIN` serves the app over valid TLS.\nIf the health poll fails, the deploy script dumps container logs â€” read those, fix,\nre-push.\n\n## Rules\n\n- **Wizard discipline:** one step at a time, confirm before advancing; never dump the\n  whole thing and call it done.\n- **Secrets are names, not values.** Never ask the user to paste a password/key into\n  chat, never write a secret into a repo file. They go to the server `.env` / GitHub\n  secrets, by the user.\n- **Prep, don't execute the risky parts.** No `git push`, no SSH to the server, no DNS\n  change, no credential entry â€” those are the user's manual steps by design.\n- **Public-safe:** every deployment-specific value is prompted, never hardcoded. Use\n  `example.com` / `203.0.113.10`-style placeholders in any illustration.\n- **Idempotent artifacts:** the generated `deploy.sh` must be safe to re-run (clone-or-\n  update, prune-before-build, trap-clears-maintenance) â€” that's the whole point.\n- Overwriting an existing deploy file? Show the diff and confirm first.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\deploy-niyoj\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: deploy-niyoj\ndescription: >-\n  Use when the user says \"niyoj\", \"set up niyoj deploy\", \"deploy without CI\",\n  \"manual deploy\", \"no github actions deploy\", \"one-button deploy from my\n  laptop\", when converting a project off `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`, or\n  when a NiYoj deploy failed and needs diagnosing. The target is a single-VPS\n  app (Docker Compose + nginx + maintenance gate + Let's Encrypt) deployed\n  over SSH from the NiYoj desktop app, with no CI runner. NOT for CI-triggered\n  deploys (use `deploy-merlin`) or managed-PaaS targets.\n---\n\n# deploy-niyoj â€” one button, one SSH connection, no CI\n\n`git push` triggers nothing. Push is \"save my work\"; Deploy is \"ship it\". Two\ndeliberate acts, and that separation is the entire point of this variant.\n\n```\n   your laptop\n   â”Œâ”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”\n   â”‚  NiYoj  [ Deploy ]  â”‚\n   â””â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”¬â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”˜\n              â”‚ ssh -i <key> -o BatchMode=yes   (script on stdin)\n              â–¼\n   VPS  /opt/<app>/deploy/deploy.sh\n                    â”‚\n   â”Œâ”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”´â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”\n nginx (front-door)          docker compose\n â”œâ”€ static frontend (dist/)   â”œâ”€ api\n â”œâ”€ /api/ â†’ 127.0.0.1:18000   â”œâ”€ workers\n â”œâ”€ maintenance gate (flag)   â”œâ”€ postgres\n â””â”€ TLS via certbot           â””â”€ redis\n```\n\nEverything downstream of the trigger is identical to `deploy-merlin`'s CI\nplaybook. Adopting this one on a project that already follows that pattern means\n**deleting `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`** and adding the app to NiYoj â€”\n`deploy.sh`, nginx, the maintenance gate and the backups all stay as they are.\n\nFull file templates (`deploy.sh`, `nginx.conf.template`, the SSH invocation, the\napp entry) are in `references/templates.md`. Read it before scaffolding; do not\nretype them from memory.\n\n## The one constraint everything else follows from\n\nNiYoj connects with `BatchMode=yes`. **A deploy can never answer a prompt.**\n\nThat single fact rules out: a passphrase on the deploy key, a sudo password,\n`read` anywhere in a script, `apt` without `DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive`,\n`certbot` without `--non-interactive`, a `docker login` prompt. Each of those\ndoesn't error â€” it *hangs*, holding the maintenance flag up until the connection\ntimes out. When a NiYoj deploy stalls with no output, this is the first thing to\ncheck.\n\n## Phase 0 â€” which job is this\n\nAsk only if the request is ambiguous; usually it's obvious:\n\n- **Scaffold** â€” new project, no deploy artifacts. Phases 1â€“3.\n- **Convert** â€” project has `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`. Phase 1 (the\n  conversion), then verify.\n- **Diagnose** â€” a deploy failed or hung. Skip to *Diagnosing a failed deploy*.\n\nEstablish the app slug, the subdomain, the server, and the branch that ships.\nNever scaffold against a guessed subdomain.\n\n## Phase 1 â€” converting off CI\n\n1. **Delete `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`.** Not disable, not rename â€” delete.\n   Two triggers racing on the same `git reset --hard` is a corrupted checkout\n   waiting to happen, and \"keeping it just in case\" is exactly how that races.\n2. **Move the DB snapshot from `.githooks/pre-push` into `deploy.sh`.** With\n   push and deploy separated, a pre-push hook fires at a moment that has nothing\n   to do with production. Drop the hook; the pre-deploy snapshot inside the\n   script is now the guarantee.\n3. Everything else â€” `deploy.sh`, nginx template, maintenance page, backup\n   functions, health endpoint â€” stays byte-identical. Say so rather than\n   rewriting working files.\n\n### What \"delete the workflow\" does and does not mean\n\n**Delete the workflow that deploys. Keep â€” or add â€” a workflow that only checks.**\n\nCI bundles two things people assume are one: **verification** (build it clean and\nrun the tests on a machine that isn't yours) and the **deployment trigger** (and\nif that passed, ship it). This skill replaces the second. It has no quarrel with\nthe first.\n\n| Workflow | Verdict | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| Deploys â€” SSHes to the box, runs `deploy.sh`, holds `DEPLOY_SSH_KEY` | **Delete** | Two triggers racing one `git reset --hard` is a corrupted checkout. This is the rule, and it is absolute |\n| Only builds, tests, lints, typechecks â€” never touches the server, holds no deploy secret | **Keep** | It cannot race: it never reaches the box. It gives you a red X *before* you press Deploy |\n\nThe distinction is **server access, not the file's existence**. A workflow with no\n`ssh`, no `DEPLOY_SSH_KEY`, and no `appleboy/ssh-action` is inert as far as this\nplaybook is concerned.\n\nWorth saying out loud to a solo developer, who is the usual audience here: the\nverification half of CI is the half that still pays when you work alone. Nothing\nabout \"you forgot to commit a file\" or \"that dep was only in your global install\"\ndepends on team size â€” and solo is arguably worse, since nobody else ever pulls\nyour branch and hits the missing file. It is the *trigger* half that stops paying,\nbecause push-to-ship is a coordination mechanism and there is nobody to coordinate\nwith. So when converting, **offer to keep or add a check-only workflow** rather\nthan assuming the user wants no CI at all. If they'd rather have nothing, that is\ntheirs to choose â€” say what they're giving up in one line and move on.\n\nIf a check-only workflow exists, the release ritual gains a step between push and\nDeploy: **wait for the green check.** That is the whole benefit; skipping it means\npaying the CI minutes for nothing.\n\n## Phase 2 â€” the artifacts\n\nScaffold into the repo, filled in with the real values from Phase 0:\n\n| File | Notes |\n|---|---|\n| `deploy/deploy.sh` | Ten phases, in order. `references/templates.md` |\n| `deploy/nginx.conf.template` | Maintenance-flag rewrite is the part that matters |\n| `deploy/maintenance.html` | Auto-refreshing static page; brand it |\n| `GET /api/health` | Public, no auth, no DB touch, returns `{\"ok\": true}` |\n| `useMaintenanceMode` + modal | Poll health, watch content-type, latch sticky |\n| localStorage persistence | In-progress UI state, saved on every change |\n\n**\"Brand it\" means the app's brand.** `maintenance.html` and the modal wear what the\napp already ships â€” its logo, palette and type; a maintenance page is not the moment\nto invent a look. Nothing to inherit and you must pick â†’ the shared design floor\ngoverns the pick: read `references/anti-slop.md` beside the installed skills, trying\n`../references/anti-slop.md` first (the `install.sh` layout), then\n`../../references/anti-slop.md` (git checkout, plugin cache). Neither resolves â†’ say\nso in one line and stay on the system font stack and the app's own colours.\n\n**Idempotency is the whole design.** Every phase is *detect â†’ skip-if-done â†’\notherwise-do*. A new app and the 200th redeploy take the identical path; the\nclone is the only conditional and everything after it is a hard reset. Re-running\nmust be safe at any moment â€” which is exactly what happens when someone presses\nDeploy twice because they weren't sure the first one took.\n\n**Exit non-zero on failure.** The exit code is NiYoj's only verdict: it colours\nthe app card red and stamps the time. `set -euo pipefail` at the top, `die` on\nevery gate, and `|| true` **only** where failure is genuinely fine (the prune,\nthe submodule update). A script that swallows errors reports a green deploy of a\nbroken box.\n\n### The maintenance gate, and why the trap is non-negotiable\n\nWhile a deploy is in flight every request â€” `/api/*` included â€” is rewritten to\n`/maintenance.html` with a **200, never a 503**: some browsers and proxies cache\n5xx and strand users on the maintenance page after the deploy finishes. The\n`if (-f ...)` runs per request, so flipping the flag needs no nginx reload.\n\n```bash\ntrap 'rm -f \"$MAINT_FLAG\"' EXIT ERR HUP INT TERM\n```\n\nManual deploys have a failure mode CI never had: **you close the laptop, or the\nWi-Fi drops.** The SSH session dies, remote bash takes SIGHUP, the trap fires,\nthe flag clears, and the site comes back on the old build. Without it, a dropped\nconnection leaves the site maintenance-stuck until you reconnect. Test it â€” kill\nthe connection mid-deploy and confirm the flag is gone. That test is part of\ndelivering this skill, not an optional extra.\n\n### Gates worth keeping\n\n- `nginx -t` **before** `systemctl reload` â€” never reload a config that doesn't\n  parse.\n- **Health-poll before clearing maintenance**, 45 Ã— 2 s, then `die`. If the API\n  never came up, the site stays on the maintenance page rather than serving a\n  broken app.\n- `docker compose ps` after `up` â€” a container not `running` is a failed deploy\n  even when `up` returned 0.\n- **Prune before `up --build`, never after.** Small VPS disks fill fast when a\n  pip install dies mid-stream. Images belonging to running containers survive,\n  so the live site keeps serving.\n- **A closing line you can grep for.** `ok \"Live\"` as the last output means\n  \"scroll to the bottom\" is a complete verification.\n\nWrite the script for someone *watching* â€” one `say` per phase, `ok` per success,\n`die` with the reason per failure. NiYoj streams the log into a popup that can be\nclosed and reopened while the deploy keeps running.\n\n## Phase 3 â€” the human-only checklist\n\nPrint it; never attempt these steps yourself.\n\n1. VPS reachable; DNS `A` record for the subdomain points at it.\n2. `ssh-keygen -t ed25519 -f ~/.ssh/niyoj -C niyoj` â€” **press Enter twice, no\n   passphrase.** Public half into `authorized_keys`. (NiYoj's SSH key panel does\n   both halves: generate, install over a one-time password login, verify with the\n   key alone.)\n3. Non-root deploy user? `echo \"deploy ALL=(ALL) NOPASSWD:ALL\" | sudo tee\n   /etc/sudoers.d/deploy` â€” `deploy.sh` calls `sudo` and BatchMode cannot type a\n   password.\n4. Add the server in NiYoj â†’ **Test connection** â†’ save. It reports the box's\n   kernel and git version, or the actual SSH error.\n5. Private repo? Deploy key on the box, **one key per repo** with a per-app host\n   alias (`git@github-<app>:`) pinned in the server's `~/.ssh/config`;\n   `ssh -T git@<alias>` must succeed *as the deploy user*. A single shared key\n   across repos is a lateral-movement gift.\n6. Add the app: server, repo, branch, `/opt/<app>`. **Scan for apps** walks\n   `/app`, `/opt`, `/srv`, `/var/www`, `/root`, `/home` for git checkouts and\n   imports an existing box in one click â€” don't hand-write entries for anything\n   already on disk.\n7. **Deploy.** First run installs everything; expect several minutes.\n8. Verify: `âœ“ Live` in the log, HTTPS on the subdomain, `/api/health` returns\n   `{\"ok\": true}`, `docker compose ps` all running.\n9. **Deploy a second time immediately.** Idempotency is only proven by the boring\n   second run.\n\n## The release ritual\n\nManual deploy means the discipline CI used to enforce is now the human's. These\nsteps, in this order, every time:\n\n1. **Merge and push to `main` â€” user-confirmed.** NiYoj deploys `origin/<branch>`,\n   not the working tree. **Do NOT** merge or `git push` unasked; confirm with the\n   user first, every time. Unpushed commits do not ship â€” this catches everyone\n   out exactly once, and it is the single most common mistake in this model.\n2. **If a check-only workflow exists, wait for its green check.** It is the only\n   thing left that reads the code on a machine that isn't yours; deploying past a\n   red X is deploying a build you know is broken.\n3. **Check the app card**: right server, right branch, right directory.\n4. **Deploy.** Watch to `âœ“ Live`, or to the `âœ—` and its reason.\n5. **Smoke test the real URL** â€” load the app, hit one authed endpoint, confirm\n   the maintenance page is gone.\n\nDeploy one app at a time when they share a box: two simultaneous\n`docker system prune -af` runs will happily delete each other's build cache.\n\n## Rollback\n\n`git reset --hard origin/<branch>` means **the branch is the deployed state**, so\nrollback is a git operation, not a NiYoj one:\n\n```sh\ngit revert <bad-sha>      # then confirm with the user before: git push â€” then press Deploy\n```\n\n**Do NOT** `git push` unasked, even mid-incident. This is invoked while a deploy\nis failing, which is exactly when an unconfirmed push does the most damage â€”\nprepare the revert, then get the user's go-ahead before it leaves your machine.\n\n`git revert`, not `reset --hard` + force-push: reverting keeps the history other\ncheckouts already have, and the next deploy is an ordinary fast-forward.\n\nEmergency pin to an exact commit â†’ set the app's **custom command** temporarily\n(see `references/templates.md`), and **clear the field once `main` is healthy**\nor the app is silently frozen at that SHA forever.\n\n**Migrations are forward-only.** Reverting code does not un-apply a migration. If\nthe bad release migrated the schema, restore from the pre-deploy snapshot or ship\na compensating migration with the next number up â€” never edit an applied one.\n\n## Diagnosing a failed deploy\n\n| Symptom | Look here first |\n|---|---|\n| Hangs with no output, no error | Something is prompting â€” key passphrase, sudo password, `apt` without `DEBIAN_FRONTEND`, `certbot` without `--non-interactive` |\n| Card red, log ends mid-phase | The `die` that fired names the gate; read the line above it |\n| Site stuck on maintenance page | The trap didn't fire or isn't there. `rm /opt/<app>/maintenance.flag` on the box, then fix the trap |\n| Deploy green, change not live | Committed but not **pushed**. `origin/<branch>` is what ships |\n| \"no such file deploy/deploy.sh\" | Repo has no deploy script â€” fine for a static site, the pull *is* the deploy |\n| Build fails on disk space | Prune ran after `up --build` instead of before, or two apps deployed at once |\n| Worked yesterday, broken today, no commits | Someone edited files on the server; the hard reset took them away |\n\n## Rules\n\n- **Merging and pushing are user-confirmed, always.** Push before Deploy is the\n  order of operations, not permission to push. Mid-incident is when this matters\n  most.\n- **Nothing in the deploy may prompt.** BatchMode is the constraint the whole\n  design bends around.\n- **The repo is the only place changes live.** Editing files on the server is\n  erased by the next hard reset, without a word.\n- **Push before Deploy.** `origin/<branch>` ships; the working tree does not.\n- **The exit code is the verdict.** Never `|| true` a real failure.\n- **`apps.json` is never committed.** It carries hosts, users, and key paths â€”\n  gitignore it; NiYoj keeps it beside the binary.\n- **Secrets never go in the custom-command field.** `apps.json` is plain local\n  JSON. Environment values live in a `.env` on the server, referenced by compose.\n- **One deploy key per repo**, one host alias per key.\n- **Delete the CI workflow that *deploys*; keep one that only checks.** The rule\n  is about server access, not about the file existing. A build-and-test workflow\n  never touches the box, so it cannot race â€” and it is the half of CI that still\n  pays when you work alone.\n- **Never run the deploy yourself.** Scaffold, verify, and hand the human the\n  checklist. Pressing Deploy is theirs; so is anything touching DNS, keys, or the\n  server.\n- Sibling skill: `deploy-merlin` is this same architecture with GitHub Actions as\n  the trigger. Changes to the shared parts (`deploy.sh` phases, nginx template,\n  maintenance gate, backups) belong in both.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\deploy-niyoj\\references\\templates.md\r\n# deploy-niyoj â€” file templates\n\nVerbatim artifacts. Fill in `<app>`, the subdomain, the ports and the health URL;\nchange nothing else without a reason you can state.\n\n---\n\n## 1. What NiYoj sends over the wire\n\nPressing Deploy opens one SSH connection and feeds this to `bash -s` on stdin â€”\nno quoting games, nothing installed on the box:\n\n```sh\nset -euo pipefail\nif [ ! -d /opt/<app>/.git ]; then git clone -b <branch> <repo> /opt/<app>; fi\ncd /opt/<app>\ngit fetch --all --prune\ngit reset --hard origin/<branch>\ngit submodule update --init --recursive || true\nif [ -f deploy/deploy.sh ]; then sudo bash deploy/deploy.sh;\n elif [ -f deploy.sh ]; then sudo bash deploy.sh;\n else echo 'no deploy.sh â€” files updated in place'; fi\n```\n\nThree properties, all deliberate:\n\n- **A new app and the 200th redeploy take the identical path.** The clone is the\n  only conditional; everything after it is a hard reset. That only works because\n  `deploy.sh` is idempotent.\n- **`git reset --hard`, never `git pull`.** A stray edit or a merge conflict on\n  the server can wedge a pull forever. The working tree is forced to match\n  origin; the server is a checkout, not a workspace.\n- **A repo with no `deploy.sh` still deploys.** For a static site the pull *is*\n  the deploy. Don't invent a script you don't need.\n\nThe SSH invocation:\n\n```sh\nssh -o BatchMode=yes -o StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new \\\n    -o ServerAliveInterval=30 -o ConnectTimeout=12 \\\n    -i <key> -p <port> <user>@<host> \"bash -s\"\n```\n\n**Anything unusual gets a custom command.** An app can carry a `cmd` that\nreplaces the script above wholesale â€” a compose file in a subdirectory, a systemd\nunit, an `ansible-playbook` line, a two-server dance. The default covers the\ncommon case; the escape hatch is one field.\n\n---\n\n## 2. App entry (`apps.json`, beside the binary â€” never committed)\n\n```json\n\"aerolens\": {\n  \"server\": \"droplet-bodish\",\n  \"repo\":   \"git@github-aerolens:<org>/<repo>.git\",\n  \"branch\": \"main\",\n  \"dir\":    \"/opt/aerolens\",\n  \"cmd\":    \"\"\n}\n```\n\n`repo` is an SSH URL with a **per-app host alias** (`git@github-aerolens:`) when\none box deploys several private repos â€” one deploy key per repo, each pinned in\nthe server's `~/.ssh/config`.\n\nEmergency pin to a known-good commit, as a temporary `cmd`:\n\n```sh\nset -euo pipefail\ncd /opt/<app>\ngit fetch --all --prune\ngit checkout --detach <good-sha>\nsudo bash deploy/deploy.sh\n```\n\nClear the field once `main` is healthy, or the app is frozen at that SHA forever.\n\n---\n\n## 3. `deploy/deploy.sh`\n\nOne bash script that does **first-run provisioning** *and* **every subsequent\nupdate**. Each phase detects and skips.\n\n```bash\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\nset -euo pipefail\n\nAPP_DIR=/opt/<app>\nSUBDOMAIN=app.example.com\nEMAIL=ops@example.com\n\nsay()  { printf \"\\nâ–¶ %s\\n\" \"$*\"; }\nok()   { printf \"  âœ“ %s\\n\"  \"$*\"; }\nwarn() { printf \"  ! %s\\n\"  \"$*\" >&2; }\ndie()  { printf \"  âœ— %s\\n\"  \"$*\" >&2; exit 1; }\n\n# 1. Base packages (skip if present)\nsay \"Installing system packages\"\nexport DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive          # BatchMode cannot answer prompts\napt-get update -qq\napt-get install -y -qq curl git nginx ca-certificates ufw \\\n                       certbot python3-certbot-nginx > /dev/null\ncommand -v docker >/dev/null || curl -fsSL https://get.docker.com | sh\n\n# 2. Firewall (idempotent)\nsay \"Configuring UFW\"\nufw allow 22,80,443/tcp >/dev/null\nufw --force enable >/dev/null\n\n# 3. â”€â”€ MAINTENANCE GATE ON â”€â”€\nMAINT_FLAG=\"$APP_DIR/maintenance.flag\"\ntrap 'rm -f \"$MAINT_FLAG\"' EXIT ERR HUP INT TERM\ncp deploy/maintenance.html \"$APP_DIR/maintenance.html\"\ntouch \"$MAINT_FLAG\"\n\n# 4. Snapshot the DB before anything rebuilds it\nsay \"Pre-deploy snapshot\"\ndocker compose exec -T api python -c \\\n  \"from app.db import create_backup; create_backup('pre-deploy')\" || warn \"snapshot skipped\"\n\n# 5. Build frontend\nsay \"Building frontend\"\ncd frontend && npm ci && npm run build && cd ..\n\n# 6. Reclaim disk BEFORE rebuild\nsay \"Reclaiming disk\"\ndf -h / | tail -1\ndocker system prune -af > /dev/null 2>&1 || true\ndf -h / | tail -1\n\n# 7. Build + start stack\nsay \"Starting compose stack\"\ndocker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f deploy/docker-compose.prod.yml \\\n  up -d --build\n\n# 8. Wait for API to be healthy BEFORE clearing maintenance\nsay \"Waiting for API\"\nfor i in $(seq 1 45); do\n  curl -fsS http://127.0.0.1:18000/api/health >/dev/null 2>&1 && break\n  sleep 2\n  [ \"$i\" -eq 45 ] && die \"API never came up\"\ndone\nok \"API healthy\"\n\n# 9. Maintenance gate OFF (the trap clears it too â€” belt-and-braces)\nrm -f \"$MAINT_FLAG\"\nok \"Live\"\n\n# 10. nginx + TLS (certbot is a no-op if the cert is fresh)\nsay \"Configuring nginx\"\nenvsubst < deploy/nginx.conf.template > /etc/nginx/sites-available/<app>\nln -sf /etc/nginx/sites-available/<app> /etc/nginx/sites-enabled/<app>\nnginx -t && systemctl reload nginx\n\ncertbot --nginx -d \"$SUBDOMAIN\" -m \"$EMAIL\" \\\n        --agree-tos --non-interactive --redirect || warn \"TLS step skipped\"\n```\n\n**Key idempotency rule:** every step is *detect â†’ skip-if-already-done â†’\notherwise-do*. Re-running the whole script must be safe at any time.\n\n---\n\n## 4. `deploy/nginx.conf.template`\n\n```nginx\nserver {\n    listen 80;\n    server_name __SUBDOMAIN__;\n    root __APP_DIR__/frontend/dist;\n    index index.html;\n\n    set $maintenance 0;\n    if (-f __APP_DIR__/maintenance.flag) { set $maintenance 1; }\n    if ($maintenance = 1) { rewrite ^.*$ /maintenance.html last; }\n\n    location = /maintenance.html {\n        root __APP_DIR__;\n        # 200, NOT 503 â€” some browsers/proxies cache 5xx and strand users\n        # on the maintenance page after the deploy finishes.\n        add_header Cache-Control \"no-cache, no-store, must-revalidate\" always;\n        add_header Pragma \"no-cache\" always;\n        expires off;\n        try_files /maintenance.html =200;\n    }\n\n    location / { try_files $uri $uri/ /index.html; }\n    location /assets/ { expires 1y; add_header Cache-Control \"public, immutable\"; }\n\n    location /api/ {\n        proxy_pass http://127.0.0.1:18000;\n        proxy_set_header Host $host;\n        proxy_buffering off;       # SSE / streaming\n        proxy_read_timeout 600s;\n        client_max_body_size 2G;\n    }\n}\n```\n\nThe `if (-f ...)` runs per request â€” no reload needed. Flip the flag, the next\nrequest is the maintenance page; remove it, the next request is the app.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Server setup commands\n\nPassphrase-less key dedicated to deploys:\n\n```sh\nssh-keygen -t ed25519 -f ~/.ssh/niyoj -C niyoj     # press Enter twice\nssh-copy-id -i ~/.ssh/niyoj.pub root@YOUR_IP\nssh -i ~/.ssh/niyoj root@YOUR_IP \"echo connected\"\n```\n\nWindows has no `ssh-copy-id`:\n\n```powershell\ntype \"$HOME\\.ssh\\niyoj.pub\" | ssh root@YOUR_IP \"mkdir -p ~/.ssh && chmod 700 ~/.ssh && cat >> ~/.ssh/authorized_keys && chmod 600 ~/.ssh/authorized_keys\"\n```\n\nNon-root deploy user â€” `deploy.sh` calls `sudo` and BatchMode cannot type a\npassword:\n\n```sh\necho \"deploy ALL=(ALL) NOPASSWD:ALL\" | sudo tee /etc/sudoers.d/deploy\n```\n\n---\n\n## 6. Disk reclaim, with readable output\n\n```bash\ndf -h / | tail -1 | awk '{printf \"  before: %s used of %s (%s)\\n\", $3, $2, $5}'\ndocker system prune -af > /dev/null 2>&1 || true\ndf -h / | tail -1 | awk '{printf \"  after:  %s used of %s (%s)\\n\", $3, $2, $5}'\n```\n\nImages belonging to running containers are \"in use\" and survive, so the live site\nkeeps serving.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Database safety\n\nKeep the backup pattern from the CI playbook unchanged: `create_backup(label)`,\n`restore_backup_append(filename)` that renames on clash instead of overwriting,\n`_safe_path(name)` blocking `..` and separators, admin-only endpoints, and\nper-label retention (`manual` never auto-prunes, each auto label keeps its last\n10).\n\nThe one change in this variant: **there is no `.githooks/pre-push` snapshot.**\nPush and deploy are separate acts, so a pre-push hook fires at a moment unrelated\nto production. The pre-deploy snapshot in phase 4 of `deploy.sh` is the guarantee,\nand it runs before the first container is touched.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Adapting for a new project\n\n| Layer | Keep | Swap |\n|---|---|---|\n| NiYoj server entry | yes | host, user, port, key path |\n| NiYoj app entry | yes | repo, branch, `/opt/<app>` |\n| Custom command | only for non-standard layouts | the command itself |\n| `deploy.sh` skeleton | yes, verbatim | package list, ports, health URL |\n| `nginx.conf.template` | yes â€” the maintenance gate especially | server_name, proxy_pass port |\n| `maintenance.html` | yes | branding |\n| Health endpoint | yes â€” `/api/health`, unauthenticated | implement in your framework |\n| `useMaintenanceMode` + modal | yes | colors, copy |\n| localStorage persistence | yes | keys, what to persist |\n| Disk prune | yes | nothing |\n| Pre-deploy DB snapshot | yes â€” it replaces the pre-push hook | DB driver, container name |\n| `.githooks/pre-push` snapshot | drop it | n/a |\n| `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` | delete it | n/a |\n\n---\n\n## 9. Check-only workflow (optional, keep alongside NiYoj)\n\nThe half of CI that still pays when you deploy manually: it builds and tests on a\nmachine that isn't yours, and it **never touches the server** â€” no `ssh`, no\n`DEPLOY_SSH_KEY`, no deploy step â€” so it cannot race the NiYoj trigger.\n\n`.github/workflows/check.yml`:\n\n```yaml\nname: check\non:\n  push:\n  pull_request:\n\njobs:\n  check:\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    steps:\n      - uses: actions/checkout@v4\n      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4\n        with:\n          node-version: '20'\n          cache: npm\n          cache-dependency-path: frontend/package-lock.json\n\n      # `npm ci` fails if package-lock.json and package.json disagree â€”\n      # which is most of the value: it catches the dep you installed\n      # locally and never committed.\n      - run: npm ci\n        working-directory: frontend\n      - run: npm run build\n        working-directory: frontend\n      - run: npm test --if-present\n        working-directory: frontend\n\n      # Backend, if there is one. Swap for your language's equivalent.\n      - uses: actions/setup-python@v5\n        with:\n          python-version: '3.12'\n      - run: pip install -r requirements.txt\n      - run: pytest -q\n```\n\nTwo rules for keeping it harmless:\n\n- **No secrets, no server.** The moment it grows an `ssh` step or a deploy key it\n  becomes a second trigger, and the racing rule applies again. Delete it then.\n- **`npm ci`, never `npm install`.** `ci` installs exactly the lockfile and fails\n  on drift; `install` silently rewrites the lock and hides the very problem you\n  are running this to catch.\n\nThe payoff arrives at one moment: the red X shows up *before* you press Deploy.\nAdd \"wait for the green check\" between push and Deploy in the release ritual, or\nthe minutes are spent for nothing.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\design-arwen\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: design-arwen\ndescription: >-\n  Use whenever the user wants to build, redesign, improve or critique a\n  UI/UX, native or web - page, iOS/Android screen, dashboard,\n  form, onboarding or design system; or asks to make something less\n  generic, bolder, less AI-made; or to pick\n  fonts/colors/layout/motion, fix hierarchy, spacing, contrast, a11y or UI\n  performance, write UX copy and error states, or share one design language\n  across native and web. NOT for backend or non-UI work; `impeccable`\n  owns craft without aesthetic direction, gstack the finished page.\nuser-invocable: true\nargument-hint: \"[build|redesign|improve|critique] [target]\"\nlicense: MIT\n---\n\n# design-arwen â€” signature design for native & web\n\nTwo failure modes kill interfaces. **Generic**: technically fine, instantly\nforgettable, unmistakably AI-made. **Broken**: distinctive but unshippable â€”\nfails contrast, ignores platform conventions, breaks on a real device. Arwen\nrefuses both. Every surface it touches ships **production-grade** *and* carries\n**one unmistakable signature** â€” an ownable device someone remembers and could\nnot have guessed from the category alone.\n\nIt treats **web, iOS, Android, and React Native as equal citizens** â€” one design\nidentity, expressed idiomatically per platform, never lowest-common-denominator.\nNo setup, no scripts.\n\n---\n\n## Modes â€” pick one, then read its reference\n\n| Mode | When | Read |\n|---|---|---|\n| `build` | New UI, or a new surface in an existing app. Nothing to preserve. | [references/build.md](references/build.md) |\n| `redesign` | An app that already exists and must get better. Audit-first, preservation rules, expert panel. | [references/redesign.md](references/redesign.md) |\n| `improve` | Scoped pass on working UI: polish, bolder, quieter, adapt, typeset, colorize, animate, harden, optimize, clarify. | [references/improve.md](references/improve.md) |\n| `critique` | Diagnosis only. Ranked findings, no code written. | [references/critique.md](references/critique.md) |\n\n**Routing.** First word matches a mode â†’ that mode, everything after is the target.\nNo mode given â†’ infer from intent and *say which you picked in one line*:\n\"make it bolder / fix the spacing / the colors feel flat / add motion\" â†’ `improve` Â·\n\"review this / what's wrong with it\" â†’ `critique` Â· existing app + \"modernise, overhaul,\nelevate\" â†’ `redesign` Â· otherwise `build`. Two modes could fit â†’ ask once.\n\n**Every mode reads Phase 0â€“2 below.** `build`, `redesign`, and `improve` also read\n[references/craft.md](references/craft.md) â€” the non-negotiable production rules\n(color, type, layout, interaction, motion, native conventions, perf, UX copy).\n`critique` reads craft.md as its checklist.\n\n**Product register also reads [references/product-ui.md](references/product-ui.md)** â€”\nforms, data tables, settings, multi-step flows, permissions, notifications, dashboards,\ni18n/RTL. These are the surfaces real apps are made of and the ones generated UI most\nreliably gets subtly wrong. Non-optional when the work touches any of them.\n\n**Every mode that writes code ends in [references/verify.md](references/verify.md)** â€”\nthe ship gate, executed with a real browser or simulator. Not a checklist you assert.\n\n**Signature is not a mode.** Forging one (Phase 2) is doctrine on every build and\nredesign. **Adapting across platforms is not a mode either** â€” Phase 3 applies whenever\nthe thing ships more than once.\n\n---\n\n## Design memory â€” read it, then leave one behind\n\n**On entry, look for `DESIGN.md` at the repo root** (also `docs/DESIGN.md`, `.design/DESIGN.md`),\nplus `PRODUCT.md` (impeccable's platform and register) and `design-system/*/MASTER.md` with\nany matching `pages/<page>.md` override (ui-ux-pro-max's). Whoever wrote them â€” **read and obey**. It\nrecords decisions already made: the theme, the type pairing, the token names, the\nsignature. Those beat every reflex-reject list arwen applies. Say in one line that you\nfound it and what you're inheriting.\n\n**On exit, after a `build` or `redesign`, write or update it.** Ten lines, no ceremony,\nno separate init flow â€” the Design Read, the dials, the direction, the signature\nsentence, the font pairing, the token names, and anything you deliberately broke a rule\nfor and why. That last one is what stops the next session (or the next model) undoing\nyour decision because it looked like slop.\n\n```markdown\n# DESIGN\nRead: dashboard Â· product Â· ops teams Â· dials 3/2/9 Â· direction terminal-dense Â· scene <â€¦>\nSignature: the run-status ribbon â€” same shape in the table row, the detail header, the empty state\nType: SÃ¶hne KrÃ¤ftig / SÃ¶hne Buch. Color: OKLCH neutrals tinted +0.008 to hue 250, one accent.\nTokens: --bg --surface --ink --muted --accent --ring, spacing 4/8/12/16/24/40\nDeliberate: mono for run IDs only (literal machine strings); numbers use tabular-nums\n```\n\n`improve` and `critique` read it and do not rewrite it. **Identity-preservation beats\nnovelty** â€” a committed decision on disk outranks anything this skill would pick fresh.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 0 â€” Orient (30 seconds, no ceremony)\n\n1. **Platform(s).** Web / iOS / Android / React Native / cross-platform. Decides\n   which conventions in craft.md are law. Ask once if unclear.\n2. **Register â€” this changes the rules below, so decide it first:**\n   - **Brand** (marketing, landing, campaign, portfolio â†’ design *is* the product):\n     identity leads, be bolder, distinctive fonts mandatory, orchestrated motion allowed.\n   - **Product** (app UI, dashboard, tool â†’ design *serves* the product): usability\n     leads. Its slop test isn't \"looks AI-made\" (familiarity is a *feature* here) â€”\n     it's \"would a user fluent in Linear/Figma/Notion/Stripe *trust* this, or pause\n     at every subtly-off component?\" **Register deltas for product:** familiar system\n     sans (Inter, SF Pro, `system-ui`) is *permitted and often right* â€” this is the\n     one explicit exception to the distinctive-font rule; use a **fixed rem type\n     scale** (ratio 1.125â€“1.2), not fluid `clamp()`; motion is **150â€“250ms, state-only,\n     no page-load show**; accent color reserved for primary action / current selection\n     / state â€” never decoration; \"modal as first thought\" is a ban (exhaust inline &\n     progressive disclosure first).\n3. **What already exists.** Read current tokens/theme/one screen. Reuse a working\n   system; branch out only where UX wins. **Identity-preservation beats novelty** â€”\n   every reflex-reject/ban list â€” this file's and the shared floor's â€” applies to *new*\n   choices only; an existing committed brand wins.\n4. **Set the three dials** â€” the tunable part of every rule downstream, and the user can\n   override any of them by asking (\"make it calmer\", \"denser\"):\n\n   | Dial | 1â€“3 | 4â€“7 | 8â€“10 |\n   |---|---|---|---|\n   | **VARIANCE** (distance from convention) | conventional, trust-first | one clear point of view | experimental, award-bait |\n   | **MOTION** (how much movement) | state-change only | purposeful reveals + micro-interactions | orchestrated, scroll-driven, ambient |\n   | **DENSITY** (information per screen) | airy, one idea per view | balanced | dense, dashboard-grade |\n\n   Presets: B2B SaaS product `3/3/7` Â· consumer landing `6/6/4` Â· agency or portfolio\n   `8/7/3` Â· dashboard or tool `3/2/9` Â· public-sector or regulated `2/1/6` Â· campaign or\n   event `9/8/4`. **Read the brief's vibe words and audience to place the dials, then\n   state them.** Quiet constraints (accessibility-first audience, regulated industry,\n   kids, trust-first commerce) **override aesthetic preference** â€” clamp VARIANCE and\n   MOTION down regardless of what the vibe words say.\n\n5. **Write one physical-scene sentence:** *who* uses this, *where*, under *what light*,\n   in *what mood* (\"a line cook glancing at a splattered tablet mid-rush\"). If it\n   doesn't force your theme/density/contrast, add detail until it does. Prevents 80% of\n   generic output.\n\n6. **Emit the Design Read â€” one line, before generating anything:**\n   `Read: <page kind> Â· <register> Â· <audience> Â· job <primary job> Â· dials <V/M/D> Â· direction <lane> Â· scene <â€¦>`\n   It is a contract the user can correct in one word. **If the brief is genuinely\n   ambiguous on an axis that changes the whole design, ask ONE question â€” never guess\n   silently, and never ask three.**\n\n   **No user to ask?** You are a subagent, or a batch run, or nobody is at the keyboard â€”\n   `build.md` Â§4 dispatches this work to subagents, so this is a normal case, not an edge\n   one. Then: **decide and build.** Take the register default, build straight through, and\n   list every choice made on the user's behalf â€” the Design Read fields you picked rather\n   than read â€” in your final report, so whoever reads it can overturn any of them cheaply.\n   That satisfies \"never guess silently\" regardless of where the list lands. `assumed:` on\n   a picked field is encouraged, not required. Blocking a subagent on a question nobody\n   will answer is the one wrong move. Same rule for every \"ask once\" here and in its\n   references: no user â†’ default, build, report the choices.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 1 â€” Commit to a direction (no timid middles)\n\nPick **one** aesthetic lane and execute with precision. Bold maximalism and refined\nminimalism both win â€” the enemy is the evenly-hedged middle. Match the lane to the\ndomain: daily-use tools default dense-quiet-scannable; portfolios/campaigns/games can\nbe expressive. Never force a landing-page hero onto a tool.\n\n**Name a real reference before committing** â€” \"Klim-style specimen\", \"Vercel pure-black\nmonochrome\", \"1970s terminal manual\". Unnamed ambition collapses into beige.\n\n**Color strategy** (choose *before* colors), on the commitment axis:\nRestrained (tinted neutrals + one accent â‰¤10%) Â· Committed (one saturated color 30â€“60%) Â·\nFull palette (3â€“4 named roles) Â· Drenched (the surface *is* the color).\nUse **OKLCH**. Tint neutrals 0.005â€“0.015 toward the brand hue when it builds cohesion â€”\nplain grey is valid; default-warm never is.\nKeep the palette multi-dimensional â€” a UI drowned in one hue family reads uncommitted.\nModular type scale **â‰¥1.25 ratio** (flat ~1.1 reads timid) â€” except product UI (above).\n\n**Font selection is where generic dies.** Procedure: (1) write three *physical-object*\nvoice words (\"warm and mechanical and opinionated\", not \"modern\"); (2) list the fonts you'd\nreach for by reflex and **reject any on the ban list**; (3) pick the font-as-object from a\nreal catalog (museum caption, diner receipt); (4) if the final pick matches your reflex,\nstart over. Pair on a *contrast* axis (serif+sans, geometric+humanist) or one family across\nweights â€” never two near-identical sans.\n\n**The product register's system-sans exemption waives this whole procedure** â€” it does not\nsend you round it again. Steps 1â€“4 have exactly one legal answer once the exemption\napplies, so step 4 would loop forever: take `system-ui` / Inter / SF Pro, say in one line\nthat the product register chose it, and move on. Run the procedure only when you are\npicking a display or brand face on top.\n\n**The reflex-reject list is not in this file â€” read `references/anti-slop.md`**, the shared\ndesign floor beside the installed skills (`../references/anti-slop.md`, else\n`../../references/anti-slop.md`; see the *Shared floor* note below). Â§1 is the banned\ntypefaces and the four exemptions, Â§3 the `â€ ` contested markers and the conflict rule.\nOne arwen-specific exemption to hold in mind: the **product register permits a familiar\nsystem sans** for body text (Phase 0.2) where the floor's list would otherwise bite.\n\n**Category-reflex check (two altitudes) â€” brand register only.** *First-order* â€” could\nsomeone guess your theme+palette from the **category alone**? Rework. *Second-order* â€”\ncould they guess it from **category + the obvious anti-reference** (\"AI tool that's *not*\nSaaS-cream â†’ editorial-typographic\"; \"fintech that's *not* navy-gold â†’ terminal-dark\")?\nRework until neither is guessable.\n\n**In the product register this check is off, and that is not a concession.** Phase 0.2\nsays familiarity is a feature and `critique.md` forbids marking a conventional dashboard\ndown for being conventional; \"unguessable from the category\" pulls the opposite way, and\non a settings screen the two cancel and you ship generic-with-a-motif believing you\nfailed. **A settings screen a Linear user would recognise instantly is a correct outcome.** What replaces it: the signature must be **one systematic UX device**, not an\naesthetic â€” a status grammar, a reimagined component, a consistent way of showing\npending-vs-saved â€” applied everywhere that state occurs and defensible in a sentence. If\nyou cannot name that one device, you have no signature; a new hue is not one. Judge it by\n\"does this make the product easier to read at a glance\", never by \"would a competitor's\nscreenshot pass for mine\" (in this register it usually would, and should).\n\n**The three saturated AI looks â€” defaults, not choices.** Each is legitimate *when the\nbrief asks for it*, and a tell when it appears anyway: (1) cream/sand body (OKLCH\nL 0.84â€“0.97, C<0.06, hue 40â€“100, regardless of the token name â€” `--paper`, `--linen`,\n`--bone` are tells in themselves) + high-contrast serif display + terracotta accent (near\n#D97757 â‰ˆ oklch 0.67 0.13 39); (2) near-black (an untinted #0B0B0B / #111 counts) + one\nacid-green or vermilion accent; (3) broadsheet â€” hairline rules,\nzero radius, dense newspaper columns. **The named reflex-reject lane is\n\"editorial-typographic\"** â€” italic display serif + small mono labels + ruled rules +\nmonochrome, no imagery. Carry warmth via accent + type + imagery, not body bg.\nWhere the brief *pins* a direction, the brief always wins, including when it asks for one\nof these.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 2 â€” Forge the signature (the heart of this skill)\n\nThe **one ownable device** that makes the interface unmistakable â€” what someone\ndescribes recounting your product from memory. One idea, applied with discipline; not\ndecoration everywhere. Method:\n\n1. **Source it from the domain's truth, not its clichÃ©s.** A real property of what the\n   product *is/does*, made visible. Transit app â†’ line-and-node route vocabulary. Writing\n   tool â†’ the caret, margin, manuscript. Mine the *thing*, not competitors.\n2. **Pick ONE carrier:** a type move Â· a spatial move (asymmetry/overlap/grid-break) Â· a\n   motion move (one transition the whole app rhymes with) Â· a material (recurring\n   texture/edge/shadow language) Â· or a reimagined component archetype. Two signatures = none.\n3. **Make it systematic.** Same logic on hero, empty state, error, loading â€” so it reads as\n   *language*, not accident. It flexes to fit what it marks; appears once = gimmick, appears\n   everywhere identically = wallpaper.\n4. **Test, by register.** Both: cover the logo â€” can someone still tell two screens are the\n   same product? Iterate until yes. **Brand only:** would a competitor's screenshot pass for\n   yours? Iterate until no. **Product:** skip that second question â€” there the answer usually\n   is yes and *should* be (Phase 1). Ask instead: is the one systematic device present on\n   every surface where its state occurs, and can you defend it in a sentence?\n\n**Spend your boldness in one place.** The signature is the memorable thing; everything\naround it stays quiet and disciplined. Before shipping, Chanel's rule: look again and\nremove one accessory.\n\nState the signature in one sentence before building. Everything downstream serves it.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 3 â€” Cross-platform identity (when it ships more than once)\n\nOne identity, **idiomatic per platform** â€” never a web layout stuffed into a phone.\n- **Shared (travels everywhere):** palette, type *personality*, the signature, motion\n  character, spacing rhythm, voice/copy.\n- **Divergent (obeys the platform):** navigation model, control shapes, gestures,\n  transitions, density, safe-area/status handling. An iOS tab bar and a web top-nav are\n  *the same identity making different correct choices*.\n- **Tokens are the bridge.** Name the theme + pin the font pairing, then define\n  color/space/type/radius/motion as platform-agnostic tokens mapped to: CSS variables (web),\n  Asset colors + Dynamic Type (iOS), theme resources (Android), a JS theme object (RN),\n  a capability-tiered palette + glyph set (terminal).\n- **Terminal is a platform too â€” CLI, TUI, tmux status.** It takes a cell grid instead\n  of a canvas and no font of your own, so identity moves to glyph vocabulary, density and\n  alignment. Read [references/terminal.md](references/terminal.md) before designing one.\n\n---\n\n## Ship gate & slop test\n\n**Shared floor â€” `references/anti-slop.md` is law here, and it is the only copy.** The\nbanned typefaces (Â§1), the banned visual clichÃ©s (Â§2), and the scope-and-authority rule with\nits `â€ ` contested markers (Â§3) all live there, shared with every skillator design skill â€”\nso they are not restated here and nothing on that list is enforced from memory. **Read the\nfile before you run the gate.** It sits beside the installed skills: try\n`../references/anti-slop.md` first (the `install.sh` layout), then\n`../../references/anti-slop.md` (git checkout, plugin cache). Neither resolves â†’ say so in\none line and enforce what this file names.\n\nArwen adopts that floor whole and adds three of its own, on the same match-and-refuse terms:\n**identical card grids** Â· **text that overflows its container at any width** Â· **the same\nfont every generation**. Inside an arwen-run task â€” any of the four modes, Phase 0 to this\ngate â€” arwen owns the work, so the floor's conflict rule resolves in arwen's favour and you\nsay in one line which skill you overrode. Never resolve it by load order.\n\n**Content & copy tells:** the \"Jane Doe\" effect â€” placeholder names, lorem, `example.com`,\nfake logos, `$XX/mo`, invented metrics shipped as real. **Em-dashes in generated UI copy**\n(the single most-violated tell) â€” use a period, a comma, or a colon. Loading messages like\n\"Herding pixels\" or \"Teaching robots to dance\". Emoji as iconography.\n\n**Theme lock:** one page never mixes light-mode and dark-mode sections by accident. Pick\nthe mode deliberately (dark is not \"because tools look cool dark\", light is not \"to be\nsafe\") and if both ship, **verify every surface in both** before calling it done.\n\n**Accessibility is arwen's inside a design task.** Not a post-hoc audit and not a\ncontrast number â€” a design constraint that changes the layout, on this design's terms.\n`skillator:a11y-toph` owns it as a *subject*: audits of code nobody is redesigning,\nfiled a11y bugs, AT and CI wiring. Hand it the failing pairs when the damage is wider\nthan the change you made; take its hand-backs when a fix needs a design decision. The floor, enforced in craft.md and verified in\nverify.md: all eight states designed with **hover â‰  focus** Â· a visible `:focus-visible`\nring â‰¥3:1 everywhere Â· every surface fully keyboard-drivable in a sensible order Â· real\n`<label>`s and `aria-describedby` errors Â· never colour as the only signal Â· reduced-motion\npaths that keep state feedback Â· targets â‰¥44pt/48dp on touch, â‰¥24 CSS px for web\npointer Â· zoom never blocked Â· text resizable to 200% without loss Â· `aria-live` for\nasync results Â· gestures never the only route. A distinctive interface that a keyboard user\ncannot operate has failed *both* of this skill's tests, not one.\n\n**Ship gate â€” this list is the only one.** `verify.md` is *how* to run it and\n`product-ui.md` is *what to build*; neither carries a competing gate. Each item is tagged\nby what it takes to prove: `static` (readable from the source, no browser),\n`browser` (needs a real render), `delegated` (another skill owns it).\n\n| # | Item | Kind |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Text contrast on real pairs â€” 4.5:1 body, 3:1 large (â‰¥24px, or â‰¥18.67px bold) | `static` |\n| 2 | **Non-text contrast â‰¥3:1** â€” input borders, focus ring, icons, chart strokes, any control edge carrying meaning (WCAG 1.4.11) | `static` |\n| 3 | Every dependency and asset URL resolves â€” no invented packages, no guessed image IDs | `static` |\n| 4 | Labels, `aria-describedby` errors, `aria-live` regions, focus order in source order, headings in sequence, zoom not blocked | `static` |\n| 5 | Loading / empty / error / success / no-permission all designed | `static` |\n| 6 | Signature present and systematic (Phase 2 test for the register) | `static` |\n| 7 | No overflow at 320 / 768 / 1280 / 1920 and at 200% text zoom | `browser` |\n| 8 | Keyboard: every surface drivable, visible ring, no traps, never under sticky chrome | `browser` |\n| 9 | Reduced-motion path works and still shows state changes â€” no global kill | `browser` |\n| 10 | Targets â‰¥44pt/48dp touch, â‰¥24 CSS px web pointer â€” **measured, not assumed** | `browser` |\n| 11 | Both themes, if both ship | `browser` |\n| 12 | **Drive it for real** â€” screenshot the browser / run the screen | `browser` |\n| 13 | LCP < 2.5s, CLS < 0.1 | `delegated` â†’ `web-perf` |\n| 14 | Native conventions, safe areas | `delegated` â†’ `ios-design-review` / platform |\n| 15 | Wider a11y damage than your own change | `delegated` â†’ `a11y-toph` |\n| 16 | Mechanical tell scan, web only, if installed | `delegated` â†’ `impeccable detect` |\n\n**No browser available? That is a terminal state, not a failure.** `static` items are\nstill mandatory â€” they need no render and there is no excuse for skipping them. Report in\nexactly this shape:\n\n```\nbuilt Â· verified: 1-6 (static) Â· not verified: 7-12 (no browser available) Â· delegated: 13-16 not run\n```\n\nNever write \"done\" or \"verified\" over an unrendered page, and never narrate a render you\ndid not perform. `build.md` Â§6 and `verify.md`'s driver list defer to this paragraph.\n\n**Run this gate, don't recite it** â€” [references/verify.md](references/verify.md) has the\ndriver, the OKLCH contrast script, the overflow and state-forcing procedures, and the\nreport format.\n\n---\n\n## Compose with these skills (delegate, don't duplicate)\n\n- **Visual artboards before code** â†’ [references/canvas.md](references/canvas.md) (the\n  optional `/design` canvas gate â€” the user tweaks the layout visually, then arwen builds it).\n  It is also skillator's visual companion for *any* design conversation: `PRACTICE.md` Â§1\n  routes here whenever a question is clearer shown than described, even from outside this skill.\n- **Charts / graphs / KPI tiles / series colors â†’ `dataviz`.** It owns the validated\n  light+dark chart palette and mark/axis/legend rules. Don't restate them here.\n- **Poster / cover / print / static art object (90% visual) â†’ `canvas-design`.**\n- **Terminal surfaces (CLI / TUI / tmux) â†’ [references/terminal.md](references/terminal.md)\n  to design, `skillator:tui-tron` to prove.** Arwen owns what the screen should look like;\n  tui-tron owns the width matrix, the ASCII-fallback scan and driving a TUI with no pty.\n- **\"Audit / profile my page's performance\" â†’ `web-perf`** (Chrome-DevTools workflow). Arwen\n  carries the design-time perf rules; web-perf runs the measured audit.\n- **Quick named theme for a static deck/report â†’ `theme-factory`.** (Not for app design\n  systems â€” arwen's token guidance is stronger.)\n- **Motion craft â†’ `emil-design-eng` (interruptibility, springs, `@starting-style`,\n  clip-path, blur-masked transitions) and `apple-design` (gesture physics, velocity\n  handoff, momentum projection, rubber-banding).** craft.md carries arwen's motion *policy*\n  â€” when motion is allowed, how long, reduced-motion, the forbidden patterns. Those two own\n  the *execution*. Building anything gesture-driven or spring-based without reading them is\n  how motion ends up technically correct and lifeless. `animate` builds a single animation\n  end-to-end; `find-animation-opportunities` finds where motion is missing.\n- **Deep React/RN implementation â†’ the Vercel skills:** `vercel-react-view-transitions`,\n  `vercel-react-native-skills`, `vercel-composition-patterns`.\n\n---\n\n## Principles that carry the whole skill\n\n- **Signature over decoration.** One ownable idea with discipline > effects everywhere.\n- **Register rules everything.** Brand rewards boldness; product rewards trust. The audience picks the aesthetic, not your taste.\n- **Diverse critique, single route.** Reconcile the lenses to one route â€” never ship a committee.\n- **Commit, then prove it.** Timid middles read as AI; evidence (contrast, breakpoints, reduced-motion, a real render) beats claims.\n- **Accessible or it isn't production-grade.** Not a gate at the end â€” a constraint that shapes the layout.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\design-arwen\\UPSTREAM.md\r\n# design-arwen upstreams\n\nArwen absorbs rules from four outside skills (F23). It prints `changed <name> <old>..<new>` with a\ncompare URL and exits 1; grayskull-power's arming turns that into one A ticket,\n`absorb <name> <old>..<new> into design-arwen`. Nothing edits arwen\nautomatically. Whoever takes that ticket:\n\n1. **Read the diff.** Open the compare URL and read every change to a watched path,\n   not only the commit messages. A version bump with no rule change still counts\n   as absorbed; skip to step 5.\n2. **Map it.** For each changed rule, name the arwen file and section it lands in,\n   or say why it does not land (another skill owns it, it is a category default\n   arwen refuses, it is a polish number). Use the F23a report's table as the\n   template, and append the new rows to that report.\n3. **RED, then GREEN, with `skill-smith`.** Write a scenario that today's arwen\n   fails because the new rule is missing. Run it and keep the failing transcript.\n   Then edit arwen in its own voice (depth in `references/`, SKILL.md within its\n   budget) and rerun until it passes. The existing arwen GREENs are the\n   regression set; they must still pass.\n4. **Nothing changed in arwen?** That is allowed when step 2 found nothing that\n   lands. Say so in the ticket.\n5. **Bump this file.** Set the row's absorbed commit to `<new>` (the full SHA),\n   set Absorbed to today, and update the sections column. Rerun\n   `upstream-check.sh`. That upstream must now print `unchanged`. Close the ticket.\n\nA watched path that moves or disappears upstream shows up as an `error` row and\nexit 2. Find the new path, update the Watched paths cell, and treat whatever\nchanged in the move as a normal absorb.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\design-arwen\\references\\build.md\r\n# `build` â€” new UI, from nothing\n\nPrereq: SKILL.md Phase 0â€“2 done (platform, register, dials, scene sentence, Design Read,\ndirection, signature). Also read [craft.md](craft.md); product register also reads\n[product-ui.md](product-ui.md). Finish through [verify.md](verify.md) and leave a\n`DESIGN.md` behind. Nothing exists to preserve â€” if\nsomething does, you want [redesign.md](redesign.md) instead.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Pick the substrate before the pixels\n\n**Reach for a real design system when the brief implies one.** Rebuilding Carbon by hand\nis not craft, it's waste â€” and the result is always subtly wrong.\n\n| Brief signal | System | Install |\n|---|---|---|\n| Google / Android-flavored web | Material Web (M3) | `npm i @material/web` |\n| Microsoft / enterprise | Fluent UI React v9 | `npm i @fluentui/react-components` |\n| IBM / data-heavy enterprise | Carbon | `npm i @carbon/react` |\n| GitHub-adjacent devtool | Primer (product) / Primer Brand (marketing) | `npm i @primer/react` |\n| UK public sector | GOV.UK Frontend | `npm i govuk-frontend` |\n| US federal | USWDS | `npm i @uswds/uswds` |\n| Atlassian app | Atlaskit | per-component `@atlaskit/*` |\n| Shopify app | Polaris web components | CDN + `shopify-api-key` meta |\n| Accessible unstyled primitives | Radix | `npm i @radix-ui/themes` |\n| Own-the-code components | shadcn/ui | `npx shadcn@latest init` |\n\n**When the brief is an aesthetic, not a system** (\"Linear-style\", \"brutalist\", \"premium\nconsumer\", \"Awwwards\"), don't install a system â€” build tokens + primitives yourself from\nthe Phase 1 direction. A design system would flatten exactly the thing you're being paid\nfor.\n\n**Verify every dependency before you write an import.** Check it exists at the version you\nname, check the API you're calling is in that version, check the component you reference\nactually ships. Inventing a package or a prop is a shipped bug, not a typo.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Two passes â€” plan, critique the plan, *then* code\n\nThe single highest-leverage habit in this skill. Do the first pass in your head or a\nscratch note; only show the user something you're confident about.\n\n### Pass 1 â€” write the plan\n\nFour blocks, compact:\n\n- **Color** â€” 4â€“6 named values in OKLCH, each with its role (`bg`, `surface`, `ink`,\n  `muted`, `accent`, and at most one more). Name the color strategy from Phase 1.\n- **Type** â€” one or two families: a characterful display face used with restraint and a\n  body face, or one family across weights. If two, make them clearly distinct. No default\n  third mono \"for captions or data\" â€” small mono labels are template chrome; numbers\n  align with `tabular-nums`. Mono only for literal code or IDs, logged as `Deliberate:`.\n  Name the scale ratio.\n- **Layout** â€” one-sentence prose per section, plus an **ASCII wireframe** for anything\n  non-obvious. Wireframes are cheap; they expose a repetitive rhythm before you've written\n  400 lines of CSS. **Say the alignment per section** (left, centred, justified) â€” left\n  unstated it comes out centred everywhere. Brand register: name what the hero opens with,\n  the most characteristic thing in the subject's world (an object, a live demo, a moment),\n  not a metric row.\n- **Signature** â€” the one sentence from Phase 2, and where in the layout it lands.\n\n### Pass 2 â€” critique the plan against the brief\n\nBefore any code: work through a *similar* prompt in your head and see where you land. If\nany block of your plan is where you'd land anyway â€” that's the training-data default, not\na choice for this brief. **Revise that block and say what you changed and why.**\n\n**Then get one lens that is not yours.** Self-critique in your own head produces the\nrationalisation you would predict â€” it did exactly that when this skill was run\nend-to-end. Dispatch **one** subagent, the UX & interaction / a11y seat from\n`redesign.md` Â§3, briefed with the plan and its lens only, and ask it for the top 3\nproblems ranked plus its non-negotiables. One seat, not a panel: `build` is a fresh\nsurface with no audit evidence to spread across three lenses, and a second visual seat\nwould only agree with you.\n\nWhere it conflicts with your plan, **decide** and say why in one line â€” the seat advises,\nyou own the route. No delegate mechanism on this host, or no budget for it? Say\n`critique: self only` in the plan so the reader knows which kind of critique it survived.\nNever report a self-critique as if a second lens had passed it.\n\nRun the three-saturated-looks check here, not after the code exists â€” and, **in the brand\nregister only**, the SKILL.md category-reflex check (both altitudes). In the product\nregister that check is off (SKILL.md Phase 1): test the signature as one systematic UX\ndevice instead, and do not rework toward unguessability.\n\nOnly once the plan survives its own critique do you write code â€” and then you follow it\nexactly, deriving every color and type value from it.\n\n**Optional visual gate:** if the user would rather move boxes than read prose, publish the\nplan as artboards first â€” see [canvas.md](canvas.md).\n\n---\n\n## 3. Walking skeleton before breadth\n\nBuild **one surface fully to spec** â€” real content, all eight states, signature present,\nresponsive, verified in the browser or on the device. It proves the route for a tenth of\nthe cost of proving it across six screens. Then widen.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Build out with subagents\n\nFor anything multi-surface, implement via subagents rather than one linear pass.\n\n- **Slice by surface or workflow.** One subagent owns one screen / flow / component\n  end to end.\n- **Brief every agent identically on the shared system** â€” the resolved direction, the\n  signature and its rules, the token values, and the craft.md bar. Independently built\n  surfaces drifting apart is *the* failure mode; the shared written brief is what keeps\n  them one product.\n- **Isolate parallel file edits.** Agents touching the same tree concurrently â†’ give each\n  a git worktree (`isolation`) so they don't collide; otherwise sequence them. This holds\n  on every host, including ones that run agents in the working tree with no sandbox.\n- **No delegation on this host?** Build the surfaces sequentially in one session off the\n  same written brief. Slower, identical output â€” the brief on disk is what matters, not\n  the parallelism.\n- **Each agent runs its own ship gate and returns evidence** (a real render or\n  screenshot), not a claim. You reconcile, check cross-surface consistency, and relay.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Stack defaults (when the user hasn't picked one)\n\nOnly defaults â€” an existing project's stack always wins.\n\n- React + TypeScript + Vite; Tailwind for utility, CSS custom properties for tokens.\n- Local state first. Reach for a store only when two distant components genuinely share\n  state; never install one for a landing page.\n- No CSS-in-JS runtime for a static page.\n- Dark mode via a `data-theme` attribute or `prefers-color-scheme`, with tokens redefined â€”\n  never a second hand-maintained stylesheet.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Finish\n\nRun the SKILL.md **ship gate** â€” the single gate, tagged `static`/`browser`/`delegated`.\nNo browser? Run every `static` item anyway and report in SKILL.md's terminal-state shape.\nDo not report done on an unrendered page.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\design-arwen\\references\\canvas.md\r\n# The artboard gate â€” tweak the layout before any code exists\n\nOptional. Used by [build.md](build.md) Â§2 and [redesign.md](redesign.md) Â§7 in place of the\nprose route gate, when the user would rather move boxes than read a plan.\n\nIt is also skillator's **visual companion** generally: `PRACTICE.md` Â§1 routes any design\nconversation here the moment a question would be clearer shown than described, including\nconversations that never entered this skill through `build` or `redesign`. Coming in that\nway, you may not have run Phase 1 and 2 â€” draft the artboards in whatever palette, type\nscale and signature the conversation has settled so far, and say in the handover line which\nof those are still open.\n\nThe premise: a written design plan is cheap to correct but hard to *see*. Artboards are\ncheap to correct **and** visible. The most expensive mistake in this skill is building six\nsurfaces off a route the user would have redirected in ten seconds had they seen it.\n\n---\n\n## When to open the gate\n\n**Offer it (one line, don't insist) when:**\n- The work is multi-surface â€” a flow, an app, a redesign of more than two screens.\n- The user is non-technical, or has been correcting you in visual terms (\"no, wider\",\n  \"move that up\").\n- Layout is the contested axis. Boxes on a canvas settle it; paragraphs don't.\n\n**Skip it when:**\n- **There is no user, or the repo is read-only.** A subagent, a batch run, a CI pass: the\n  gate needs someone to tweak the boxes and an `Artifact` publish to put them there, and\n  neither exists. Say `canvas: skipped (no user)` in one line and go straight to code.\n  This is the first check, before the offer conditions above â€” offering a canvas to\n  nobody costs a turn and returns nothing.\n- One component or one scoped `improve` pass. Building it is faster than drawing it.\n- The question isn't visual. A requirements, tradeoff or scope question belongs in the\n  terminal; a single architecture diagram belongs in a mermaid block, not a canvas.\n- The user said \"just do it\", or is clearly in a hurry.\n- The direction is already pinned by an existing design system or a Figma file.\n\nNever make it mandatory and never make it a second full design cycle. It replaces the\nroute-confirmation screen; it doesn't add a phase.\n\n---\n\n## How to run it\n\n1. **Delegate to the `design` skill** â€” it owns the canvas format.
