Don't hand-roll\n   artboards or reimplement the editor.\n2. **Draft one artboard per surface**, laid out on the single pan/zoom canvas in the order\n   a user would meet them (entry â†’ main â†’ detail â†’ empty/error). Include the unglamorous\n   states: an empty list and an error are where routes usually turn out wrong.\n3. **Carry the real design into them.** The artboards use the Phase 1 palette (actual OKLCH\n   values), the chosen typefaces, the real type scale, and the Phase 2 signature visibly\n   present on at least two artboards. An artboard in generic greybox proves nothing â€” the\n   whole point is that the user is reacting to *this* direction.\n4. **Use real content**, not lorem. Placeholder copy makes a layout look fine that isn't.\n5. **Publish and hand over the link**, with one line on what you want feedback on\n   (\"the nav model and where the signature lands â€” the rest is settled\").\n\n---\n\n## The round trip â€” turning their saved artboard into code that matches\n\nThis is the part that makes the gate worth more than a mockup. The user edits artboards in\nthe published canvas and saves; that save is a republish of the same artifact. Everything\nbelow happens **once**, between their save and the first line of code. It is a gate, not a\nsecond design cycle â€” one pass, one clarification round at most.\n\n### 1. Re-read, then diff mechanically\n\n- `Artifact` with `action: \"read\"` and the canvas URL. **Never build from the version you\n  drafted** â€” build from the version they saved.\n- You still have the local `.dc.html` you published. Write the returned version beside it\n  in the scratch dir and run an actual `diff` of the two. Eyeballing two canvases misses\n  the 12px nudges, and those are exactly the ones that make the build \"not quite it\".\n- Turn the diff into a **change ledger**: one line per edit, bucketed as *geometry*\n  (moved / resized / reordered), *content* (copy, data, labels), *style* (color, weight,\n  size, radius), or *cut/added*. Anything absent from the diff is **approved as drafted** â€”\n  say so, and stop re-litigating it.\n\n### 2. Translate each edit into a concrete code decision\n\nA moved box is not a vibe. Each geometry edit resolves to one named change:\n\n| What they did on the canvas | What it means in code |\n|---|---|\n| Widened / narrowed a column | Re-derive the **ratio**, not a pixel width: 62/38 â†’ `grid-template-columns: 1.6fr 1fr`. Change the track, never a hard px on one child. |\n| Moved a block above another | **Reading order changed.** Move it in the DOM. Never fake it with `order:` or absolute positioning â€” keyboard and screen-reader order must match what they see. |\n| Grew or shrank a gap | Snap to the nearest step on the spacing scale (4/8/12/16/24/40). Ship the token, not `27px`. |\n| Bumped a text size | Snap to the nearest step of the Phase 1 type scale. If nothing fits, add exactly one step to the scale and say you did. |\n| Rewrote copy | Their words are **final copy, verbatim** â€” capitalization and punctuation included. Don't \"tidy\" it. |\n| Deleted an element | It is cut, not hidden. Remove it *and* the data fetch that fed it; ask once whether it needs to live somewhere else. |\n| Moved or resized the signature | Re-run the Phase 2 systematic test in the new position. If the move breaks its rhyme across artboards, that is a conflict â€” Â§3. |\n\n**The canvas has no responsive model.** Every geometry edit is a decision about *one* width\nâ€” the artboard's. Deriving the other breakpoints is yours: state in one line what each\nedited block does at â‰¤640px before you build it, and don't let a widened desktop column\nbecome a 320px overflow.\n\n### 3. When an edit collides with a craft floor\n\nDon't silently obey and don't silently fix. Both produce a build the user didn't approve.\n\n- **Name the floor, give the number, offer the nearest compliant version** â€” one message,\n  one round: *\"the lighter label you set reads 3.1:1 on that surface; craft.md's floor is\n  4.5:1 â€” nearest version is the same grey one step darker, or the same value on a\n  half-tone panel. Which?\"*\n- The floors that edits actually hit: body contrast 4.5:1 / large text (â‰¥24px, bold\n  â‰¥18.67px) & UI 3:1 Â· target 44pt / 48dp on touch, 24 CSS px web pointer Â· overflow at 320px Â· a focus ring that survives the new background Â·\n  reduced-motion Â· a colour-only signal.\n- **Accessibility floors are not overridable**, by the user or by you. If no variant they\n  like clears one, restructure the surface until one does â€” that is the design work, not a\n  concession.\n- Everything else *is* overridable. When they override, it stops being a rule violation and\n  becomes a decision: one line in `DESIGN.md` under **Deliberate**, with the reason.\n- **If the edits add up to a different direction** â€” new palette, new nav model, the\n  signature abandoned â€” that is not gate feedback. Say so and go back to Phase 1 for one\n  question. Do not iterate the canvas a third time.\n\n### 4. Restate the contract, then carry it into the build\n\nBefore any code, restate in â‰¤5 lines: the route as approved, the ledger's geometry\ndecisions as numbers, the floors you flagged and how each resolved. That paragraph *is* the\nbrief now.\n\nWhat travels into the build so the implementation cannot quietly drift:\n\n- **The canvas URL and version** in every subagent's task brief, plus the name of the one\n  artboard that task implements. A subagent that can't see the artboard will invent one.\n- **The derived numbers as tokens, defined once** â€” grid ratios, spacing steps, type steps,\n  the OKLCH values as they ended up on the canvas. Per-component pixel guesses are how six\n  surfaces stop matching each other.\n- **The copy verbatim**, in the brief, not paraphrased.\n- **A drift check at the ship gate:** screenshot each built surface at the artboard's width\n  and put it beside that artboard. Walk the ledger; every line is either visible in the\n  build or is a departure you name in the report with its reason. Silent departures are the\n  failure this whole gate exists to prevent.\n- **One line in `DESIGN.md`:** `Artboard: <url> v<n> â€” approved <date>`, plus any floor\n  that was overridden.\n\nThen continue at [build.md](build.md) Â§3 â€” walking skeleton, subagent build-out, ship gate.\nThe artboards are the brief now; the code must match them, and the ship gate still applies\nin full. **A canvas the user approved is not evidence the interface works** â€” it still has\nto be rendered, measured, and seen.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\design-arwen\\references\\craft.md\r\n# Craft rules â€” production-grade, non-negotiable\n\nRead by `build`, `redesign`, and `improve`. `critique` reads it as the checklist.\nThese are the floor. The direction and signature (SKILL.md Phase 1â€“2) are what\nyou spend creativity on; none of this is where you get inventive.\n\n---\n\n## Color & type\n\n- **Contrast is law:** body â‰¥4.5:1, placeholders 4.5:1 (not the muted-gray default),\n  large text â‰¥3:1 â€” and **large means â‰¥24px, or â‰¥18.67px bold**. WCAG defines it as 18pt\n  / 14pt bold and a CSS px is 3/4 of a pt; \"18px / 14px\" is a unit slip that lets a 20px\n  muted subline pass at 3:1 when it needs 4.5:1. The #1 AI failure is muted gray body\n  text on a tinted near-white â€” if it's even close, push toward the ink end of the ramp.\n  Light gray \"for elegance\" is the single biggest reason AI designs are hard to read.\n- Gray on a colored bg looks washed out â†’ use a darker shade of the bg's own hue.\n  **Explicit colours over chains of translucent overlays**: an alpha-stacked token has no\n  contrast until it knows what is under it, so the static check in verify.md cannot pass\n  it. OKLCH ramps drop chroma near white and near black.\n- **Light text on dark compensates on three axes:** +0.05â€“0.1 line-height, a touch more\n  tracking, one step more weight. Over a blurred or translucent surface, no flat gray\n  text: higher contrast, slightly heavier weight, measured against the worst content that\n  scrolls beneath it (verify.md).\n- **Dark mode is designed, not inverted.** Lighter, desaturated tonal variants; surface\n  elevation stated explicitly; contrast re-checked in that theme on its own.\n- Body line length 65â€“75ch; serif body can run to 75ch and wants ~+0.05 more line-height\n  than a sans. Brand heroes: `clamp()` max â‰¤6rem (~96px) â€” above that the page is\n  shouting, not designing.\n- **Tracking and leading track size.** Line-height per scale step, tight on display and\n  loose on body â€” never one global value. Display letter-spacing floor â‰¥ -0.04em (tighter\n  and letters touch); small text and all-caps get slightly *positive* tracking.\n- `text-wrap: balance` on h1â€“h3, `pretty` on long prose. Respect Dynamic Type on native.\n- Don't pair two near-identical faces (two geometric sans, two humanist sans). Pair on a\n  contrast axis, or one family across weights. **Two families is the ceiling**; a mono\n  face earns a place only for literal code, IDs or machine strings, and is recorded under\n  `Deliberate:` in DESIGN.md. Numeric columns align with `tabular-nums`, not with mono.\n- Theme the small surfaces from the palette: `::selection`, `caret-color`,\n  `text-underline-offset`, the focus ring. Custom scrollbars: brand register only â€”\n  product UI keeps the platform's.\n\n## Layout & space\n\n- Vary spacing for rhythm â€” uniform spacing reads as a wireframe. **More space above a\n  heading than below it** â€” the heading belongs to what follows; read the computed margins.\n- Spacing in `rem`/`em`, not fixed px, so layout scales with the text (the cause behind\n  most 200%-zoom failures in gate item 7).\n- **Cards are the lazy answer.** Use them only when genuinely the best affordance;\n  nested cards are always wrong; identical icon+heading+text grids are slop.\n- **Radius and elevation are tiered by hierarchy.** One radius on everything and one soft\n  grey shadow on every raised thing is the SaaS kit, not a system. A shadow has an offset\n  and a soft blur; a zero-offset coloured halo is decoration.\n- **Every rule, border and divider separates something the reader needs separated.**\n  Removal test: take it away â€” if nothing merges that should not, it goes.\n- Flex for 1D, Grid for 2D â€” don't default to Grid when `flex-wrap` is simpler.\n  Responsive grid without breakpoints: `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(280px, 1fr))`.\n- Semantic z-index scale (dropdown â†’ sticky â†’ modal-backdrop â†’ modal â†’ toast â†’ tooltip).\n  Never 999 or 9999.\n- **Give boards / grids / toolbars / tiles fixed dimensions** so they don't shift when\n  labels or hover states appear (design-time CLS).\n- Test heading copy at every width â€” overflow is a bug, not a rendering quirk. The\n  viewport is part of the design. The fix for a long token (URL, ID, filename) is\n  `overflow-wrap: anywhere` plus `min-width: 0` on the flex/grid child that holds it â€”\n  never `word-break: break-all` on prose.\n- Full-height heroes and sheets use `100dvh` / `min-height: 100dvh`, not `100vh` â€” on a\n  phone `100vh` puts the CTA under the URL bar.\n- **Vary the section rhythm.** A page where every section is centered-heading +\n  three-column-grid is one layout repeated, not a design. Alternate: asymmetric split,\n  full-bleed, offset image, list, editorial column.\n\n## Interaction (the a11y-critical part most AI code skips)\n\n- **Design all eight states:** default, hover, focus, active, disabled, loading, error,\n  success. **Hover â‰  focus** â€” keyboard users never see hover. Gate hover styles behind\n  `@media (hover: hover)`; `(hover: none)` gets the active state instead, `(pointer:\n  coarse)` gets more padding. Sticky hover on a phone is a bug.\n- **Press feedback paints within 100ms, on pointer-down** (`:active`), not on release â€”\n  it sits outside the 150â€“250ms state band. `touch-action: manipulation` removes the\n  tap delay; only pay the double-tap delay where a double-tap action exists.\n- **Never `outline: none` without a replacement.** Use `:focus-visible`; ring â‰¥3:1\n  contrast, 2â€“3px, offset *outside*, consistent everywhere.\n- **Sticky chrome never hides focus** (WCAG 2.4.11). Every sticky header, toolbar, banner\n  or table `thead` you add owes a `scroll-padding-top` (on the page, or on the scroll\n  container the sticky element lives in) at least its own height, so a tabbed-to row\n  never lands underneath it. Sticky bottom bars owe `scroll-padding-bottom`.\n- **Target size, by input:** touch â‰¥44Ã—44pt (iOS) / 48Ã—48dp (Android) with 8dp between;\n  web pointer â‰¥24Ã—24 CSS px (WCAG 2.5.8, AA) â€” the floor a dense table's sort buttons,\n  row actions and inline links must meet. 44 is the AAA target, right for touch and for\n  primary actions; applying it to every web control makes DENSITY 9 impossible, so do not.\n- Forms: **placeholders are not labels** (ship a visible `<label>`); **validate on blur**,\n  not per-keystroke; errors *below* the field, wired via `aria-describedby`.\n- **Undo beats confirmation** â€” remove immediately + undo toast; reserve confirm dialogs\n  for truly irreversible or batch actions. Optimistic updates only for low-stakes state,\n  never payments.\n- Menus / tooltips / modals â†’ **Popover API or native `<dialog>`** (light-dismiss, correct\n  stacking, focus trap, Escape for free; `inert` the background). Fixes the #1 generated\n  bug: a `position: absolute` dropdown clipped by an `overflow: hidden/auto` ancestor.\n- **Roving tabindex** for tabs / menus / radio groups, plus skip links.\n- **Gestures are invisible** â€” never gesture-only; hint via partial reveal or coach mark,\n  and always ship a visible fallback. **Pointer drag counts** (WCAG 2.5.7): every\n  sortable list, kanban card, slider or resizable column has a single-pointer and a\n  keyboard route (buttons, a menu, arrow keys).\n- **Feedback is continuous during a gesture** â€” a drag, slider or sheet tracks the finger\n  1:1 and never animates only on completion. Interrupted gestures clear their state on\n  `pointercancel`, `lostpointercapture`, release outside the control and window `blur`; a\n  second pointer never hijacks the drag; the drag surface sets `touch-action`. How it\n  *feels* is `apple-design`'s; that it cannot get stuck is yours.\n- **Every screen answers:** where am I, where can I go, what is here, how do I get out.\n  Put a control near what it affects â€” if it needs a label to explain the mapping, the\n  mapping is weak.\n\n## Motion\n\n- Intentional, designed in from the start â€” not a layer added at the end.\n- One orchestrated entrance (staggered) beats scattered micro-interactions. **Brand\n  register only** â€” product motion is state-only, 150â€“250ms, no page-load show.\n- **Durations** (the band `improve` step 10 checks against): press â‰¤100ms Â· state\n  change 150â€“250ms Â· brand routine 150â€“300ms Â· overlay or view change 300â€“500ms Â·\n  authored brand entrance 500â€“800ms. **Exit faster than entrance.** A gesture-driven\n  spring has no duration â€” judge it by response (~0.3â€“0.4s), not by this band.\n- Ease-out exponential (quart / quint / expo). **No bounce, no elastic â€” one exception:**\n  a spring released from a momentum gesture (flick, throw, drag release) may overshoot,\n  damping â‰¥0.8, and never under reduced motion. Gesture-driven motion runs on springs,\n  not CSS transitions or `@keyframes`; the spring itself is `apple-design`'s.\n- **Interruptible, always.** Never lock out input during a transition; a reversed or\n  re-triggered animation starts from the current (presentation) value, not the target;\n  never make state correct only when `transitionend` / `animationend` fires.\n- **Enter and exit along the same path**; a popover or dialog grows from its trigger\n  (`transform-origin` at the trigger).\n- Hover motion only on things that are actually clickable â€” a hover lift on every card is\n  the kit, not a design.\n- Don't animate layout properties unless truly needed. Premium motion is not only\n  transform/opacity: blur, `backdrop-filter`, `clip-path`, mask, and shadow/glow are part\n  of the palette when they materially improve the effect and stay smooth.\n- **Reduced motion is mandatory, and it means fewer and gentler, not none.** Under\n  `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` / Reduce Motion, drop movement (translate, scale,\n  parallax, overshoot, loops) and keep what confirms state â€” a checkmark, a colour change,\n  a panel's open/closed â€” as a short crossfade (â‰¤150ms) or an instant state swap that is\n  still visible. A global kill (`*, *::before, *::after { transition-duration: 0.01ms\n  !important }` or `transition: none` on everything) fails the gate: it trades a\n  vestibular fix for a comprehension bug. Scope the override to the moving properties.\n- **Reveals enhance an already-visible default.** Never gate content visibility on a\n  class-triggered transition: transitions pause on hidden tabs and headless renderers, so\n  the reveal never fires and the section ships blank.\n- Staggering items *within one list* is legitimate. The tell is the uniform reflex â€” one\n  identical entrance applied to every section. Suppressing the reflex is never a reason to\n  ship a page with zero motion.\n- Nonessential loops stop when offscreen or the tab is hidden. Autoplay and auto-advance\n  (carousels, tickers) get a pause control and stop on focus and under reduced motion\n  (WCAG 2.2.2).\n- **Forbidden:** parallax on body text Â· scroll-jacking Â· anything that delays first\n  meaningful paint Â· a spinner where a skeleton or optimistic state belongs Â·\n  **vestibular triggers** â€” full-viewport moving backgrounds, slow loops near 0.2 Hz (one\n  cycle per ~5s), abrupt brightness jumps (ease a darkâ†”light theme switch).\n- Reach for a library when the need is real (motion, GSAP, anime.js, Lenis) rather than\n  hand-rolling a timeline.\n\n## Delight â€” earn it\n\n- **Budget:** each moment <1s, skippable, subtle, never delays core function. **Vary the\n  response** (not the identical animation every time); it must still please on the 100th view.\n- Confetti only for real milestones. Default success = checkmark-draw or gentle scale+fade.\n- **Loading-message ban** (instant AI tell): \"Herding pixels\", \"Teaching robots to dance\".\n  Write messages specific to what the product does (\"Syncing your team's changesâ€¦\").\n- Match copy personality to brand (a bank can be warm, not wacky). **Never playful during\n  a critical error.** Reward non-visual users too (alt-text wit, a console message).\n\n## Backgrounds & imagery\n\n- Depth over flat fills: grain/noise, geometric pattern, layered\n  transparency, dramatic shadow â€” matched to the lane, never decorative-by-reflex.\n- **Translucent materials** (`backdrop-filter`, vibrancy) are a committed direction on any\n  platform, never a default. When they ship: a `prefers-reduced-transparency: reduce`\n  fallback that goes frostier or solid, and `prefers-contrast: more` near-solid with a\n  defined border; never a light translucent surface on another; contrast measured against\n  the worst content beneath (verify.md).\n- **Imagery is mandatory when the brief implies it** (restaurant, hotel, travel, fashion,\n  product). Zero images is a bug; colored `<div>` placeholders are worse than stock.\n  Unsplash `https://images.unsplash.com/photo-{id}?auto=format&fit=crop&w=1600&q=80` â€”\n  **verify each ID resolves** (guessed IDs 404). Search the physical object, not the\n  category. Alt text in brand voice.\n- Icons: one set, consistently (Lucide, Phosphor, Heroicons, SF Symbols, Material\n  Symbols). Never mix sets, never emoji as iconography. A decorative icon beside visible\n  text is `aria-hidden=\"true\"`; an icon-only control has an accessible name and, for a\n  toggle, its state (`aria-pressed` / `aria-expanded`).\n\n## Performance is design (perceived quality)\n\n- **LCP < 2.5s:** preload the hero image and critical font; the largest paint must not\n  hang off a JS-loaded chain. Fonts pulled via JS or `@import` are late-discovered â€”\n  preload them and avoid swap reflow.\n- **CLS < 0.1:** explicit `width`/`height` or `aspect-ratio` on all media; reserve space\n  for injected content.\n- **INP < 200ms.** Ship right-sized modern formats (WebP/AVIF), not oversized PNGs.\n  `async` / `defer` on head scripts.\n- Animate on the compositor (`transform`, `opacity`); `will-change` only on elements\n  actually about to animate, removed after.\n- Keep DOM cost sane â€” a 5000-node landing page is a bug regardless of how it looks.\n\n## UX copy â€” words are design material\n\nCopy makes a design feel as templated as the layout does. Bring the same intentionality\nto it as to spacing and color.\n\n- **Write from the user's side of the screen.** Name things by what people control and\n  recognize, never by how the system is built. A person manages notifications, not\n  webhook config.\n- **Active voice, sentence case, plain verbs.** A control says exactly what happens:\n  \"Save changes\", not \"Submit\". An action keeps the same name through the whole flow â€”\n  the button that says \"Publish\" produces a toast that says \"Published\".\n- **Specific beats clever.** Describe what something does; don't sell it.\n- **Errors don't apologize and are never vague.** Say what went wrong and how to fix it,\n  in the interface's voice. An empty screen is an invitation to act, not a shrug.\n- **Name navigation for its contents** (\"Invoices\", \"Library\"), not an umbrella (\"Home\",\n  \"Hub\").\n- **Each element does exactly one job.** A label labels, an example demonstrates, nothing\n  quietly does double duty.\n- **Never ship placeholder content as if it were real** â€” no \"Jane Doe\", no lorem, no\n  `example.com`, no fake logos, no invented metrics. **No em-dashes in UI copy**; use a\n  period, a comma, or a colon.\n\n## Native & framework conventions (first-class)\n\nObey the platform's grammar; break it only with intent, only where the signature demands,\nnever breaking accessibility. Touch targets â‰¥44Ã—44pt (iOS) / 48Ã—48dp (Android) with 8dp\nbetween them (web pointer: 24 CSS px, see Interaction); respect safe areas, notches,\nkeyboard insets. A tab bar or bottom nav holds at most 5 sections (sections, never\nactions), each with a label beside its icon.\n\n- **iOS (HIG):** nav/tab bars, sheets with grabber, SF Symbols, Dynamic Type, large-title\n  â†’ inline, haptics for meaningful moments on the same frame as the visual change, native\n  back-swipe. Blur/vibrancy is the platform default here; elsewhere it is a direction\n  choice (Backgrounds & imagery).\n- **Android (Material 3):** top app bar + FAB, nav bar/rail, Material You dynamic color,\n  tonal elevation, ripple, predictive back, edge-to-edge with insets, type in `sp`, never\n  fixed px. Don't ship an iOS layout on Android.\n- **React Native:** `expo-image`, `Pressable` over `TouchableOpacity`, native stack/tabs,\n  native modals/menus, `StyleSheet.create` or Nativewind, custom fonts via Expo config\n  plugin. **60fps:** animate only `transform`/`opacity` via Reanimated (`useDerivedValue`\n  for computed), `Gesture.Tap` for animated presses. **Lists are the #1 jank source:**\n  FlashList + memoized items + hoisted styles + item types. Footguns: never falsy `&&`\n  render (stray `0`), wrap all text in `<Text>`, `onLayout` not `measure()`.\n- **Web:** semantic HTML, headings in sequence (h1â†’h2â†’h3, no skipped level â€” pick the\n  level by structure, the size by CSS), keyboard nav + visible focus, responsive from\n  320px and in landscape. **Viewport meta is `width=device-width, initial-scale=1` and\n  never blocks zoom** â€” no `user-scalable=no`, no `maximum-scale=1`. `prefers-*` queries\n  honored by name: `reduced-motion`, `reduced-transparency`, `contrast`, `color-scheme`.\n  Form controls render at â‰¥16px or iOS Safari zooms the page on focus.\n- **View Transitions (web/React):** declare *what* with `<ViewTransition>`, trigger *when*\n  via `startTransition`/`Suspense` (never call `startViewTransition` yourself); set\n  `default=\"none\"` and enable only intended triggers. Directional slide only for\n  hierarchical (listâ†’detail) or ordered nav; **tab-to-tab fades, not slides**.\n  Shared-element morph = same unique `name` (`photo-${id}`). After a client-side route\n  change, move focus to the new page's `<main>` (or its h1) â€” otherwise it stays on a link\n  that no longer exists.\n\n## Push past limits (only when the brief earns it)\n\n**Propose 2â€“3 directions and get an explicit pick before building** â€” ambition misfires\nmost. No user to pick (subagent, batch run)? Do not propose into the void and do not take\nthe boldest by default: take the one the register argues for, name the two you rejected\nand why in one line each, and carry on.  Context defines \"extraordinary\": a particle system dazzles on a portfolio and\nembarrasses on a settings page. For functional UI the wow is how it *feels* â€” a dialog\nmorphing from its trigger, a 100k-row table at 60fps.\n\nTools: **View Transitions** (shared-element morph) Â· **`@starting-style`** (animate\n`display:none` â†’ visible in CSS) Â· **scroll-driven** `animation-timeline: scroll()` with a\nstatic fallback Â· **`@property`** (makes gradients and colors animatable) Â· **virtual\nscrolling** for huge lists.\n\n**Progressive enhancement is non-negotiable** â€” gate with `@supports`, fall back\nWebGPU â†’ WebGL2 â†’ CSS; the un-enhanced experience is still good. **Removal test:** take\nthe effect away â€” if nobody notices, it wasn't earning its place. Never layer competing\nwow moments; focus makes impact, excess makes noise.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\design-arwen\\references\\critique.md\r\n# `critique` â€” diagnosis only, no code\n\nPrereq: SKILL.md Phase 0 (platform + register, so you judge against the right bar) and\n[craft.md](craft.md) as the checklist â€” plus [product-ui.md](product-ui.md)'s twelve-cell\nscale test and slop test when the target is product UI. See it rendered first\n([verify.md](verify.md)); a critique written from source is a guess.\n\n**Write no code in this mode.** Not \"here's a quick fix\", not a patch at the end. The\ndeliverable is a ranked, evidenced list the user can act on or hand to `improve` /\n`redesign`. Offering to implement afterwards is fine; doing it unasked is not.\n\n---\n\n## 1. See the real thing\n\nScreenshot every in-scope surface (drive the browser, run the screen) and read the actual\ncode for the ones you'll cite. A critique of a described interface is worthless. If you\ncannot render it, say so and critique the code only â€” labelled as such.\n\nCapture both themes if both ship, and at least 320px / tablet / desktop widths.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Judge against the register, not your taste\n\n- **Product register:** the bar is trust and fluency. \"Would someone fluent in\n  Linear/Figma/Notion/Stripe trust this, or pause at every subtly-off component?\"\n  Familiarity is a feature here â€” do not mark a conventional dashboard down for being\n  conventional.\n- **Brand register:** the bar is memorability and craft. \"Could someone say *AI made\n  that* without doubt?\" Here, conventional *is* the finding.\n\nSay which bar you're using in the first line.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Run the checklist\n\nIn this order â€” the top of the list is where the real damage lives:\n\n1. **Accessibility & correctness** â€” contrast on real pairs, focus visibility, hover-only\n   affordances, labels vs placeholders, keyboard traps, gesture-only actions, touch target\n   sizes, reduced-motion.\n2. **Broken states** â€” overflow at any width, missing loading/empty/error/success,\n   clipped dropdowns, mixed light/dark sections, CLS on hover.\n3. **Hierarchy & information architecture** â€” can a first-time user find the primary\n   action in two seconds? Is anything competing for the same rank? Count it: one primary,\n   one or two secondary, the rest grouped; â‰¤4 choices at a decision point is easy, 5â€“7\n   is pushing, 8+ is overload; ~5 top-level nav items at most. Does every screen say\n   where you are and how to get out? Is each control next to what it affects?\n4. **Craft** â€” type scale, line length, spacing rhythm, alignment, component consistency,\n   palette discipline.\n5. **Distinctiveness** â€” the shared anti-slop floor's bans (`anti-slop.md`, cited from\n   SKILL.md's ship gate) plus arwen's three, the content/copy tells, and the signature test\n   (cover the logo: is it still this product?). The two-altitude category-reflex check\n   applies **in the brand register only** â€” per Â§2 above, a conventional product surface is\n   not a finding, so in the product register judge whether one systematic UX device is\n   present and consistent, never whether the category is guessable.\n6. **Performance as perceived quality** â€” obvious LCP/CLS offenders. Measured numbers only\n   if you actually measured.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Report\n\nOne finding per line. Ranked by severity, most damaging first. Each finding carries:\n\n- **Severity** â€” P0 blocks shipping (a11y failure, broken state, unreadable text) Â·\n  P1 costs real quality Â· P2 is polish. Torn between two? Would a user contact support\n  about it â€” if yes, it is at least P1.\n- **Where** â€” the surface and, when you read it, `file:line`.\n- **What's wrong** â€” one sentence, factual. Not \"the spacing feels a bit tight\".\n- **The fix** â€” one sentence, concrete enough to act on without a second conversation.\n\nClose with **one line** naming the single highest-leverage change, and the `improve` lane\nor the `redesign` depth that would deliver it. Nothing else â€” no score-out-of-ten unless\nasked, no encouragement paragraph, no \"overall this is a solid foundation\".\n\n**Panel option.** For a whole app rather than one screen, run the 2â€“3 expert lenses from\n[redesign.md](redesign.md) Â§3 and reconcile their findings into one ranked list before\nreporting. Same rule holds: reconcile, don't average, and still write no code.\n\n**Scored or persona review.** Asked for heuristic scores, personas, or design judgment\nkept apart from detector evidence? That is `impeccable critique`, not this pass â€” name it.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\design-arwen\\references\\improve.md\r\n# `improve` â€” a scoped pass on working UI\n\nPrereq: SKILL.md Phase 0â€“2 (abbreviated â€” read the register and the existing tokens; you\ndon't need a fresh scene sentence if the identity is already committed) plus\n[craft.md](craft.md). Read `DESIGN.md` if the repo has one â€” it is the identity you are\ntuning, not replacing. Product-register surfaces (forms, tables, settings, flows) also\nread [product-ui.md](product-ui.md). Every lane ends in [verify.md](verify.md), scoped to\nwhat the lane touched.\n\n`improve` is deliberately narrow. The UI works; one dimension of it doesn't. Do that\ndimension well and leave the rest alone. **Scope creep is the failure mode here** â€” if\nyou find yourself rewriting the layout during a color pass, you wanted `redesign`.\n\n---\n\n## Route the intent\n\nName the lane in one line before you start, so the user can redirect you cheaply.\n\n| The user said | Lane | What you actually do |\n|---|---|---|\n| \"polish\", \"final pass\", \"ship-ready\" | **polish** | The full procedure below â€” the most-used lane, so it is written out rather than described. |\n| \"bolder\", \"it's bland\", \"make it pop\" | **bolder** | Raise VARIANCE by 2â€“3. Commit the color strategy one step up the axis, raise the type scale ratio, strengthen the signature. One bold move, not five. |\n| \"quieter\", \"too much\", \"calm it down\" | **quieter** | Lower VARIANCE and MOTION. Cut decoration, reduce the palette, cut competing wow moments, keep the single signature. |\n| \"spacing\", \"hierarchy\", \"alignment\", \"it feels off\" | **layout** | Spacing scale, rhythm variation, optical alignment, section-rhythm repetition, overflow at every width. |\n| \"fonts\", \"typography\", \"hard to read\" | **typeset** | Pairing on a contrast axis, scale ratio, line length, line height, weights, `text-wrap`, tracking floor. |\n| \"colors are flat\", \"add color\", \"washed out\" | **colorize** | Color strategy first, then OKLCH values, tinted neutrals, accent discipline, contrast re-verified. |\n| \"add motion\", \"it feels static\" | **animate** | State transitions first. Reveals only if MOTION â‰¥4. Reduced-motion path in the same commit, always. |\n| \"mobile\", \"tablet\", \"responsive\", \"it breaks onâ€¦\" | **adapt** | Every breakpoint from 320px, touch targets, safe areas, keyboard insets, and on native the platform-idiomatic layout, not a shrunk web one. |\n| \"production-ready\", \"edge cases\", \"i18n\" | **harden** | Error and empty states, loading, long strings, RTL, zero/one/many, offline, permission-denied, slow network. |\n| \"it's slow\", \"janky\", \"laggy\" | **optimize** | LCP/CLS/INP against craft.md budgets. Measure first â€” delegate a real audit to `web-perf` rather than guessing. |\n| \"this copy is bad\", \"reword the error\" | **clarify** | The UX copy rules in craft.md. Rewrite from the user's side of the screen. |\n| \"it's boring\", \"add personality\" | **delight** | The earn-it budget in craft.md. One moment, varied, skippable. |\n| \"too complex\", \"strip it back\" | **distill** | Remove, don't add. Cut every element that isn't doing a job. Chanel's rule, applied hard. |\n| \"first-run\", \"empty state\", \"onboarding\" | **onboard** | Activation path, empty states as invitations, progressive disclosure over a wall of setup. |\n\nTwo lanes clearly apply â†’ do both, say so. Five apply â†’ the user wanted `redesign`; say\nthat and ask.\n\n---\n\n## How every lane runs\n\n1. **Look at the real thing first.** Screenshot it, or read the exact file. Never improve\n   from a description.\n2. **Say what's wrong and what you'll change** â€” a short ranked list, not an essay.\n3. **Change only what the lane owns.** An adjacent bug you spot goes in the report, not\n   in the diff, unless it's a contrast or a11y failure â€” those you fix on sight.\n4. **Preserve the identity.** Existing committed brand colors, fonts, and the existing\n   signature win over the shared floor's reflex-reject lists. You are tuning, not\n   re-branding.\n5. **Verify.** Re-render, re-screenshot, re-check contrast. The relevant subset of the\n   SKILL.md ship gate, not the whole thing â€” but the parts your lane touched, in full.\n6. **Report the before/after** in one or two lines per change. Evidence, not adjectives.\n\n---\n\n## The `polish` procedure (written out, because it's the one people actually run)\n\nPolish is not \"look at it again and improve things\". It is a fixed sweep in a fixed order,\nat low amplitude, changing **nothing structural**. If a fix requires moving a section or\nre-picking a font, it belongs to `layout` or `typeset` â€” note it and move on.\n\nWork top to bottom. Each pass gets a one-line verdict: fixed / already fine / out of scope.\n\n**1. See it first.** Screenshot at 320/768/1280/1920 in both themes ([verify.md](verify.md)).\nRead the console. Polish decided from source instead of pixels is guesswork.\n\n**2. Contrast, measured.** Body, muted, placeholder, disabled, on-accent text, focus ring â€”\ncomputed off the rendered elements, not the tokens. Numbers in the report. This finds a real\nfailure roughly every time; it is the highest-yield pass and it is first for that reason.\n\n**3. The eight states.** default Â· hover Â· focus Â· active Â· disabled Â· loading Â· error Â·\nsuccess, for every interactive element. **Hover â‰  focus** is the usual miss. A disabled\ncontrol with no reason is a bug ([product-ui.md](product-ui.md)).\n\n**4. Focus, keyboard, order.** Tab the whole surface. Visible ring everywhere, sensible\norder, nothing trapped, Escape closes what it opens, skip link present.\n\n**5. Optical alignment.** Not \"is it on the grid\" â€” is it *on the grid to the eye*. Icon\nbaselines against text, numerals right-aligned and tabular, punctuation hung where it\nmatters, cap-height vs. line-box centring in buttons and chips, consistent inline gaps.\nThis is where \"designed\" and \"assembled\" separate.\n\n**6. Spacing consistency.** Every value comes from the scale. Adjacent components that\nshould share a rhythm do. Nothing is a one-off `13px`.\n\n**7. Empty, loading, error, success â€” as rendered, not as intended.** Force each one.\nSkeletons match the real layout. Empty states carry an action. Errors say what and what next.\n\n**8. Copy.** craft.md's UX-copy rules at close range: sentence case, active voice, one job\nper element, consistent action naming across a flow, **no em-dashes**, no placeholder names\nor fake metrics that survived from the mockup.\n\n**9. Overflow and long strings.** The snippet in verify.md at each width â€” then again with\nthe longest real label, not the placeholder.\n\n**10. Motion, if any.** Durations in band for the register, ease-out, reduced-motion path\npresent and *content still visible* under it.\n\n**11. Assets.** Every font, image, and icon resolves. No guessed IDs. One icon set.\n\n**12. Theme parity.** Every fix re-checked in the other theme. Polish that only holds in\nlight mode is half a pass.\n\n**Then the signature check.** Cover the logo on two screens â€” is it still one product?\nPolish is where signatures quietly get sanded off: someone normalises the one asymmetric\nthing because it looked like a mistake. Confirm it survived.\n\n**Report:** the numbers and the diffs, one line each, plus what you found and deliberately\nleft for another lane.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\design-arwen\\references\\product-ui.md\r\n# Product UI â€” the surfaces where real apps actually live\n\nRead this whenever the register is **product** (SKILL.md Phase 0) and the work touches a\nform, a table, settings, a multi-step flow, permissions, search, or bulk actions.\n[craft.md](craft.md) is the floor for every surface; this file is the grammar for the\nspecific ones â€” the patterns that carry an app after the landing page has done its job.\n\n**Why this file exists.** Landing-page craft is well covered everywhere. The surfaces\nbelow are where most product work happens and where generated UI most reliably falls\napart: it renders beautifully with three rows of fake data and collapses at four hundred\nreal ones, with a German label, on a slow connection, for a user without permission.\n\n**The register still rules.** Everything here assumes product register â€” usability leads,\nfamiliarity is a feature, the signature (Phase 2) shows up in the *chrome and the moments*\n(empty states, transitions, the one component you reimagined), never in the data path.\n\n---\n\n## The scale test â€” run it before designing any of these\n\nEvery surface below gets designed against **four data volumes**, not one:\n\n| Volume | What it proves |\n|---|---|\n| **Zero** | The empty state is an invitation with an action, not a shrug. |\n| **One** | Nothing assumes plurality â€” no \"1 items\", no grid that needs three cards to look right. |\n| **Typical** | The design you're actually pitching. |\n| **Thousands** | Pagination or virtualisation, a real scroll story, no 5,000-node DOM. |\n\nAnd **three conditions**: loading (skeleton matching the real layout, never a spinner\nwhere content shape is known), error (what broke + what to do, in-place not a toast that\nvanishes), and no-permission (the surface explains, it doesn't 404 or show a dead button).\n\nA product surface designed for one volume and one condition is a mockup. **Twelve cells,\nevery time** â€” if you only designed the happy typical one, you are not done.\n\n---\n\n## Forms\n\nForms are where product UI is won or lost, and they are the most-skipped surface in\ngenerated code.\n\n**Structure**\n- **One column.** Multi-column forms cause misreads and break on mobile. The only\n  legitimate side-by-side pairs are genuinely atomic: city/state, expiry/CVC, first/last.\n- **Group by the user's mental model, not the database schema.** If two fields are always\n  filled together, they sit together; a fieldset legend beats a floating heading.\n- **Ask less.** Every field is a cost. Optional fields that nobody needs are the cheapest\n  thing to delete and the most common thing to keep.\n- Mark **optional** fields, not required ones, when most are required (and the reverse).\n  A form of fifteen asterisks communicates nothing.\n\n**Labels & help**\n- A visible `<label>` always. Placeholders are not labels â€” they vanish on focus, fail\n  contrast, and break autofill and screen readers. This is in craft.md and it is still\n  the most-violated rule in generated forms.\n- Help text sits **above** the input when it changes how you fill it, **below** when it\n  clarifies after. Never inside the placeholder.\n- Formatting requirements are stated **before** the error, not discovered through it.\n\n**Validation**\n- **Validate on blur, never per-keystroke.** Telling someone their email is invalid while\n  they type the third character is hostile.\n- Re-validate on submit, and **move focus to the first error** with the count announced\n  (`aria-live`): \"3 fields need attention\". Past ~5 fields, or with more than one error,\n  put a focusable **error summary** at the top instead, each item a link to its field,\n  focus it, and keep the inline errors too (the GOV.UK pattern).\n- Errors sit **below the field**, wired with `aria-describedby`, in text â€” never colour\n  alone, never a red border as the only signal.\n- **Never clear the form on error.** Never clear a password field on an unrelated error.\n  Losing typed input is a data-loss bug, not a styling choice.\n- Server errors land on the field that caused them when the server can say which.\n\n**Submission**\n- The submit button is **disabled only while in flight**, never as a validation gate â€” a\n  permanently-disabled button with no explanation is the single most frustrating form\n  pattern. Let people submit and show them what's wrong.\n- Show in-flight state on the button itself (label swap + spinner), keep the width fixed\n  so the layout doesn't jump.\n- **Double-submit protection** is design, not just engineering: the second click must be\n  inert.\n- Long forms: save draft state. A tab close that loses twenty minutes of typing is the\n  bug people remember.\n\n**Inputs**\n- Correct `type`, `inputmode`, `autocomplete` â€” this is free mobile UX and free autofill.\n  `autocomplete=\"one-time-code\"` for OTP, `inputmode=\"decimal\"` for money. Inputs,\n  selects and textareas render at â‰¥16px, or iOS Safari zooms the page on focus.\n- Native `<select>` beats a custom dropdown until it genuinely can't do the job (search,\n  multi-select, rich rows). A custom one owes you: keyboard nav, typeahead, `aria-expanded`,\n  Escape, click-outside, and a portal so it isn't clipped.\n- Destructive and primary actions are **never adjacent** at the same visual weight.\n\n---\n\n## Data tables\n\nThe single most under-designed component in generated product UI.\n\n**Before styling anything, decide:** is this a table, or a list? Tables are for comparing\nvalues across a shared set of columns. If people scan one record at a time, a list or a\ncard layout reads better and survives mobile.\n\n**Columns**\n- Right-align numbers, use **tabular figures** (`font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums`), and\n  align decimal points. Left-align text. Never centre either.\n- Column widths are **stable across pages** â€” a table that reflows when page 2 has a longer\n  name is broken. Set widths or `table-layout: fixed`.\n- Truncate with a tooltip *and* a title attribute, never mid-word wrap in a numeric column.\n- The first column identifies the row and stays visible (sticky) when scrolling\n  horizontally.\n- More than ~7 columns needs a column-visibility control, not a horizontal scrollbar as the\n  whole answer.\n\n**Rows**\n- Row height stays constant. Content that varies gets truncated or a detail view, not a\n  jagged table.\n- **Zebra striping is usually the lazy answer** â€” a 1px row separator and generous cell\n  padding reads cleaner. Stripe only when rows are dense and wide.\n- Hover highlights the row; the whole row is clickable **only if there is exactly one\n  obvious action**, and then a keyboard user needs the same affordance.\n- Sticky header, always, past one screen of rows â€” and the scroll container that holds it\n  gets `scroll-padding-top` â‰¥ the header's height, so a keyboard user's focused row never\n  sits under it (craft.md, Interaction).\n- **Dense is not tiny.** Compact rows are fine; the sort buttons, row actions and links\n  inside them still meet 24Ã—24 CSS px. Make the sort control a `<button>` filling the\n  header cell, not a 12px arrow glyph.\n\n**Sorting, filtering, search**\n- Sort state is visible on the column (direction arrow + `aria-sort`), and **survives a\n  reload** â€” put it in the URL. Same for filters and page. A shareable table URL is a\n  feature people notice.\n- Filters show what's applied as removable chips, with a \"clear all\". A filtered table that\n  looks empty must say *why* it's empty: \"No results for these filters. Clear filters.\"\n  This is a different empty state from \"you have no data yet\" â€” design both. Too many\n  chips wrap to a new line before any label shrinks; a `+3` summary is a button that\n  opens the rest, never a way of hiding them.\n- Search debounced ~300ms, with a visible in-flight indicator that doesn't move the layout.\n\n**Scale**\n- Under ~100 rows: render them. Over: paginate or virtualise. **Say which one you chose and\n  why.** Infinite scroll is wrong for tables people need to compare or return to â€” it\n  breaks the back button and makes footers unreachable.\n- Show total count. \"1â€“50 of 1,284\" tells people where they are; \"Page 1\" doesn't.\n\n**Bulk actions**\n- Checkbox column, a header checkbox with an **indeterminate** state, shift-click for a\n  range.\n- The action bar appears without shifting the table (reserve its space or overlay it) and\n  states the count: \"3 selected\".\n- **Select-all means the page, not the query** â€” unless you explicitly offer \"Select all\n  1,284\" as a second, separate step. Silently deleting a whole filtered set because someone\n  clicked a header checkbox is a catastrophe.\n- Bulk destructive actions are the one place a confirm dialog beats undo, and it must name\n  the count and the thing: \"Delete 47 invoices?\"\n\n---\n\n## Settings\n\n- **Organise by what people are trying to change**, not by which service owns the value.\n  A settings page mirroring your backend modules is a schema on screen.\n- **Save model: pick one and be consistent.** Either auto-save each control with a quiet\n  confirmation, or an explicit Save with a dirty-state bar. Mixing them within one page is\n  how people lose changes.\n- Auto-save needs a visible result â€” a brief \"Saved\" near the control, not a toast in the\n  far corner. And it needs a failure path: what happens when the save fails and the toggle\n  is now lying?\n- **Dangerous settings live apart** â€” a separate section, different treatment, typed\n  confirmation for the truly irreversible ones (delete account, rotate keys, transfer\n  ownership).\n- Show the **effective** value when it's inherited or overridden: \"Using the workspace\n  default (Weekly)\". A toggle that doesn't say where its value came from is a support ticket.\n- Search across settings once there is more than one screen of them.\n- Every toggle states what happens when it's on, in a label a person would say out loud.\n\n---\n\n## Multi-step flows (onboarding, checkout, wizards)\n\n- **Show where they are and how much is left.** A stepper with real names beats \"Step 2 of\n  5\", which beats nothing.\n- **Back must work** â€” the browser's back button included. A flow that breaks on back is\n  broken.\n- **Persist between steps.** Going back and forward loses nothing, ever. Never ask for\n  what the flow already has (billing = shipping, \"confirm your email\") unless re-entry is\n  the point (WCAG 3.3.7). Help and contact links sit in the same place on every step.\n- Validate per step, not all at the end. But never block forward motion on something that\n  can be fixed later â€” let people skip and come back where the data genuinely allows it.\n- **Ask for the minimum that unblocks the next step.** Everything else belongs in settings\n  after activation. The most common onboarding failure is a wall of setup before any value.\n- The final step states exactly what happens on confirm, and it is the only step with an\n  irreversible action.\n- **Exit is always available**, and says what will be kept: \"Your progress is saved.\"\n- One step per screen beats a scroll of accordions.\n\n---\n\n## Permissions, roles, and the disabled state\n\n- **A disabled control must say why.** A greyed-out button with no tooltip, no adjacent\n  text, and no path forward is the least helpful state in software. Either explain it, or\n  don't render it.\n- Prefer **explaining over hiding** for capability gaps (\"Upgrade to invite teammates\") and\n  **hiding over explaining** for things a role should never see.\n- Design the **read-only** variant of every editable surface. Viewers get a legible page,\n  not an editor full of dead inputs.\n- Never rely on hidden UI for security â€” but never show an action that will fail either.\n- Permission errors after the fact (\"You don't have access to do that\") name who can grant\n  it.\n\n---\n\n## Notifications, toasts, and feedback\n\n- **Match the message to the surface.** Inline for anything tied to a specific element;\n  toast only for background or navigational results; a banner for account-wide state.\n- Toasts: bottom-corner, ~4â€“6s, stack to a max of 3, pause on hover, dismissible, never\n  carrying the only copy of important information, and **never the only report of an\n  error** the user must act on.\n- A toast that says \"Something went wrong\" is not a design. Say what, and offer the retry\n  in the toast.\n- **Undo in the toast** beats a confirm dialog for anything reversible â€” this is craft.md\n  policy and it applies hardest here.\n- Announce async results to screen readers (`role=\"status\"` / `aria-live=\"polite\"`; assertive\n  only for genuine interruptions).\n\n---\n\n## Modals, drawers, and inline editing\n\n- **\"Modal as first thought\" is banned in product register** (SKILL.md Phase 0). Exhaust\n  inline editing, then progressive disclosure, then a drawer, before a modal.\n- Reach for each one deliberately: **inline** for a single value Â· **popover** for a small\n  focused choice Â· **drawer** for a related side-task that keeps context visible Â· **modal**\n  for a genuine interruption that must be resolved Â· **full page** for anything with more\n  than ~7 fields or its own sub-navigation.\n- **Never nest modals.** A modal that opens a modal means the flow needed a page.\n- Native `<dialog>` or the Popover API â€” focus trap, Escape, light-dismiss, and correct\n  stacking for free (craft.md).\n- A modal with unsaved changes confirms before closing â€” including on Escape and on the\n  backdrop click. Otherwise Escape is a data-loss key.\n\n---\n\n## Dashboards\n\n- **Lead with the answer, not the chart.** The number people came for is the largest thing\n  on screen; the visualisation supports it.\n- Every metric states its **time range and its comparison** (\"Last 30 days, vs previous\n  30\"). A number with no period is decoration.\n- **Charts and series colours belong to `dataviz`** â€” it owns the validated light+dark\n  palette and the mark/axis/legend rules. Don't re-derive them here.\n- The hero-metric template is banned as a *reflex* (the shared floor, Â§2), not as a\n  concept â€” a big number is often right. What's banned is the four-tile scaffold applied\n  because dashboards look like that.\n- Density is the point (DENSITY 8â€“10). Airy dashboards waste the screen people chose a\n  dashboard to fill.\n- Loading: skeletons shaped like the real tiles, and each tile resolves independently â€” one\n  slow query must not block the page.\n\n---\n\n## Internationalisation, RTL, and long strings\n\nGenerated UI is designed in English at typical length and breaks everywhere else.\n\n- **Design at +40% string length.** German and Finnish routinely run 30â€“50% longer than\n  English; a nav or button that fits exactly in English is already broken. Test the longest\n  real label, not the placeholder.\n- **Never concatenate sentences from fragments.** Word order differs; `\"Delete \" + n + \" items\"`\n  is untranslatable. One string with a parameter.\n- **Pluralisation is not `n === 1`.** Use `Intl.PluralRules`; several languages have more\n  than two forms and some have none.\n- **Dates, numbers, currency, and names go through `Intl`.** Never hand-format. Never assume\n  a first-name/last-name split, a fixed name order, or that a name fits in one line.\n- **RTL:** use logical properties (`margin-inline-start`, `padding-block`, `inset-inline`)\n  rather than left/right throughout â€” it costs nothing to write and makes RTL a flag rather\n  than a rewrite. Mirror directional icons (back, next, indent); **do not** mirror media\n  controls, clocks, or logos.\n- Line-height and font stacks must handle CJK and Arabic without clipping ascenders or\n  diacritics. `lang` attributes set correctly so the right font and hyphenation apply.\n- Time zones: display in the user's zone with the zone named where it matters. \"Today\" and\n  \"Yesterday\" are computed in their zone, not the server's.\n\n---\n\n## The product-UI slop test\n\nProduct register's failure mode is not \"looks AI-made\" â€” familiarity is a feature here.\nIt is **\"subtly wrong in a way that erodes trust.\"** Ask:\n\n1. Would a user fluent in Linear / Stripe / Notion / Figma pause at any component here,\n   sensing something is off â€” a focus ring that doesn't match, a dropdown that closes\n   wrong, a table that reflows between pages?\n2. Does every surface survive the **twelve cells** (four volumes Ã— three conditions)?\n3. Does any control lie â€” a toggle that shows on before the save confirms, a disabled\n   button with no reason, a count that doesn't match the filter?\n4. Can someone drive the entire surface with a keyboard, and see where they are the whole\n   time?\n5. Is there exactly **one** save model, one selection model, one empty-state voice across\n   the app?\n6. **Can the eye hold it?** One primary action and one or two secondary per view, the\n   rest grouped; at most ~5 top-level nav items. Eight competing choices is overload,\n   however tidy.\n\nFailing any of these ships a product that *works* and still feels untrustworthy. That is\nthe specific way product UI fails, and no amount of aesthetic polish repairs it.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\design-arwen\\references\\redesign.md\r\n# `redesign` â€” an app that already exists\n\nPrereq: SKILL.md Phase 0â€“2, plus [craft.md](craft.md) â€” and `DESIGN.md` if the repo has\none: it is the inherited identity the preservation rules below protect. Product register\nalso reads [product-ui.md](product-ui.md); finish through [verify.md](verify.md). The difference from `build` is\nthat something is already working and someone already recognizes it. **Identity\npreservation beats novelty.** A redesign that erases what users recognize is a rewrite\nsold as an improvement.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Audit before touching anything\n\nOne opinion on a described app is a guess. Critique reality:\n\n- **Capture the real thing** â€” screenshots of every in-scope surface (drive the browser,\n  run the screen). A picture is worth a thousand tokens.\n- **Read the code** â€” tokens/theme file, the two or three key screens, the component\n  primitives, the existing breakpoints.\n- **Inventory what exists**: palette (actual values, not vibes), typefaces and scale,\n  spacing system, component library or lack of one, motion, dark mode support, and the\n  framework you're constrained to.\n- **List what's actually broken** vs what's merely dated. These get different treatments.\n\nWrite the inventory down. Every subagent downstream reads it.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Decide the depth â€” targeted evolution or full redesign\n\n| Signal | Verdict |\n|---|---|\n| Layout works, palette/type are dated, components inconsistent | **Targeted evolution** |\n| Brand is committed and recognizable, only craft is failing | **Targeted evolution** |\n| Information architecture itself is wrong; users can't find things | **Full redesign** |\n| No system at all â€” every screen invented separately | **Full redesign** (build the system first) |\n| User explicitly said \"overhaul / start over\" | **Full redesign** |\n\nSay which one you picked and why, in one line. Defaulting to full redesign because it's\nmore fun to build is the most expensive mistake in this file. **Refinement preserves;\nredesign replaces.** Never split the difference â€” a full redesign that keeps polishing\nthe look it is discarding ships neither.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Convene the expert panel (2â€“3 lenses, not clones)\n\nDispatch each as a **subagent** (Agent tool on Claude Code; the host's delegate mechanism\nper `PLATFORMS.md` â€” beside the installed skills or at the repo root â€” elsewhere;\nsequential in-session passes where there is none). Brief each with the audit evidence,\narwen's doctrine, and **its lens only**:\n\n- **Visual / brand director** â€” aesthetic direction, signature, type and color, hierarchy.\n- **UX & interaction / a11y lead** â€” flows, the eight states, focus and forms, cognitive\n  load, empty and error states.\n- **Native platform specialist** â€” HIG / Material 3 / RN idioms. Include when native.\n- **Product / growth strategist** â€” conversion, activation, information scent. Brand\n  register only.\n\nRequire each to return, structured: **top 3â€“5 problems** (ranked, naming the offending\nsurface), a **proposed design route** (direction + the one signature it would forge), and\nits **non-negotiables vs nice-to-haves**.\n\nRun them concurrently â€” they're independent. Diverse lenses beat N identical reviewers;\noptionally give the visual seat a more creative model and the a11y seat a more rigorous one.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Resolve to ONE route â€” reconcile, don't average\n\nWhere the panel agrees = strong signal, act on it. Where it conflicts, **decide** with a\none-line rationale (in *product* register usability outranks flourish; in *brand* register\nidentity outranks safety). Never ship a committee.\n\nOutput three things:\n1. the chosen **direction** (Phase 1 lane + named reference),\n2. the single **signature** (Phase 2),\n3. a **ranked change list** â€” what Â· why Â· which surface.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Preservation rules â€” what never changes silently\n\nThese require an explicit user decision, never a designer's initiative:\n\n- **The logo, the brand name, and the committed brand color.** You may re-tune a palette\n  *around* them; you may not replace them because a trendier hue scores better.\n- **Product terminology and menu labels** users have learned.\n- **URL structure, page inventory, and nav order** â€” content disappearing in a \"redesign\"\n  is a bug report waiting to happen.\n- **Working flows.** If checkout, onboarding, or search works, restyle it; don't rebuild\n  it because a new pattern is fashionable.\n- **Anything the user explicitly named as fixed.**\n\nIf the route requires breaking one of these, say so out loud and get a yes first.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Modernisation levers â€” in priority order\n\nApply top-down and stop when the app is good. Most tired interfaces are fixed by the\nfirst three, and the first three are also the cheapest and safest.\n\n1. **Contrast and type scale.** Fix unreadable muted text and a flat ~1.1 scale. Biggest\n   perceived-quality jump per line changed.\n2. **Spacing rhythm and alignment.** Consistent scale, varied section rhythm, real\n   optical alignment.\n3. **Component consistency.** One button, one input, one card â€” not five near-copies.\n4. **Palette re-tune.** Tinted neutrals in OKLCH around the existing brand hue; commit to\n   a color strategy.\n5. **Typeface change.** High-impact and high-risk; only when the current face actively\n   fights the brand.\n6. **Motion.** State transitions first, reveals second, orchestration last and only in\n   brand register.\n7. **Layout restructure.** Highest cost, highest risk. Justify it with a real UX problem\n   from the audit, never with \"it felt dated\".\n\n---\n\n## 7. Confirm the route (one screen), then build\n\nPresent the resolution â€” direction, signature, ranked change list, depth verdict, anything\nhitting a preservation rule â€” and let the user correct it **before any code**. This gate\nis cheap; a wrong route is expensive; their correction is the highest-value input you'll\nget. Skip only if they said \"just do it\" â€” or if there is **no user to present to** (a\nsubagent, a batch run): then take the resolved route and build; your report lists the\nranked changes, any preservation-rule hits, and the depth verdict as decisions already\nmade on the user's behalf (`assumed:` optional, not required). Do not block on a\nconfirmation nobody will give.\n\n**Optional visual gate:** publish the route as before/after artboards instead of prose â€”\nsee [canvas.md](canvas.md).\n\nThen hand off to [build.md](build.md) Â§3â€“6: walking skeleton on the highest-traffic\nsurface, subagent build-out off the shared brief, ship gate on every surface.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\design-arwen\\references\\terminal.md\r\n# terminal.md â€” designing for the terminal (CLI Â· TUI Â· tmux)\n\nThe terminal is a platform like iOS or the web, and Phase 3 treats it as one: the\nidentity is shared, the material is not. Read this when the surface is a\ncommand-line tool, a full-screen terminal app, or a line in someone's status bar.\n\n**Phases 0â€“2 transfer unchanged.** The Read line, the dials, committing to a\ndirection, and forging a signature all work here exactly as written â€” a terminal\ntool has a register, an audience and a scene like anything else. Do not re-derive\nthem and do not soften them because the medium is text. What changes is only what\nthe material can carry.\n\n---\n\n## 1. The material\n\n**A cell grid, not a canvas.** Position is integer columns and rows. There is no\nsub-pixel, no fractional spacing, no arbitrary radius. The spacing rhythm from\nPhase 2 survives as *cell counts* â€” a 2/4/8 rhythm becomes 1/2/4 cells â€” and\nanything that relied on fine spacing has to be re-expressed as alignment,\ngrouping, or blank lines.\n\n**You do not own the font.** The reader's terminal picks it, and they may have\nchosen it for reasons you cannot see. So type *personality* â€” arwen's usual\ncarrier of identity â€” is unavailable. There is no scale, no pairing, no weight\naxis beyond what SGR gives you: bold, dim, italic (often unsupported), underline,\nreverse. Identity has to move somewhere else.\n\n**Where identity actually lives here**, in rough order of strength:\n\n1. **Glyph vocabulary** â€” the small set of marks the tool reuses everywhere.\n2. **Density and rhythm** â€” how much breathes, what groups, where the rules fall.\n3. **Colour** â€” as accent and state, never as the sole carrier (Â§2).\n4. **Alignment grammar** â€” what is right-aligned, what is elided, what is fixed.\n5. **Copy voice** â€” terse, active, lowercase or not; a terminal reads copy closely.\n\n## 2. Three capability axes, all read from the stream\n\nColour, glyphs and width are **independent**. A stream can be a 24-bit-colour\nterminal that cannot encode `â”€`, or a 200-column pipe that must not receive a\nsingle escape byte. Design for each separately and let each degrade on its own.\n\n**Decide every one from the stream, never from the environment.** Environment\nvariables describe a terminal that may not be on the other end of your output. On\na real machine this session, `TERM=xterm-256color` and `COLORTERM=truecolor` while\n`sys.stdout.isatty()` was `False` and `stdout.encoding` was `cp1252` â€” all three\nsignals disagreeing at once. A tool that picks its tier from `TERM` writes\ntruecolor escapes into a pipe.\n\n| Axis | Tiers | Decide from |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Colour** | none Â· 16 ANSI Â· 256 Â· truecolor | `isatty()` first; then `NO_COLOR` (set, any value, wins), `TERM=dumb`, then `COLORTERM`/`TERM`. Offer an explicit `--color=always/never/auto` |\n| **Glyph** | unicode Â· ASCII twin | whether the stream's encoding can actually encode the glyph set â€” try it, don't infer it |\n| **Width** | the number of columns you were given | the stream's reported size, with a sane default; never a constant |\n\n**The signature must survive tier collapse.** Whatever carries identity has to\nstill read at the bottom of all three ladders: no colour, ASCII glyphs, 60\ncolumns. If the design dies there, it was decoration. State in particular is\n**glyph + colour + word, always all three** â€” that is one rule serving both the\ncolour-blind reader and the monochrome pipe, and it is why it is not optional.\n\n**Every non-ASCII glyph is chosen with its ASCII twin at startup.** `skillator:tui-tron`\nÂ§4 owns this rule and the assertion that proves it; do not restate it here. Two\npoints belong to *design*: pick the twin when you pick the glyph, so the fallback\nis a considered mark rather than a substitution someone makes later in a hurry â€”\nand never \"fix\" an encoding problem by deleting the glyph for everyone.\n\n## 3. The three surfaces have different contracts\n\nThey are as different from each other as a web page is from a watch face. One\nidentity, three correct expressions.\n\n**Declare the narrowest width you support, and lay out to it.** 60 columns is the\nusual floor â€” a split pane. That number is a *design* decision and it is made here,\nnot later: every column budget is derived from it, every fixed-width field is checked\nagainst it, and prose is wrapped to it. A layout that reads the terminal width and\nthen draws a constant-width table has not used the number it fetched. When there is\nno terminal â€” a pipe â€” you are choosing a fixed default instead, and that default is\nstill bounded by the floor.\n\n**The floor binds the longest line, on every surface.** Not the rule, not the\nborder â€” the longest *rendered* line. A divider is the one element whose width you\ncan see at a glance, so it is the element that gets clamped, and the data row that\noverruns it by two cells is the one that ships. A format string like\n`\"%s  %-16s %-6s %-12s %s %s\"` has a width: add it up and check it against the floor,\nbecause nothing else will.\n\nMeasure each surface separately and list them so none is skipped â€” the one-shot\nreport, the full-screen frame, the status line, `--help`, and the error paths. The\nsurface you are thinking about while you fix the floor is the one that ends up\ncorrect; the others are where the overflow lives.\n\n**CLI â€” one-shot, line-oriented, someone else's input.** Its output is data:\ngreppable, pipeable, pasteable. Stable column order, one record per line where\nthat is plausible. **stdout is the data, stderr is the narration** â€” progress,\nwarnings and spinners go to stderr so a pipe stays clean. Not a tty means no\ncolour, no cursor control, no spinner, no clear. Exit codes are part of the\ndesign. `--help` is a designed surface, not a dump: the common path first, the\nfull list behind `--help --all`.\n\n**TUI â€” full-screen, redraw, yours until you exit.** Use the alternate screen\nbuffer (`\\033[?1049h` / `\\033[?1049l`) so the user's scrollback is returned to\nthem intact; hide the cursor and restore it in a `finally`, always. Every screen\nneeds visible focus, a key hint row, and an answer for the narrowest width you\nsupport. Motion is state-change only: a redraw tick, a progress line rewritten\nwith `\\r`. No continuous animation â€” it burns a CPU core and wrecks a shared\ntmux pane.\n\n**tmux status â€” one line in a bar you do not own.** It is never a tty, so colour\ncomes from tmux's own `#[fg=...]` format, not raw SGR, and plain text is the\ncorrect fallback. You get a tight, shared, unpredictable budget: design the\n*compressed* form first â€” counts and marks, not sentences â€” and make it\ntruncate from the least important end. It is refreshed on tmux's interval, so it\nmust be cheap and must never block.\n\n## 4. What you own, and what `tui-tron` owns\n\nArwen designs it; **`skillator:tui-tron` proves it renders** â€” the width matrix\nacross every surface, the ASCII-fallback scan, and the capture rig for hosts with\nno pty. Hand over at the ship gate rather than eyeballing one terminal.\n\n**Delegating the proof does not delegate the constraint.** Naming tui-tron is not a\nsubstitute for choosing a width behaviour: a layout with no answer at its own declared\nfloor is unfinished before tui-tron is ever invoked, and shipping it because the\nmatrix \"belongs to the other skill\" is how an overflowing design passes a ship gate.\nFitting the floor is yours. Proving it across the matrix, on every surface and at\nevery cursor position, is theirs.\n\nThree failures from this skill's own test runs, each from designing a terminal\ntool without these rules, each worth recognising on sight:\n\n- A single hardcoded glyph set with the encoding \"fixed\" by reconfiguring stdout.\n  It works until the stream is a pipe or a legacy console, and then the tool's\n  state grammar is mojibake. One glyph tier is a bug, not a choice.\n- Width discipline applied to the full-screen surface only, while the one-shot\n  CLI overflowed at 60 columns. **Every surface gets the matrix**, including the\n  one that looks like plain text.\n- The floor applied to the dividers and not the rows: `min(width, 60)` on every\n  rule, while the row format summed to 62 and overran each one by two cells at\n  every width. The report surface was clean and the dashboard was not, because\n  only the report had been measured.\n\n## Related\n\n- `references/craft.md` â€” the production rules the terminal inherits (colour\n  meaning, copy, motion policy); this file overrides only where the material differs\n- `skillator:tui-tron` â€” proves the frame fits, at every width, with no pty\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\design-arwen\\references\\verify.md\r\n# Verify â€” the ship gate, executed\n\nThe gate in SKILL.md lists *what* must be true. This file is *how you prove it*. A gate\nyou assert instead of run is not a gate; it is a claim, and claims are exactly what this\nskill exists to replace.\n\n**The rule: nothing is done until it has been seen rendering.** Not \"the code looks\nright\". Seen.\n\n---\n\n## Pick your driver â€” in this order\n\n1. **`claude-in-chrome`** (the MCP browser tools). Invoke the `claude-in-chrome` skill\n   first, then load the tools in **one** `ToolSearch` call. Best when the dev server is\n   already running and you want screenshots, console, and network in one place.\n2. **`browse`** (gstack's headless daemon) â€” faster for a screenshot-and-assert loop when\n   you don't need to interact.\n3. **`webapp-testing`** â€” when the check needs real interaction scripted and repeated.\n4. **Native:** the iOS Simulator / Android emulator, or `ios-design-review` when the app\n   runs on real hardware.\n5. **`screenshot-argus`** â€” when the user is driving and dropping screenshots in for you.\n   Read them, act, delete the consumed ones.\n\nIf none is available, **say so explicitly and report in SKILL.md's terminal-state shape**\n(`built Â· verified: â€¦ Â· not verified: â€¦ Â· delegated: â€¦`). The `static` items of the gate\nare still mandatory without a browser â€” they need no render. Do not narrate a render you\ndid not perform. An honest \"built, not yet seen\" is worth more than a confident \"verified\"\nthat was inferred from the source.\n\n**There is one gate and it lives in SKILL.md.** This file never restates it; the steps\nbelow are the procedures that prove its items. If a step here seems to add a requirement\nSKILL.md does not list, SKILL.md wins and this file is the bug.\n\n---\n\n## The run\n\nTake the screenshots **before** you report, not after the user asks.\n\n1. **Boot it.** Find the dev command (`package.json` scripts, README, Makefile). Start it,\n   wait for the port, navigate.\n2. **Console first.** Read console messages before looking at pixels â€” a React key warning\n   or a failed font fetch explains most of what you're about to see. Filter with a pattern\n   rather than dumping everything.\n3. **Network.** Confirm every font, image, and asset actually resolved. This catches the\n   invented Unsplash ID and the CDN path that 404s â€” gate item 3, and it fails silently in\n   a screenshot because the fallback font looks fine.\n4. **Screenshot the widths that matter:** 320, 768, 1280, and 1920, plus a phone in\n   landscape (~640Ã—320, where sheets and sticky bars eat the screen). Not \"mobile and\n   desktop\" â€” 320 is where headings overflow and 1920 is where a max-width you forgot lets\n   a line run to 200 characters. Settle or disable entrance motion first, then **open every\n   screenshot once** and confirm it shows what its name claims â€” a black, blank or\n   mid-transition capture proves nothing.\n5. **Screenshot both themes** if both ship. Toggle it the way a user would.\n6. **Drive the states.** Screenshots of the default view prove almost nothing. Get to\n   loading, empty, error, and success â€” force them (throttle the network, block the\n   request, clear the data) rather than describing them.\n7. **Keyboard the whole surface.** Tab from the top: is focus visible at every stop, in\n   order, never trapped, and does Escape close what it should? Then scroll to the bottom\n   and **Shift+Tab back up** â€” that is when a sticky header lands on the focused control.\n\n**Bounded rounds.** Build fully, inspect in one batched round, fix, confirm in at most one\nmore round, and stop. Two rounds is the ceiling; a third is polishing by anxiety. What is\nstill open goes in the report's \"not verified\" line, not into round four.\n\n---\n\n## Checks that need a procedure, not an eyeball\n\n**Contrast.** Do not eyeball it and do not trust the token names. Pull the computed colours\noff the real rendered elements and compute the ratio:\n\n```js\n// in the page, via the javascript tool\nconst ratio = (a, b) => {                       // a, b: [r,g,b] 0-255\n  const L = c => { const s = c.map(v => v/255)\n    .map(v => v <= 0.03928 ? v/12.92 : ((v+0.055)/1.055) ** 2.4)\n    return 0.2126*s[0] + 0.7152*s[1] + 0.0722*s[2] }\n  const [x, y] = [L(a), L(b)].sort((p, q) => q - p)\n  return (x + 0.05) / (y + 0.05)\n}\n```\n\nSample the pairs that actually fail in practice: body text on its real background,\n**placeholder text**, muted/secondary text, disabled labels, text on a coloured button,\nand the focus ring against both the element and the page. Body â‰¥4.5, large â‰¥3, focus ring\nâ‰¥3 â€” where **large is â‰¥24px, or â‰¥18.67px at bold weight** (18pt / 14pt). A 20px regular\nsubline is body text. Over a translucent surface, sample against the worst\ncontent that can sit beneath it, not the tint. Report the numbers, not \"contrast checked\".\n\n**Targets and obscured focus.** Measure, don't infer from padding:\n\n```js\n[...document.querySelectorAll('a[href],button,input,select,textarea,[role=button]')]\n  .map(el => [el, el.getBoundingClientRect()])\n  .filter(([, r]) => r.width && (r.width < 24 || r.height < 24))   // web pointer floor\n  .map(([el, r]) => `${el.tagName} ${el.textContent.trim().slice(0,20)} ${r.width}x${r.height}`)\n```\n\nA hit fails unless it is a link inside a sentence or has 24px of clear space around it\n(WCAG 2.5.8's exceptions). For obscured focus, after each Shift+Tab stop:\n`document.elementFromPoint(centre of the focused rect)` must be the focused element or\ninside it, never a sticky header.\n\n**Colour-blind check.** DevTools â†’ Rendering â†’ Emulate vision deficiencies:\ndeuteranopia, protanopia, achromatopsia. Every state that reads by colour must still read.\n\n**Overflow.** At each width, find anything wider than its container:\n\n```js\n[...document.querySelectorAll('*')]\n  .filter(el => el.scrollWidth > el.clientWidth + 1)\n  .map(el => `${el.tagName}.${el.className}`)\n```\n\nEmpty array or it isn't done. Then repeat with the **longest real string** in place, not\nthe placeholder â€” see the +40% rule in [product-ui.md](product-ui.md).\n\n**Reduced motion.** Emulate it (`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`) and reload. Every reveal\nmust still show its content. The failure this catches is fatal and invisible otherwise:\ncontent gated behind a class-triggered transition ships blank. Then drive the state\nchanges â€” open the panel, toggle, sort, submit â€” and confirm each is still *visibly*\nconfirmed. Grep the stylesheet too: a `*` / `*::before` / `*::after` rule setting\n`transition` or `animation` to `none` or ~0 inside the media query is the global kill, and\nit fails the item (craft.md, Motion). Where `backdrop-filter` ships, emulate\n`prefers-reduced-transparency` as well.\n\n**Motion at 10% speed.** DevTools â†’ Animations â†’ 10%. Play each transition, then trigger\nit again mid-flight: it must reverse from where it is, never jump to the end or ignore the\ninput.\n\n**Mechanical scan, if `impeccable` is installed.** Run `impeccable detect --json` once on\nthe changed web files and triage its hits (contrast, tiny text, skipped headings, bounce\neasing, overused fonts). Not installed, or a native target: skip it and say so in one line.\n\n**LCP / CLS / INP.** Delegate to `web-perf` â€” it owns the Chrome DevTools workflow and the\nmeasured numbers. Arwen owns the design-time rules; it does not re-implement the audit.\n\n**Native.** Run the screen on a device with the largest Dynamic Type setting and with a\nnotch and home indicator present. Touch targets â‰¥44Ã—44pt (iOS) / 48Ã—48dp (Android), 8dp\napart â€” measure them, don't assume the component library got it right.\n\n---\n\n## Signature check (Phase 2, verified rather than asserted)\n\nWith the screenshots in hand:\n\n- Put two different screens side by side, **cover the logo** â€” is it still obviously the\n  same product? If not, the signature isn't systematic yet.\n- **Brand register only:** find a real competitor screenshot in the same category. Could it\n  pass for yours? If yes, you shipped the category default.
