In the **product** register skip\n  this â€” it usually could, and should (SKILL.md Phase 1). Check instead that the one\n  systematic device is present wherever its state occurs.\n- Point at where the signature appears on the empty state and the error state. If it only\n  appears on the hero, it is decoration, not a signature.\n\n---\n\n## Reporting\n\nReport evidence, not adjectives. One block, short:\n\n```\nVerified: 320/768/1280/1920 Â· light+dark Â· loading/empty/error/success\nContrast: body 8.1 Â· muted 4.7 Â· placeholder 4.6 Â· focus ring 3.4  (all pass)\nOverflow: none at any width, incl. longest label (\"RechnungsempfÃ¤nger\")\nReduced motion: content visible, reveals crossfade\nConsole: clean Â· Network: all 24 assets 200\nNot verified: INP (no field data) â€” run web-perf for measured vitals\n```\n\n**Always state what you did not verify.** The list of unchecked items is the most useful\nline in the report, and omitting it is how \"done\" comes back at 3am.\n\n---\n\n## OKLCH â†’ contrast, without a browser\n\nPhase 1 mandates OKLCH tokens; the DOM snippets above need `[r,g,b]`. Without this you\ncannot check gate items 1-2 until something renders, which is exactly when it is too late\nand too expensive to change a palette. Run it on the token values themselves:\n\n```js\n// node contrast.mjs â€” oklch(L C H) with L as 0..1, plus WCAG ratio.\nconst f=(n)=>n<=0.0031308?12.92*n:1.055*Math.pow(n,1/2.4)-0.055;\nconst oklch2rgb=(L,C,H)=>{const h=H*Math.PI/180,a=C*Math.cos(h),b=C*Math.sin(h);\n  const l=(L+0.3963377774*a+0.2158037573*b)**3, m=(L-0.1055613458*a-0.0638541728*b)**3,\n        s=(L-0.0894841775*a-1.2914855480*b)**3;\n  return [ 4.0767416621*l-3.3077115913*m+0.2309699292*s,\n          -1.2684380046*l+2.6097574011*m-0.3413193965*s,\n          -0.0041960863*l-0.7034186147*m+1.7076147010*s]\n        .map(v=>Math.round(Math.min(1,Math.max(0,f(v)))*255));};\nconst lum=([r,g,b])=>{const c=[r,g,b].map(v=>{v/=255;\n  return v<=0.03928?v/12.92:((v+0.055)/1.055)**2.4;});\n  return 0.2126*c[0]+0.7152*c[1]+0.0722*c[2];};\nexport const ratio=(A,B)=>{const [x,y]=[lum(A),lum(B)].sort((p,q)=>q-p);\n  return +((x+0.05)/(y+0.05)).toFixed(2);};\n// ratio(oklch2rgb(0.215,0.012,240), oklch2rgb(0.985,0.003,240)) -> body text vs page\n```\n\nCheck **every** pair the gate names, not just body-on-page: placeholder, disabled text,\neach border against its surface (item 2 â€” a 1.06:1 input border passes every text check\nand is still a defect), the focus ring against the page, and on-accent / on-danger text.\nRing-vs-filled-button is not required: WCAG 1.4.11 measures the ring against the adjacent\nbackground, so a 2px `outline-offset` that keeps the ring on the page background is the\ncorrect construction, not a dodge.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\designui-galadriel\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: designui-galadriel\ndescription: >-\n  Use when an EXISTING UI-only mockup must become real and\n  working - the user says their app is \"just a UI\", \"not functional\", \"fake\n  / hardcoded data\", \"no backend\", \"the buttons don't do anything\", or asks\n  to \"make it real\", \"wire it up\", \"connect it to a backend/API/database\".\n  Ends at a confirmed plan; does not build. NOT for Figma-to-UI,\n  design/UX/a11y review, styling bugs, debugging a feature that already\n  works, a brand-new app with no mockup, or backend-only fixes.\n---\n\n# designui-galadriel â€” a mockup becomes a working app\n\nA mockup lies. Every hardcoded list, every button with no handler, every screen\nthat reads from a fixture instead of a database â€” they all *imply* a working\nsystem that isn't there. This skill's job is to find exactly where the illusion\nends, agree with the user on what \"functional\" actually means for each workflow,\nand produce a plan to make it true. It does **not** write the implementation â€”\nit stops at a plan the user has confirmed, so the build phase starts from\nagreement, not guesswork.\n\nWork the three phases in order. Don't skip to the plan: a plan written before\nthe workflows are confirmed is a plan for the wrong system.\n\n**No user to answer?** They're in meetings, you're a subagent or a batch run, or\nthe brief was \"do whatever you think is right â€” just go ahead\". None of that is\nsign-off, and on a skill that ends at a plan, \"go ahead\" means go ahead *to the\nplan*. Absence is never permission to build. Work the phases anyway: answer\nPhase 1b's questions yourself from the code, prefix every answer you picked\nrather than were told with `assumed:`, write the Phase 2 artifacts and the\nPhase 3 plan to a file in the repo (`docs/plans/ui-to-functional.md`), and stop\nthere â€” assumptions listed at the top so the user can overturn them in one pass.\nA plan waiting on disk costs them one read; an implementation built on guessed\nintent costs them a review of every line.\n\n## Phase 1 â€” Map the illusion, then interview\n\nGoal: a shared, honest picture of what's real, what's faked, and what the user\nactually intends. The code answers *what exists*; only the user can answer *what\nit's supposed to do*. Gather the code half first so your questions are concrete.\n\n### 1a. Scan the codebase (do this before asking anything)\n\nDetect the stack and the frontendâ†”backend seam first: framework, language, and\nhow the UI talks to a server if at all (REST/fetch, RPC/IPC, GraphQL, none yet).\nThen find the four tells of a UI-only system:\n\n- **Hardcoded / mock data** â€” literal arrays and objects rendered as if real;\n  fixture or `mock*/fake*/sample*` files; in-memory \"fallback\" datasets a\n  provider falls back to when no backend is present.\n- **Dead controls** â€” buttons, forms, and menu items whose handlers are missing,\n  empty, or `console.log`; forms whose values are never submitted anywhere.\n- **Calls into the void** â€” UI code that calls an endpoint/command/path the\n  server doesn't actually serve (diff the frontend's call sites against the\n  backend's registered routes/handlers).\n- **Faked state** â€” \"Running / Connected / Saved\" indicators, charts, counters,\n  and timers driven by constants, `setInterval`, or random data rather than a\n  real source.\n\nProduce an **inventory** as you go â€” for each screen/feature: what it displays,\nwhere that data comes from (real vs mock), what actions it offers, and whether\neach action does anything. This inventory is the spine of everything downstream.\n\n### 1b. Interview the user\n\nThe code can't tell you the *intent*. Ask in small, grounded batches â€” reference\nwhat you found, so questions are concrete (\"Your `StudentsPage` renders a\nhardcoded roster of 4 names; should students live in a database, and who's\nallowed to add them?\") rather than abstract (\"what are your data requirements?\").\n\nCover, roughly in this order, stopping to let the user answer:\n\n1. **Actors & roles** â€” who uses this, and what is each role allowed to do?\n2. **Per workflow, the real effect** â€” for each meaningful action you found,\n   what should actually happen? (A \"Start\" button â€” does it spawn a process,\n   call an API, write a row, send a message?) What's the success result, and\n   what can go wrong?\n3. **Source of truth** â€” where does real data live or come from? (database,\n   external/3rd-party API, a device or process, files, the user's own input)\n4. **Persistence & identity** â€” does state survive a refresh/restart? Are there\n   accounts, auth, permissions?\n5. **Real-time / external systems** â€” anything that streams, polls, or talks to\n   hardware/another service?\n6. **Scope of \"functional\"** â€” which surfaces must become real, and which are\n   intentionally demo/decorative and should stay as-is (flagged, not faked)?\n\nAsk the high-leverage questions, not an exhaustive survey. Prefer multiple-choice\nwhen the options are knowable. If the user is non-technical, drop the jargon and\nask in terms of what they want to *happen*.\n\n## Phase 2 â€” Workflow artifacts (the confirmation gate)\n\nBefore any plan, write down what you now understand and get the user to confirm\nit. Cheap to fix on paper, expensive to fix in code. Produce:\n\n**Actor catalog** â€” each role and its capabilities, in one short table.\n\n**Workflow specs** â€” one per real workflow. Keep them concrete and verifiable:\n\n```\n## <Workflow name>\nActor:        <who initiates>\nTrigger:      <the UI action / event>\nPreconditions:<what must be true first>\nSteps:\n  1. <UI action> â†’ <system response> â†’ <data change>\n  2. ...\nData in:      <what the user/system provides>\nData out:     <what is stored / returned / shown>\nBackend need: <the real capability this requires â€” endpoint, job, query, device>\nDone when:    <observable acceptance check â€” how you'd prove it works for real>\n```\n\n**System map** â€” UI surfaces â†” data sources â†” backend services, showing\n*current* (mock) vs *target* (real) for each, so the gap is explicit.\n\n**Reality inventory** â€” the Phase-1 inventory, now labelled: REAL / MOCK /\nTO-BUILD / KEEP-AS-DEMO.\n\nPresent these and ask the user to correct them. Do not proceed to the plan until\nthey sign off â€” their corrections here are the most valuable input you'll get.\nNo one there to sign off? Take the no-user branch above: mark the unconfirmed\nparts `assumed:` and carry on to the plan, which is still where you stop.\n\n## Phase 3 â€” Implementation plan (then stop)\n\nTurn the confirmed workflows into a dependency-ordered plan to wire UI â†’ real\nbackend. The plan is the deliverable; you hand it off (to the user, to\n`writing-plans`/`/do`, or a build agent) rather than executing it.\n\nStructure the plan so it builds a **walking skeleton first** â€” one workflow made\nfully real end to end (UI action â†’ backend â†’ real data â†’ UI reflects it) â€” then\nwidens. A thin slice that genuinely works de-risks the architecture before you\npour breadth into it; ten half-wired features prove nothing.\n\nCover:\n\n- **Data model** â€” entities, fields, and where they're stored (DB/schema, files,\n  external system). The source of truth from Phase 1.\n- **Backend capabilities** â€” one unit of work per workflow need: the\n  endpoint/command/job/query to build, with its contract (inputs â†’ outputs,\n  errors). Name what's new vs what already exists.\n- **Frontend rewiring** â€” for each screen: which mock/hardcoded source to replace\n  with the real call, and which dead control to connect to its action. Include\n  the unglamorous states the mock skipped: loading, empty, and error.\n- **Cross-cutting** â€” auth/permissions, persistence, validation, and any\n  real-time/external integration, called out once rather than scattered.\n- **Phasing** â€” ordered phases, each with its dependencies and a **verification\n  step**: the concrete check that the slice works against the *real* backend, not\n  the mock fallback. (A feature isn't \"wired\" until something proves it end to\n  end â€” start the thing, watch the real data move.)\n- **Honest cut list** â€” what stays mock/demo for now and why, so nothing fake is\n  left masquerading as real.\n\nEnd by stating the plan stops here and offering the handoff: \"Confirmed? I can\nhand this to `/do` / writing-plans, or start the walking-skeleton phase.\" The\noffer is a question, and an unanswered question is a no â€” building starts when\nsomeone answers it, never because no one did.\n\n## Principles that carry the whole skill\n\n- **Honesty over polish.** The deliverable's value is telling real from fake.\n  Never let the UI's *implied* capability pass as a real one â€” that's the exact\n  bug being fixed.\n- **Intent before code.** Scanning shows what's there; the interview and the\n  confirmation gate are what make the plan correct. Don't shortcut them.\n- **Skeleton before breadth.** One workflow fully real beats every workflow\n  half-real.\n- **Verify against the real thing.** Many UI-only apps have a \"fallback\" that\n  fakes success when the backend is absent; a wiring is only done when checked\n  against the real backend, with the fallback out of the picture.\n- **Don't boil the ocean.** Wire what's actually used. Decorative UI can stay,\n  but label it so it's a choice, not an accident.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\grayskull-power\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: grayskull-power\ndescription: >-\n  Use when the user says \"dev mode\", \"activate the programming skills\", \"turn\n  on the skillator workflow\", \"use our skills\", \"by the power of grayskull\",\n  \"grayskull\", \"check screenshot\", \"set up for coding\", or starts real\n  development work in a repo with no skillator skill active. Ground rules\n\n- **Reproduce â†’ read â†’ map â†’ tag â†’ fix.** No repro, no remedy. Read the actual\n  file, not memory of the library. Tag every claim `verified` / `inferred` /\n  `guessed`; a `guessed` root cause never justifies an edit.\n- **Blast radius named in one line before the edit â€” no line, no edit.** Not in\n  your head: in your visible output. Smallest change that fixes the cause; no refactoring inside it.\n- **Before a commit:** regression sweep the callers you named, then\n  `code-review:code-review` over the staged diff. `audit-sherlock` is the\n  whole-app / pre-release sweep, **not** a per-commit gate â€” a Fable fan-out an\n  implementer subagent cannot run.\n- **Revert first.** Never stack a fix on a broken fix.\n- **Run to the end.** Six things stop a staged plan â€” destructive op Â·\n  security-sensitive action Â· side effect outside this worktree Â· 7-day limit\n  at 90% Â· scope breach Â· failed repro. Too big for a chip â†’ build a\n  local artifact, then ask.\n- Re-announce the state line only when it changes.\n\n**Load [`references/ground-rules.md`](references/ground-rules.md)** â€” the full\nprocedure behind each: grounding steps, the scope contract, the pre-commit loop\nand its 3-pass cap, the shape of a good question.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\grayskull-power\\references\\arming.md\r\n# Arming the standing skills â€” the detail behind SKILL.md Â§1\n\nRun once, on invoke. Report the open set: `B` bugs,\n  `F` features, `A` agent-found.\n- **`ponytail`** â€” confirm the laziness level is active (the badge in the\n  statusline). Act on the output:\n  - `skipped:` or exit 0: nothing to do.\n  - each `changed <name> <old>..<new> <url>` line (exit 1, and also exit 2,\n    because one failed row does not hide another's change): look for an open\n    (`[ ]` or `[~]`) ticket line in `TICKETS.md` that names both `<name>` and\n    `<new>`. Nothing else in the block\nvaries per repo.\n\n`<SKILL_DIR>` is the directory that contains grayskull-power's own `SKILL.md`:\n\n- **Normally** â€” the skill is installed outside the repo â€” write its **absolute**\n  path, forward slashes, no trailing slash. e.g.\n  `C:/Users/me/.claude/plugins/skillator/skills/grayskull-power`.\n- **If that directory is inside the repo being activated** (skillator itself,\n  dogfooding), write it **relative to the repo root** instead â€”\n  `skills/grayskull-power` â€” so the file stays correct for every clone.\n\nLeave the `/../watch-cortana/â€¦` tail exactly as written; do **not** pre-normalise\nthe `/../` away. Say that once, don't repeat it.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\grayskull-power\\references\\ground-rules.md\r\n# Ground rules â€” grounding, scope, and the pre-commit gate\n\nThe laws are in SKILL.md Â§3. This is how each one is carried out.\n\n## Ground it before you remedy\n\n**No presumption.** A fix for code you have not read is a guess, and a guess that\nhalf-lands is how one bug becomes three `A` tickets.\n\n1. **Reproduce first.** No failing test, failing command, or copied error text â†’\n   no remedy. A fix for a bug you cannot trigger is untestable by definition.\n   Cannot reproduce (needs hardware, a device, a build)? Say so and ticket it â€”\n   do not fix blind.\n2. **Read the source of truth** â€” the actual file, the actual error, the actual\n   upstream docs. Not memory of how the library \"usually\" behaves. The map is\n   second-hand; a stale index lies confidently, so code beats graph on conflict.\n3. **Map before you cut.** `codegraph explore \"<area>\"` for the lay of the land,\n   `codegraph callers <symbol>` and `codegraph impact <symbol>` for who breaks.\n   `understand-anything:understand` for architecture, `graphify` for a repo-wide\n   graph, `claude-mem:mem-search` for \"have we hit this before\".\n4. **Tag every claim** â€” `verified` (you ran it or read it), `inferred` (follows\n   from what you read), `guessed` (neither). One word, in the diagnosis. A\n   `guessed` root cause never justifies an edit; go back to step 1.\n\nState the blast radius in one line before editing â€” **no line, no edit**:\n\n```\nfoo() â†’ impact: 3 callers (a.ts:40, b.ts:12, worker.ts:88) Â· no schema change Â· affected tests: 2 of 3 Â· verified\n```\n\n**The consequence, because a rule without one is decoration.** Gathering the\ncallers in your head and skipping the line does not count â€” a tested agent did\nexactly that and called itself compliant. If you reach the edit and the line is\nnot in your visible output, you have two moves and neither is \"proceed\": write it\nnow from what you actually checked, or, if you cannot fill a field without\nguessing, say which field and go back to step 3 above (map before you cut). A\nblast-radius line written *after* the edit is a report, not a check, and must be\nlabelled as one. The same holds for Â§1's state line: unannounced means unarmed.\n\n## Scope contract\n\nEvery ticket names the files it may touch, before work starts. Then:\n\n- **Two-file rule** â€” a fix spanning more than 2 unrelated files stops and asks\n  via `AskUserQuestion`. Needing a second unrelated file to stay correct is a\n  design problem, not a bigger diff.\n- **Out-of-contract file** â†’ new ticket, not a wider commit. That is the whole\n  point of the board.\n- **Smallest change that fixes the cause** and breaks nothing downstream.\n  Refactoring while fixing hides the fix inside the noise.\n\n## Before a commit â€” sweep, then review the diff\n\n**Regression sweep first.** The blast-radius line is worthless if nobody checks\nit afterwards. Run the callers you named, plus `codegraph affected <changed\nfiles>` for the tests that cover them. Green, or the fix is not done.\n\nThen **`code-review:code-review` over the staged diff** â€” the cheap, correct tool\nfor a working diff, and the one `audit-sherlock` itself points at for this job.\nScope it to the files in this commit (`git diff --cached --name-only`), so a\nfinding is about this change and not the file's whole history. Loop:\n\n1. Findings? Fix them, re-stage, run it again.\n2. Real but out of scope â†’ `A` ticket via `tickets-zordon`, then commit.\n3. Clean â†’ commit, then `codegraph sync` so the map matches the tree.\n\nCap at 3 passes. Still surfacing new findings on the third â†’ stop and\n`AskUserQuestion`. Never commit past a finding by declaring it unrelated.\n\n**`audit-sherlock` is the whole-app sweep, not a per-commit gate.** Run it\nbefore a release or handover, on a repo with unknown-cause rot, or when the user\nasks for it by name. It is a Fable fan-out over the entire codebase; wiring it\ninto every commit both burns the budget and is impossible for an implementer\nsubagent that has been told not to spawn subagents of its own.\n\n**Revert first.** A fix that caused a regression gets reverted before it gets\nre-fixed. Never stack a fix on a broken fix â€” that is how a one-line bug becomes\nan afternoon.\n\n## Blocked? Ask properly\n\nAnything that stops for the user â€” a decision, a missing fact, an approval â€”\nuses `AskUserQuestion`, never a plain paragraph of prose. Every option carries:\n\n- **what it does** â€” the concrete change, in one line\n- **where it hurts** â€” the cost, risk, or thing it rules out\n- **(Recommended)** on the first option, which is the one you'd pick\n\nIf the choice needs more than a chip's worth of context to judge â€” a layout, a\ndiff, a table of trade-offs, a plan, competing designs â€” build it as an\n**artifact** first (load `artifact-design`), hand it over, then ask. Terminal\nscrollback is not where a decision gets made.\n\n**Keep artifacts local.** Write the HTML to a file in the repo or scratchpad and\ngive the user the path â€” do not call the `Artifact` tool. Publishing puts the\npage on claude.ai; only do that when the user asks for a link or says to share\nit.\n\n## Run to the end\n\nA staged plan stops for exactly six things:\n\n1. An **irreversible or destructive** operation.\n2. A **security-sensitive** action.\n3. A **side effect outside this worktree** that norms say you ask about first â€”\n   a merge, a push to a shared branch, a publish, a deploy.\n4. The **7-day** usage window at 90% â€” `watch-cortana` fires it, and its order\n   ends with `AskUserQuestion` for the next direction. The 5-hour window is a\n   pause, not a stop; it gets the three-step preserve and nothing more.\n5. A **scope-contract breach** â€” the work has reached outside the ticket.\n6. A **failed repro**, or a plan so broken every path forward is a guess.\n\nPlus the obvious one: the plan being **done**. 1-3 are the upstream\n`subagent-driven-development` list, and they are the half an earlier draft of\nthis section left out â€” \"run to the end\" was never licence to run a destructive\ncommand unasked.\n\nNothing else. \"Shall I continue?\" between stages, a progress summary nobody\nasked for, a clarifying question whose answer you could decide â€” those are\nstalls. The spec is the binding authority, the plan is its argument, and your\njudgement settles what neither answers. Write the decision down instead:\n\n```\nRuling: <what you decided> - <why> - <what it costs if wrong>\n```\n\nin `.skillator/run.md`, appended, never rewritten. A wrong ruling costs rework\nthe user can see and undo. A stall costs the whole session's momentum and\ntells them nothing.\n\n**Before the stage, not after it.** The run file is written when the stage is\ndispatched, with the exact prompt that was sent. Three isolated baseline runs\nexecuted a four-stage plan and wrote nothing to disk until the last write\n(`practice/baselines/scenario-relay-morpheus.txt`), which is the failure `relay-morpheus` exists\nto stop: a session that ends mid-run leaves a tree full of diff and no record\nof which stage produced it.\n\nIndependent tasks go to a fresh implementer each â€” one task per agent, a\nconstructed prompt, never the session history (`PRACTICE.md` Â§4). Tasks that\ntouch the same files do not fan out; that is `scenario-tasks-sentinels-v2.txt`,\nwhere a run correctly refused to parallelise three stages that all landed in\nthe same file. `skillator:tasks-sentinels` is that loop's entry point, and\n`practice/task-loop.md` + `practice/prompts.md` are the loop itself.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\grayskull-power\\references\\hosts.md\r\n# Host adapters â€” what changes per host, and nothing else\n\n`grayskull-power` is written against generic mechanics. This table is the only\ntranslation layer it needs; the canonical, fuller mapping is `PLATFORMS.md`\n(see SKILL.md Â§0 for where that file lives in each layout).\n\n| SKILL.md says | Claude Code | Everywhere else |\n|---|---|---|\n| \"invoke `<skill>`\" | `Skill` tool | `/skill:<name>` or `/<name>` where it exists; otherwise read that skill's `SKILL.md` and follow it yourself â€” never hand skill *interpretation* to a subagent |\n| \"Fable subagents\" | `Agent` + `model: \"fable\"` | the host's strongest **reasoning** tier: cursor `gpt-5.6-sol-medium` via `Task`, codex `gpt-5.6-sol` at `reasoning_effort: high`, antigravity/pi `/model` to the best reasoner. Classification, questioning, plan-grade\ntasks, design self-review, test-first, fresh-evidence verification, requesting\nand receiving review, debugging, branch lifecycle. The routing table in SKILL.md\npoints at its sections by number; **Â§7 and Â§5 are the ground rules in\n`references/ground-rules.md`, written out**. This file carries every row in full, the long tail, and the reasoning\nbehind the order. A skill added to the plugin gets its full row here first; Â§2\ngains a name only if turn one needs it, and Â§2 stays under the ~800-word router\nbudget (`practice/scripts/context-audit.sh`).\n\nMatch the request, invoke that skill, follow it. One skill at a time â€” chaining\nevery skill \"to be safe\" is the failure this is meant to prevent.\n\n## Process before implementation â€” but only where it adds something\n\n`build-vision` / `build-ultron` / `build-jarvis` **are** the process for a build: it runs `PRACTICE.md` Â§Â§1-6\nand adds tier routing, a session record and a rework loop on top. Getting\nthat backwards is how a session produces confident wrong work.\n\n## Practice â€” always in play\n\n| When | Skill |\n|---|---|\n| Anything creative that ends in code | `build-vision` Â· When | Skill |\n|---|---|\n| Farming a task out to subagents | PRACTICE Â§4 â€” one task per agent, fresh context, review before the next dispatch |\n| Writing a `Workflow` script (user opted in) | `workflow-authoring` **before** the script |\n| \"Have we hit this before?\" â€” recall past sessions | `claude-mem:mem-search` |\n| A codebase nobody here knows yet | `understand-anything:understand`, `claude-mem:learn-codebase` |\n| Who calls this / what breaks if I change it | `codegraph` (`callers`, `impact`, `explore`) |\n| A whole-repo knowledge graph, docs and code together | `graphify` |\n\n**Tricky analysis and review go to Fable.** Anything where the answer is a\njudgement rather than a lookup â€” root-cause analysis, a code or design review, an\narchitecture critique, a subtle-correctness or concurrency read, \"why is this\nactually happening\" â€” is farmed to subagents on Fable:\n`Agent({subagent_type: \"claude\", model: \"fable\", prompt: ...})`, one per angle,\nin a single message so they run in parallel â€” on another host, its reasoning\ntier per `references/hosts.md`. Tricky means: cause unknown, the reasoning spans\nfiles, or being wrong is expensive. A grep, a file read, a one-file diff, a\nmechanical sweep is not tricky â€” do it yourself; spawning an agent for it costs\nmore than the answer. You keep the verdict: read the reports, reconcile them, and\nre-check anything an agent asserts without evidence.\n\n## Skillator â€” our own\n\n| The request isâ€¦ | Skill |\n|---|---|\n| A bug, a feature, \"log this\", \"what's pending\", \"mark done\" | `tickets-zordon` |\n| 4+ open tickets, a sweep, \"ultracode\", \"work the board\" | `tickets-zordon` (workflow mode) |\n| \"why is this broken\", audit a whole app, unknown-cause rot, pre-release sweep | `audit-sherlock` |\n| Build a real feature, design-then-implement | `build-vision` (ceremony, Fable design) Â· `build-ultron` (all-Opus, no ceremony) Â· `build-jarvis` (Sonnet offload) |\n| Any UI/UX or front-end craft â€” build, redesign, improve, critique, native or web | `design-arwen` (never `frontend-design`) |\n| A static/mock UI that needs to actually work | `designui-galadriel` |\n| What a feature must do exists only in a conversation â€” a spoken brief, a chat thread, a ticket body, a handover â€” and the work outlives the session | `spec-watson` |\n| \"check screenshot\", or verify a change in a running app visually | `screenshot-argus` |\n| Ready to merge a branch | `mergeprep-oracle`, then `merge-smith` |\n| Ship to a VPS / set up deployment | `deploy-merlin`, then `deploy-niyoj` |\n| A staged run the session may not outlive â€” fan-out, long build, flaky link | `relay-morpheus` (the ledger; the build skill still does the building) |\n| A plan whose tasks are written and now have to be built | `tasks-sentinels` â€” one task, one fresh agent, reviewed before the next |\n| Session ending, context or usage running out | `handoff-cortana` |\n| Starting from someone else's handoff doc | `resume-cortana` |\n\n**Nothing matches?** Do the work directly. A one-line edit needs no skill, and\nrouting it through one is the opposite of the point. Skipping the *skill* is not\nskipping the *ground rules* â€” \"one line\" describes the diff, never the thinking,\nand `ground-rules.md` binds a one-line edit exactly as hard as a refactor. The\ntempting one-liner someone senior already diagnosed for you is the specific case\nthose rules exist for.\n\n## Related\n\n`watch-cortana` fires the end-of-session sequence automatically at 92% usage;\nthat sequence is the same route this table's last two rows describe.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\handoff-cortana\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: handoff-cortana\ndescription: >-\n  Use when the user asks to \"hand off\", \"write a handoff\", \"create a handoff\n  doc\", \"document where we are\", \"prepare for the next session/person\", or\n  wants a continuity/checkpoint summary of the current session â€” an in-depth,\n  verified session-handoff document (a Markdown file) so a different person or\n  AI can continue the work with zero loss of context, plan, or intent.\n---\n\n# handoff-cortana â€” the session, written down\n\nProduce a single, self-contained Markdown handoff document that lets the next\nworker (human or AI) resume **exactly where this session left off** â€” with the\nsame understanding of what is done, what remains, why choices were made, and what\nto do next. The output must prevent loss of plan or intent.\n\n## Prime directive: verify, don't narrate\n\nA handoff that just recounts the chat is dangerous â€” the chat contains intentions,\nabandoned ideas, and claims that may not match reality. **Ground every\n\"done\" against the repo.** Before writing a status, confirm it:\n\n- `git log --oneline -20` and `git status --short` â€” what actually landed vs. what\n  is uncommitted/dirty vs. only discussed.\n- Read/`ls`/`grep` the files a claim depends on â€” does the module/route/setting\n  actually exist? (Absence of evidence â‰  done.)\n- Run the tests that would prove a claim; note pass/fail. A test file that\n  exists is not a test that passed â€” locating one earns **CLAIMED**, never\n  **DONE (verified)**, and a test that skips, or asserts nothing that could\n  fail, proves nothing however green it prints. If you can't run them, say so â€”\n  never imply verification you didn't do.\n\nClassify every item as one of: **DONE (verified)**, **CLAIMED (asserted in chat\nbut not confirmed in repo)**, **IN-PROGRESS (partial / uncommitted)**, or\n**NOT STARTED**. Never collapse CLAIMED into DONE.\n\n## Method\n\n1. **Reconstruct the goal.** Re-read the session from the top. State the original\n   objective and any scope changes in the user's own terms. Distinguish the\n   *mission* (why) from the *tasks* (what).\n2. **Inventory the work.** List everything attempted. For each, assign a status\n   from the four above **with evidence** (commit hash, file path:line, test name,\n   or \"asserted in chat, unverified\").\n3. **Capture the reasoning.** For every non-obvious decision: what was chosen,\n   what was rejected, and *why*. This is what prevents the next worker from\n   re-litigating settled questions or repeating dead ends. Pull rationale out of\n   the conversation â€” it is the most perishable, highest-value content.\n4. **Surface the constraints & gotchas.** Environment quirks, air-gap/offline\n   limits, credentials, flaky steps, \"do not re-fix this\" traps (check the repo docs\n   and whichever instruction file this host loads â€” CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md,\n   SYSTEM.md). These cost the next worker hours if lost.\n5. **Define the resume path.** The exact next 1â€“3 actions, the first commands to\n   run to get oriented, and the branch/PR state. Be concrete enough that someone\n   can start in under five minutes.\n6. **List open questions.** Anything awaiting a user decision, unknowns, or risks.\n\n## Writing the document\n\nFill in `template.md` (in this skill's directory). Read it and follow its\nstructure and section-by-section guidance. Rules for the output:\n\n- **Self-contained.** The reader has *not* seen this chat. Spell out acronyms,\n  link file paths as `path:line`, quote the relevant commit messages. Do not write\n  \"as discussed above\" â€” there is no above.\n- **Evidence over assertion.** Prefer \"`geo/mosaic_qa.py` â€” ABSENT (grep returned\n  0 matches)\" to \"QA judge not done\".\n- **Ruthlessly current.** If the plan changed mid-session, document the *final*\n  plan and note what was superseded, so nobody follows a stale branch of thought.\n- **Depth where it matters, brevity where it doesn't.** In-depth on decisions,\n  rationale, gotchas, and resume steps; terse on boilerplate.\n- **No invention.** If something is unknown, write \"UNKNOWN â€” needs checking\",\n  never a plausible guess. A confident wrong handoff is worse than an honest gap.\n\n## Output location & naming\n\nDefault to `docs/handoffs/HANDOFF-<YYYY-MM-DD>-<short-topic-slug>.md` (create the\nfolder if absent). If the repo has an obvious conventions dir (e.g.\n`docs/superpowers/`), prefer matching it. If the user named a path or the\nscratchpad, honor that. Confirm the location in your reply.\n\nOptional args: treat any argument as the output path or a focus hint\n(e.g. `/handoff docs/notes/handoff.md` or `/handoff mosaic tracks`).\n\n## Finish\n\nAfter writing, reply with: the file path, a 3â€“5 line summary of the current state\nit captured, and the single recommended next action. Do **not** commit or push\nunless the user asks â€” the handoff is theirs to place.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\handoff-cortana\\template.md\r\n<!--\nHandoff template. Armed as standard by grayskull-power. NOT for taking screenshots\n  (screenshot-argus) and NOT a substitute for tests â€” it is exposure, not\n  verification.\n---\n\n# live-friday â€” start it first, then work\n\nThe default failure this fixes: the agent edits for five minutes, *then* runs the\napp, and the user has spent five minutes watching a spinner with nothing to look\nat. Invert it. **The runnable thing starts before the first edit.**\n\n## The move\n\n1. **Detect** the launcher (table below). None applies â†’ say so in one line, skip\n   this skill, work normally. Do not invent a launcher.\n2. **Start it in the background** â€” `Bash` with `run_in_background: true`. It\n   survives across turns.\n3. **Hand it over in the opening reply**, before any edit, on its own line:\n\n   ```\n   live: http://localhost:5173 â€” open it, hot reload will show my edits as they land\n   ```\n\n   or for a compiled build:\n\n   ```\n   live: cargo watch running Â· rebuilds + relaunches on each save Â· last-good binary at target/debug/app still runs\n   ```\n\n4. **Then do the work.** Normal edits, normal verification.\n5. **Report the state** at the end: still running (and how to stop it), or exited\n   with what.\n\nSteps 2 and 3 are the whole point. An app started at the *end* of the turn is\nwhat this skill exists to prevent.\n\n## Launchers\n\nPick by what the repo actually contains â€” read the manifest, do not guess.\n\n| Repo has | Launch | What the user watches |\n|---|---|---|\n| `package.json` with a `dev`/`start` script | that script | the app; hot reload shows edits live |\n| `vite.config.*`, `next.config.*`, `astro.config.*` | `npm run dev` (or pnpm/yarn/bun per lockfile) | the app, HMR |\n| `Cargo.toml` | `cargo watch -x run` if installed, else `cargo run` | rebuild + relaunch per save; `cargo build` prints its own progress |\n| `CMakeLists.txt` / `build.ninja` | `cmake --build build -j` or `ninja -C build` | `[142/380]` progress lines |\n| `Makefile` | `make -j` | compiler output as it goes |\n| `go.mod` | `go run .` | the app |\n| `pyproject.toml` / `manage.py` / `app.py` | the project's dev command (`uvicorn --reload`, `flask run`, `manage.py runserver`) | the app, autoreload |\n| Electron main in `package.json` | `npm run dev`/`electron .` | the window |\n| iOS / Android | **do not auto-launch** â€” say what to run; simulators are slow and stateful |\n| A library with no entry point | nothing to launch â€” say so, rely on tests |\n\nAmbiguous or several candidates â†’ `AskUserQuestion` with the two most likely,\nrecommended first. Do not start three servers to be safe. A repo whose only\nscripts are `dev:web`/`dev:mobile`/`start` is ambiguous â€” ask, do not pick.\n\n**Deps are not proven by a `node_modules/` directory.** It can exist and still be\na field of dangling symlinks (a cleared pnpm store, a pruned install), and the\nlaunch dies with `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` a second later. Check the launcher binary\nitself â€” `node_modules/next/`, `node_modules/.bin/vite`, `target/`, `build/` â€”\nand on a missing-module crash, **report it and offer the install**. Never run\n`npm install` unasked: it rewrites a lockfile the user did not ask you to touch.\n\n## Compiled builds are different â€” be honest about it\n\nJS hot-reloads; C++ and Rust do not. There is no preview of a binary mid-link.\nWhat you *can* expose, and should:\n\n- **Progress instead of silence.** `ninja`, `cmake --build`, and `cargo build`\n  already emit `[n/total]` or per-crate lines. Surface them; that is the\n  difference between \"compiling, 142/380\" and a dead terminal.\n- **The last-good binary stays runnable.** Do not delete or overwrite it while\n  the new one builds. The user can keep using the previous build.\n- **`cargo watch -x run`** (Rust) and a `--watch`-capable generator (C++) close\n  the loop automatically on save. Offer to install `cargo-watch` once; never\n  install silently.\n\nThe ceiling is real: a four-minute link step stays four minutes. Say that rather\nthan implying a preview exists.\n\n## Rules\n\n- **One live process per project.** Already running from earlier in the session â†’\n  reuse it, say so, do not start a second. Port already bound is the usual tell.\n- **Never start something destructive.** A dev server, a build, a local run â€”\n  yes. Migrations, deploys, seed scripts, anything writing to a shared or remote\n  target â€” no, ask first.\n- **Do not block on it.** No foreground waits, no polling loops. Background it\n  and move on; the harness notifies you when it exits.\n- **A crash on startup is a finding, not a footnote.** Read the output, report\n  it, fix it before continuing â€” that is the first thing the user would have\n  seen anyway.\n- **Leave it running** at the end of the turn unless the user says otherwise;\n  tell them how to stop it. Killing the server they are looking at is rude.\n- **Exposure â‰  verification.** The app running proves it boots. It does not\n  prove the change is correct â€” tests and `PRACTICE.md` Â§5 (fresh evidence, or no\n  claim) still apply.\n\n## Related\n\n- `run` â€” the deeper \"launch and drive this project's app\" skill; use it when the\n  point is to *verify* a change in the running app rather than to expose it early.\n  `live-friday` starts things; `run` inspects them.\n- `screenshot-argus` â€” the return path: the user screenshots what they saw here\n  and drops it in the folder.\n- `grayskull-power` â€” arms this as standard, so the first reply of any change to\n  a runnable project carries a `live:` line.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\merge-smith\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: merge-smith\ndescription: >-\n  Use when the user wants to combine/consolidate several GitHub branches into\n  one integration branch, merge a feature branch into a base (main/develop),\n  or reconcile divergent branches that touched the same code â€” and wants\n  conflicts handled intelligently rather than by hand. The merge direction\n  (`<source>` INTO `<destination>`) is confirmed before anything is touched;\n  the base branch is never touched directly and nothing is pushed without\n  explicit approval. NOT for a single trivial fast-forward, or non-git tasks.\n---\n\n# merge-smith â€” analyse branches, merge by risk\n\nAgents do the reading and the routing; **git does the merging on a throwaway\nintegration branch**, so nothing is risked. The base branch (main/develop) is\nnever modified directly and nothing leaves the machine without an explicit yes.\n\n**Model tiers (best model where being wrong is expensive, cheap everywhere\nelse):** branch **analysis â†’ Sonnet**, **trivial conflict auto-resolve â†’\nSonnet**, **semantic conflict resolution proposed â†’ Fable**, **that resolution\napplied, plus verification failures and rework â†’ Opus**. Dispatch via the Agent\ntool with the matching `model` override.\n\nSame split the rest of skillator uses â€” **Fable decides, Opus builds.** A bad\nsemantic merge is the worst failure this skill has: it exits 0, the tests pass,\nand a change quietly disappears until production. *Deciding* what the merged hunk\nshould say is judgement, not lookup, so it goes to the strongest model\n(`model: \"fable\"`; on another host, its top reasoning tier per Â§Other hosts).\n*Writing it in*, and fixing whatever the verification then catches, is\nimplementation â€” that is Opus's job, and handing it to the deep tier is both\nworse and dearer.\n\n**Neither tier touches the mechanical majority.** Fable sees only the semantic\nconflict hunks â€” typically a handful, not the merge. Opus sees only those\nresolutions and a failing check. Enumerating branches, regenerating a lockfile\nand re-running a green suite stay on Sonnet.\n\n**The aim:** the source's work arrives in the destination, and the destination\nis not disturbed on the way in. Both halves are *verified* in Phase 4 â€” a merge\nthat lands the feature while silently reverting base's newer work is a failed\nmerge that reports success.\n\n**Vocabulary, shared with `mergeprep-oracle`:** the **source** is the branch being\nmerged, the **destination** (`<base>`) is what it merges into. Same words, same\n`apply --reverse --check` reconcile, same rule that a non-zero exit is evidence.\n\n**Mechanism:** local git for all merge/conflict work; `gh` for PR context (titles,\ndescriptions, review/check state) and the optional final PR. If `gh` isn't\nauthenticated, fall back to pure git and say so.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 0 â€” Orient & confirm\n\nEstablish, then confirm back before touching anything:\n- **Branches** in scope and the **base/target** (main, develop, a release branch).\n- **Mode:** `consolidate` (many â†’ one integration branch) Â· `into-base` (one\n  feature â†’ base) Â· `reconcile` (branches that edited the same code differently â€”\n  conflicts are the main event, prefer best-version selection over union).\n  Detect from the request; if ambiguous, ask once.\n- **Direction â€” always stated, never inferred.** Write it back as one line and get a\n  yes before anything else: `merging <source> INTO <destination>`. Branch names lie\n  (`develop` merged into a feature is a legitimate and completely different\n  operation), and the two directions produce different trees, different conflict\n  winners, and different blast radii. If the user's phrasing is reversible at all\n  (\"merge dev and my branch\"), ask â€” this is the one question that is never worth\n  guessing.\n- `git fetch --all`, then `git fetch origin <base>:<base>` â€” **`fetch --all` moves\n  `origin/<base>`, not the local `<base>` ref.** The integration branch is cut from the\n  local ref, so without that second fetch the whole merge happens on a stale base and\n  every later check still reports clean. If it refuses, local `<base>` has diverged from\n  origin â€” stop and ask which is the real base.\n- Confirm the branches exist and their base. Note anything already merged (skip it) or\n  wildly stale.\n\nNever start merging on an unconfirmed branch list or base.\n\n**No user to confirm?** A subagent, a batch run, or a \"just go ahead\" brief â€” that\nis not the direction and it is not push approval.\n\n1. **Direction first.** If the request does not say which branch goes INTO which\n   (\"merge X and Y\", \"merge the A work and the B work\"), stop before the first\n   merge â€” no `git merge` even on a throwaway branch â€” and report the two\n   directions that exist (`X INTO Y` / `Y INTO X`) and what each would change.\n   One side already being on `main` is not a direction. The\n   direction is never an `assumed:` call: both answers look sensible, they give\n   different trees, and \"go ahead\" answers neither.\n2. **Direction stated â†’ continue locally** rather than blocking. Everything happens\n   on a throwaway integration branch, so mark every other unanswered call\n   `assumed:` in the merge log â€” semantic conflicts applied as proposed,\n   verification run, Phase 5 ending at **hand off locally** â€” and list those\n   assumptions in the report.\n3. **Push, PR and swap-in need a real yes** â€” deleting an integration branch costs\n   nothing, an unapproved push costs everyone.\n\n> If a branch is stale or carries unrelated/no-op churn, run **`mergeprep-oracle`**\n> on it first â€” it preps the branch **in place** (onto current base, unintended paths\n> dropped) and commits a prep document to it, so this merge integrates nothing old or\n> untouched. Read that document (`docs/merges/prep-<branch>-*.md`) before merging: it\n> names every path deliberately dropped and every reviewer edit.\n\n## Phase 1 â€” Analyse the branches (Sonnet, in parallel)\n\nOne analysis subagent per branch (`model: \"sonnet\"`), each returns structured:\n\n```\nBRANCH:     <name>  (ahead <n> / behind <n> vs base)\nINTENT:     <what it does â€” from commits, diff, and the PR body if gh has one>\nTOUCHES:    <key files / areas>\nRISK:       <low|med|high> + why (migrations, shared core, deletions, deps)\n```\n\nThen build two things from the results (main session, cheap):\n- **Overlap matrix** â€” which branches touch the same files (the conflict predictor).\n- **Recommended merge order** â€” least-overlapping / lowest-risk first, dependencies\n  respected, so early merges don't compound conflicts. In `reconcile` mode, order\n  by which branch is the intended base of truth.\n\nPresent the analysis + predicted conflicts + proposed order. This *is* the \"analyse\nthe branches\" deliverable â€” a light confirm here is cheap; a wrong order is not.\n\n## Phase 2 â€” Set up the integration branch\n\nCreate a throwaway branch from the base â€” `integration/<YYYY-MM-DD>-<slug>` â€” and\nopen a **merge log** file (`docs/merges/merge-<date>-<slug>.md` or scratchpad):\nrecord base, branches, order, and (as you go) every conflict + how it was resolved.\nThe log makes the whole run auditable and revertible (drop the branch = zero harm).\n\n## Phase 3 â€” Merge by risk\n\nMerge each branch onto the integration branch in order. On a conflict, **classify\neach conflicted file, then route**:\n\n- **Trivial / mechanical â†’ auto-resolve (`model: \"sonnet\"`):** lockfiles\n  (`package-lock.json`, `yarn.lock`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `Cargo.lock`, `go.sum` â†’\n  prefer regenerate over hand-merge), import ordering, formatting/whitespace-only,\n  append-only files (changelogs), both-sides-added non-overlapping code, generated\n  files.\n- **Semantic / logic â†’ escalate (`model: \"fable\"` proposes, user approves,\n  `model: \"opus\"` applies):** the\n  same function/body edited both sides, signature/API changes, one side deleted\n  what the other modified, config/schema/migration conflicts, any overlapping logic.\n  The agent explains **both sides + a proposed resolution + why**; the user\n  approves or adjusts before it's applied.\n- **Unsure â†’ treat as semantic.** Escalation is the safe default.\n\n**Resolve per hunk, not per file.** A conflicted file is rarely wholly one side's;\n`--ours`/`--theirs` on a whole file is the single most common way a real change\ndisappears with exit 0. Open the conflict with all three versions visible and decide\neach hunk on its own:\n\n```\ngit checkout --conflict=diff3 -- <path>   # shows base|ours|theirs, not just two sides\ngit checkout --ours   -- <path>   # ONLY legitimate for lockfiles and generated files\n```\n\n`diff3` markers matter: two-way markers show you what the sides *say* and hide what\nthey *started from*, so \"both changed this line\" and \"one side changed it, the other\ninherited it\" look identical. With the base section visible, most hunks resolve\nthemselves.\n\nFor each conflicted hunk, decide and log one of:\n\n| Take | When |\n|---|---|\n| `source` | The hunk is the feature. The destination's version is the old world. |\n| `destination` | Base moved ahead here and the source is stale â€” its version predates base's change. |\n| `both` | Non-overlapping additions in the same region: keep both, order deliberately. |\n| `rewrite` | Neither side is right once combined â€” Fable proposes, user approves, Opus applies, and the log carries the proposed text. |\n\n**Ask the direction on any hunk where the two sides genuinely disagree about\nbehaviour** â€” do not resolve it from the merge's overall direction. Merging a feature\ninto base does *not* mean the feature wins every hunk: a hunk where base fixed a bug\nthe source still carries must go to `destination`, or the merge reintroduces the bug.\nPresent both versions, say which way you'd go and why, and let the user pick.\n\nWhere a hunk was taken from one side wholesale, prefer applying it rather than editing\nmarkers by hand â€” same pipe as `mergeprep-oracle`, so both skills fail the same way and the\nfailure is visible:\n\n```\ngit diff --binary -M <merge-base>...<side> -- <path> | git apply --3way\n```\n\nRecord each resolution in the merge log â€” **one row per hunk**, not per file\n(file Â· hunk/lines Â· trivial/semantic Â· take: source/destination/both/rewrite Â· who\ndecided Â· why). Phase 4's reconcile checks hunks; a log written at file granularity\ncannot explain the failures it will find. If a merge goes sideways, `git merge --abort`, note it, and re-plan that step â€”\nnever leave the tree half-merged.\n\n## Phase 4 â€” Verify (ask at run time)\n\n**First, reconcile against every source branch.** Tests prove the merge *works*; this\nproves it is *complete*. Do it per hunk â€” a `--name-only` tip diff works at path\ngranularity, so on any file that base or another branch also moved it lists the path\neither way, and \"another branch moved it ahead\" then truthfully explains the file while\nabsolving a hunk that vanished inside it. That is precisely the failure mode this step\nexists to catch, so check the hunks:\n\n```\ngit checkout <integration>\n# for each source branch, for each path it changed â€” must exit 0:\ngit diff --binary -M <base>...<source> -- <path> | git apply --reverse --check\n```\n\nExit 0 means every hunk that source intended is present verbatim in the integration\ntree. Non-zero is legitimate **only** when a merge-log row names that path *and that\nhunk* (a semantic conflict you resolved the other way) or a prep-document row shows the path\nwas deliberately dropped. Anything else is a change that silently vanished â€” find it and\nre-apply it before going further. A green test suite will never catch this.\n\n**Then reconcile against the destination â€” it must be undisturbed.** The check above\nproves the sources arrived; it proves nothing about base's own work, and a merge that\nlands every feature while reverting base is the failure this whole skill is built to\nprevent. Same pipe, other side:\n\n```\ngit checkout <integration>\n# for each path BASE changed since the merge base with each source â€” must exit 0:\ngit diff --binary -M $(git merge-base <base> <source>)...<base> -- <path>   | git apply --reverse --check\n```\n\nNon-zero is legitimate **only** where a merge-log row names that path *and that hunk*\nas `take: source` â€” a deliberate, recorded overwrite of base. Anything else is base's\nwork silently reverted. This is the check that would have caught it; `git status`,\na name-only diff, and a green test suite will all report success.\n\nThen detect the project's build/test command. **Ask whether to run verification**\n(default **yes** if a test command is detected â€” a conflict-free merge is not a\ncorrect one). If yes, run it (Sonnet agent or main session). A **failure is\nOpus's**, not the runner's: a broken test after a merge is a real defect in the\nmerged code, and Opus fixes it. Escalate back to Fable only when the fix means\nre-deciding a semantic resolution rather than repairing one â€” re-run, and update\nthe log.\nGate \"done\" on green.\n\n## Phase 5 â€” End state (ask at run time)\n\nRelay a summary: what merged, conflicts resolved (trivial vs escalated counts),\n**reconcile result â€” both directions: every source hunk present, every destination\nhunk present, each exception named in the log**,\nverification result, integration branch name, merge-log path. Then **ask**:\n- **Hand off locally** â€” leave the integration branch + log; the user pushes/PRs.\n- **Swap-in (local only)** â€” rename the base aside and promote the merge into its\n  name, so the working branch name keeps meaning what it meant:\n  ```\n  git branch -m <base> <base>_old_before_<source>   # e.g. dev -> dev_old_before_ksk_aewag2\n  git branch -m <integration> <base>                # merged result becomes dev\n  git branch --unset-upstream <base>_old_before_<source>\n  git branch -u origin/<base> <base>\n  ```\n  Those last two lines are not optional: `git branch -m` carries `branch.<name>.*`\n  config with the rename, so without them the **archive** tracks `origin/<base>` (and\n  under `push.default=upstream` a habitual `git push` from it pushes the pre-merge state\n  over origin's base) while the promoted branch tracks nothing. Note also that the\n  promoted `<base>` has now diverged from `origin/<base>` â€” the next `pull` will merge\n  origin's base back into the merged result, which is expected but worth saying out loud.\n  Name the archived base `<base>_old_before_<source>`, not a bare `<base>_old`:\n  months later the only question anyone asks of that branch is *\"old before what?\"*\n  Strip any `-merge-ready`/`-prep` suffix from `<source>` first (older preps made such\n  branches; current `mergeprep-oracle` preps in place, so usually there is nothing to strip).\n  If the name is\n  already taken, append `_2`, `_3` â€” never overwrite an existing archive.\n  End state: sources unchanged (`f1` is still `f1`), `dev` = merged result,\n  `dev_old_before_ksk_aewag2` = previous base. Only after verification is green, and\n  only if `<base>` isn't checked out in **another worktree** (renaming the branch you\n  are on is fine â€” HEAD follows it; a second worktree silently ends up on the archive).\n  **Local refs only.** Never rename on the remote: that means deleting and\n  re-pushing a branch, which breaks open PRs, branch protection, CI, and every\n  other clone. To publish afterwards, push the renamed branch under a *new* remote\n  name and PR it, or merge `<base>_old_before_<source>..<base>` normally â€” both need\n  the explicit yes below.\n- **Push + open a draft PR** â€” only on an explicit yes: push the integration branch\n  and open a **draft** PR via `gh` (never merge it; never touch base; never\n  force-push).\n\nPushing, opening a PR, or merging a PR are never done without that explicit approval.\n\n---\n\n## Rules\n\n- **Base branch is sacred.** Only ever a merge *source*; never checked out for\n  modification, never pushed to, never force-pushed. The one exception is the\n  approved local **swap-in** rename in Phase 5 â€” which preserves the old base as\n  `<base>_old_before_<source>` and still touches nothing on the remote.\n- **Completeness is verified in both directions, not assumed.** Before calling a merge\n  done: every hunk each source intended is present, **and** every hunk the destination\n  gained since the merge base is present. Each exception is named â€” path and hunk â€” in\n  the merge log or the branch's prep document. Unexplained difference = lost change.\n- **Direction is asked, not inferred.** `<source> INTO <destination>` is confirmed\n  before the first merge (nobody to ask â†’ Phase 0's branch), and the overall\n  direction never decides an individual hunk.\n- **Conflicts resolve per hunk.** Whole-file `--ours`/`--theirs` is for lockfiles and\n  generated files only; anywhere else it is a change discarded with exit 0.\n- **Everything on the integration branch**, everything in the merge log â€” the run is\n  auditable and 100% revertible by deleting the branch.\n- **Route conflicts by class, not by vibe.** Trivialâ†’Sonnet,\n  semanticâ†’Fable proposes + approval + Opus applies, analysisâ†’Sonnet, broken\n  verificationâ†’Opus. Unsure = semantic.\n- **Agents read and route; git merges.** Don't hand-edit conflict markers when a\n  clean `git` resolution (regenerate lockfile, `--theirs`/`--ours` on a lockfile) is right.\n- **Two run-time gates: verification and push/PR.** Both ask; push/PR needs an\n  explicit yes. Never claim a push/PR/merge that wasn't approved.\n- If `gh` is unavailable, do the whole merge with pure git and tell the user PR\n  context/opening isn't available.\n- If a subagent dies / returns null, stop and report rather than merging blind.\n- **Kept in sync with `mergeprep-oracle`** â€” same source/destination vocabulary, same\n  `git diff --binary -M ... | git apply --reverse --check` reconcile in both\n  directions, same per-hunk granularity, same treatment of a non-zero exit as\n  evidence. A change to any of those in either skill belongs in both.\n\n## Other hosts\n\nSonnet/Fable/Opus and the Agent tool above are the **Claude Code** defaults. On\nCursor, Codex, Antigravity, Pi, or Prime Agent, map through `PLATFORMS.md` (beside the\ninstalled skills, or at the repo/plugin root): branch analysis â†’ **cheap tier**,\ntrivial conflicts â†’ **cheap tier**, semantic conflicts *decided* by **the host's\nstrongest reasoning tier** (cursor `gpt-5.6-sol-medium`, codex `gpt-5.6-sol` at\n`reasoning_effort: high`, antigravity/pi the best reasoner), then *applied* â€”\nwith verification failures and rework â€” by its **build tier**. Where the host has\nonly one top tier, it does both. No delegation available â†’ resolve semantic\nconflicts in-session and say so; never hand them to a cheaper agent. Where\nthe host can't run analysis agents in parallel, analyse branches sequentially and\nwrite each summary to the merge log as you go â€” slower, same result. The approval\ngates (verification, push/PR) do not relax on any host.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\mergeprep-oracle\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: mergeprep-oracle\ndescription: >-\n  Use when the user wants to \"prep a branch for merge\", \"clean up a branch\n  before merging\", \"make sure only the real changes get merged\", \"strip\n  stale/old parts\", or is about to run merge-smith and wants the branch\n  normalized first. Everything happens ON THE BRANCH ITSELF â€” never a side\n  branch, never a history rewrite, never a force-push, never the base. NOT for\n  the actual merge (use merge-smith) or for non-git tasks.\n---\n\n# mergeprep-oracle â€” the branch carries only its real changes, and says so\n\n**The aim, in one sentence:** a developer has finished a feature; that feature â€”\nand nothing else â€” arrives in the destination branch, and the destination branch\nis not disturbed on the way in. Two failure modes, equally bad: the branch drags\nin old or unrelated content (this skill's job), and the merge quietly reverts\nwork the destination did while the branch was away (verified in Phase 3, and\nagain by `merge-smith`).\n\n**Vocabulary, shared with `merge-smith`:** `<branch>` is the **source** (the\nfeature), `<base>` is the **destination** (main/develop). The two skills use the\nsame words, the same `apply --reverse --check` reconcile, and the same rule that\na non-zero exit is evidence, not an obstacle.\n\nThe problem this fixes: a branch that's behind base, or carries stale file\nversions, whitespace churn, reverted-to-zero edits, or files it never meant to\ntouch â€” so merging it drags in \"old or untouched parts\". This skill brings the\nbranch **onto the current base**, removes what it didn't mean to change, and\ncommits a **prep document** to the branch so the next person (or `merge-smith`)\ninherits every decision.\n\n**Safety rails:** the branch is only ever **appended to** â€” no rebase, no history\nrewrite, no force-push, base untouched. A `<branch>-preprep-<date>` tag is cut before\nanything changes; `git reset --hard <tag>` undoes the entire run.\n\n## Phase 0 â€” Orient\n\nEstablish and confirm: the **branch** to prep and its **base** (main/develop).\n\n```\ngit fetch --all\ngit fetch origin <base>:<base>     # fast-forward the LOCAL base ref; refuses if diverged\ngit checkout <branch>\ngit tag <branch>-preprep-<date>    # the undo button; cut it before touching anything\n```\n\n`git fetch --all` updates `origin/<base>` only â€” **the local `<base>` ref does not\nmove.** Working off a stale local `<base>` silently defeats this entire skill: the\nresult carries old content while every later check reports \"clean\". If the second fetch\nrefuses, local `<base>` has diverged from origin â€” stop and ask which is the real base.\nNote how far behind the branch is (`git rev-list --left-right --count <base>...<branch>`).\n\nIf the branch is already pushed, say so now: this prep adds commits that will need a\n(normal, non-force) push, and anyone else on the branch will pull them.\n\n## Phase 1 â€” Inventory the branch's true changes\n\nCompute the branch's diff against the **merge base**, per file:\n`git diff --stat <base>...<branch>` and the full diff. Classify every changed path\n(a cheap-tier analysis agent â€” Sonnet on Claude Code, matching `merge-smith`'s\nPhase 1; the equivalent row in the\n`PLATFORMS.md` (beside the installed skills or at the repo root) elsewhere, or just the main session where no delegation\nexists â€” can summarize the branch's *intent* from its commits/PR body and flag\noff-purpose files, cheap and it's the whole judgment):\n\n- **INTENDED** â€” substantive changes that match the branch's apparent purpose. Keep.\n- **NO-OP** â€” whitespace/formatting-only, files touched but byte-identical to base,\n  hunks reverted to base within the branch, EOL churn. **Auto-drop** (report them).\n- **SUSPICIOUS / maybe-unintended** â€” substantive but off-purpose: unrelated files,\n  debug/commented-out leftovers, committed secrets (`.env`), large generated/vendored\n  blobs, lockfile churn with no matching dependency change. **Escalate to the user**\n  (include or exclude, one line each) before deciding.\n\nPresent the inventory (INTENDED / NO-OP / SUSPICIOUS) and get the suspicious calls.\n\n**No user to make them?** A subagent, a batch run, or a \"just go ahead\" brief is\nnot a reviewer's call. Then **keep every SUSPICIOUS path**, recorded as\n`decision: keep (assumed â€” nobody to decide)` owned by `auto`: excluding a path\nunattended deletes work on a guess, while keeping it leaves it visible to\n`merge-smith` and to review. The exception is a committed secret (`.env`, a key) â€”\nexclude it and say so, since nobody intends that one. Auto-drop NO-OPs as usual,\nprep the branch, write the document, and don't push it.\n\n**Two different intents are in play and must never be blended:**\n- **Developer intent** â€” what the branch's commits/PR say it set out to do. It is the\n  default, and it owns *content*.\n- **Reviewer intent** â€” the calls made during this prep (drop this file, strip that\n  debug block, exclude the committed `.env`). It owns *inclusion*, not content.\n\nDraft the **prep document** â€” `docs/merges/prep-<branch>-<date>.md`, in the repo, since\nit gets committed to the branch â€” and record every path as one row, before changing\nanything:\n\n```\n| path        | class      | decision                   | decided by | why                   |\n|-------------|------------|----------------------------|------------|-----------------------|\n| src/auth.ts | INTENDED   | keep                       | developer  | matches branch intent |\n| .env        | SUSPICIOUS | exclude                    | reviewer   | committed secret      |\n| src/api.ts  | SUSPICIOUS | keep, debug block stripped | reviewer   | leftover console.log  |\n| README.md   | NO-OP      | drop                       | auto       | whitespace only       |\n```\n\n`decided by: developer` means kept as written, no judgment applied. `reviewer` means a\ncall this prep made that the developer never saw. Auto-dropped NO-OPs are logged too â€”\nsilent drops are how \"but I *did* change that file\" happens at review.\n\nThe document also carries: base and pre-prep tag, the merge conflicts resolved in\nPhase 2 and how, the Phase-3 verification result, and what a reader should revert if\nthey disagree. It is the handoff â€” `merge-smith` and the next human both read it.\n\n## Phase 2 â€” Prep the branch in place\n\nUp to four appended commits, in this order, on `<branch>` itself. Any of them may be\nempty and is then simply skipped.\n\n```\ngit checkout <branch>\n\n# 1. onto current base â€” a merge, not a rebase: no history rewrite, no force-push\ngit merge <base>\n#   Conflicts here are real: base and the branch collide. Resolve them as merges, log\n#   each one in the prep doc, and never `git checkout --ours`/`--theirs` a whole file\n#   to make the conflict go away â€” that is how base's newer work disappears with exit 0.\n\n# 2. remove what the branch never meant to carry (NO-OPs + reviewer exclusions).\n#    EVERY dropped path goes through ONE pipe â€” modified, added, deleted, renamed, binary:\ngit diff --binary -M <base>...<pre-prep tag> -- <path> | git apply --reverse\n#   renames: pass BOTH pathspecs -- <old> <new>, or the rename degrades to a bare\n#   add or delete and one side's content is lost.\n#   CHECK THE EXIT CODE EVERY TIME. Non-zero means the branch's change to that path is\n#   no longer present verbatim (the step-1 merge moved it) â€” escalate; never route\n#   around it with `git rm` or `git checkout <base> -- <path>`, both of which also\n#   destroy whatever base did to that file, exit 0, and leave no trace.\ngit commit -m \"mergeprep-oracle: drop unintended and no-op paths\"\n\n# 3. reviewer content edits (stripped debug blocks, etc.), ONLY if any\ngit commit -m \"mergeprep-oracle: reviewer decisions\"\n\n# 4. the handoff document\ngit add docs/merges/prep-<branch>-<date>.md\ngit commit -m \"mergeprep-oracle: handoff document for <branch>\"\n```\n\n**Why each piece is its own commit, and all of them sit on top of untouched developer\nhistory.** `git log` still shows exactly what the developer wrote. Everything this prep\ndecided sits above it, named, and each piece is independently `git revert`-able. If the\ndeveloper disagrees with an exclusion, they revert one commit; they don't have to argue\nit out or unpick a squash. That is the deconfliction, and it is why this runs in place\nrather than on a side branch: the decisions live in the branch's own history, where the\nperson who owns the code will actually see them.\n\n**Apply the diff, not the file â€” for every path class, with no shortcuts.**\n`git checkout <base> -- <path>` copies base's *whole file*, discarding anything the\nbranch legitimately changed in it. `git rm <path>` is the same bug wearing a different\nhat: if base fixed that file, `git rm` deletes the fix, exits 0, and leaves no trace in\nany diff or log. The `apply --reverse` pipe refuses (exit 1) and surfaces the collision,\nwhich is the point. `--binary` matters too â€” without it `git diff` emits\n`Binary files â€¦ differ`, an unappliable stub.\n\nThree-dot (`<base>...<pre-prep tag>`) against the **pre-prep tag** is deliberate: it is\nmerge-baseâ†’branch-as-it-was, exactly what the branch did before this prep started.\nTwo-dot, or three-dot against the post-merge tip, would smuggle in reversals of base's\nown progress.\n\n**Never `git add -A` in this phase.** A conflicted merge or apply leaves unmerged index\nentries (which make `git commit` refuse â€” a useful accident); `git add -A` stages the\nconflict markers and commits them. Check exit codes per path, and `git diff --check`\nbefore each commit.\n\nBecause the branch now sits on current base, untouched files carry base's latest version\n(no old parts), and dropped no-op/unintended paths no longer appear in its diff.\n\n## Phase 3 â€” Verify the diff is exactly the intended set\n\n`git diff --stat <base> <branch>` â€” confirm it equals the INTENDED (+approved) set and\nnothing else, plus the prep document itself.\n\n**If `TICKETS.md` is in the diff, check it before anything else:**\n\n```\nsh practice/scripts/check-tickets.sh\n```\n\nThe board is append-only so git can merge it, which means both branches append at the\nsame point, conflict there, and get resolved by keeping both sides â€” the obvious move\nand the wrong one, because two branches may have allocated the same ID. A duplicate\n`A43` is not a merge artefact you can live with: an ID is permanent and referenced from\ncommits and chat, so one meaning two things is unrecoverable by convention alone. The\ncheck fails on duplicates and on committed conflict markers. Fix per `tickets-zordon`'s\ncollision rule â€” the **later** line takes a fresh number and keeps ` (was A43)`; the\nearlier one is never renumbered â€” then re-run it.\n\nThen **reconcile against the pre-prep tag.** A name-only tip-vs-tip diff is *not*\nenough: it works at path granularity, so on any file base also moved it lists the path\neither way, and the doc row \"base moved ahead\" truthfully explains the file while\nabsolving a hunk that vanished inside it. Check per hunk instead â€” if the intended\nchange is present, removing it succeeds:\n\n```\ngit checkout <branch>\n# for each KEPT path â€” must exit 0:\ngit diff --binary -M <base>...<pre-prep tag> -- <path> | git apply --reverse --check\n# for each DROPPED path â€” must print nothing:\ngit diff --stat <base> <branch> -- <dropped paths>\n```\n\nExit 0 means every intended hunk for that path is present verbatim in the prepped tree.\nNon-zero means a hunk is missing or altered â€” legitimate only if a prep-doc row names\n*that path and that hunk* (an exclusion, or a merge conflict resolved the other way);\nanything else is a change that vanished. Stop and find it. Comparing the branch to its\nown intended list is circular; this is the only check that can actually fail.\n\n**Then reconcile the other way â€” the destination must be undisturbed.** The check\nabove proves the feature survived; it says nothing about what the step-1 merge did\nto base's own work. Same pipe, other side:\n\n```\ngit checkout <branch>\n# for each path BASE changed since the merge base â€” must exit 0:\ngit diff --binary -M $(git merge-base <base> <pre-prep tag>)...<base> -- <path>   | git apply --reverse --check\n```\n\nExit 0 means every hunk the destination gained while the branch was away is still\npresent verbatim. Non-zero is legitimate **only** where a prep-doc row names that\npath and that hunk as a merge conflict resolved toward the branch â€” a deliberate,\nrecorded overwrite. Anything else is the branch reverting the destination's work,\nwhich is the failure this skill exists to prevent and which no test suite, no\n`git status`, and no name-only diff will ever show you.\n\nRecord the verification result in the prep document (amend commit 4, or add a fifth).\nThen **ask** whether to run the project's build/tests on the prepped branch (default yes\nif a test command exists) â€” since it now sits on current base, this is where you'd catch\nintegration breakage early.\n\n## Phase 4 â€” Hand off\n\nRelay: the branch name (unchanged), the pre-prep tag, the kept-vs-dropped counts, the\nverified diff, and the **path of the committed prep document**. If a reviewer-decisions\ncommit exists, say so explicitly and list what it changed â€” the developer hasn't seen\nthose edits and gets the last word on their own code; if they object, they revert that\ncommit. Then **ask at run time**: hand off locally (the user / `merge-smith` targets the\nbranch), or â€” only on an explicit yes â€” push it (normal push, never force). `merge-smith`\ncan now integrate a branch that carries only its real changes and documents why.\n\n## Rules\n\n- **In place, append-only.** No side branch, no rebase, no history rewrite, no\n  force-push, base untouched. The pre-prep tag makes the whole run reversible with\n  `git reset --hard <tag>`.\n- **The prep document is the deliverable and it is committed to the branch.** A decision\n  that lives only in a chat transcript is a decision nobody downstream has.\n- **Merge base in, don't rebase onto it.** This is what guarantees \"no old/untouched\n  parts\" while leaving the developer's commits byte-identical.\n- **Route the judgment:** NO-OP â†’ auto-drop (reported), SUSPICIOUS â†’ user decides,\n  INTENDED â†’ keep. Unsure = suspicious (escalate), never silently include.\n- **Developer intent owns content; reviewer intent owns inclusion.** A reviewer may\n  exclude a path freely, but changing what the code *says* is an edit â€” it goes in the\n  separate reviewer commit, named in the handoff, never folded in with anything else.\n- **Every path gets a logged decision and an owner** (developer / reviewer / auto).\n  A change nobody is recorded as having decided is a change nobody can defend at review.\n- **One pipe for every dropped path class.** Modified, added, deleted, renamed, binary â€”\n  all go through `git diff --binary -M <base>...<pre-prep tag> -- <path> | git apply\n  --reverse`. Whole-file `checkout` and `git rm` both destroy base's newer edits with\n  exit 0 and no trace.\n- **A non-zero `git apply` is data, not an obstacle.** It means base and the branch\n  genuinely collide on that path. Escalate it; never reach for a command that succeeds.\n- **The destination is not disturbed.** Every hunk base gained since the merge base\n  must still be present in the prepped branch. The only exception is a conflict the\n  prep document names, path *and* hunk, as resolved toward the branch. \"The feature\n  arrived\" is half the goal; \"and nothing of base left\" is the other half.\n- **Reconcile per hunk, not per path,** via `git apply --reverse --check` against the\n  pre-prep tag. Name-only diffs cannot see a hunk lost inside a file base also touched â€”\n  which is where losses actually hide.\n- **\"Current base\" is the local ref, and `git fetch` does not move it.** Fast-forward\n  `<base>` explicitly or the whole skill quietly operates on stale content.\n- Pairs with `merge-smith`: prep first, then merge the clean branch. The two are kept\n  in sync deliberately â€” same source/destination vocabulary, same `git diff --binary\n  -M ... | git apply --reverse --check` reconcile run in both directions, same\n  per-hunk granularity, same treatment of a non-zero exit as evidence. A change to\n  one of those in either skill belongs in both.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\relay-morpheus\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: relay-morpheus\ndescription: >-\n  Use when work runs in stages and the session may not survive to the end of\n  them â€” a multi-stage plan, a fan-out of subagents, a laptop that sleeps, a\n  dropping connection, a usage limit closing in.\n  Also when the user says \"staged workflow\", \"keep going across sessions\",\n  \"resume the run\", \"what was in flight\", or asks why a dispatched agent went\n  quiet. NOT for writing a session handoff (`handoff-cortana`) and NOT for the\n  board (`tickets-zordon`).\n---\n\n# relay-morpheus â€” staged runs that outlive the session\n\nA staged run has two records. The transcript, which dies with the session, and\nthe tree, which does not. Everything that matters has to be in the second one\n**while the run is still going**, because there is no guarantee you reach the\nend to write it down.\n\n## The rule\n\n```\nWRITE THE STAGE DOWN BEFORE YOU DISPATCH IT, NOT AFTER IT COMES BACK\n```\n\nThis is the one thing this skill exists for, and it is the one thing three\nisolated baseline runs did not do (`practice/baselines/scenario-relay-morpheus.txt`).\nAll three executed a four-stage plan end to end and left no on-disk record of\nprogress until the very last write â€” one left none at all. A session ending\nanywhere before that leaves a working tree full of unattributed diff and no way\nto tell which stages produced it, which were finished, or what was still\nrunning.\n\nReading a mid-run tree is **not** this skill's problem. The companion baseline\n(`scenario-relay-morpheus-resume.txt`) shows a fresh session doing that correctly\nunprompted, against pressure to trust stale checkboxes. Relay adds nothing\nthere and says nothing about it.\n\n**Violating the letter of this is violating the spirit of it.** \"I'll record it\nwhen the stage returns\" is the failure, not a variation on it â€” the stages that\nneed recording most are the ones that never return.\n\n## Handing a run to another session\n\nEvery run gets a short number when it is created, and `init` prints the\nsentence that resumes it:\n\n```\nhand this to any session:  continue RUN-3\n```\n\nThat sentence is the whole interface. A fresh session â€” another machine,\nanother host, a colleague â€” needs nothing else: **\"continue RUN-3\"**. It has no\ntranscript, so the ledger is what it reads, and the ledger is on disk because\nit was written before the work, not after.\n\n```sh\nrelay-morpheus.sh list          # every run, what is open, and its resume sentence\nrelay-morpheus.sh resume 3      # everything a session that has never seen it needs\nrelay-morpheus.sh 3 status      # any command takes the id first\n```\n\n`RUN-3`, `run-3` and `3` all resolve to the same run, because whoever types it\nnext is working from memory of a conversation rather than from a path. With one\nrun open and no id given, that run is assumed; with two or more, the script\nrefuses rather than guessing.\n\n## The run file\n\n`.skillator/run-<id>-<slug>.md`, one per run, in the repo. Markdown because a human and any\nhost both have to read it with no tooling.
