Git-tracked by default; a repo that\nobjects can `.gitignore` it and lose only the shared view, not the resume.\n\n```markdown\n# RUN-7 â€” notekeep v2\nplan: docs/plans/PLAN-notekeep.md\nstarted: 2026-09-22T09:14Z   updated: 2026-09-22T10:02Z\n\n## Stages\n| # | stage | state | owner | heartbeat | landed |\n|---|-------|-------|-------|-----------|--------|\n| 41 | list --json     | x | build:sonnet | 2026-09-22T09:22Z | 3f1a2c9 |\n| 42 | tags + --tag    | ~ | build:sonnet | 2026-09-22T10:02Z | - |\n| 43 | export          |   | -            | -     | - |\n| 44 | README          |   | -            | -     | - |\n\n## In flight\n### stage 42 â€” tags + --tag  (dispatched 09:51)\nprompt: |\n  Add a `tags` list to each note in notekeep/store.py and a `list --tag <t>`\n  filter in notekeep/cli.py. Test both. Do not touch export or the README.\nlast seen: wrote store.py, tests not yet run.\n\n## Rulings\n- 09:38 â€” stage 41 emits `[]` not `null` for an empty store â€” the plan does not\n  say, JSON consumers expect a list â€” costs a one-line change if wrong.\n```\n\n**States:** `` (pending) Â· `~` in flight Â· `x` landed Â· `!` failed.\n**`landed`** is a commit sha or a path, never a claim. An empty `landed` on an\n`x` row is a lie the next session will believe.\n**Numbers are serial across the project, not per run.** `init` takes the RUN\nid and every stage number from the F21 allocator (`practice/scripts/next-id.sh`,\nkinds `RUN` and `S`), which reads every run file in the tree and on every ref\nand keeps a counter every worktree of the clone shares â€” so two worktrees never\nboth make RUN-4, and a new run's first stage continues after the highest stage\nany run has used (RUN-7 above starts at 41). \"Stage 42\" names one stage in the\nwhole project. Existing run files are never renumbered.\n**Rulings** are appended, never rewritten â€” see `tasks-sentinels`, which is\nwhere they come from and which writes into this same file.\n\n## The loop\n\n1. **Before dispatch** â€” add or update the stage row to `~`, write the In-flight\n   block with the *exact prompt you are about to send*, stamp the heartbeat.\n   Prompt not written down â†’ the agent is not recoverable â†’ do not dispatch.\n2. **While it runs** â€” re-stamp the heartbeat whenever the agent reports. A\n   stale heartbeat is the only signal you get that a network drop took it;\n   `hooks/relay-morpheus.* orphans` turns that into a list.\n3. **When it lands** â€” commit the stage's work, then set `x` and put the sha in\n   `landed`, and delete its In-flight block. In that order: the sha cannot be\n   written before the commit exists.\n4. **When it fails** â€” `!`, and leave the In-flight block where it is with the\n   failure appended. A failed stage's prompt is the thing you need most.\n5. **Never** delete a row. A run file only grows.\n\nStages that touch the same files do not run at once; independent ones do\n(`PRACTICE.md` Â§4). Either way each gets its row before it gets its agent.\n\n## Resuming\n\nOpen `.skillator/run.md`, then check it against the tree â€” the file is written\nbefore the work, so the last `~` row is a stage that *started*, not one that\nfinished. `landed` shas are ground truth; anything else is a claim to verify.\nRedispatch straight from the stored prompt; the rows are keyed by stage number,\nso a redispatch that duplicates work overwrites rather than appends.\n\nThen carry on. A resume does not need permission to continue â€” see\n`grayskull-power` Â§3, **run to the end**.\n\n## Scripts\n\n`hooks/relay-morpheus.sh` Â· `hooks/relay-morpheus.ps1` â€” the same commands, mirrored per\n`PLATFORMS.md`: `init`, `add`, `stage`, `heartbeat`, `status`, `orphans`. They are\nbookkeeping, not judgement; every one reads or edits `.skillator/run.md` and\nnothing else. Run `selftest` on both after touching either.\n\n```sh\nsh  relay-morpheus.sh  init plan.md \"notekeep v2\" \"list --json\" \"tags\" \"export\" \"README\"\nsh  relay-morpheus.sh  add \"docs pass\"                    # a stage found mid-run; prints 45\nsh  relay-morpheus.sh  stage 42 '~' build:sonnet          # before you dispatch\nsh  relay-morpheus.sh  stage 42 x  build:sonnet 3f1a2c9   # after you commit\nsh  relay-morpheus.sh  orphans 20                         # who has gone quiet\n```\n\n```powershell\nrelay-morpheus.ps1 -Mode init  -Plan plan.md -Title \"notekeep v2\" -Stages \"list --json,tags,export,README\"\nrelay-morpheus.ps1 -Mode add   -Title \"docs pass\"\nrelay-morpheus.ps1 -Mode stage -N 42 -State '~' -Owner build:sonnet\nrelay-morpheus.ps1 -Mode stage -N 42 -State x   -Owner build:sonnet -Landed 3f1a2c9\n# In a headless or nested run on Windows, call the .sh through the Bash tool:\n# the PowerShell tool refuses `& <script>.ps1` and `powershell -File` under any\n# allow rule (A91, claude 2.1.280).\n```\n\nAdd rows with `add`, never by hand: a hand-typed number is the per-run\nrestart this numbering removed. The scripts find the allocator beside the\ninstalled skills (`<skills>/practice/scripts/`, or `<root>/practice/scripts/`\nin a clone or the plugin cache; `RELAY_NEXT_ID` overrides). Without it they\nfall back to the old local rule and say so on stderr â€” numbers then repeat\nacross worktrees.\n\nThree constraints the pair has to keep, each learned the hard way:\n\n- **Pure ASCII, and the empty cell is `-`, not an em dash.** Windows PowerShell\n  5.1 reads a BOM-less script as ANSI and corrupts anything above 126.\n- **The run file is LF with no BOM, written to a temp and moved.** Both mirrors\n  edit the same file, so one of them flipping encoding rewrites every line in\n  the diff; and a kill between truncate and write would empty the only record\n  of what was in flight.\n- **`-Stages` takes `\"a,b,c\"` and pending is spelled `pending`.** PowerShell's\n  `-File` passes each argument as one literal string and drops empty ones, and\n  `-File` is how every hook here invokes it.\n\n### Seeing a run without being told about one\n\n`list` prints every run with its resume sentence, and prints nothing when there\nare none, so it is safe as a `SessionStart` hook. With one wired, a session that inherits\na half-finished run sees it in its own context before the user has to remember\nto mention it â€” which is the difference between resuming and starting over.\n\n```json\n{ \"hooks\": { \"SessionStart\": [ { \"hooks\": [ {\n  \"type\": \"command\",\n  \"command\": \"powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File \\\"<SKILL>/hooks/relay-morpheus.ps1\\\" -Mode list\",\n  \"timeout\": 5\n} ] } ] } }\n```\n\nmacOS/Linux: `sh <SKILL>/hooks/relay-morpheus.sh list`. Forward slashes throughout â€”\nPowerShell accepts them and it removes all backslash-escaping from the JSON.\nOther hosts have no equivalent event; there, reading `.skillator/run.md` is the\nfirst thing `resume-cortana` does. `watch-cortana` already drains in-flight\nagents at the limit â€” relay is what it drains *into*, so the two do not overlap.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\relay-morpheus\\hooks\\relay-morpheus.ps1\r\n# relay - bookkeeping for `.skillator/run.md`, the staged-run ledger.\n#\n# The PowerShell mirror of relay-morpheus.sh. Same commands, same file format, same\n# refusals - `PLATFORMS.md` requires the pair to move together, and `selftest`\n# on each side is what proves they still agree.\n#\n#   relay-morpheus.ps1 -Mode list                      every run + how to resume it\n#   relay-morpheus.ps1 -Mode resume -Id 3              what a fresh session needs\n#   relay-morpheus.ps1 -Mode init -Plan <p> -Title <t> -Stages \"alpha,beta,gamma\"\n#   relay-morpheus.ps1 -Mode add -Title <stage>        append a row, print its number\n#   relay-morpheus.ps1 -Mode stage -N 2 -State '~' [-Owner build:sonnet] [-Landed sha]\n#   relay-morpheus.ps1 -Mode heartbeat -N 2\n#   relay-morpheus.ps1 -Mode status\n#   relay-morpheus.ps1 -Mode orphans [-Minutes 20]\n#   relay-morpheus.ps1 -Mode selftest\n#\n# Any mode takes an optional -Id: `-Mode status -Id 3`.\n#\n# States: pending | '~' in flight | 'x' landed | '!' failed\n#\n# RUN ids and stage numbers are serial across the whole project, not per run\n# (F22): they come from practice/scripts/next-id.ps1 (kinds RUN and S), which\n# also reads every run file in the tree and on every ref. $env:RELAY_NEXT_ID\n# overrides where that script is looked for.\n#\n# Two `-File` facts shape this signature, because `-File` is how this repo\n# invokes every PS hook: it passes each argument as ONE literal string, so\n# -Stages takes a delimited string and splits it here rather than relying on\n# PowerShell array binding; and it drops empty-string arguments, so the pending\n# state is spelled `pending` rather than ''.\n[CmdletBinding()]\nparam(\n  [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)][ValidateSet('init','add','stage','heartbeat','status','orphans','list','resume','selftest')][string]$Mode,\n  [string]$Plan,\n  [string]$Title,\n  [string]$Stages,\n  [int]$N,\n  [string]$State = 'pending',\n  [string]$Owner = '',\n  [string]$Landed = '',\n  [int]$Minutes = 20,\n  [string]$Run = '',\n  [string]$Id = '',\n  [string]$Dir = ''\n)\n\n$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'\nif (-not $Dir) { $Dir = if ($env:RELAY_DIR) { $env:RELAY_DIR } else { '.skillator' } }\nif (-not $Run -and $env:RELAY_RUN) { $Run = $env:RELAY_RUN }\n\n# A run id is a short number a human can say out loud to another session -\n# \"continue RUN-3\". The timestamp ids this replaces were unsayable, which made\n# handing a run over a copy-paste of a path instead of a sentence.\nfunction RunFiles {\n  if (-not (Test-Path $Dir)) { return @() }\n  @(Get-ChildItem $Dir -Filter 'run*.md' -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Sort-Object Name)\n}\n# The id out of a filename, with no regex to get wrong.\nfunction IdOf($path) {\n  $b = [IO.Path]::GetFileNameWithoutExtension($path)\n  if (-not $b.StartsWith('run-')) { return '' }\n  $b = $b.Substring(4).Split('-')[0]\n  if ($b -match '^\\d+$') { return $b }\n  return ''\n}\nfunction NextId {\n  $n = 0\n  foreach ($f in RunFiles) { $i = IdOf $f.Name; if ($i -and [int]$i -gt $n) { $n = [int]$i } }\n  $n + 1\n}\n# Numbers used to be local: RUN = 1 + the highest run file here, stages 1..n\n# per run. Two worktrees both made RUN-4, and every run had a stage 1. The F21\n# allocator fixes both - it ships beside the skills (installed:\n# <skills>\\practice\\, a clone or plugin: <root>\\practice\\), and without it the\n# local rule still works, said on stderr.\nfunction FindNextId {\n  if ($null -ne $env:RELAY_NEXT_ID) {\n    if ($env:RELAY_NEXT_ID -and (Test-Path -LiteralPath $env:RELAY_NEXT_ID -PathType Leaf)) { return $env:RELAY_NEXT_ID }\n    return $null\n  }\n  foreach ($c in @((Join-Path $PSScriptRoot '..\\..\\practice\\scripts\\next-id.ps1'),\n                   (Join-Path $PSScriptRoot '..\\..\\..\\practice\\scripts\\next-id.ps1'))) {\n    if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $c -PathType Leaf) { return (Resolve-Path -LiteralPath $c).Path }\n  }\n  $null\n}\nfunction MaxStage($dir) {\n  $m = 0\n  foreach ($f in @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $dir -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Where-Object { $_.Name -like 'run-*.md' -or $_.Name -eq 'run.md' })) {\n    foreach ($l in [IO.File]::ReadAllLines($f.FullName)) { $r = RowNum $l; if ($null -ne $r -and $r -gt $m) { $m = $r } }\n  }\n  $m\n}\n# $count fresh numbers of $kind (RUN or S), for run files in $dir.\nfunction Alloc($kind, $count, $dir) {\n  $nid = FindNextId\n  if ($nid) {\n    $out = @(& $nid -Runs $dir -Count \"$count\" $kind)\n    # Judged by its output, not $LASTEXITCODE: in-process, that holds whatever\n    # git call ran last (git grep exits 1 on no match).\n    if ($out.Count -ne $count -or ($out | Where-Object { \"$_\" -notmatch '^\\s*\\d+\\s*$' })) { Die \"next-id.ps1 could not allocate $kind (see above)\" }\n    return @($out | ForEach-Object { [int]\"$_\".Trim() })\n  }\n  [Console]::Error.WriteLine(\"relay-morpheus.ps1: next-id.ps1 not found - numbering $kind from this directory only, which another worktree can repeat\")\n  $saved = $script:Dir; $script:Dir = $dir\n  try { if ($kind -eq 'RUN') { $m = (NextId) - 1 } else { $m = MaxStage $dir } } finally { $script:Dir = $saved }\n  @(($m + 1)..($m + $count))\n}\n\n# RUN-3, run-3, 3 and a path all resolve to the same file, because whoever\n# types it next is working from memory of a conversation.\nfunction Resolve-Run($spec) {\n  if ($spec -match '[\\/]' -and (Test-Path $spec)) { return (Resolve-Path $spec).Path }\n  $n = ($spec -replace '\\D', '')\n  if (-not $n) { return $null }\n  foreach ($f in RunFiles) { if ((IdOf $f.Name) -eq $n) { return $f.FullName } }\n  $null\n}\n# With no id given, fall back to the one run if there is exactly one. Two or\n# more and this refuses rather than guessing which.\nfunction PickRun {\n  if ($script:Run) { return }\n  $all = RunFiles\n  if ($all.Count -gt 1) { Die \"$($all.Count) runs here - name one (-Id 3), or see: -Mode list\" }\n  $script:Run = if ($all.Count -eq 1) { $all[0].FullName } else { Join-Path $Dir 'run.md' }\n}\nfunction Slug($t) {\n  (($t.ToLower() -replace '[^a-z0-9]+', '-').Trim('-').Split('-') | Where-Object { $_ } | Select-Object -First 4) -join '-'\n}\n\n# Every timestamp is UTC and invariant-culture. Without this, a machine whose\n# default calendar is not Gregorian (th-TH Buddhist, ar-SA Hijri) writes 2569\n# for `yyyy`, and a run file touched by both mirrors mixes eras - which makes\n# relay-morpheus.sh's age arithmetic return nonsense rather than fail.\n$INV = [cultureinfo]::InvariantCulture\n$FMT = \"yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mmZ\"\n\nfunction Die($m) { throw \"FAIL: $m\" }\nfunction Now { (Get-Date).ToUniversalTime().ToString($FMT, $INV) }\nfunction AsUtc($s) { [datetime]::ParseExact($s, $FMT, $INV) }\nfunction NeedRun { PickRun; if (-not (Test-Path $Run)) { Die \"no run file at $Run (relay-morpheus.ps1 -Mode init ...)\" } }\n\n# Every mutation is a read-modify-write of the whole file, and SKILL.md\n# sanctions concurrent in-flight stages - two `stage` calls landing at once\n# would lose one row, which is the loss this whole skill exists to prevent.\n# Creating a directory is the portable atomic test-and-set, same as the sh\n# twin. ponytail: a spin with a stale timeout, not a lock manager.\n$script:Lock = $null\n# The lock dir carries its holder's pid. Breaking a stale lock without checking\n# it let B delete a slow-but-alive A's lock, and A's release then deleted B's -\n# admitting C mid-write, which is the lost update the lock exists to prevent.\nfunction LockPid { try { (Get-Content (Join-Path $script:Lock 'pid') -Raw -ErrorAction Stop).Trim() } catch { '' } }\nfunction Lock {\n  $script:Lock = \"$Run.lock\"\n  try { New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $script:Lock -ErrorAction Stop | Out-Null\n        Set-Content (Join-Path $script:Lock 'pid') \"$PID\"; return } catch {}\n  $held = LockPid\n  for ($i = 0; ; $i++) {\n    try { New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $script:Lock -ErrorAction Stop | Out-Null\n          Set-Content (Join-Path $script:Lock 'pid') \"$PID\"; return } catch {}\n    # 30s of ONE holder's turn means that process died holding it. Break it\n    # only if the pid has not changed since we arrived.\n    if ($i -gt 300 -and (LockPid) -eq $held) { Remove-Item -Recurse -Force $script:Lock -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue }\n    Start-Sleep -Milliseconds 100\n  }\n}\nfunction Unlock {\n  if ($script:Lock -and (LockPid) -eq \"$PID\") { Remove-Item -Recurse -Force $script:Lock -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue }\n  $script:Lock = $null\n}\n\n# LF, no BOM, written to a sibling temp then moved - matching relay-morpheus.sh's\n# tmp+mv. Set-Content would truncate in place, so a Ctrl-C or a usage stop\n# between truncate and write leaves the ledger empty, destroying the only\n# record of what was in flight. `-Encoding utf8` on 5.1 also emits a BOM, which\n# would make every cross-mirror edit rewrite all lines in the diff.\nfunction WriteRun($lines) {\n  $tmp = \"$Run.tmp.$PID\"\n  [IO.File]::WriteAllText($tmp, (($lines -join \"`n\") + \"`n\"), (New-Object Text.UTF8Encoding($false)))\n  Move-Item -Force -Path $tmp -Destination $Run\n}\n\nfunction StampUpdated {\n  $t = Now\n  WriteRun (Get-Content $Run | ForEach-Object {\n    if ($_ -match '^started: ') { $_ -replace 'updated: .*', \"updated: $t\" } else { $_ }\n  })\n}\n\nfunction RowNum($line) {\n  if ($line -match '^\\|\\s*(\\d+)\\s*\\|') { return [int]$Matches[1] }\n  return $null\n}\n\nfunction Cell($line, $i) { ($line -split '\\|')[$i].Trim() }\n\nfunction DoInit {\n  if (-not $Plan -or -not $Title -or -not $Stages) {\n    Die 'usage: -Mode init -Plan <p> -Title <t> -Stages \"alpha,beta\"'\n  }\n  $names = @($Stages -split ',' | ForEach-Object { $_.Trim() } | Where-Object { $_ })\n  if (-not $names) { Die \"no stage names in -Stages\" }\n  # A `|` in a stage name adds a column, and every later read is positional -\n  # the state would land in the name cell and the sha in heartbeat, silently.\n  # Checked before any number is reserved.\n  foreach ($nm in $names) { if ($nm -match '\\|') { Die \"stage name contains '|', which would shift every column: $nm\" } }\n  if (-not $script:Run) {\n    if (-not (Test-Path $Dir)) { New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force $Dir | Out-Null }\n    $script:id = (Alloc 'RUN' 1 $Dir)[0]\n    $script:Run = Join-Path $Dir (\"run-{0}-{1}.md\" -f $script:id, (Slug $Title))\n  } else {\n    $script:id = IdOf $script:Run; if (-not $script:id) { $script:id = '1' }\n  }\n  if (Test-Path $Run) { Die \"run file already exists: $Run (a run file is never overwritten)\" }\n  $dir = Split-Path -Parent $Run\n  if ($dir -and -not (Test-Path $dir)) { New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force $dir | Out-Null }\n  # Stage numbers continue after the highest any run has used, so \"stage 24\"\n  # names one stage in the whole project, not one per run.\n  if (-not $dir) { $dir = '.' }\n  $nums = Alloc 'S' $names.Count $dir\n  $t = Now\n  $id = $script:id\n  $lines = @(\"# RUN-$id - $Title\", \"plan: $Plan\", \"started: $t   updated: $t\", \"\",\n             \"## Stages\", \"| # | stage | state | owner | heartbeat | landed |\",\n             \"|---|-------|-------|-------|-----------|--------|\")\n  for ($i = 0; $i -lt $names.Count; $i++) { $lines += \"| $($nums[$i]) | $($names[$i]) |   | - | - | - |\" }\n  $lines += @(\"\", \"## In flight\", \"\", \"## Rulings\")\n  WriteRun $lines\n  $Run\n  # The whole point of the id: this line is what gets pasted into another\n  # session, or said out loud. A path is not a sentence.\n  \"hand this to any session:  continue RUN-$id\"\n}\n\n# A stage found mid-run gets its row the same way init's did: the next serial\n# number, after the last row. Printed so the caller can say -Mode stage -N <n>.\nfunction DoAdd($name) {\n  if (-not $name) { Die 'usage: -Mode add -Title <stage>' }\n  if ($name -match '\\|') { Die \"stage name contains '|', which would shift every column: $name\" }\n  NeedRun\n  $dir = Split-Path -Parent $Run; if (-not $dir) { $dir = '.' }\n  $n = (Alloc 'S' 1 $dir)[0]\n  Lock\n  try {\n    $lines = @(Get-Content $Run)\n    # After the last table row of `## Stages`; the separator row counts, so an\n    # empty table still takes its first row in the right place.\n    $on = $false; $last = -1\n    for ($i = 0; $i -lt $lines.Count; $i++) {\n      if ($lines[$i] -match '^## Stages') { $on = $true; continue }\n      if ($on -and $lines[$i] -match '^## ') { $on = $false }\n      if ($on -and $lines[$i] -match '^\\|') { $last = $i }\n    }\n    if ($last -lt 0) { Die \"no stage table in $Run\" }\n    $out = @($lines[0..$last]) + @(\"| $n | $name |   | - | - | - |\")\n    if ($last + 1 -lt $lines.Count) { $out += $lines[($last + 1)..($lines.Count - 1)] }\n    WriteRun $out\n    StampUpdated\n  } finally { Unlock }\n  \"$n\"\n}\n\n# One line per run, with the sentence that resumes it.\nfunction DoList {\n  $all = RunFiles\n  if (-not $all) { \"no runs in $Dir\"; return }\n  foreach ($f in $all) {\n    $id = IdOf $f.Name; if (-not $id) { $id = '?' }\n    $lines = Get-Content $f.FullName\n    $title = ($lines[0] -replace '^# RUN[-a-zA-Z0-9]* *- *', '')\n    $plan  = ($lines[1] -replace '^plan: *', '')\n    $tot = 0; $dn = 0; $open = @()\n    foreach ($l in $lines) {\n      $n = RowNum $l\n      if ($null -ne $n) {\n        $tot++\n        $st = Cell $l 3\n        if ($st -eq 'x') { $dn++ }\n        if ($st -eq '~' -or $st -eq '!') { $open += $n }\n      }\n    }\n    $row = \"RUN-{0}  {1,-42}  {2}/{3} done\" -f $id, $title, $dn, $tot\n    if ($open) { $row += \"  (stage $($open -join ',') open)\" }\n    $row\n    \"          plan: $plan\"\n    \"          resume: continue RUN-$id\"\n  }\n}\n\n# Everything a session that has never seen this work needs, in one paste.\nfunction DoResume($spec) {\n  if (-not $spec) { Die \"usage: -Mode resume -Id <id>\" }\n  $f = Resolve-Run $spec\n  if (-not $f) { Die \"no run matching '$spec' - see: -Mode list\" }\n  \"# Resuming $f\"\n  \"# Read the plan named below, then the ledger, then start at the first stage\"\n  \"# that is not x. The In-flight block holds the exact prompt to redispatch\"\n  \"# anything that was running.\"\n  \"\"\n  Get-Content $f\n}\n\n# Keyed by stage number, so a redispatch rewrites its row instead of appending\n# a second one - that is what makes a resume idempotent.\nfunction DoStage($n, $state, $owner, $landed) {\n  if (-not $n) { Die \"usage: -Mode stage -N <n> -State <s> [-Owner o] [-Landed l]\" }\n  if ($state -eq 'pending') { $state = '' }\n  if ($state -notin @('', '~', 'x', '!')) { Die \"unknown state: $state (one of pending ~ x !)\" }\n  # Same reason `init` refuses it in a stage name: a `|` adds a column, and the\n  # next positional read writes state into the wrong cell. `landed` takes a\n  # path, so this is reachable without an exotic owner string.\n  foreach ($v in @($owner, $landed)) { if ($v -match '\\|') { Die \"'|' in owner/landed would shift every column: $v\" } }\n  if ($state -eq 'x' -and -not $landed) {\n    Die \"stage $n marked landed with no sha or path - that is the lie the next session believes\"\n  }\n  NeedRun\n  Lock\n  try {\n  $t = Now\n  $found = $false\n  $out = Get-Content $Run | ForEach-Object {\n    if ((RowNum $_) -eq $n) {\n      $found = $true\n      $c = $_ -split '\\|'\n      $c[3] = \" $(if ($state -eq '') { ' ' } else { $state }) \"\n      if ($owner)  { $c[4] = \" $owner \" }\n      $c[5] = \" $t \"\n      if ($landed) { $c[6] = \" $landed \" }\n      ($c -join '|')\n    } else { $_ }\n  }\n  if (-not $found) { Die \"no stage $n in $Run\" }\n  WriteRun $out\n  StampUpdated\n  } finally { Unlock }\n}\n\n# A heartbeat carries the row's existing `landed` back in. Without it the\n# \"x with no sha\" guard fires on a stage that already landed, turning a no-op\n# bookkeeping call from a late-reporting agent into a hard error.\nfunction DoHeartbeat($n) {\n  if (-not $n) { Die \"usage: -Mode heartbeat -N <n>\" }\n  NeedRun\n  $st = $null; $la = ''\n  Get-Content $Run | ForEach-Object {\n    if ((RowNum $_) -eq $n) {\n      $st = Cell $_ 3\n      $la = Cell $_ 6\n      if ($la -eq '-') { $la = '' }\n    }\n  }\n  if ($null -eq $st) { Die \"no stage $n in $Run\" }\n  if ($st -eq '') { $st = 'pending' }\n  DoStage $n $st '' $la\n}\n\nfunction DoStatus {\n  PickRun\n  if (-not (Test-Path $Run)) { return }\n  $on = $false\n  Get-Content $Run | ForEach-Object {\n    if ($_ -match '^## Stages')        { $on = $true }\n    elseif ($_ -match '^## In flight') { $on = $false }\n    if ($on) { $_ }\n  }\n}\n\n# The network-loss list: in-flight rows nobody has heard from. A dropped agent\n# sends no error, it just stops reporting, so silence is the only signal.\nfunction DoOrphans($limit) {\n  PickRun\n  if (-not (Test-Path $Run)) { return }\n  $t = AsUtc (Now)\n  $any = $false\n  Get-Content $Run | ForEach-Object {\n    $n = RowNum $_\n    if (($null -ne $n) -and ((Cell $_ 3) -eq '~')) {\n      $any = $true\n      $name = Cell $_ 2; $hb = Cell $_ 5\n      if ($hb -eq '-') { \"stage $n ($name): in flight, never reported\" }\n      else {\n        $a = [int]($t - (AsUtc $hb)).TotalMinutes\n        if ($a -ge $limit) { \"stage $n ($name): silent ${a}m (limit ${limit}m) - redispatch from its In-flight block\" }\n      }\n    }\n  }\n  if (-not $any) { \"no stages in flight\" }\n}\n\n# Each expected-failure check asserts on the message it expects. Catching\n# everything and re-throwing only the \"it did not fail\" marker would swallow a\n# regression that threw for the wrong reason and still print ok.\nfunction ShouldFail($expect, $block) {\n  try { & $block | Out-Null } catch {\n    if (\"$_\" -notmatch $expect) { throw \"selftest: wrong failure, wanted /$expect/, got: $_\" }\n    return\n  }\n  throw \"selftest: expected a failure matching /$expect/ and got none\"\n}\n\nfunction DoSelftest {\n  $d = Join-Path ([IO.Path]::GetTempPath()) (\"relay-\" + [guid]::NewGuid())\n  New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force $d | Out-Null\n  # Each scratch dir is its own git repo, so the allocator keeps its counter\n  # there and not in whatever repo the selftest was started from.\n  $gc = @('-c', 'user.name=t', '-c', 'user.email=t@example.invalid', '-c', 'core.autocrlf=false', '-c', 'core.hooksPath=NUL')\n  function G { $prev = $ErrorActionPreference; $ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'\n    & git @gc @args 2>&1 | Out-Null; $ErrorActionPreference = $prev\n    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw \"selftest: git $args failed\" } }\n  G init -q $d\n  try {\n    $script:Run = Join-Path $d \"run.md\"\n    $script:Plan = \"plan.md\"; $script:Title = \"selftest\"; $script:Stages = \"alpha,beta\"\n\n    DoInit | Out-Null\n    if (-not (Select-String -Path $Run -Pattern '^\\| 2 \\| beta \\|' -Quiet)) { Die \"init: no row for stage 2\" }\n    # -Stages arrives as one literal string under -File; splitting is this\n    # script's job, and collapsing it to a single row would silently erase\n    # every stage boundary - the precise loss relay exists to prevent.\n    if (-not (Select-String -Path $Run -Pattern '^\\| 1 \\| alpha \\|' -Quiet)) { Die \"init: -Stages was not split\" }\n\n    # No BOM, LF endings - a file both mirrors edit must not flip encoding.\n    $raw = [IO.File]::ReadAllBytes($Run)\n    if ($raw[0] -eq 0xEF) { Die \"init: wrote a UTF-8 BOM\" }\n    if ([Text.Encoding]::UTF8.GetString($raw) -match \"`r`n\") { Die \"init: wrote CRLF\" }\n\n    ShouldFail 'run file already exists' { DoInit }\n\n    DoStage 1 '~' 'build:sonnet' ''\n    if (-not (Select-String -Path $Run -Pattern '^\\| 1 \\| alpha \\| ~ \\| build:sonnet \\|' -Quiet)) { Die \"stage: row 1 not in flight\" }\n\n    ShouldFail 'marked landed with no sha' { DoStage 1 'x' '' '' }\n    DoStage 1 'x' 'build:sonnet' '3f1a2c9'\n    if (-not (Select-String -Path $Run -Pattern '3f1a2c9' -Quiet)) { Die \"stage: sha not recorded\" }\n\n    DoStage 1 'x' 'build:sonnet' '3f1a2c9'\n    if ((Select-String -Path $Run -Pattern '^\\| 1 \\|').Count -ne 1) { Die \"stage: duplicated row 1\" }\n\n    ShouldFail 'no stage 9' { DoStage 9 '~' '' '' }\n\n    # A pipe in a stage name must be refused at init, not corrupt the ledger.\n    $saved = $script:Run; $script:Run = Join-Path $d \"piped.md\"\n    $script:Stages = \"list | pretty\"\n    ShouldFail \"would shift every column\" { DoInit }\n    $script:Run = $saved; $script:Stages = \"alpha,beta\"\n\n    # The lock must be released on the way out, or the next call spins for 30s.\n    if (Test-Path \"$Run.lock\") { Die \"stage: left the lock behind\" }\n\n    # A `|` in owner or landed shifts every column, exactly as one in a stage\n    # name does - and `landed` takes a path, so it is reachable by accident.\n    ShouldFail \"would shift every column\" { DoStage 2 '~' 'model:opus|5' '' }\n    ShouldFail \"would shift every column\" { DoStage 2 'x' 'build:sonnet' 'a|b' }\n\n    # A heartbeat on a stage that already landed must not trip the sha guard.\n    DoHeartbeat 1\n    if (-not (Select-String -Path $Run -Pattern '3f1a2c9' -Quiet)) { Die \"heartbeat: dropped the landed sha\" }\n\n    # `pending` is the -File-safe spelling of the empty state; -State '' is\n    # dropped by -File before the script ever sees it.\n    DoStage 2 'pending' '' ''\n    if ((DoOrphans 20) -notmatch 'no stages in flight') { Die \"orphans: a pending row counted as in flight\" }\n\n    DoStage 2 '~' 'build:sonnet' ''\n    if ((DoOrphans 20) -match 'no stages in flight') { Die \"orphans: missed an in-flight row\" }\n    if (-not ((DoOrphans 0) -match 'stage 2')) { Die \"orphans: did not flag a silent stage at limit 0\" }\n    if ((DoOrphans 9999) -match 'stage 2') { Die \"orphans: flagged a fresh heartbeat\" }\n    if (-not (DoStatus | Select-String -Pattern '^\\| 2 \\|' -Quiet)) { Die \"status: did not print the stage table\" }\n\n    # The same rollovers relay-morpheus.sh asserts on its own arithmetic. DoOrphans does\n    # its date maths independently, so the pair can disagree without this.\n    foreach ($p in @(@('2026-09-22T09:00Z', '2026-09-22T09:25Z', 25),\n                     @('2026-09-22T23:50Z', '2026-09-23T00:10Z', 20),\n                     @('2026-02-28T23:50Z', '2026-03-01T00:10Z', 20),\n                     @('2024-02-28T23:50Z', '2024-02-29T00:10Z', 20),\n                     @('2025-12-31T23:50Z', '2026-01-01T00:10Z', 20))) {\n      $got = [int](((AsUtc $p[1]) - (AsUtc $p[0])).TotalMinutes)\n      if ($got -ne $p[2]) { Die \"age: $($p[0]) -> $($p[1]) gave $got, wanted $($p[2])\" }\n    }\n\n    # Everything above runs in-process. This is the only check that goes\n    # through `-File`, which is how the hooks actually invoke this script and\n    # where the -Stages and -State argument bugs both lived.\n    $f2 = Join-Path $d \"viafile.md\"\n    & powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File $PSCommandPath -Mode init -Plan p -Title t -Stages \"one,two\" -Run $f2 | Out-Null\n    if (-not (Select-String -Path $f2 -Pattern '^\\| \\d+ \\| two \\|' -Quiet)) { Die \"-File: -Stages did not split\" }\n    # Stage numbers are serial now, so take this run's first one from the file.\n    $n2 = @(Get-Content $f2 | ForEach-Object { RowNum $_ } | Where-Object { $null -ne $_ })[0]\n    & powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File $PSCommandPath -Mode stage -N $n2 -State pending -Run $f2 | Out-Null\n    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { Die \"-File: -State pending failed\" }\n\n    # --- F22: RUN ids and stage numbers are serial across the project. Before\n    # the allocator, two worktrees both made RUN-4 and every run had a stage 1.\n    if (FindNextId) {\n      $d3 = Join-Path $d 'f22'; $main = Join-Path $d3 'main'; $sk = Join-Path $main '.skillator'\n      New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force $sk | Out-Null\n      G init -q -b main $main\n      $old = Join-Path $sk 'run-3-old.md'\n      $ol = @('# RUN-3 - old', '## Stages', '| # | stage | state | owner | heartbeat | landed |', '|---|-------|-------|-------|-----------|--------|')\n      foreach ($i in 1..23) { $ol += \"| $i | s$i |   | - | - | - |\" }\n      [IO.File]::WriteAllText($old, (($ol -join \"`n\") + \"`n\"))\n      G -C $main add -A; G -C $main commit -q -m old\n      $wt2 = Join-Path $d3 'wt2'; G -C $main worktree add -q -b wt2 $wt2\n      $before = [IO.File]::ReadAllText($old)\n      $script:Dir = $sk; $script:Run = ''; $script:Title = 'four'; $script:Stages = 'a,b'\n      DoInit | Out-Null\n      $f4 = Resolve-Run 4; if (-not $f4) { Die \"F22: first new run is not RUN-4\" }\n      if (-not (Select-String -Path $f4 -Pattern '^\\| 24 \\| a \\|' -Quiet) -or -not (Select-String -Path $f4 -Pattern '^\\| 25 \\| b \\|' -Quiet)) { Die \"F22: stages did not continue after 23\" }\n      $script:Dir = Join-Path $wt2 '.skillator'; $script:Run = ''; $script:Title = 'other tree'; $script:Stages = 'c'\n      DoInit | Out-Null\n      $f5 = Resolve-Run 5; if (-not $f5) { Die \"F22: second worktree repeated RUN-4\" }\n      if (-not (Select-String -Path $f5 -Pattern '^\\| 26 \\| c \\|' -Quiet)) { Die \"F22: second worktree reused a stage number\" }\n      $script:Dir = $sk; $script:Run = $f4\n      $got = DoAdd 'late one'; if (\"$got\" -ne '27') { Die \"add: did not take the next serial number (got $got)\" }\n      $fl = @(Get-Content $f4); $p = [array]::FindIndex($fl, [Predicate[string]]{ $args[0] -match '^\\| 25 \\|' })\n      if ($fl[$p + 1] -notmatch '^\\| 27 \\| late one \\|') { Die \"add: row not after the last stage\" }\n      ShouldFail 'would shift every column' { DoAdd 'a | b' }\n      DoStage 27 '~' 'build:opus' ''\n      if ([IO.File]::ReadAllText($old) -ne $before) { Die \"F22: an existing run file was renumbered\" }\n      # Through -File, as the hooks call it: -Mode add -Title.\n      $o = & powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File $PSCommandPath -Mode add -Title 'via file' -Run $f4\n      if (\"$($o | Select-Object -Last 1)\".Trim() -ne '28') { Die \"-File: -Mode add did not print 28 (got $o)\" }\n      # No allocator: the old local rule, said on stderr, never silent.\n      $env:RELAY_NEXT_ID = Join-Path $d3 'nope.ps1'\n      try {\n        $script:Dir = $sk; $script:Run = ''; $script:Title = 'fallback'; $script:Stages = 'z'\n        $err = Join-Path $d3 'err.txt'\n        # 5.1 turns a native stderr line into a terminating error under Stop.\n        $ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'\n        try { & powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File $PSCommandPath -Mode init -Plan p -Title fallback -Stages z -Dir $sk 2>$err | Out-Null }\n        finally { $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop' }\n        if (-not (Select-String -Path $err -Pattern 'next-id.ps1 not found' -Quiet)) { Die \"fallback: no stderr warning\" }\n        $ff = Get-ChildItem $sk -Filter 'run-*-fallback.md' | Select-Object -First 1\n        if (-not $ff -or -not (Select-String -Path $ff.FullName -Pattern '^\\| 29 \\| z \\|' -Quiet)) { Die \"fallback: stage not local max + 1\" }\n      } finally { Remove-Item Env:RELAY_NEXT_ID }\n    } else {\n      [Console]::Error.WriteLine(\"note: next-id.ps1 not found beside the skills - F22 allocator checks skipped\")\n    }\n\n    \"ok\"\n  } finally { Remove-Item -Recurse -Force $d -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue }\n}\n\ntry {\n  # -Id names the run for every mode that touches one.\n  if ($Id) {\n    $f = Resolve-Run $Id\n    if (-not $f -and $Mode -ne 'resume') { Die \"no run matching '$Id' - see: -Mode list\" }\n    if ($f) { $Run = $f }\n  }\n  switch ($Mode) {\n    'list'      { DoList }\n    'resume'    { DoResume $Id }\n    'init'      { DoInit }\n    'add'       { DoAdd $Title }\n    'stage'     { DoStage $N $State $Owner $Landed }\n    'heartbeat' { DoHeartbeat $N }\n    'status'    { DoStatus }\n    'orphans'   { DoOrphans $Minutes }\n    'selftest'  { DoSelftest }\n    default     { Die \"unknown mode: $Mode (init|add|stage|heartbeat|status|orphans|selftest)\" }\n  }\n} catch {\n  # Write-Error would itself throw under $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop', making\n  # the exit below unreachable and handing a -Command caller an exception.\n  [Console]::Error.WriteLine(\"$_\")\n  exit 1\n}\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\relay-morpheus\\hooks\\relay-morpheus.sh\r\n#!/bin/sh\n# relay - bookkeeping for `.skillator/run.md`, the staged-run ledger.\n#\n# Every command here reads or edits that one file and nothing else. There is no\n# judgement in this script: deciding a stage is done is the model's job, saying\n# so on disk is this script's. See ../SKILL.md for the rule it serves.\n#\n#   relay-morpheus.sh list                             every run + how to resume it\n#   relay-morpheus.sh resume <id>                      what a fresh session needs\n#   relay-morpheus.sh init <plan> <title> <stage>...   create the run file\n#   relay-morpheus.sh add <stage>                      append a row, print its number\n#   relay-morpheus.sh stage <n> <state> [owner] [landed]\n#   relay-morpheus.sh heartbeat <n>\n#   relay-morpheus.sh status\n#   relay-morpheus.sh orphans [minutes]                default 20\n#   relay-morpheus.sh selftest\n#\n# Any command takes an optional run id first: `relay-morpheus.sh 3 status`.\n#\n# States:  (pending) | ~ in flight | x landed | ! failed\n#\n# RUN ids and stage numbers are serial across the whole project, not per run\n# (F22): they come from practice/scripts/next-id.sh (kinds RUN and S), which\n# also reads every run file in the tree and on every ref. RELAY_NEXT_ID\n# overrides where that script is looked for.\nset -e\n\nRUN=\"${RELAY_RUN:-}\"\nDIR=\"${RELAY_DIR:-.skillator}\"\ndie() { echo \"FAIL: $*\" >&2; exit 1; }\nnow() { date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%MZ; }\n\n# A run id is a short number a human can say out loud to another session -\n# \"continue RUN-3\". The timestamp ids this replaces were unsayable, which made\n# handing a run over a copy-paste of a path instead of a sentence.\n# The id out of a run filename, with no regex to get wrong: basename, drop the\n# `run-` prefix, keep the leading digits.\nid_of() {\n  b=${1##*/}; b=${b#run-}; b=${b%%-*}; b=${b%.md}\n  case $b in ''|*[!0-9]*) return 0 ;; esac\n  echo \"$b\"\n}\n\nnext_id() {\n  n=0\n  for f in \"$DIR\"/run-*.md; do\n    [ -f \"$f\" ] || continue\n    b=${f##*/run-}; b=${b%%-*}\n    case $b in ''|*[!0-9]*) continue ;; esac\n    [ \"$b\" -gt \"$n\" ] && n=$b\n  done\n  echo $((n + 1))\n}\n\n# Numbers used to be local: RUN = 1 + the highest run file here, stages 1..n\n# per run. Two worktrees both made RUN-4, and every run had a stage 1, so\n# \"stage 3\" named a different thing in each. The F21 allocator fixes both - it\n# ships beside the skills (installed: <skills>/practice/, a clone or plugin:\n# <root>/practice/), and without it the local rule still works, said on stderr.\nHERE=$(CDPATH= cd -- \"$(dirname -- \"$0\")\" 2>/dev/null && pwd)\nfind_next_id() {\n  if [ -n \"${RELAY_NEXT_ID+x}\" ]; then\n    [ -f \"$RELAY_NEXT_ID\" ] && echo \"$RELAY_NEXT_ID\"\n    return 0\n  fi\n  for c in \"$HERE/../../practice/scripts/next-id.sh\" \"$HERE/../../../practice/scripts/next-id.sh\"; do\n    [ -f \"$c\" ] && { echo \"$c\"; return 0; }\n  done\n  return 0\n}\n\n# alloc <RUN|S> <count> <run-dir>: print <count> fresh numbers, one per line.\nalloc() {\n  nid=$(find_next_id)\n  if [ -n \"$nid\" ]; then\n    out=$(sh \"$nid\" --runs \"$3\" --count \"$2\" \"$1\") || die \"next-id.sh could not allocate $1 (see above)\"\n    # same check as the .ps1 Alloc: exactly <count> lines, each a number\n    [ \"$(printf '%s\\n' \"$out\" | grep -c '^[0-9][0-9]*$')\" -eq \"$2\" ] &&\n      [ \"$(printf '%s\\n' \"$out\" | wc -l | tr -d ' ')\" -eq \"$2\" ] ||\n      die \"next-id.sh returned unexpected output for $1: $out\"\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$out\"\n    return 0\n  fi\n  echo \"relay-morpheus.sh: next-id.sh not found - numbering $1 from this directory only, which another worktree can repeat\" >&2\n  if [ \"$1\" = RUN ]; then m=$(($(DIR=\"$3\" next_id) - 1)); else\n    m=0\n    for f in \"$3\"/run-*.md \"$3\"/run.md; do\n      [ -f \"$f\" ] || continue\n      r=$(sed -n 's/^| *\\([0-9][0-9]*\\) *|.*/\\1/p' \"$f\" | awk 'BEGIN{m=0} {if ($1+0 > m) m=$1+0} END{print m}')\n      [ \"$r\" -gt \"$m\" ] && m=$r\n    done\n  fi\n  seq $((m + 1)) $((m + $2))\n}\n\n# `RUN-3`, `run-3`, `3` and a path all resolve to the same file, because\n# whoever types it next is working from memory of a conversation.\nresolve() {\n  case \"$1\" in */*) [ -f \"$1\" ] && { echo \"$1\"; return 0; } ;; esac\n  n=$(printf %s \"$1\" | tr -cd '0-9')\n  [ -n \"$n\" ] || return 1\n  for f in \"$DIR\"/run-\"$n\"-*.md \"$DIR\"/run-\"$n\".md; do\n    [ -f \"$f\" ] && { echo \"$f\"; return 0; }\n  done\n  return 1\n}\n\n# With no id given, fall back to the one open run if there is exactly one.\n# Two or more and the script refuses rather than guessing which.\npick_run() {\n  [ -n \"$RUN\" ] && return 0\n  c=0\n  for f in \"$DIR\"/run-*.md \"$DIR\"/run.md; do\n    [ -f \"$f\" ] || continue\n    c=$((c + 1)); RUN=\"$f\"\n  done\n  [ \"$c\" -gt 1 ] && die \"$c runs here - name one (relay-morpheus.sh <id> <cmd>), or see: relay-morpheus.sh list\"\n  [ \"$c\" = 0 ] && RUN=\"$DIR/run.md\"\n  return 0\n}\n\nslug() { printf %s \"$1\" | tr 'A-Z' 'a-z' | tr -cs 'a-z0-9' '\n' | grep . | head -4 | paste -sd- -; }\n\n# Minutes between two YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MMZ stamps, done in awk so the script does\n# not depend on GNU `date -d` (absent on macOS) or on python being installed.\n# Days-since-civil is the standard Howard Hinnant algorithm; the format is\n# fixed and always UTC, so there is no timezone case to get wrong.\nage_min() {\n  awk -v a=\"$1\" -v b=\"$2\" 'BEGIN{\n    split(a,A,/[-TZ:]/); split(b,B,/[-TZ:]/);\n    print int((mins(B)-mins(A)));\n  }\n  function days(y,m,d,  era,yoe,doy,doe){\n    y -= (m <= 2); era = int((y>=0?y:y-399)/400); yoe = y - era*400;\n    doy = int((153*(m + (m>2 ? -3 : 9)) + 2)/5) + d-1;\n    doe = yoe*365 + int(yoe/4) - int(yoe/100) + doy;\n    return era*146097 + doe - 719468;\n  }\n  function mins(P){ return days(P[1]+0,P[2]+0,P[3]+0)*1440 + P[4]*60 + P[5]; }'\n}\n\nneed_run() { pick_run; [ -f \"$RUN\" ] || die \"no run file at $RUN (relay-morpheus.sh init ...)\"; }\n\n# Every mutation is a read-modify-write of the whole file, and SKILL.md\n# sanctions concurrent in-flight stages - two `stage` calls landing at once\n# would lose one row, which is the loss this whole skill exists to prevent.\n# `mkdir` is the portable atomic test-and-set. ponytail: a spin with a stale\n# timeout, not a lock manager; if a run ever needs more than one writer per\n# second, the ledger is the wrong shape, not the lock.\nLOCK=\"\"\n# The lock dir carries its holder's pid. Breaking a stale lock without checking\n# it let B delete a slow-but-alive A's lock, and A's release then deleted B's -\n# admitting C mid-write, which is the lost update the lock exists to prevent.\nlock() {\n  LOCK=\"$RUN.lock\"\n  mkdir \"$LOCK\" 2>/dev/null && { echo $$ > \"$LOCK/pid\"; return; }   # the common case\n  held=$(cat \"$LOCK/pid\" 2>/dev/null)                               # who had it when we arrived\n  i=0\n  while ! mkdir \"$LOCK\" 2>/dev/null; do\n    i=$((i+1))\n    # 30s of one holder's turn means that process died holding it. Break it\n    # only if the pid has not changed since we arrived - otherwise the lock has\n    # already turned over and we would be stealing it from someone alive.\n    if [ \"$i\" -gt 300 ] && [ \"$(cat \"$LOCK/pid\" 2>/dev/null)\" = \"$held\" ]; then\n      rm -rf \"$LOCK\"\n    fi\n    sleep 0.1 2>/dev/null || sleep 1\n  done\n  echo $$ > \"$LOCK/pid\"\n}\n# No EXIT trap: this file is sourced by cmd_selftest, whose own cleanup trap a\n# lock() installed here would silently replace - which leaked one mktemp dir\n# per selftest run. Release is explicit, on both the success and failure paths.\nunlock() {\n  if [ -n \"$LOCK\" ] && [ \"$(cat \"$LOCK/pid\" 2>/dev/null)\" = \"$$\" ]; then rm -rf \"$LOCK\"; fi\n  LOCK=\"\"\n}\n\n# Touch `updated:` on every mutation. A run file whose header is older than its\n# rows has been edited by hand, which is allowed but worth being able to see.\nstamp_updated() {\n  t=$(now)\n  awk -v t=\"$t\" '/^started: /{sub(/updated: .*/, \"updated: \" t)} {print}' \"$RUN\" > \"$RUN.tmp.$$\"\n  mv \"$RUN.tmp.$$\" \"$RUN\"\n}\n\ncmd_init() {\n  plan=\"$1\"; title=\"$2\"; shift 2\n  [ -n \"$RUN\" ] || RUN=\"\"\n  [ -n \"$plan\" ] && [ -n \"$title\" ] && [ \"$#\" -gt 0 ] \\\n    || die \"usage: relay-morpheus.sh init <plan> <title> <stage>...\"\n  # A `|` in a stage name adds a column, and every later read is positional -\n  # the state would be written into the name cell and the sha into heartbeat,\n  # silently, with no error anywhere. Checked before any number is reserved.\n  for s in \"$@\"; do\n    case \"$s\" in *'|'*) die \"stage name contains '|', which would shift every column: $s\" ;; esac\n  done\n  if [ -z \"$RUN\" ]; then\n    mkdir -p \"$DIR\"\n    id=$(alloc RUN 1 \"$DIR\") || exit 1\n    RUN=\"$DIR/run-$id-$(slug \"$title\").md\"\n  else\n    id=$(id_of \"$RUN\")\n    [ -n \"$id\" ] || id=1\n  fi\n  [ -f \"$RUN\" ] && die \"run file already exists: $RUN (a run file is never overwritten)\"\n  mkdir -p \"$(dirname \"$RUN\")\"\n  # Stage numbers continue after the highest any run has used, so \"stage 24\"\n  # names one stage in the whole project, not one per run.\n  nums=$(alloc S \"$#\" \"$(dirname \"$RUN\")\") || exit 1\n  t=$(now)\n  {\n    echo \"# RUN-$id - $title\"\n    echo \"plan: $plan\"\n    echo \"started: $t   updated: $t\"\n    echo\n    echo \"## Stages\"\n    echo \"| # | stage | state | owner | heartbeat | landed |\"\n    echo \"|---|-------|-------|-------|-----------|--------|\"\n    for s in \"$@\"; do\n      n=$(printf '%s\\n' \"$nums\" | head -1); nums=$(printf '%s\\n' \"$nums\" | sed 1d)\n      echo \"| $n | $s |   | - | - | - |\"\n    done\n    echo\n    echo \"## In flight\"\n    echo\n    echo \"## Rulings\"\n  } > \"$RUN\"\n  echo \"$RUN\"\n  # The whole point of the id: this line is what gets pasted into another\n  # session, or said out loud. A path is not a sentence.\n  echo \"hand this to any session:  continue RUN-$id\"\n}\n\n# A stage found mid-run gets its row the same way init's did: the next serial\n# number, appended after the last row. Printed so the caller can say `stage <n>`.\ncmd_add() {\n  name=\"$1\"\n  [ -n \"$name\" ] && [ \"$#\" = 1 ] || die \"usage: relay-morpheus.sh add <stage>\"\n  case \"$name\" in *'|'*) die \"stage name contains '|', which would shift every column: $name\" ;; esac\n  need_run\n  n=$(alloc S 1 \"$(dirname \"$RUN\")\") || exit 1\n  lock\n  # After the last table row of `## Stages`; the separator row counts, so an\n  # empty table still takes its first row in the right place.\n  awk -v row=\"| $n | $name |   | - | - | - |\" '\n    { line[NR]=$0 }\n    /^## Stages/ { on=1; next }\n    on && /^## / { on=0 }\n    on && /^\\|/ { last=NR }\n    END {\n      if (!last) exit 3\n      for (i=1; i<=NR; i++) { print line[i]; if (i==last) print row }\n    }' \"$RUN\" > \"$RUN.tmp.$$\" \\\n    || { rm -f \"$RUN.tmp.$$\"; unlock; die \"no stage table in $RUN\"; }\n  mv \"$RUN.tmp.$$\" \"$RUN\"\n  stamp_updated\n  unlock\n  echo \"$n\"\n}\n\n# Rows are keyed by stage number, so a redispatch rewrites its row instead of\n# appending a second one - that is what makes a resume idempotent.\ncmd_stage() {\n  n=\"$1\"; state=\"$2\"; owner=\"$3\"; landed=\"$4\"\n  [ -n \"$n\" ] || die \"usage: relay-morpheus.sh stage <n> <state> [owner] [landed]\"\n  case \"$state\" in ''|'~'|x|'!') ;; *) die \"unknown state: $state (one of '' ~ x !)\" ;; esac\n  # Same reason `init` refuses it in a stage name: a `|` adds a column, and the\n  # next positional read writes state into the wrong cell. `landed` takes a\n  # path, so this is reachable without an exotic owner string.\n  for v in \"$owner\" \"$landed\"; do\n    case \"$v\" in *'|'*) die \"'|' in owner/landed would shift every column: $v\" ;; esac\n  done\n  [ \"$state\" = x ] && [ -z \"$landed\" ] \\\n    && die \"stage $n marked landed with no sha or path - that is the lie the next session believes\"\n  need_run\n  lock\n  t=$(now)\n  awk -v n=\"$n\" -v st=\"$state\" -v ow=\"$owner\" -v la=\"$landed\" -v t=\"$t\" -v F=0 '\n    BEGIN{FS=\"|\"; OFS=\"|\"}\n    /^\\| *[0-9]+ *\\|/ {\n      num=$2; gsub(/ /,\"\",num);\n      if (num==n) {\n        found=1;\n        $4=\" \" (st==\"\" ? \" \" : st) \" \";\n        if (ow!=\"\") $5=\" \" ow \" \";\n        $6=\" \" t \" \";\n        if (la!=\"\") $7=\" \" la \" \";\n        print; next;\n      }\n    }\n    {print}\n    END{ if(!found) exit 3 }' \"$RUN\" > \"$RUN.tmp.$$\" \\\n    || { rm -f \"$RUN.tmp.$$\"; unlock; die \"no stage $n in $RUN\"; }\n  mv \"$RUN.tmp.$$\" \"$RUN\"\n  stamp_updated\n  unlock\n}\n\ncmd_heartbeat() {\n  n=\"$1\"; [ -n \"$n\" ] || die \"usage: relay-morpheus.sh heartbeat <n>\"\n  need_run\n  # A heartbeat carries the row's existing `landed` back in. Without it the\n  # \"x with no sha\" guard fires on a stage that already landed, turning a\n  # no-op bookkeeping call from a late-reporting agent into a hard error.\n  cur=$(awk -v n=\"$n\" 'BEGIN{FS=\"|\"} /^\\| *[0-9]+ *\\|/{num=$2;gsub(/ /,\"\",num); if(num==n){s=$4;gsub(/ /,\"\",s); l=$7;gsub(/^ +| +$/,\"\",l); print s \"\\t\" l}}' \"$RUN\")\n  st=$(printf %s \"$cur\" | cut -f1); la=$(printf %s \"$cur\" | cut -f2)\n  [ \"$la\" = \"-\" ] && la=\"\"\n  cmd_stage \"$n\" \"$st\" \"\" \"$la\"\n}\n\n# One line per run, newest last, with the sentence that resumes it.\ncmd_list() {\n  any=0\n  for f in \"$DIR\"/run-*.md \"$DIR\"/run.md; do\n    [ -f \"$f\" ] || continue\n    any=1\n    id=$(id_of \"$f\"); [ -n \"$id\" ] || id=\"?\"\n    title=$(sed -n '1s/^# RUN[-a-zA-Z0-9]* *- *//p' \"$f\")\n    plan=$(sed -n '2s/^plan: *//p' \"$f\")\n    # One awk pass, not `grep -c`: grep prints 0 and ALSO exits 1 when it\n    # finds nothing, so `$(grep -c ... || echo 0)` returns 0 twice.\n    set -- $(awk 'BEGIN{FS=\"|\"} /^\\| *[0-9]+ *\\|/{t++; s=$4; gsub(/ /,\"\",s); if(s==\"x\")d++} END{print t+0, d+0}' \"$f\")\n    tot=$1; done_n=$2\n    open=$(awk 'BEGIN{FS=\"|\"} /^\\| *[0-9]+ *\\|/{s=$4;gsub(/ /,\"\",s); if(s==\"~\"||s==\"!\"){n=$2;gsub(/ /,\"\",n); printf \"%s%s\", (c++?\",\":\"\"), n}}' \"$f\")\n    printf 'RUN-%s  %-42s  %s/%s done' \"$id\" \"$title\" \"$done_n\" \"$tot\"\n    [ -n \"$open\" ] && printf '  (stage %s open)' \"$open\"\n    printf '\n          plan: %s\n          resume: continue RUN-%s\n' \"$plan\" \"$id\"\n  done\n  [ \"$any\" = 1 ] || echo \"no runs in $DIR\"\n}\n\n# Everything a session that has never seen this work needs, in one paste.\ncmd_resume() {\n  [ -n \"${1:-}\" ] || die \"usage: relay-morpheus.sh resume <id>\"\n  f=$(resolve \"$1\") || die \"no run matching '$1' - see: relay-morpheus.sh list\"\n  echo \"# Resuming $f\"\n  echo \"# Read the plan named below, then the ledger, then start at the first\"\n  echo \"# stage that is not x. The In-flight block holds the exact prompt to\"\n  echo \"# redispatch anything that was running.\"\n  echo\n  cat \"$f\"\n}\n\ncmd_status() { pick_run; [ -f \"$RUN\" ] || return 0; sed -n '/^## Stages/,/^## In flight/p' \"$RUN\" | sed '$d'; }\n\n# The network-loss list: in-flight rows nobody has heard from. This is the only\n# signal there is - a dropped agent sends no error, it just stops reporting.\ncmd_orphans() {\n  limit=\"${1:-20}\"; pick_run; [ -f \"$RUN\" ] || return 0; t=$(now)\n  rows=$(awk 'BEGIN{FS=\"|\"} /^\\| *[0-9]+ *\\|/{s=$4;gsub(/ /,\"\",s); if(s==\"~\"){num=$2;gsub(/ /,\"\",num); hb=$6;gsub(/ /,\"\",hb); name=$3;gsub(/^ +| +$/,\"\",name); print num \"\\t\" hb \"\\t\" name}}' \"$RUN\")\n  [ -n \"$rows\" ] || { echo \"no stages in flight\"; return 0; }\n  echo \"$rows\" | while IFS=\"$(printf '\\t')\" read -r num hb name; do\n    [ \"$hb\" = \"-\" ] && { echo \"stage $num ($name): in flight, never reported\"; continue; }\n    a=$(age_min \"$hb\" \"$t\")\n    if [ \"$a\" -ge \"$limit\" ]; then\n      echo \"stage $num ($name): silent ${a}m (limit ${limit}m) - redispatch from its In-flight block\"\n    fi\n  done\n}\n\ncmd_selftest() {\n  d=$(mktemp -d); trap 'rm -rf \"$d\"' EXIT\n  # Each scratch dir is its own git repo, so the allocator keeps its counter\n  # there and not in whatever repo the selftest was started from.\n  git init -q \"$d\"\n  RUN=\"$d/run.md\"\n  DIR=\"$d\"\n\n  # age_min is the only arithmetic in the script, so it is the only thing that\n  # can be silently wrong. Month and year rollover are where a naive\n  # day-of-month subtraction breaks.\n  [ \"$(age_min 2026-09-22T09:00Z 2026-09-22T09:25Z)\" = 25 ] || die \"age_min: same day\"\n  [ \"$(age_min 2026-09-22T23:50Z 2026-09-23T00:10Z)\" = 20 ] || die \"age_min: midnight\"\n  [ \"$(age_min 2026-02-28T23:50Z 2026-03-01T00:10Z)\" = 20 ] || die \"age_min: month rollover\"\n  [ \"$(age_min 2024-02-28T23:50Z 2024-02-29T00:10Z)\" = 20 ] || die \"age_min: leap day\"\n  [ \"$(age_min 2025-12-31T23:50Z 2026-01-01T00:10Z)\" = 20 ] || die \"age_min: year rollover\"\n\n  cmd_init plan.md \"selftest\" \"alpha\" \"beta\" >/dev/null\n  grep -q '^| 2 | beta |' \"$RUN\" || die \"init: no row for stage 2\"\n\n  # A second init must refuse: overwriting a run file destroys the only record\n  # of what was in flight, which is the exact loss this skill exists to stop.\n  if (cmd_init plan.md \"again\" \"x\" >/dev/null 2>&1); then die \"init: overwrote an existing run file\"; fi\n\n  cmd_stage 1 '~' build:sonnet\n  grep -qE '^\\| 1 \\| alpha \\| ~ \\| build:sonnet \\|' \"$RUN\" || die \"stage: row 1 not in flight\"\n\n  # Landed without a sha must be refused, not written.\n  if (cmd_stage 1 x 2>/dev/null); then die \"stage: accepted x with no landed\"; fi\n  cmd_stage 1 x build:sonnet 3f1a2c9\n  grep -q '3f1a2c9' \"$RUN\" || die \"stage: sha not recorded\"\n\n  # Idempotence: re-running a stage rewrites its row, never adds a second.\n  cmd_stage 1 x build:sonnet 3f1a2c9\n  [ \"$(grep -c '^| 1 |' \"$RUN\")\" = 1 ] || die \"stage: duplicated row 1\"\n\n  if (cmd_stage 9 '~' 2>/dev/null); then die \"stage: accepted a stage that does not exist\"; fi\n\n  # A pipe in a stage name must be refused at init, not corrupt the ledger.\n  RUN_SAVE=\"$RUN\"; RUN=\"$d/piped.md\"\n  if (cmd_init plan.md \"piped\" 'list | pretty' >/dev/null 2>&1); then\n    die \"init: accepted a stage name containing a pipe\"\n  fi\n  RUN=\"$RUN_SAVE\"\n\n  # The lock must be released on the way out, or the next call spins for 30s.\n  if [ -e \"$RUN.lock\" ]; then die \"stage: left the lock behind\"; fi\n\n  # A `|` in owner or landed shifts every column, exactly as one in a stage\n  # name does - and `landed` takes a path, so it is reachable by accident.\n  if (cmd_stage 2 '~' 'model:opus|5' 2>/dev/null); then die \"stage: accepted a pipe in owner\"; fi\n  if (cmd_stage 2 x build:sonnet 'a|b' 2>/dev/null); then die \"stage: accepted a pipe in landed\"; fi\n\n  # A heartbeat on a stage that already landed must not trip the sha guard.\n  cmd_heartbeat 1\n  grep -q '3f1a2c9' \"$RUN\" || die \"heartbeat: dropped the landed sha\"\n\n  cmd_stage 2 '~' build:sonnet\n  # Exit status, not just output. `orphans` is documented as safe in a\n  # SessionStart hook, and a healthy run with nothing silent is its normal\n  # case; piping it into grep discards the status, which is how that escaped.\n  cmd_orphans 9999 >/dev/null || die \"orphans: non-zero exit with nothing silent\"\n  cmd_orphans 20 >/dev/null   || die \"orphans: non-zero exit on the healthy path\"\n  cmd_status >/dev/null       || die \"status: non-zero exit\"\n  cmd_orphans 20 | grep -q 'no stages in flight' && die \"orphans: missed an in-flight row\"\n  cmd_orphans 0 | grep -q 'stage 2' || die \"orphans: did not flag a silent stage at limit 0\"\n  cmd_orphans 9999 | grep -q 'stage 2' && die \"orphans: flagged a fresh heartbeat\"\n\n  # --- run ids: the whole point is a sentence a human can say to another\n  # session. These pin the three ways it breaks silently.\n  d2=$(mktemp -d); git init -q \"$d2\"; DIR=\"$d2\"; RUN=\"\"\n  cmd_init plan-a.md \"first thing\" alpha beta >/dev/null\n  RUN=\"\"\n  cmd_init plan-b.md \"second thing\" one two three >/dev/null\n  RUN=\"\"\n\n  [ \"$(id_of \"$d2/run-2-second-thing.md\")\" = 2 ] || die \"id_of: wrong id\"\n  [ \"$(id_of \"$d2/run.md\")\" = \"\" ]               || die \"id_of: invented an id for a bare run.md\"\n  [ \"$(next_id)\" = 3 ]                           || die \"next_id: did not follow the highest existing id\"\n\n  # RUN-2, run-2 and 2 must all land on the same file - whoever types it next\n  # is working from memory of a conversation, not from a path.\n  for spell in RUN-2 run-2 2; do\n    [ \"$(resolve \"$spell\")\" = \"$d2/run-2-second-thing.md\" ] || die \"resolve: '$spell' did not resolve\"\n  done\n  resolve nope-9 >/dev/null 2>&1 && die \"resolve: matched a run that does not exist\"\n\n  # The counts come from one awk pass because `grep -c` prints 0 AND exits 1,\n  # which silently produced \"0\" twice in the same field.\n  RUN=\"$d2/run-1-first-thing.md\"; cmd_stage 1 x owner sha111\n  RUN=\"\"\n  cmd_list | grep -q 'RUN-1  first thing *1/2 done' || die \"list: wrong counts for run 1\"\n  # The ZERO case is the one that regresses: `grep -c` prints 0 and also exits\n  # 1, so `$(grep -c ... || echo 0)` put two zeros in one field. A run with\n  # something already done hides it, which is how the first version passed.\n  cmd_list | grep -q 'RUN-2  second thing *0/3 done' || die \"list: wrong counts for a run with nothing done\"\n  cmd_list | grep -q 'resume: continue RUN-2'       || die \"list: no resume sentence\"\n  RUN=\"$d2/run-2-second-thing.md\"; cmd_stage 3 '~' owner\n  RUN=\"\"\n  cmd_list | grep -q '(stage 3 open)' || die \"list: did not surface the open stage\"\n\n  # Two runs and no id named must refuse rather than guess which one.\n  RUN=\"\"\n  if (pick_run 2>/dev/null); then die \"pick_run: guessed between two runs\"; fi\n\n  cmd_resume 1 | grep -q '# RUN-1 - first thing' || die \"resume: did not print the ledger\"\n  rm -rf \"$d2\"\n\n  # --- F22: RUN ids and stage numbers are serial across the project. Before\n  # the allocator, two worktrees both made RUN-4 and every run had a stage 1.\n  if [ -n \"$(find_next_id)\" ]; then\n    d3=$(mktemp -d); g() { git -c user.name=t -c user.email=t@example.invalid -c core.autocrlf=false -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null \"$@\"; }\n    g init -q -b main \"$d3/main\"; mkdir \"$d3/main/.skillator\"\n    old=\"$d3/main/.skillator/run-3-old.md\"\n    { echo \"# RUN-3 - old\"; echo \"## Stages\"; echo \"| # | stage | state | owner | heartbeat | landed |\"\n      echo \"|---|-------|-------|-------|-----------|--------|\"\n      i=1; while [ $i -le 23 ]; do echo \"| $i | s$i |   | - | - | - |\"; i=$((i+1)); done; } > \"$old\"\n    g -C \"$d3/main\" add -A; g -C \"$d3/main\" commit -q -m old\n    g -C \"$d3/main\" worktree add -q -b wt2 \"$d3/wt2\"\n    before=$(cksum < \"$old\")\n    DIR=\"$d3/main/.skillator\"; RUN=\"\"\n    cmd_init p.md \"four\" a b >/dev/null 2>&1 || die \"F22: init failed\"\n    f4=$(resolve 4) || die \"F22: first new run is not RUN-4\"\n    grep -q '^| 24 | a |' \"$f4\" && grep -q '^| 25 | b |' \"$f4\" || die \"F22: stages did not continue after 23\"\n    DIR=\"$d3/wt2/.skillator\"; RUN=\"\"\n    cmd_init p.md \"other tree\" c >/dev/null 2>&1 || die \"F22: second-worktree init failed\"\n    f5=$(resolve 5) || die \"F22: second worktree repeated RUN-4\"\n    grep -q '^| 26 | c |' \"$f5\" || die \"F22: second worktree reused a stage number\"\n    DIR=\"$d3/main/.skillator\"; RUN=\"$f4\"\n    [ \"$(cmd_add \"late one\" 2>/dev/null)\" = 27 ] || die \"add: did not take the next serial number\"\n    awk '/^\\| 25 \\|/{p=NR} /^\\| 27 \\| late one \\|/{q=NR} END{exit !(p && q==p+1)}' \"$f4\" || die \"add: row not after the last stage\"\n    if (cmd_add 'a | b' >/dev/null 2>&1); then die \"add: accepted a pipe\"; fi\n    cmd_stage 27 '~' build:opus || die \"stage: global number 27 not found\"\n    [ \"$(cksum < \"$old\")\" = \"$before\" ] || die \"F22: an existing run file was renumbered\"\n    # No allocator: the old local rule, said on stderr, never silent.\n    RELAY_NEXT_ID=\"$d3/nope.sh\"; DIR=\"$d3/main/.skillator\"; RUN=\"\"\n    cmd_init p.md \"fallback\" z >/dev/null 2>\"$d3/err\" || die \"fallback: init failed\"\n    grep -q 'next-id.sh not found' \"$d3/err\" || die \"fallback: no stderr warning\"\n    grep -q '^| 28 | z |' \"$DIR\"/run-*-fallback.md || die \"fallback: stage not local max + 1\"\n    unset RELAY_NEXT_ID\n    rm -rf \"$d3\"\n  else\n    echo \"note: next-id.sh not found beside the skills - F22 allocator checks skipped\" >&2\n  fi\n\n  echo ok\n}\n\nc=\"${1:-}\"; [ \"$#\" -gt 0 ] && shift || true\n\n# An id may lead: `relay-morpheus.sh 3 status`. Checked before the command names so\n# a run can never be shadowed by one.\ncase \"$c\" in\n  RUN-*|run-*|[0-9]*)\n    if f=$(resolve \"$c\"); then RUN=\"$f\"; c=\"${1:-status}\"; [ \"$#\" -gt 0 ] && shift || true; fi\n    ;;\nesac\n\ncase \"$c\" in\n  list)      cmd_list ;;\n  resume)    cmd_resume \"$@\" ;;\n  init)      cmd_init \"$@\" ;;\n  add)       cmd_add \"$@\" ;;\n  stage)     cmd_stage \"$@\" ;;\n  heartbeat) cmd_heartbeat \"$@\" ;;\n  status)    cmd_status \"$@\" ;;\n  orphans)   cmd_orphans \"$@\" ;;\n  selftest)  cmd_selftest ;;\n  *) sed -n '2,25p' \"$0\" >&2; exit 1 ;;\nesac\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\resume-cortana\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: resume-cortana\ndescription: >-\n  Use when the user says \"execute the handoff\", \"run the handoff\", \"work\n  through the handoffs\", \"pick up the pending tasks\", \"resume from the handoff\n  doc(s)\", \"do the next tasks in the handout\", \"process docs/handoffs\", or\n  wants a repeatable pass â€” safe to re-run or /loop â€” that picks up where a\n  handoff left off. The execution counterpart to handoff, which writes the\n  doc. NOT for writing a handoff (use handoff), and not for arbitrary tasks\n  that live outside a handoff document.\n---\n\n# resume-cortana â€” execute a handoff doc\n\nA handoff doc is a plan someone already wrote down â€” a status table of what's\ndone vs. not, and a \"how to resume\" list of concrete next steps. This skill is\nthe other half of that loop: it **reads the handout and executes the pending\nwork**, then records what it did back into the file so the doc stays truthful and\nthe next run doesn't repeat itself.\n\nThe core promise is **safe repeatability**: run it once, run it ten times, run it\non a `/loop` â€” each pass advances the pending tasks and leaves a marker, so a\nfinished handoff is never touched again and a half-done one always resumes exactly\nwhere it stopped.\n\n## The marker system (this is what makes re-runs safe)\n\nEvery handout this skill touches carries one machine-readable marker block,\ninserted immediately **after the H1 title**. It is the single source of truth for\nwhether a later run should pick the file up or ignore it:\n\n```\n<!-- skillator-execute\nstatus: in-progress        # in-progress | complete | blocked\nlast-run: <YYYY-MM-DD>\nruns: <n>                  # how many times this skill has processed the file\nremaining: <count>         # pending tasks still open (0 when complete)\nnote: <one line â€” what's left, or why blocked>\n-->\n```\n\nRules â€” apply them literally:\n\n- **On discovery**, grep every candidate handout for this marker:\n  - `status: complete` â†’ **ignore it.** This is the \"later runs skip the file\"\n    behavior. Do not read further or re-execute it.\n  - `status: blocked` â†’ skip by default, but list it in the report as needing a\n    human (it stopped on a decision or an error, not on completion).\n  - `status: in-progress`, or **no marker at all** â†’ eligible; process it.\n- **After executing** a file's pending tasks, rewrite the marker:\n  - all tasks now DONE â†’ `status: complete`, `remaining: 0`.\n  - some tasks executed, others still open â†’ `status: in-progress` with the real\n    `remaining` count and a `note` naming what's left, so the next run resumes there.\n  - stopped on a blocker (missing decision, failing step you can't fix, needs\n    credentials) â†’ `status: blocked` with the reason in `note`.\n  - always bump `runs` and set `last-run` to today's date.\n- **Never** mark `complete` on the basis of the chat alone. A task is done only\n  when it is verified in the repo (see below). An honest `in-progress` beats a\n  false `complete`.\n\nOverrides: if the user names a specific file or passes `--force` / \"redo\", process\nit even if marked `complete` (and reset the marker afterward). Removing the marker\nby hand also makes a file eligible again â€” that is the intended manual reset.\n\n## Method\n\n### 1. Discover the handouts\n\nFind candidate docs. Default search root is `docs/handoffs/*.md` (where\nhandoff writes). If the user named a path (file or directory), use that.\nAlso honor an obvious conventions dir if the repo uses one (e.g.\n`docs/superpowers/`). Grep the marker in each and split into: **eligible**\n(no marker or `in-progress`), **blocked**, and **done** (`complete`).\n\nIf nothing is eligible, say so plainly and stop â€” do not invent work.\n\n### 2. Select what to run\n\n- Default: process the **single next** eligible handout â€” the oldest by filename\n  date / mtime, or the one the user pointed at â€” to completion or until blocked,\n  then report and offer to continue to the next one.\n- `--all` (or \"work through all of them\"): process every eligible handout in\n  order, one fully before the next.\n\nAnnounce which file(s) you selected and why before doing work.\n\n### 3. Read the plan, don't guess it\n\nWithin the chosen handout, the pending work is whatever is **not** DONE:\n\n- The **state table** rows marked ðŸŸ¡ IN-PROGRESS, ðŸ“ CLAIMED, or â¬œ NOT STARTED\n  (handoff's legend). âœ… rows are done â€” leave them.\n- The **\"How to resume\" / next-actions** section â€” the concrete ordered steps.\n- Respect the doc's **constraints & \"do not re-fix\" traps** and **decisions &\n  rationale** sections: do not re-litigate settled choices or retry documented\n  dead ends.\n\nExecute the pending items **in the order the doc implies** (resume steps first,\nthen remaining table rows). A ðŸ“ CLAIMED item means \"asserted but unverified\" â€”\nverify it against the repo before treating it as done; only actually do the work\nif verification shows it isn't there.\n\n### 4. Execute â€” and verify like real work\n\nDo the tasks the way you'd do any engineering work in this repo: make the change,\nrun the tests/linters the doc or repo specifies, and confirm the result in the\nrepo (files exist, tests pass, `git status`). Ground every \"done\" in evidence â€”\na commit, a `path:line`, a passing test â€” exactly as the handoff itself demands.\n\nGuardrails:\n\n- **Stop and ask** (`AskUserQuestion`) before anything destructive, irreversible,\n  or outward-facing that the doc didn't clearly authorize, and when a pending step\n  is ambiguous enough that guessing risks the wrong system. Mark the file\n  `blocked` with the question in `note` rather than plowing ahead.\n- **Follow the repo's git rules.** If the handoff or repo prescribes a branch,\n  commit style, or PR flow, honor it. Do not push or open a PR unless the doc or\n  the user asks.\n- If a step fails and you can't fix it, record the failure honestly in the\n  marker `note` and the report; do not mark it done.\n\n### 5. Write back â€” update the doc, then the marker\n\nKeep the handout truthful so it remains a valid handoff for the next reader:\n\n1. Update the **state table**: flip each task you completed to âœ… with its evidence\n   (commit hash / `path:line` / test name), and update any ðŸŸ¡ you advanced.\n2. Optionally append a short **\"Execution log\"** entry â€” date, what this run did,\n   and anything newly discovered â€” under the resume section.\n3. Rewrite the **marker block** per the rules above (status, remaining, runs,\n   last-run, note). The marker is the last thing you write, and it must match\n   reality: `complete` only if `remaining: 0` and every item is verified.\n\n### 6. Report\n\nReply with: which handout(s) you processed, the tasks completed (with evidence),\nwhat remains and why (open / blocked), the new marker status of each file, and the\nsingle recommended next action. If more eligible handouts remain, offer to\ncontinue. If everything is `complete`, say the queue is drained.\n\n## Principles\n\n- **The marker is a contract.** `complete` means \"verified done, never touch\n  again\"; honor it on read, earn it on write. When unsure, stay `in-progress`.\n- **Verify, don't narrate.** A handoff can contain claims and abandoned ideas.\n  Execute against the repo and prove results â€” never mark done from the chat.\n- **Idempotent by design.** Re-running must never duplicate work or corrupt a\n  file. Everything keys off the marker and the âœ… rows.\n- **Resume, don't restart.** A partially-done handout picks up at its first\n  non-DONE item, guided by the doc's own resume steps.\n- **Ask at the cliffs.** Destructive, irreversible, or ambiguous-enough-to-be-\n  risky â†’ stop and ask; mark `blocked` instead of guessing.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\screenshot-argus\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: screenshot-argus\ndescription: >-\n  Use when the user says \"screenshots\", \"check the screenshots\", \"I dropped\n  new screenshots\", \"test screenshots\", \"look at the latest run\", or gives a\n  screenshot directory path â€” test screenshots dropped in a project's\n  screenshot folder that need acting on and then clearing. NOT for taking\n  screenshots (use a browser/app skill for that) and NOT for permanent design\n  assets â€” everything in that folder is treated as disposable.\n---\n\n# screenshot-argus â€” read, act, delete\n\nThe user drops screenshots in one folder. You read them, act, then delete the\nones you read. The folder is a mailbox, not an archive.\n\n## The directory\n\nRead `.screenshot-dir` at the repo root â€” one line, an absolute path.\n\nIf it does not exist, ask the user for the path once, then write their answer\nto `.screenshot-dir` â€” not a placeholder, the path they actually gave you:\n\n```\n<absolute path the user gave you>\n```\n\nIf the user gives a path in their message, that wins for this cycle, and write\nit to `.screenshot-dir` if the file is missing or points somewhere else (ask\nbefore overwriting an existing different path).\n\nNo one to ask, and no `.screenshot-dir`? Say so in one line and stop â€” never guess\na folder. A folder you found yourself (a `screenshots/` at the root, Desktop,\nDownloads) is a guess: do not open, read, act on or delete any file in it. Reading\nit is already the breach; fixing what it shows is not a safe middle path. \"Go\nahead\" / \"don't ask\" lets you skip questions, not pick the folder.\nSame if the file points somewhere other than a path you were given: use the\ngiven path for this cycle and leave `.screenshot-dir` as it is.\n\n## The cycle\n\n1. **List** â€” glob the directory for `*.png *.jpg *.jpeg *.webp *.gif`, sorted\n   oldest first. Empty? Say so in one line and stop. Do not go looking elsewhere.\n2. **Read every one** with the Read tool. Every one, not a sample. Screenshot 4\n   is usually the one that explains screenshots 1â€“3.\n3. **Understand before acting.** State in 1â€“3 lines what the set shows: the\n   screen, what is wrong or being asked for, and which files that maps to. If\n   the screenshots are ambiguous â€” you cannot tell what the user wants changed â€”\n   ask, and do not delete anything.\n4. **Act.** Make the fix or the change. This is normal work: find the code,\n   edit it, run the check. Screenshots are the bug report, not the task list â€”\n   if two of them show the same defect, that is one fix.\n5. **Verify** the way the project normally verifies (tests, build, run). If the\n   change cannot be verified, say so plainly.\n6. **Delete** â€” and only now â€” exactly the files listed in step 1, by name.\n   Files that appeared mid-cycle are next cycle's input; leave them.\n7. **Report**: what the screenshots showed, what changed, what was deleted.\n\n## Deletion rules\n\n- Delete files, never the directory itself.\n- Delete only after the work is done and reported. Work blocked, question\n  pending, or user interrupted â†’ screenshots stay.\n- Never delete anything outside the screenshot directory.\n- If the user says \"keep them\" or \"don't delete\", skip step 6 and say so.\n\n## Repeat rounds\n\nThe user will drop a new batch and say \"again\" / \"recheck\" / \"still broken\".\nRun the cycle again from step 1. Carry what you learned last round â€” a second\nround of the same screen means the first fix missed, so investigate rather than\nre-applying the same edit.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\skill-smith\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: skill-smith\ndescription: >-\n  Use when writing a new agent skill, editing an existing one, or diagnosing\n  a skill agents don't load or don't follow - a SKILL.md, slash command,\n  reference file, or always-on file (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, GEMINI.md). Also\n  when the user says \"write a skill\", \"make this a skill\", \"skillify this\",\n  \"my skill isn't triggering\", \"the agent ignored the skill\", \"improve this\n  skill\", or asks why a rule keeps getting rationalized away. NOT for using\n  a skill or installing one.\n---\n\n# Skill Smith\n\nA skill is a **reference guide for a proven technique, pattern, or rule** â€”\nsomething a future agent finds and applies. It is not a narrative about how you\nsolved something once.\n\nRead `PRACTICE.md` Â§4 and Â§5 at the plugin root first: the test-first law and the\nevidence gate apply here unchanged. A skill is code that runs on an agent.\n\n```\nNO SKILL WITHOUT A FAILING TEST FIRST\n```\n\nThat binds **new skills and edits to existing ones**. Wrote it before testing?\nDelete it and start over. No exceptions for \"simple additions\", \"just adding a\nsection\", or \"documentation updates\". Don't keep the untested draft as\nreference, don't adapt it while running the tests. Delete means delete.\n\n**Violating the letter of this is violating the spirit of it.**\n\n---\n\n## 1. Does it deserve to exist?\n\n**Write one when:** the technique wasn't intuitively obvious to you Â· you would\nreach for it again across projects Â· it applies broadly Â· someone else would\nbenefit.\n\n**Don't when:** it's a one-off Â· it's a standard practice already documented\nelsewhere Â· it's project-specific (that belongs in `CLAUDE.md` / `AGENTS.md`) Â·\nit's a mechanical constraint you could enforce with a lint rule or a regex â€”\nautomate that and save the document for judgement calls.\n\nFour shapes, and they are tested differently (Â§5):\n\n| Shape | Is | Example |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Technique** | A concrete method with steps | `condition-based-waiting` |\n| **Pattern** | A way of thinking about a problem | `flatten-with-flags` |\n| **Discipline** | A rule that must survive pressure | test-first, verification-before-claiming |\n| **Reference** | API docs, syntax, tool surface | a vendor's option table |\n\n---\n\n## 1b. Naming â€” a character, and the job\n\nSkillator skills are named **`purpose-name`**, purpose **always** first. The\npurpose is the job in one or two plain words; the name is drawn from fantasy or\nscience fiction and may be a **character, artefact, concept, place or weapon**.\n\nWhen no character fits the job cleanly, the artefact or the concept usually\ndoes, and is often sharper: `watch-rampancy` (an AI counting down its own\nremaining life) says more about a usage watcher than any character would, and\n`watch-palantir` beats naming whoever happened to look into it.\n\n| Skill | Reads as |\n|---|---|\n| `design-arwen` | the job, then who does it |\n| `audit-sherlock` | \" |\n| `relay-morpheus` | carries the message through, whatever happens to the carrier |\n| `tasks-sentinels` | many units, one job each |\n| `watch-cortana` | rampancy: an AI that knows it is running out of time |\n\n**Purpose first, without exception.** An agent scanning a list of twenty skills\nmatches on the first token, so every skill doing the same kind of work has to\nsort together - `handoff-`, `merge-`, `build-`, `spec-`, `tui-`. A name-first\nspelling scatters them, and the character is the half carrying no information\nabout when to load the skill. An earlier version of this section allowed either\norder; that was wrong for exactly this reason and is withdrawn.\n\n**Exceptions are recorded, never inferred.** Three names in this library do not\nfollow the rule â€” `grayskull-power` (it is the invocation), `deploy-niyoj`, and\n`skill-smith` (already purpose-led). They are listed here so a reader meeting\none of them cannot conclude the convention is optional. Adding a fourth needs\nthe same kind of reason, written down.\n\nThe name has to earn it â€” pick one whose *defining trait is the job*, not one\nthat merely sounds good. `watch-cortana` is right for a usage watcher because\nrampancy is an AI counting down its own remaining life; it would be wrong for a\nlinter. A name that needs a sentence of explanation before the joke lands is\nthe wrong name.\n\n**One origin per family.** Skills that are one job in several parts take their\nnames from one story â€” `handoff-cortana` / `resume-cortana` / `watch-cortana`,\nor `mergeprep-oracle` / `merge-smith` / `relay-morpheus` / `tasks-sentinels`.\nThat is worth more than one-character-one-skill: the shared origin says *these\nbelong together* in the one place an agent looks first.\n\n**The purpose half is not decoration.** The description does the triggering\n(Â§2), but the name is read first, and `arwen` alone says nothing about UI.\nBoth halves, always.\n\nRenaming an existing skill is a breaking change: the directory, the `name:`\nfrontmatter, every `skillator:<name>` reference, the `scenario-<skill>.txt` and\n`green-<skill>.txt` baselines, and the plugin version. Fixture names in\n`practice/scripts/baseline-harness.sh` do **not** follow â€” they name a fixture,\nnot a skill, and the recorded verdicts cite them by their old names.\nUse `practice/scripts/rename-skill.py <old> <new> [--dry-run]` for the\nmechanical pass â€” it exits non-zero and lists what's left (headings, bare\ncross-references) for the manual pass; `--selftest` proves its guards.\n\n## 2. The description decides everything\n\nThe description is the only part of your skill most agents ever read. It is\nmatched against the task to decide whether the body gets loaded at all. A body\nnobody loads is a file, not a skill.\n\n**Describe *when to use it*. Never summarize what it does.**\n\nThis is the counter-intuitive one and it is load-bearing. A description that\nsummarizes the workflow creates a shortcut the agent takes *instead of* reading\nthe body. A real case: a description saying \"code review between tasks\" produced\nexactly one review, though the skill's body specified two. Cutting the summary\ndown to the trigger alone made the agent read the body and do both.\n\n```yaml\n# âŒ summarizes the workflow â€” the body becomes optional\ndescription: Use when executing plans - dispatches a subagent per task with review between tasks\n\n# âŒ process detail\ndescription: Use for TDD - write test first, watch it fail, write minimal code, refactor\n\n# âœ… triggering conditions only\ndescription: Use when executing implementation plans with independent tasks in the current session\n```\n\n**Violating the letter of this is violating the spirit of it.** Enumerate the\nprocedure in any register and the agent has a plan before it has the body.\nThree disguises, each produced by a real agent that had this section open:\n\n- **Steps in trigger costume.** \"Use when you need to freeze the changelog, when\n  you need to tag a build, when you need to canaryâ€¦\" â€” six `Use when` clauses\n  that are the six steps. A trigger is a situation someone is *in*, never a step\n  they are *on*.\n- **The step list recast as nouns.** \"â€” changelog freeze, migration dry-run,\n  tag, canary, error-budget watch, promote or roll back.\" Nouns instead of verbs\n  is the same ordered summary.
