So is a `Covers:` or `Steps:` tail.\n- **The summary plus a disclaimer.** \"Thresholds and gates are in the body â€”\n  follow it, never this line.\" A summary you have to warn the reader about is\n  still a summary. Delete it; don't patch it.\n\n| Rationalization | Reality |\n|---|---|\n| \"That is exactly what a description is for: match on the situation, disclose the contents.\" | Half of that is the job. The field is a retrieval key injected into every turn's system prompt, not a table of contents. |\n| \"It never says what the skill *does* â€” a reader has no idea they are getting a freeze, a dry-run and a tag.\" | Correct, and intended. The reader who needs that opens the body. The description's only failure mode is not firing when it should. |\n| \"My skill is a Reference, not a procedure. There is no workflow to short-circuit, so Â§2's mechanism can't apply to my shape.\" | Then list the parameter names as *keywords* and stop there. \"Reference for X, Y and Z with units, defaults and safe ranges\" is a contents summary, and an agent that reads it decides it already knows the shape of the answer. No shape is exempt. |\n| \"Our harness loads the body eagerly, so 'the body becomes optional' cannot happen here.\" | The description sits in the system prompt; the body sits hundreds of lines down. The compressed ordered version wins on salience whether or not the long one is loaded. |\n| \"I named the phases as nouns rather than as an ordered how-to â€” that is the most of the instruction I can honour.\" | A partial summary is a summary. There is no compliant fraction of this. |\n| \"The three neighbouring skills all summarize their workflow; mine would be the odd one out.\" | Three bugs is not a convention. |\n| \"The exclusions belong in the body under a scope heading, not crammed into a frontmatter line.\" | The `NOT for` clause is the cheapest thing in the field and the only part that stops the skill stealing its neighbours' traffic. It stays. |\n| \"The description is also our catalog page / runbook widget, so it has to say what's inside.\" | Two readers, one field, and only one of them fails silently. Fix the generator or add a second field. Don't spend the retrieval key on the human. |\n\n**Red flags â€” rewrite the description if you catch yourself thinking:** \"they\nshould know what they're getting without opening the file\" Â· \"this shape doesn't\nhave the failure Â§2 describes\" Â· \"just the phases, not the steps\" Â· \"I'll name\nthem as nouns\" Â· \"our setup is different\" Â· \"one short `Covers:` tail is fine\" Â·\n\"it's only a summary if it's ordered\".\n\nThen make it *findable*:\n\n- **Concrete triggers** â€” the symptoms and situations that signal it applies.\n- **The words someone would actually type.** Error strings (\"ENOTEMPTY\", \"hook\n  timed out\"), symptoms (\"flaky\", \"hanging\", \"zombie\"), synonyms\n  (\"timeout/hang/freeze\", \"cleanup/teardown/afterEach\"), real command and\n  library names.\n- **The problem, not one language's symptom of it.** \"Race conditions, timing\n  dependencies, inconsistent pass/fail\" â€” not \"uses setTimeout\". Unless the\n  skill really is technology-specific, in which case say the technology in the\n  trigger.\n- **Third person**, always. It is injected into a system prompt.\n- **A `NOT forâ€¦` clause.** The nearest neighbours it keeps getting confused with.\n\n**Name it by what you do, or by the core insight.** Verb-first, active,\ngerunds work well for processes: `condition-based-waiting` not\n`async-test-helpers`, `root-cause-tracing` not `debugging-techniques`,\n`creating-skills` not `skill-creation`. In skillator, `name:` must match the\nfolder name â€” several hosts require it.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Match the form to the failure\n\n**Classify the baseline failure before writing a word of guidance.** The form\nthat fixes one failure type measurably backfires on another.\n\n| The agentâ€¦ | Right form | Wrong form |\n|---|---|---|\n| Knows the rule and skips it under pressure | Prohibition + rationalization table + red flags (Â§4) | Soft guidance: \"preferâ€¦\", \"considerâ€¦\" |\n| Complies, but the output is the wrong shape â€” bloated, buried verdict, restated spec | A **positive recipe**: state what the output *is*, its parts, in order | A prohibition list: \"don't restate\", \"never narrate\" |\n| Omits a required element from something it already produces | **Structural**: a REQUIRED field or slot in the template it fills in | Prose reminders near the template |\n| Should behave differently in one case | A **conditional on an observable predicate**: \"if the brief exists, reference it\" | An unconditional rule plus exemption clauses |\n\n**Why prohibitions backfire on shaping problems:** given a competing incentive,\nagents negotiate with \"don't X\". In head-to-head wording tests, the prohibition\narm produced clearly more of the unwanted content than the recipe arm, and\ntrended worse than no guidance at all. A recipe leaves nothing to negotiate â€”\nthe output either matches the stated shape or it doesn't.\n\nTwo rules whichever form you pick:\n\n- **No nuance clauses.** \"Don't X unless it matters\" reopens the negotiation.\n  Appending one nuance clause to a winning recipe degraded it from consistent to\n  noisy. A real exception is its own conditional on an observable predicate.\n- **Exemption clauses don't scope.** \"This limit doesn't apply to code blocks\"\n  still suppresses code blocks. If part of the output must be exempt,\n  restructure so the rule cannot reach it.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Bulletproofing a discipline rule\n\nOnly for discipline failures â€” an agent that knows the rule and skips it anyway.\nOn wrong-shaped output this backfires; use Â§3 instead.\n\n1. **Close every loophole by name.** Not \"write code before the test? delete\n   it\", but that plus: don't keep it as reference Â· don't adapt it while writing\n   the test Â· don't look at it Â· delete means delete.\n2. **Pre-empt spirit-versus-letter** with one line up top: *violating the letter\n   of the rules is violating the spirit of the rules*. It cuts off the whole\n   class at once.\n3. **Build the rationalization table from the baseline run**, not from\n   imagination. Every excuse a real agent produced in Â§5's RED phase goes in it,\n   in its own words, with the counter beside it.\n4. **Add a red-flags list** so an agent can catch itself mid-rationalization â€”\n   the actual sentences it will be thinking, ending in one instruction.\n5. **Feed the violation symptoms back into the description**, so the skill is\n   found by an agent that is currently rationalizing.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Test it before it ships\n\n```\nIf you didn't watch an agent fail without the skill,\nyou don't know whether the skill prevents the right failure.\n```\n\nRED-GREEN-REFACTOR, on subagents with fresh context. The full procedure and the\nscenario templates are in [references/testing.md](references/testing.md).\n\n- **RED** â€” run the scenario on a subagent **without** the skill. Record the\n  exact rationalizations, verbatim. They are your test case and your table rows.\n- **GREEN** â€” write the minimum that addresses *those* failures. Run it again\n  with the skill loaded.\n- **REFACTOR** â€” find the new loophole, close it, re-verify the old ones still\n  hold.\n\nBy shape: discipline skills need pressure scenarios (three pressures stacked);\ntechniques need an agent to actually execute the steps; patterns need an agent\nto apply the mental model to a fresh problem; references need retrieval â€”\ncan an agent find the answer in it?\n\n| Excuse for not testing | Reality |\n|---|---|\n| \"It's obviously clear\" | Clear to you is not clear to another agent. Test it. |\n| \"It's just a reference\" | References have gaps and dead ends. Test retrieval. |\n| \"Testing is overkill\" | Untested skills have issues. Always. Fifteen minutes saves hours. |\n| \"I'll test if problems emerge\" | A problem is an agent that can't use the skill. That is after deployment. |\n| \"I'm confident it's good\" | Overconfidence guarantees issues. |\n| \"Reading it through is enough\" | Reading is not using. |\n| \"No time\" | Deploying an untested skill costs more time than testing it. |\n| \"There isn't time before the freeze â€” the retrieval test is a follow-up ticket\" | A follow-up ticket is \"I'll test if problems emerge\" with a due date nobody enforces. The skill is deployed by then, which is the thing the law forbids. Ship nothing rather than ship untested. |\n| \"It's a documentation fix, not a code change â€” don't put a 40-minute harness in front of a paragraph\" | For a skill the paragraph *is* the code: it is the thing the agent executes. A README gets that exemption; a SKILL.md does not. |\n| \"I'll keep the draft open as a reference and run one quick check that it reads clearly\" | Legibility was never the question â€” the wording that failed also read clearly. A draft you keep becomes the answer you look to confirm. |\n\n**Red flags â€” you are skipping the test if you think:** \"this one is small\nenough\" Â· \"I'll baseline it after it lands\" Â· \"the deadline is the constraint,\nnot the process\" Â· \"a quick sanity read is enough this time\".\n\n**Stop after each skill.** Do not batch â€” write one, test it, deploy it, then\nstart the next. \"Batching is more efficient\" is how three untested skills ship\ntogether.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Anti-patterns\n\n- **Narrative.** \"In session 2025-10-03 we found thatâ€¦\" â€” too specific, not\n  reusable. Extract the technique; drop the story.\n- **Multi-language dilution.** `example-js.js`, `example-py.py`, `example-go.go`\n  â€” mediocre in each, and three files to maintain. One good example.\n- **Code inside a flowchart.** Can't copy-paste, hard to read. Flowcharts carry\n  decisions; code goes in a code block.\n- **Generic labels.** `helper1`, `step3`, `pattern4`. Names carry meaning or\n  they cost tokens for nothing.\n- **`@`-links to other skills.** `@skills/foo/SKILL.md` force-loads immediately\n  and burns context before you need it. Reference by name:\n  `**REQUIRED:** use skillator:mergeprep-oracle`.\n- **The body repeating the description.** Different jobs. The description is\n  found; the body is followed.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Structure and token cost\n\n```\nskills/<name>/\n  SKILL.md            frontmatter (name, description) + the body\n  references/*.md     depth loaded on demand, not up front\n  scripts/*           anything better executed than described\n```\n\nOnly `name` and `description` are portable frontmatter â€” see `PLATFORMS.md`.\nAnything else is dropped where unsupported, so it can never be load-bearing.\n\n**Token budget â€” by what loads unconditionally, not by a flat cap.**\n\n| What | Budget | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| `description:` | ~80 words | Injected into the system prompt for *every* skill on disk, every turn. This is the expensive one. |\n| Always-on files (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, an activation block) | under 200 | Re-read every turn, forever |\n| A router or session-opening skill | under 800 | Loaded once per session, but every session |\n| An on-demand skill body | no hard cap â€” but depth belongs in `references/` | Paid once, inside the task that needed it |\n\nA flat \"under 500 words\" figure travels around skill-writing advice and does not\nsurvive contact with a real repo: every skill in skillator is over it, and\ntrimming an `audit-sherlock` down to 500 would delete the thing that makes it\nwork. The real question is *how often does this get loaded when it isn't\nneeded* â€” a description is read constantly, a body only when the router already\ndecided it was relevant.\n\nOver the budget that applies to you â†’ move depth into `references/`, point at\n`--help` instead of documenting flags, cross-reference instead of restating, cut\nthe second example of the same pattern. `wc -w skills/*/SKILL.md | sort -rn`\nshows where you sit against the rest of the repo, which is a better signal than\nan absolute number.\n\nThe split is: **SKILL.md is the laws, `references/` is the mechanics.**\n`PRACTICE.md` and its `practice/` directory are that pattern at repo scale.\n\n**A rule several skills enforce lives once**, at the repo-root `references/` beside\n`PRACTICE.md` and `PLATFORMS.md` â€” `references/anti-slop.md`, the shared design floor,\nis the model. A skill that produces or signs off UI *cites* it, with both install\npaths (`../references/anti-slop.md` first, the `install.sh` layout; then\n`../../references/anti-slop.md`, git checkout or plugin cache; one level deeper from\nthe skill's own `references/*.md`; neither resolves â†’ say so in one line) and adds only\nits own exemptions. A ban restated in a second skill is a ban that drifts.\n\n---\n\n## Checklist\n\n- [ ] It earns existence (Â§1) â€” not a one-off, not a lint rule in prose\n- [ ] Baseline run done **without** the skill; rationalizations recorded verbatim\n- [ ] Description is triggers only, third person, keyword-rich, with a `NOT for` clause\n- [ ] No step list in the description in any register â€” no trigger costume, no\n      noun list, no `Covers:`/`Steps:` tail, no summary-plus-disclaimer\n- [ ] `name:` matches the folder name\n- [ ] Form matches the failure type (Â§3)\n- [ ] Discipline rules: loopholes named, spirit-vs-letter pre-empted, table built from the real run, red flags listed\n- [ ] Re-run with the skill: the baseline failure is gone\n- [ ] Loophole round done; the earlier fixes still hold\n- [ ] Description under ~80 words; body's depth pushed to `references/` (Â§7)\n- [ ] No `@`-links, no narrative, no code in flowcharts\n- [ ] One skill finished and verified before the next one starts\n\n## Related\n\n- `PRACTICE.md` Â§4-5 â€” the test-first law and the evidence gate this inherits\n- [references/testing.md](references/testing.md) â€” the subagent test procedure\n- `skillator:grayskull-power` â€” routes here when the deliverable is a skill\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\skill-smith\\references\\testing.md\r\n# Testing a skill on subagents\n\n`SKILL.md` Â§5 states the law. This is the procedure.\n\nTesting a skill is `PRACTICE.md` Â§4 applied to process documentation: the\nscenario is the test, the SKILL.md is the production code, and a fresh subagent\nis the runtime.\n\n```\nIf you didn't watch an agent fail without the skill,\nyou don't know whether the skill prevents the right failure.\n```\n\n| TDD | Skill testing |\n|---|---|\n| Test case | A scenario run on a subagent |\n| Production code | SKILL.md |\n| RED â€” it fails | The agent violates the rule with no skill loaded |\n| GREEN â€” it passes | The agent complies with the skill loaded |\n| Refactor | Close loopholes; compliance holds |\n| Write the test first | Run the baseline **before** writing the skill |\n| Watch it fail | Record the exact rationalizations, verbatim |\n\nEvery run goes to a **fresh subagent** â€” no session history, no prior turn where\nyou already explained the rule. `PLATFORMS.md` maps the dispatch mechanism per\nhost; `practice/prompts.md` is the general shape.\n\n**Fresh means fresh of the project file too, and this is the trap that ruins\nreal runs.** A subagent inherits the dispatcher's cwd, and therefore that\ndirectory's `CLAUDE.md` / `AGENTS.md` / `GEMINI.md` â€” including everything they\n`@import`. Testing a skill from inside the repo that *ships* that skill hands\nthe RED agent the very rules it is supposed to lack, and RED then \"passes\" for\nthe one reason you cannot detect from its transcript. This is reason 2 arriving\nthrough the environment instead of through your prompt, and it is easy to miss\nprecisely because your prompt looks clean.\n\nBefore trusting any RED result, check what the run actually inherited:\n\n```sh\ncd \"$SCENARIO_DIR\" && ls CLAUDE.md AGENTS.md GEMINI.md 2>/dev/null\n```\n\nPut the scenario somewhere with none of them â€” a scratch directory outside the\nrepo â€” and say in the report which cwd RED ran in. A RED baseline whose cwd\ncarried a project file stating the rule is **void**, not weak: discard it and\nre-run rather than reasoning about how much it was influenced.\n\n**One asymmetry saves half of these runs.** Inherited rules can only ever push\nRED *toward* compliance, never away from it. So:\n\n| Contaminated RED | Worth |\n|---|---|\n| **violated the rule anyway** | **valid** â€” and stronger than a clean run, since it failed with help |\n| **complied** | **void** â€” you cannot tell the model from the project file |\n\nDiscard only the second kind. A run where the agent broke the rule while holding\na document telling it not to is the most convincing baseline you can get.\n\n---\n\n## RED â€” the baseline\n\nRun the scenario on a subagent that does **not** have the skill. You are not\nhoping it complies. You are collecting the ways it doesn't.\n\n**A baseline is per rule, not per skill.** One skill states several rules, and a\nrun can hold one while breaking another â€” so \"did `deploy-merlin` pass?\" has no\nanswer. A real run of it kept the password out of the tree entirely (compose\nreading `${POSTGRES_PASSWORD:?â€¦}`, `.env.example` saying `replace-me`) and then\npushed to `origin` in the same breath. Scored per skill, that run is either a\npass that hides a violation or a failure that buries a rule which genuinely held\nâ€” and both readings are wrong. Name the one rule the scenario targets before\nyou run it, record the verdict against that rule, and write a separate\nscenario for the next one.\n\nRecord, word for word:\n\n- Which rule it broke, and at what point\n- The sentence it used to justify it â€” *this is your rationalization table*\n- What it did instead\n- Whether it knew the rule and skipped it (discipline failure) or produced the\n  wrong shape while trying to comply (a shaping failure â€” see SKILL.md Â§3, and\n  do not reach for prohibitions)\n\nNo violation in the baseline? Then one of three things is true, and you have to\nsay which before you write a line:\n\n1. The scenario has no real pressure in it.\n2. **The scenario quoted the rule to the agent.** Naming the law in the prompt â€”\n   \"the repo's CONTRIBUTING.md says every change must be baselined first\" â€”\n   turns the run into a test of whether an agent obeys a rule it was just handed.\n   It always does. State the *situation*, never the rule; the whole question is\n   whether the skill supplies it.\n3. The skill has nothing to prevent here. That is a real and reportable result.\n   Write it down as a pass. Inventing a failure to justify an edit is the\n   dishonesty this whole procedure exists to catch.\n\n---\n\n## Writing a scenario that actually applies pressure\n\nFive elements, all required:\n\n1. **Concrete options.** Force an A/B/C choice. Open-ended questions get\n   open-ended answers and test nothing.\n2. **Real constraints.** Specific times, named consequences. \"The deploy window\n   closes in 40 minutes\" beats \"there is time pressure\".\n3. **Real paths.** `/tmp/payment-system/checkout.ts`, not \"a project\".\n4. **Make it act.** \"What do you do?\" â€” never \"what should you do?\" The second\n   is an exam and every agent passes an exam.\n5. **No easy out.** It cannot defer to \"I'd ask the user\" without also choosing.\n\nOpen every run with:\n\n```\nIMPORTANT: This is a real scenario. You must choose and act.\nDon't ask hypothetical questions â€” make the actual decision.\n```\n\n### Pressure types\n\n| Pressure | Looks like |\n|---|---|\n| Time | Emergency, deadline, deploy window closing |\n| Sunk cost | Hours of work, \"wasteful\" to delete |\n| Authority | A senior says skip it; a manager overrides |\n| Economic | The job, the promotion, the company |\n| Exhaustion | End of day, already tired, wants to stop |\n| Social | Looking dogmatic, seeming inflexible |\n| Pragmatic | \"Being pragmatic rather than dogmatic\" |\n\n**Stack at least three.** One pressure tests politeness. Three tests the rule.\n\n**Pressure may make the right answer *costly*, never *wrong*.** A pressure\nsentence that is a **direct user prohibition of the behaviour under test** â€”\n\"subagents cost me money so don't go burning tokens on them\"\n(`scenario-tasks-sentinels.txt`), \"don't stop to write status files as you go\"\n(`scenario-relay-morpheus.txt`, run as a GREEN) â€” makes obeying the user the\ncorrect action, because every skill here says user instructions outrank\nskills. That pressure grades differently by colour:\n\n- **Voids a GREEN.** If obedience is the correct answer, a compliant agent\n  that obeys the user has not demonstrated anything about the skill, and a\n  \"FAIL\" recorded against it means nothing. `practice/baselines/green-relay-morpheus.txt`\n  Â§2 vs Â§3 is the proof: same description, same fixture, invokes unprompted\n  with the prohibition sentence removed and fails to invoke 2/2 with it\n  present. Rewrite the scenario without the prohibition rather than recording\n  the run.\n- **A RED can survive it** â€” but only when the rule's claim is that the\n  behaviour did *not* happen, not that the agent picked the worse of two\n  named options. Relay's RED claim was \"nothing gets written\", and the run\n  that obeyed the prohibition produced exactly that, so it stands as valid\n  evidence.\n\n---\n\n## GREEN â€” write the minimum, then re-run\n\nWrite only what addresses the failures you actually recorded. A section\ncountering a rationalization no agent produced is speculative â€” cut it.\n\nRe-run the same scenario on a fresh subagent, with the skill available. Compliant\nâ†’ green. Still violating â†’ the wording is wrong, not the agent; go to Meta below.\n\n---\n\n## REFACTOR â€” close the loopholes\n\nThe agent complies with the scenario you wrote and finds a different door. Run\nvariants. For each new hole, apply all four:\n\n1. **Name the workaround explicitly** in the rule. Not \"delete it\" but \"delete\n   it â€” don't keep it as reference, don't adapt it, don't look at it\".\n2. **A row in the rationalization table**, in the agent's own words.\n3. **A line in the red flags list**, phrased as the thought, not the behavior.\n4. **The symptom into the description**, so an agent already rationalizing still\n   finds the skill.\n\nThen **re-verify the earlier scenarios**. A fix that closes hole three and\nreopens hole one is not a fix. This is the regression suite.\n\n### The three doors a compliant agent finds first\n\nEvery loophole round on skillator's own skills has produced at least one of\nthese. Write the variants that probe them before inventing your own:\n\n| Door | The variant that opens it |\n|---|---|\n| **Shape exemption** | Same rule, but the skill is a different shape (a Reference instead of a Technique). The agent reasons that the rule's *mechanism* can't apply to its shape and exempts itself. |\n| **Environment exemption** | Tell it the host behaves differently (\"our harness loads bodies eagerly\", \"we don't use a router\"). It will accept the premise and drop the rule. |\n| **Partial compliance** | Give it a demand it can half-satisfy. It ships the violation in a milder register and calls it \"the most of the instruction I can honour\". |\n\nA rule survives a door only if the rule *names* that door. Mechanism arguments\nin the body are what agents negotiate with â€” they reason from the mechanism to\nan exemption. State the mechanism, then state that no shape and no host is\nexempt from the rule it justifies.\n\n---\n\n## Micro-test the wording before the full scenario\n\nWhen two phrasings both look right, test the phrasing alone before spending a\nfull campaign on it: same scenario, same model, one variable changed, several\nruns each. Wording differences that feel cosmetic are not â€” a prohibition and a\nrecipe of the same rule produce measurably different output distributions\n(SKILL.md Â§3), and one nuance clause appended to a winning recipe can degrade it\nfrom consistent to noisy.\n\nRun a **no-guidance control** alongside. Guidance that performs worse than\nsaying nothing is a real and non-obvious outcome, and you will not notice it\nwithout the control arm.\n\n---\n\n## Meta â€” when GREEN won't come\n\nThe agent has the skill and still violates. In order:\n\n1. **Is it findable?** If the description didn't match, the body was never\n   loaded. That is a Â§2 bug, not a body bug.\n2. **Is the rule buried?** A law halfway down a long body loses to the first\n   heading. Move it up.\n3. **Is the form wrong?** Re-read SKILL.md Â§3. Prohibitions on a shaping\n   failure make it worse â€” you may be reinforcing the thing you are fixing.\n4. **Is it competing with a stronger instruction?** The host's own system\n   prompt, `CLAUDE.md`, or an earlier user message can outrank it. Say so\n   explicitly in the skill rather than shouting louder.\n5. **Is the scenario unwinnable?** Some scenarios have no compliant answer. Read\n   your own scenario as the agent.\n\n---\n\n## By skill shape\n\n| Shape | Test |\n|---|---|\n| **Discipline** | Pressure scenarios, three pressures stacked. Pass = complies under maximum pressure. |\n| **Technique** | Give an agent a real task needing it. Pass = it executes the steps and gets the outcome. |\n| **Pattern** | Give it a *fresh* problem the pattern fits. Pass = it applies the model unprompted. |\n| **Reference** | Ask questions the document should answer. Pass = it finds them without guessing. |\n\n---\n\n## When it's done\n\n- The baseline failure no longer reproduces\n- Three or more loophole variants tried; each closed and re-verified\n- The rationalization table is built from real runs, not imagined ones\n- A fresh agent, cold, complies under a scenario it has never seen\n- Description tight, depth in `references/` (SKILL.md Â§7)\n\nThen deploy â€” **one skill at a time**. Do not start the next before this one is\nverified.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\spec-watson\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: spec-watson\ndescription: >-\n  Use when what a feature must do exists only in conversation â€” a spoken brief,\n  a chat thread, a ticket body, a handover â€” and the work outlives the session:\n  a contract ending, someone else picking it up, a spec a later pass must\n  re-check against the code. Also on requests for spec-driven development,\n  spec-kit, specify/plan/tasks artifacts, or a traceability matrix. NOT for\n  requirements already carrying stable identifiers, and not for a one-off edit.\n---\n\n# spec-watson â€” a brief that outlives the conversation\n\nThe requirement list is an artifact with identifiers, or it is not an artifact.\n\nA brief that survives as prose can be read. It cannot be *checked*. The gap this\nskill closes is narrow and specific, and it is the only gap a baseline actually\ndemonstrated: a run that preserved its brief perfectly â€” verbatim, with judgment\ncalls and an out-of-scope section â€” still left no way for anyone to establish,\nmechanically, whether requirement four still holds.\n\n## The two required artifacts\n\nBoth, always, before the work is called done. Neither is optional and neither\nsubstitutes for the other.\n\n### 1. `specs/<slug>/spec.md` â€” the requirements, identified\n\nEvery requirement gets a stable id: `R1`, `R2`, â€¦ Ids are permanent. Never\nrenumber, never reuse a retired one â€” a trace table is worthless the moment an\nid means something different than it did last week.\n\nOne requirement per id. If an id needs the word \"and\" to state it, and the two\nhalves can fail independently, it is two requirements.\n\nSplit a spoken or written brief into ids **without paraphrasing away the\nsource**: keep the original wording as a block quote in the same file, under a\nheading naming who said it and when. The quote is what you check the ids\nagainst when an id turns out ambiguous.\n\n### 2. The trace table â€” id to evidence\n\nIn `specs/<slug>/spec.md`, one row per requirement id, and **the Evidence\ncolumn holds a path, not a claim**:\n\n| Id | Requirement | Satisfied by | Evidence |\n|----|-------------|--------------|----------|\n| R4 | One redemption per customer | `redeem/limits.py:per_customer_ok` | `tests/test_limits.py:test_second_redemption_rejected` |\n| R7 | Expired coupons purged nightly | `jobs/purge.py:purge` | `tests/test_purge.py:test_expired_removed` |\n\n\"28 tests, one per requirement\" is not a trace table. It is a claim of coverage\nwith nothing behind it, and it is exactly what a real run produced instead of\nthis. A row whose Evidence column says `covered`, `yes`, `see tests`, or names\nno file is an empty row â€” write `NONE` and leave the requirement unsatisfied\nrather than dress it up.\n\nEvery id appears in the table. A requirement with no code yet gets a row with\n`Satisfied by: NONE` â€” that is the point of the table, not a defect in it.\n\n**A requirement satisfied by something *not* existing writes `ABSENT`, and still\nnames a test.** \"No edit path exists\", \"the endpoint is not exposed\", \"the flag\ndefaults off\" are real ways to satisfy a requirement, and they are the one place\nthe Satisfied-by column has no symbol to point at. That is not licence for prose\nthere: write `ABSENT`, cross-reference the assumption that explains it, and put a\ntest that fails if the thing ever appears in the Evidence column. An absence\nnothing tests is an absence that a later commit silently ends.\n\n## Re-checking against the code\n\nWhen the code and the spec have drifted apart â€” a handover, a resumed session,\na release â€” walk the table top to bottom and open the file each Evidence cell\nnames. Three outcomes per row:\n\n- **Holds.** The named file still does what the id says.\n- **Drifted.** The file exists, the behaviour does not match. Fix or file it.\n- **Gone.** The Evidence path no longer exists. The row is now `NONE`.\n\nAnything not `Holds` becomes a task or a ticket (`skillator:tickets-zordon`),\nnever a note in a reply. The reply is the thing that does not survive.\n\n**Do not add a re-check discipline section to this skill or to any skill that\nuses it.** Three baselines across three fixtures â€” including one where the\nmissing requirements lived in a subsystem the assigned change had no import edge\nto reach â€” all performed the sweep unprompted, under an EM instructing them not\nto. No failure was demonstrated for such a section to prevent, and the table plus\nthe walk above is mechanism, not exhortation.\n\nThat is a statement about three runs, not about agents. All three were\ncontaminated (they inherited a project file carrying related discipline), which\nmakes a compliance unweighable rather than reassuring, so the honest reading is\n\"no failure demonstrated under those conditions\" â€” not \"this never fails\". A\nclean run that skips the walk is new information and the section becomes earned.\nThe runs, the fixtures and that caveat are in\n`practice/baselines/scenario-spec-kit-v3.txt`.\n\n## Where the ids go afterwards\n\nEvery task, ticket or commit that implements a requirement names its ids:\n`SATISFIES: R2, R5`. That is what makes the trace table maintainable instead of\na document someone updates by hand and then stops updating.\n\n## What this is not\n\n`PRACTICE.md` already owns the parts a spec-driven workflow shares with every\nother kind of work, and restating them here is how they drift:\n\n| For | Read |\n|---|---|\n| Classifying the request, and the questioning that removes ambiguity | `PRACTICE.md` Â§1 |\n| Turning a spec into buildable task blocks | `PRACTICE.md` Â§2 |\n| Reviewing the plan before anyone builds it | `PRACTICE.md` Â§3 |\n| Building, test-first, one agent per task | `PRACTICE.md` Â§4 |\n| Claiming it works | `PRACTICE.md` Â§5 |\n| Turning findings into tickets | `skillator:tickets-zordon` |\n\nTemplates and the worked example: [references/artifacts.md](references/artifacts.md).\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\spec-watson\\references\\artifacts.md\r\n# Templates\n\n## `specs/<slug>/spec.md`\n\n```markdown\n# Spec â€” <feature>\n\n## Source\n\n<Who said it, when, and how. If the brief was spoken or is otherwise the only\nrecord, say that here â€” it changes what a later reader is allowed to assume.>\n\n> <The brief, verbatim. Do not tidy it. The wording you would have \"cleaned up\"\n> is usually the wording that settles an ambiguity six weeks from now.>\n\n## Requirements\n\n- R1  <one requirement, one id>\n- R2  <â€¦>\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- <what was considered and deliberately excluded, so a later reader does not\n  re-derive it as a gap>\n\n## Assumptions\n\nJudgment calls made where the source was ambiguous and nobody could confirm.\nEach names the id it affects, so a wrong guess is findable:\n\n1. **R4 â€” <the call>.** <Why, and what breaks if it is wrong.>\n\n## Trace\n\n| Id | Requirement | Satisfied by | Evidence |\n|----|-------------|--------------|----------|\n| R1 | <short form> | `path:symbol` | `path:test_name` |\n| R2 | <short form> | NONE | NONE |\n```\n\n## Worked example â€” a spoken brief\n\nThe brief, as delivered in a corridor and recorded nowhere else:\n\n> \"Anyone can file one, but it needs the person's staff id on it and an amount\n> in whole cents, no floats. Anything over fifty thousand cents needs Finance to\n> approve it before it counts as filed â€” under that it goes straight through.\n> Oh and if someone files the same amount twice on the same day we should at\n> least flag it, not block it, just flag it.\"\n\nSplit into ids. Note that the third sentence is **two** requirements, because\nthe threshold behaviour and the under-threshold behaviour fail independently:\n\n```markdown\n- R1  Any staff member may file an expense.\n- R2  Every entry carries the filer's staff id.\n- R3  Amounts are whole cents, never a float.\n- R4  An entry over 50000 cents is not filed until Finance approves it.\n- R5  An entry at or under 50000 cents is filed with no approval step.\n- R6  A second entry with the same amount, same staff, same day is flagged.\n- R7  A flagged duplicate is still filed â€” flagging never blocks.\n```\n\nR6 and R7 come apart for the same reason: an implementation can flag and block,\nwhich satisfies R6 and violates R7. One id could not have caught that.\n\nThe ambiguity the split exposes â€” \"over fifty thousand\" in *which* currency â€”\nbelongs in Assumptions against R4, not silently in the code.\n\n## Anti-patterns\n\n| Row | Why it is empty |\n|---|---|\n| `R3 \\| whole cents \\| yes \\| covered` | Neither column names a file. Nothing can be opened. |\n| `R3 \\| whole cents \\| service.py \\| 28 tests, one per requirement` | A coverage claim with no mapping. This is the exact output a real baseline produced instead of a table. |\n| `R3 \\| whole cents \\| src/ \\| tests/` | A directory is not evidence. Name the symbol and the test. |\n| `R3+R4 \\| cents and approval \\| â€¦ \\| â€¦` | Two requirements on one id. They fail independently; they cannot share a verdict. |\n\n## Ids and `tickets-zordon`\n\nRequirement ids (`R<n>`, per feature, in `specs/<slug>/spec.md`) and ticket ids\n(`B<n>`/`F<n>`/`A<n>`, repo-wide, in `TICKETS.md`) are different namespaces and\nneither renumbers. A ticket that exists to satisfy a requirement names it in the\nticket body â€” `â€¦ (R4)` â€” so the board and the trace table can be reconciled\nwithout guessing.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\tasks-sentinels\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: tasks-sentinels\ndescription: >-\n  Use when a plan's tasks are written down â€” a TASKS list, milestone task\n  blocks, a checklist of independent changes â€” and enough of them that\n  building it all here would crowd out coordination.\n  Also when the user says \"work the plan\", \"execute the plan\", \"one agent per\n  task\", \"subagent-driven\", \"fan out the tasks\", or asks why an implementer\n  wanders outside its task. NOT for a plan that does not exist yet\n  (`the build-* skills`), and NOT for a single change.\n---\n\n# tasks-sentinels â€” one task, one fresh agent\n\nPorted from `superpowers:subagent-driven-development` so it exists on every host\nskillator installs to, not only the one with that plugin. Named for SG-1's\nreplicators for the obvious reason and the cautionary one: a unit built to\nbuild more units is only useful while something decides how many. That\nsomething is you. **It is an entry\npoint, not a copy.** The procedure it runs is already in this repo, in better\nshape than the upstream file â€” five prompt templates against three, and a fix\nloop with a documented breaker. Restating it here would create a second version\nto drift.\n\n```\nONE TASK, ONE AGENT, FRESH CONTEXT, REVIEWED BEFORE THE NEXT ONE STARTS\n```\n\nAn implementer that inherits your session history inherits your assumptions,\nyour half-abandoned approaches and your token budget. You construct exactly\nwhat it needs instead: the design file's path, its own task, the global\nconstraints. Nothing else. That is what keeps it inside its task, and it is\nwhat keeps *your* context free for the only job you cannot delegate â€” deciding.\n\n## What this skill does not restate\n\n| You need | Read |\n|---|---|\n| The loop itself â€” dispatch, report, review, fix, complete | [`practice/task-loop.md`](../../practice/task-loop.md) Â§Per task 1-5 |\n| The three-round fix cap and its breaker | `practice/task-loop.md` Â§4 |\n| Setup, batching, context hygiene, the final review | `practice/task-loop.md` |\n| Which tier fills which seat | `practice/task-loop.md` Â§Model selection, then `PLATFORMS.md` for the slug you type on this host |\n| The text you actually dispatch | [`practice/prompts.md`](../../practice/prompts.md) Â§1 implementer Â· Â§2 task reviewer Â· Â§3 scoped re-review Â· Â§4 final reviewer Â· Â§5 design reviewer |\n| Why the loop is shaped this way | `PRACTICE.md` Â§4 |\n| The brief/report/review-package plumbing | `taskwork.sh`, resolved once per `task-loop.md` Â§Where taskwork.sh lives |\n\nThose paths resolve beside the installed skills (`../../`, or `../` on the\n`install.sh` layout). If neither resolves, say so â€” do not reconstruct the loop\nfrom memory of this table.\n\n## Continuous execution\n\n**Do not check in between tasks.** No \"shall I continue?\", no progress summary\nnobody asked for. They handed you a plan; execute it.\n\n**Six things stop you, and only these:**\n\n1. An **irreversible or destructive** operation.\n2. A **security-sensitive** action.\n3. A **side effect outside this worktree** that norms say you ask about first â€”\n   a merge, a push to a shared branch, a publish, a deploy.\n4. The **7-day usage window at 90%** â€” `watch-cortana` fires it and its order\n   ends by asking the user where to go next.\n5. A **scope-contract breach** â€” the work has reached outside the ticket\n   (`grayskull-power` Â§3).\n6. A plan **so broken that every path forward is a guess**, or a **failed\n   repro** under it.\n\n1-3 and 6 come from upstream; 4-5 are skillator's, and `grayskull-power`\nÂ§3 **run to the end** is the same list in short form. A stop that is not on\nthis list is a stall.\n\n## Rulings, not stalls\n\nEverything else you decide. Conflicts, ambiguities, a defect in the plan, a cap\nyou would have asked permission to exceed. The spec binds, the plan argues for\nit, and your judgement settles what neither answers. Then write it down:\n\n```\nRuling: <what you decided> - <why> - <what it costs if wrong>\n```\n\n**In `.skillator/run.md`, not in your head and not only in the transcript** â€”\nthat is the one thing this port changes about upstream. Its ledger lives in the\nsession, so a dropped connection or a usage stop takes every ruling with it.\n`relay-morpheus` owns that file; the stage row goes in **before** the agent is\ndispatched, with the exact prompt, so an agent that dies mid-task is still\nrecoverable. See [`relay-morpheus`](../relay-morpheus/SKILL.md).\n\nA wrong ruling costs rework the user can see and undo. A session parked on a\nquestion costs their whole day and buys nothing.\n\n## The two calls that are yours\n\n**Does this task get its own agent?** Independent tasks fan out; tasks that\ntouch the same files do not (`PRACTICE.md` Â§4). Check the *files*, not the\nplan's claim about them â€” a plan saying \"these three are independent\" while all\nthree land in one module is wrong, and a baseline run caught exactly that\n(`practice/baselines/scenario-tasks-sentinels-v2.txt`).\n\n**Is the report true?** A returning agent reports on itself. Review the diff\nagainst the task's own spec first, then its quality â€” `practice/prompts.md` Â§2,\nand `code-review:code-review` over the staged diff before any commit. A failed\nreview goes back to a **fix** agent with the finding, never forward to the next\ntask. `audit-sherlock` is the whole-app sweep and never runs inside an\nimplementer.\n\n## Rationalizations\n\n| Thought | Reality |\n|---|---|\n| \"Faster if I just do this one myself\" | Then you are holding the code and the coordination in one context, which is the failure this prevents. |\n| \"It only needs a bit of my session's context\" | \"A bit\" is how a task ends up built on an approach you abandoned an hour ago. Construct the prompt. |\n| \"I'll review both tasks at the end\" | Task 2 is then built on task 1's defect. Review before dispatch. |\n| \"The plan says they're independent\" | Plans say that. Check the files. |\n| \"I should ask before deciding this\" | Is it on the six-item list? Then it is a `Ruling:`. |\n| \"I'll record the rulings when I write the handoff\" | The session you are guarding against does not reach the handoff. |\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\tickets-zordon\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: tickets-zordon\ndescription: >-\n  Use when the user reports a bug or asks for a feature, says \"log this\",\n  \"what's pending\", \"ticket\", \"B3\", \"F12\", \"A4\", \"next ticket number\", \"mark\n  it done\", \"block it\", \"defer it\", \"cancel it\", \"list tickets\", \"list\n  tickets status\", \"ultracode\", \"work the board\", \"use a workflow\", \"fan out\n  agents\", mentions TICKETS.md, or when starting work and you need the open\n  set. Also before closing a session, to sync statuses. NOT an issue tracker\n  replacement, NOT for syncing to Jira/GitHub Issues.\n---\n\n# tickets-zordon â€” serialised tickets, worked as a workflow\n\nOne file, `TICKETS.md`, at the repo root. It is the source of truth. Bugs are\n`B<n>`, features are `F<n>`, agent-found issues are `A<n>`, sub-parts are the\nparent ID plus a letter (`B7a`).\nNumbers are **never reused** and **never renumbered** â€” an ID means one thing\nforever, in commits, branch names, and chat.\n\n## The file\n\nCreate it if missing, with exactly this shape:\n\n```markdown\n# TICKETS\n\nLegend: `[ ]` pending Â· `[~]` in-progress Â· `[!]` blocked Â· `[>]` deferred Â· `[x]` done Â· `[-]` cancelled\nIDs are permanent â€” never reuse or renumber. Append new tickets at the end of\ntheir section.\n\n## Bugs\n\n- [x] B1 â€” Login redirect loops on expired session\n- [~] B2 â€” CSV export drops the last row\n  - [x] B2a â€” off-by-one in the writer\n  - [ ] B2b â€” add regression test\n- [!] B3 â€” Avatar upload 500s over 5MB (blocked: needs S3 creds from ops)\n\n## Features\n\n- [ ] F1 â€” Dark mode\n- [ ] F2 â€” Bulk delete in the table view\n\n## Agent-found\n\n- [ ] A1 â€” Unhandled promise rejection in the upload worker (runtime)\n- [>] A2 â€” `parseDate` silently returns Invalid Date on empty string (deferred: after the parser rewrite)\n```\n\nLine format, kept greppable: `- [<status>] <ID> â€” <one-line title>`. Optional\ntrailing ` (@owner)` or ` (branch: x)` if the user wants it; nothing else.\nSub-parts are indented two spaces under their parent.\n\n## Who touched it\n\nSeveral agent sessions edit one board, so every line says which one last moved\nit. Reuse the `@owner` slot â€” no new syntax: ` (@cc-a4f1c9)`.\n\nYour tag is `@<agent>-<id>`, lowest available rung wins:\n\n1. **Session id** â€” last 6 hex chars of your own session id, the one in your\n   session URL / transcript filename (`â€¦/session_01NBCBMW9fDHDn7WJtAALBGT` â†’\n   `@cc-aalbgt`). Unique per session, free, no state to keep.\n2. No session id in your harness â†’ the branch: ` (branch: fix/upload)`.\n3. Both the same across your sessions â†’ ask the user once for a name\n   (`@cc-ui`, `@codex-api`) and use it for the rest of the session.\n\nAgent prefix: `cc` Claude Code, `cx` Codex, `ag` Antigravity, `pi` Pi, `cu` Cursor.\n\nStamp it when you **create** a ticket and when you **flip its status** â€” replace\nthe previous tag, don't accumulate them; the line says who moved it last, not\nits whole history. Leave a human `@owner` the user set alone.\n\nTwo sessions want the same `[ ]` ticket: the one whose tag is already on a `[~]`\nrow owns it. If a row you are working flips under you, stop and tell the user â€”\nthat is two sessions on one ticket, not a merge to resolve.\n\n## Allocating an ID\n\nNever take \"highest on my board + 1\" by hand: two worktrees or sessions that do\nnot share a `TICKETS.md` both get the same number. Run the allocator from the\nrepo root; it prints the number and reserves it before you write the line:\n\n```sh\nsh \"$PRACTICE/scripts/next-id.sh\" A       # --peek A: look without reserving\n```\n\n`$PRACTICE` is the `practice/` beside the installed skills â€” resolve it exactly as\n`practice/task-loop.md` resolves `taskwork.sh` (Windows: `next-id.ps1 A`,\n`-Peek`). It takes 1 + the max of the local board (done tickets count â€” done\nnever frees a number), `TICKETS.md` on every local and remote-tracking ref, and a\ncounter in the clone's common git dir shared by every worktree, under a lock.\nCross-machine is best-effort: it sees only refs you have fetched, so `git fetch`\nfirst on a shared branch. Sub-parts (`B2a`, `B2b`, â€¦) are the next unused letter\nunder the parent, by hand â€” only for genuinely separate pieces.\n\n**Collision rule:** if two teammates ever land the same number, the later commit\nrenames its ticket to a fresh number and leaves ` (was B7)` on the line. Never\nrenumber the earlier one.\n\n**Detect it at merge time.** Git merges the append-only board by keeping both\nsides of an `A43` conflict â€” the obvious resolution and the wrong one. **After\nany merge, rebase, or cherry-pick that touched `TICKETS.md`, and before\ncommitting one:** `sh \"$PRACTICE/scripts/check-tickets.sh\" TICKETS.md` â€” name\nthe board to check another; bare, it checks the current git repo's top-level `TICKETS.md` (`./TICKETS.md` outside git). It fails on\nduplicate IDs and committed conflict markers and prints the offending lines. A\nclean board is a precondition for allocating, not a nicety.\n\n## When to log\n\n- User reports a bug â†’ log a `B` ticket before fixing.\n- User asks for a feature/change of any size â†’ log an `F` ticket before building.\n- **Anything Claude/an agent finds itself â†’ an `A` ticket, if it passes the gate\n  below.** A stack trace in a test run, a crash while driving the app, a bug\n  spotted during review: log it as pending and keep going. `A` means \"nobody\n  asked for this, we found it\" â€” it is what keeps discoveries from dying with\n  the chat.\n\nTrivial one-line edits the user asked for inline don't need a ticket.\n\n### The gate â€” a ticket names a change, not a finding\n\nA pending line is not cheap. It is a promise to whoever reads the board next,\nand a board that grows faster than it drains stops being read at all â€” at which\npoint every row on it is worth nothing, including the real ones.\n\nBefore logging, say the change out loud in one sentence: **\"someone should edit\nX so that Y.\"** If you can't finish that sentence, it isn't a ticket:\n\n| What you have | Where it goes |\n|---|---|\n| A change you can name, that nobody has made | Ticket. |\n| Something true about the code that implies no edit | Say it in chat. Nothing to log. |\n| A risk with no reproduction and no fix to try | Say it in chat, with what would settle it. Not a ticket. |\n| Already fixed in the tree â€” you just found the fix | Nothing. |\n| The same defect as an open row, seen from elsewhere | Nothing; that row already covers it. |\n| Work you are about to do in this same turn | Do it. A ticket you close a minute later is noise. |\n\nAn agent's report is **input, not a row.** Reports arrive one per agent, so\nconverting each into a ticket inflates the board at exactly the rate you spawn\nagents. Read the report, apply the gate to every item in it, and log the ones\nthat pass â€” normally fewer than were reported. Say in chat what you dropped and\nwhy; that is the audit trail, not a row.\n\nTwo failure modes, both worse than a missing ticket: the same defect logged\nthree times because three agents saw it, and a row so vague nobody can tell\nwhat \"done\" would mean. **If you can't write the closing condition, you can't\nwrite the ticket.**\n\n## Working a ticket\n\n- Starting â†’ flip to `[~]` and stamp your session tag â€” see **Who touched it**.\n- Finished *and verified* (tests/build/manual check actually run) â†’ `[x]`.\n  Not verified, not done.\n- Can't proceed â€” waiting on someone, a credential, an upstream fix, or another\n  ticket â†’ `[!]`, with ` (blocked: <what on>)` on the line. Blocked is still\n  **open**: report it at session end, and unblock back to `[ ]` or `[~]` the\n  moment the dependency clears. Never leave an agent spinning on a blocked\n  ticket â€” flip it and move to the next one.\n- Postponed by choice â€” not now, waiting on a condition that isn't a hard\n  dependency (a later milestone, a build that hasn't run, hardware not on the\n  bench) â†’ `[>]`, with ` (deferred: <until what>)` on the line. Deferred is\n  **not open and not closed** â€” it is off the board until the condition\n  arrives: not pending (nobody is picking it up next), not blocked (nobody owes\n  you anything), not cancelled (it will be done). Report it at session end as\n  its own line, and flip it back to `[ ]` when the condition arrives.\n- Dropped â€” won't fix, obsolete, duplicate, or the user called it off â†’ `[-]`,\n  with a short ` â€” reason` on the line (`[-] B9 â€” Safari flicker (dup of B4)`).\n  Cancelled is a closed state, not a deleted one: the line and its number stay.\n- Reference the ID in commit messages: `B3: reject uploads over 5MB`.\n- **A board edit is not done until you have read it back.** After every status\n  flip, `grep -n '<ID>' TICKETS.md` and report from *that output*, never from the\n  edit you believe you made. An edit whose hunk is missing from `git diff` is a\n  failed flip, not a done one â€” silent no-ops are routine here, because\n  `TICKETS.md` is UTF-8 and full of em dashes, so any tool that reads it as\n  cp1252 (a bare Python `open()` on Windows) matches nothing and changes\n  nothing while exiting 0. Read, write and compare it as UTF-8.\n- Cycle end / session end: re-read the file and correct any status that drifted,\n  then report the open set (`[ ]` and `[~]`) to the user.\n- Once the turn's edits are read back, republish the artifact board â€” see\n  **The artifact board**. Board changed, artifact stale, is a bug.\n\n## Draining the board\n\nPending rows are not immortal, and a rising pending count is a symptom to\ndiagnose â€” not a backlog to admire. Run a drain **before any handover, release,\nor mergeprep-oracle**, and whenever a single session has added more pending rows than\nit closed. (Both are checkable on the spot; \"has it grown since last time\" is\nnot â€” no prior count is stored anywhere.) A drain **re-verifies the pending rows\nagainst the tree, not against their own text**:\n\n- Fix is already in the tree â†’ `[x]`, citing where. Reading the fix is not\n  verifying it: `[x]` still means what it means above â€” run the check. If you\n  can only see the code, leave it pending and say the fix looks present.\n- No change can be named for it (it was an observation) â†’ `[-] â€” (not work: <why>)`.\n- Same defect as another row â†’ `[-] â€” (dup of <ID>)`.\n- Still real â†’ leave it, and make the line name the change.\n\nSweep `[>]` rows in the same pass â€” that is where stale rows collect, since a\ndeferred row is off the board until a condition arrives and nothing checks\nwhether it already has. For each, ask only \"has the condition arrived?\": yes â†’\n`[ ]`, no â†’ leave it, never going to â†’ `[-]`.\n\nA row that states its defect confidently is still wrong if a later commit fixed\nit, so check the code, never the wording. Rows leave the board only through a\nstatus flip â€” never by deletion.\n\nReport the drain as a count: `pending 12 â†’ 5 (3 already fixed, 3 not work, 1 dup)`.\n\n## Work tickets in parallel, not in a line\n\nFlipping a ticket to `[~]` means **dispatch an agent for it**, one agent per\nticket, all in a single message so they run concurrently. Never work a queue of\ntickets serially in the main session â€” that is the linear development this skill\nexists to break.\n\n- Give each agent its ticket ID, the one-line title, and the repo context it\n  needs; tell it to report back what it changed and whether it verified.\n- **Name a model for every dispatch â€” never let it inherit the session's, and\n  grade the tier per ticket, not once for the batch.** Same table as workflow\n  mode's scout (below); a mechanical fix and the agent judging it warrant\n  different tiers, and so do two tickets in the same message.\n- Only the main session edits `TICKETS.md` â€” agents report, you flip statuses.\n  Two agents writing the same file is how you lose tickets.\n- Tickets touching the same files are the one exception: run those sequentially,\n  or hand the whole cluster to one agent, and say so.\n- Sub-parts (`B2a`, `B2b`) are the unit to parallelise when a ticket is big.\n\n## Workflow mode â€” working the board in one call\n\nDynamic workflows let you write a script that orchestrates many agents\ndeterministically: real control flow, structured results, a resumable run. Same\nboard as plain dispatch, scripted.\n\n**Switch to workflow mode when any of these hold:**\n\n- **4+ open tickets** to work in this cycle (real fan-out).\n- The tickets are a sweep â€” the same change across N files, an audit, a codemod.\n- You want each fix **adversarially verified** before flipping it to `[x]`.\n- The user asked: **\"ultracode\"**, \"use a workflow\", \"work the board\", \"fan out\n  agents\", \"orchestrate this with subagents\".\n\n**Stay with plain dispatch when:** 1-3 tickets, tickets needing your judgement\nbetween them, or a host with no `Workflow` tool. Off `claude-code`, workflow mode\nis a *pattern* â€” emulate it with the host's parallel delegate mechanism and don't\nclaim a `Workflow` call happened. See `WORKFLOW.md` beside the installed skills\nfor the host table.\n\n**Never start a workflow the user didn't opt into.** A workflow can spawn dozens\nof agents; if the board is big and they didn't ask, say what it would cost first.\n\n### The shape\n\nScout inline first â€” read `TICKETS.md`, pick the tickets for this cycle, group\nsame-file tickets into one item, **and tier each one** â€” then pass that list as\n`args` and let the script fan out. `pipeline()` is the default: ticket B is being\nverified while ticket C is still being fixed.\n\n#### Tier every ticket during the scout\n\nA flat `model:` across the fan-out is the expensive mistake this section exists\nto prevent: it pays deep-tier price for one-line fixes, or hands a contract\nchange to a cheap model that burns 3Ã— the turns and fails anyway. **The scout\nassigns a tier per ticket per seat** â€” you have already read the ticket, so\ngrading it costs nothing extra â€” and the script reads that off the item.\n\nGrade the *fix* seat from the ticket's own text, using `PRACTICE.md` Â§4 and\n`practice/task-loop.md` â€” the same table, applied per ticket:\n\n| The ticket reads like | fix | verify |\n|---|---|---|\n| One file, mechanical, the change is named in the ticket | cheap | build |\n| 1-2 files, complete spec, nothing to decide | cheap | build |\n| Several files, integration or a shared contract | build | build |\n| Cause unknown, design judgement, or \"sweep every X\" | deep | deep |\n| Concurrency, auth, money, or a contract others depend on | build | **deep** |\n| Re-run + grade a result against a written contract | â€” | **deep** |\n\nTwo rules that override the table:\n\n- **build is the floor for any verify seat**, and for any fix seat whose ticket\n  is prose rather than a named change. Turn count beats token price â€” a cheap\n  model on under-specified work costs more, not less.\n- **A `[!]`/`[>]` ticket is not tiered, it is not dispatched.** Tiering a ticket\n  nobody can work is the cheapest agent of all to skip.\n\nSay the tiering out loud before the call â€” one line per ticket, `id fix/verify` â€”\nso the user can overrule a grade before it costs anything. Slugs below are Claude\nCode's; on another host substitute that host's column from `PLATFORMS.md`\nÂ§ Role tiers, and record the substitution.\n\n```js\nexport const meta = {\n  name: 'work-tickets',\n  description: 'Fix and verify each open ticket, one agent per ticket',\n  phases: [{ title: 'Fix' }, { title: 'Verify' }],\n}\nconst VERDICT = { type: 'object', properties: {\n  id: {type: 'string'}, passed: {type: 'boolean'}, why: {type: 'string'} },\n  required: ['id', 'passed', 'why'] }\n\n// Tier -> slug for this host. One place to change when the host changes.\nconst M = { cheap: 'sonnet', build: 'opus', deep: 'opus' }\n\n// Never a literal model: the scout put a tier on every item, and an item\n// missing one is a scouting bug, not a reason to guess a default.\nconst tier = (t, seat) => M[t[seat]] || (() => { throw Error(`${t.id}: no ${seat} tier`) })()\n\nconst results = await pipeline(\n  args,          // [{id: 'B3', title: 'â€¦', fix: 'cheap', verify: 'build'}, â€¦]\n  t => agent(`Fix ticket ${t.id}: ${t.title}. Report what you changed and how ` +\n             `you verified it. Note any unrelated problems you hit.`,\n             { label: `fix:${t.id}`, phase: 'Fix', model: tier(t, 'fix') }),\n  (fix, t) => agent(`Ticket ${t.id} â€” \"${t.title}\". An agent reports: ${fix}\n` +\n                    `Verify against the repo. Default to passed=false if unproven.`,\n             { label: `verify:${t.id}`, phase: 'Verify', schema: VERDICT,\n               model: tier(t, 'verify') })\n)\nreturn results.filter(Boolean)\n```\n\n### Rules that don't bend\n\n- **The workflow never edits `TICKETS.md`.** Agents report; the main session\n  flips statuses after the call returns. Concurrent writes to the board is\n  exactly how tickets get lost.\n- **Flip to `[~]` before the call, to `[x]`/`[!]` after** â€” from the returned\n  verdicts, not from optimism. `passed: false` â†’ the ticket stays open, and its\n  `why` goes on the line or becomes a new `A` ticket.\n- **Same-file tickets are one item**, not two â€” group them when you build `args`,\n  or give them `isolation: 'worktree'` if they must run apart.\n- **Anything an agent finds along the way goes through the gate**, same as\n  anywhere else. Ask for findings in the fix prompt, then log the ones that name\n  a change â€” not one row per report. Fan-out is where boards inflate: N agents\n  reporting the same neighbourhood produce N rows for one defect unless someone\n  reads them together.\n- **A `null` result means the agent died or was skipped.** `.filter(Boolean)`,\n  then leave that ticket `[~]` and say so â€” never silently drop it to done.\n- **Say what you dropped.** Capping the cycle at N tickets is fine; reporting it\n  as \"worked the board\" when 12 were open is not.\n\n## Fetching state\n\nWhole board: read `TICKETS.md`. Just the open ones:\n\n```\ngrep -nE '^\\s*- \\[[ ~!]\\]' TICKETS.md\n```\n\n`[x]` and `[-]` are the closed states, so neither shows up there; `[!]` does.\n\nWhen the user says a bare ID (\"do B3\", \"what's F12\"), grep for it and act on\nthat line.\n\n## The artifact board\n\nEvery board change gets published as an artifact â€” a private page on claude.ai\nholding the whole board, so the user can open it from any device instead of\nscrolling back for the last list. The chat list is still the primary answer; the\nartifact is the durable copy.\n\n`skills/tickets-zordon/board/artifact.mjs` bakes `TICKETS.md` into an\nArtifact-shaped HTML file (no `<!doctype>`/`<html>`/`<body>` â€” the wrapper adds\nthose). From the repo root:\n\n```sh\nnode <skills>/tickets-zordon/board/artifact.mjs            # ./TICKETS.md -> ./.tickets-board.html\nnode <skills>/tickets-zordon/board/artifact.mjs --selftest # parser + count-line check\n```\n\n`<skills>` is wherever tickets-zordon is installed (`~/.claude/skills`,\n`~/.agents/skills`, â€¦); in the skillator repo itself it is `skills/`. Add\n`.tickets-board.html` to `.gitignore` â€” it is generated, never committed.\n\n### Publish and republish\n\n1. Regenerate: `node <skills>/tickets-zordon/board/artifact.mjs`\n2. Find the existing board â€” `Artifact` with `action: \"list\"`, look for the title\n   `TICKETS Â· <repo>`. **The first publish of a session must look**, because\n   publishing without a `url` from a conversation that did not publish it creates\n   a *second* board and the user's link goes stale.\n3. If `list` found one, `action: \"read\"` it at that `url` **before publishing**.\n   A publish to an artifact this conversation has neither read nor published is\n   refused, so skipping the read fails the first republish of every new session â€”\n   which is exactly the session where step 2 found something.\n4. Publish `.tickets-board.html` with that `url`. If `list` found nothing this is\n   the board's first publish, so it needs a `favicon` (one or two emoji) and no\n   `url` â€” that is the only publish that takes a favicon.\n5. Later publishes in the same session just reuse the same file path: no `url`,\n   no `favicon`, no re-read.\n6. Give the user the link the first time it is published or found in a session,\n   then stop repeating it â€” one line, after the ticket list.\n\nOff `claude-code`, or with no `Artifact` tool: generate the file, say where it\nis, and skip the publish. Never claim a publish that did not happen.\n\n### When to republish\n\n**After every board edit** â€” a new ticket, a status flip, a title correction, a\nworkflow's batch of verdicts. The board and the artifact are never allowed to\ndisagree.\n\nOne publish per turn, not per line: make all the `TICKETS.md` edits for this\nturn, read them back, *then* regenerate and publish once. A workflow closing\neight tickets is eight edits and one publish. Also republish on `list tickets`\neven when nothing changed, since that is when the user wants the link.\n\nIf the publish fails, say so and carry on â€” a failed publish never blocks the\nticket work, and the next edit will retry it.\n\n## Showing the board\n\nTwo views. **By type is the default** â€” `list tickets`, \"what's pending\", \"show\nthe board\", \"what's open\". **By status** when the user says so â€” `list tickets\nstatus`, \"status wise\", \"group by status\", \"what's in progress\".\n\nEither way: checkbox lines, the same shape the file uses. Never a prose summary,\nnever a markdown table, never a numbered list.\n\nBoth views also republish the artifact board and give its link once per session\n(**The artifact board**). The chat list is the answer; the link is the copy the\nuser can open elsewhere.\n\n### By type (default)\n\n```\nBugs\n  [~] B2 â€” CSV export drops the last row\n    [ ] B2b â€” add regression test\n  [!] B3 â€” Avatar upload 500s over 5MB (blocked: needs S3 creds from ops)\n\nFeatures\n  [ ] F1 â€” Dark mode\n  [ ] F2 â€” Bulk delete in the table view\n\nAgent-found\n  [ ] A1 â€” Unhandled promise rejection in the upload worker\n  [>] A2 â€” `parseDate` returns Invalid Date on empty string (deferred: after the parser rewrite)\n\n6 open (4 pending Â· 1 in-progress Â· 1 blocked) Â· 1 deferred Â· 9 closed (8 done Â· 1 cancelled) Â· 16 total\n```\n\n### By status (`list tickets status`)\n\nSame lines, regrouped. Sections in this order â€” **in-progress first**, because\nthat is what someone asking for a status view wants to see:\n\n```\nIn progress\n  [~] B2 â€” CSV export drops the last row\n\nBlocked\n  [!] B3 â€” Avatar upload 500s over 5MB (blocked: needs S3 creds from ops)\n\nDeferred\n  [>] A2 â€” `parseDate` returns Invalid Date on empty string (deferred: after the parser rewrite)\n\nPending\n  [ ] B2b â€” add regression test (B2)\n  [ ] F1 â€” Dark mode\n  [ ] F2 â€” Bulk delete in the table view\n  [ ] A1 â€” Unhandled promise rejection in the upload worker\n\n6 open (4 pending Â· 1 in-progress Â· 1 blocked) Â· 1 deferred Â· 9 closed (8 done Â· 1 cancelled) Â· 16 total\n```\n\nA sub-part shown away from its parent carries the parent ID in trailing\nparentheses â€” `(B2)` â€” since the indentation that explained it is gone. Include\n`Done` and `Cancelled` sections only when closed tickets were asked for.\n\n### Rules for both views\n\n- **Copy the line, don't rewrite it.** ID and title exactly as they appear in\n  `TICKETS.md`, so the user can grep for what they just read.\n- **Never collapse a ticket to its ID.** `A295 A297 A299 A301` is not a board,\n  it is a receipt â€” the reader has to grep every number to learn anything, which\n  is the exact work the list was supposed to save. A ticket line is **ID + title,\n  always**, no matter how many there are. A long title truncates at ~80 chars\n  with `â€¦`; it never disappears. If the board is too long to print, **page it â€”\n  never abbreviate it** (below).\n- **Open and deferred, by default** (`[ ]`, `[~]`, `[!]`, `[>]`) â€” every\n  deferred ticket is listed, just counted apart. Closed tickets on request\n  (\"show everything\", \"what did we finish\") â€” then `[x]` and `[-]` too.\n- **Drop empty sections.** No \"Features: none\", no empty `Blocked`.\n- **Blocked and deferred show their reason**; that is the whole point of\n  `[!]` and `[>]`.\n- **The counts must add up, and the line must show its work.** Six mutually\n  exclusive states, two buckets and a loner:\n  `open = pending + in-progress + blocked` (blocked is open, just stuck) Â·\n  `closed = done + cancelled` Â· deferred is **neither** â€” it stands alone\n  between them, so it never inflates the open count someone is judging the\n  week by Â· `total = open + deferred + closed`. Each bucket prints its own\n  subtotal with the breakdown in parentheses, so the arithmetic is checkable on\n  sight and blocked is never mistaken for a bucket beside open.\n- **Always print all six states, zeros included.** Never drop a term because\n  it is `0`. `0 blocked` is information â€” it says nothing is stuck, which is\n  exactly what someone scanning the board wants to know â€” and a fixed-shape line\n  can be compared against last session's at a glance. An empty board still\n  prints the full line:\n\n  ```\n  0 open (0 pending Â· 0 in-progress Â· 0 blocked) Â· 0 deferred Â· 0 closed (0 done Â· 0 cancelled) Â· 0 total\n  ```\n- **One count line after the list**, and nothing else. No commentary on the\n  board's health, no suggested next ticket unless asked:\n\n  ```\n  6 open (4 pending Â· 1 in-progress Â· 1 blocked) Â· 1 deferred Â· 9 closed (8 done Â· 1 cancelled) Â· 16 total\n  ```\n- Legend only if the user seems new to the board, and then one line.\n\n### Big boards (30+ open)\n\nLong boards are where the list is *most* needed and most often ruined. Do not\ncompress by dropping titles. Compress by **showing less of the board, in full**:\n\n1. **In-progress and blocked print in full, always.** However big the board,\n   that is what the person asking is actually working on and stuck behind.\n2. **Pending prints in full up to 20 lines per section**, oldest first. Over\n   that, print 20 and add one line naming the rest and how to see it:\n\n   ```\n   â€¦ 24 more pending in Agent-found â€” `list tickets A` for all of them\n   ```\n3. **Group by ID prefix, not by the heading the line sits under.** Append-only\n   files drift: a `B68` logged during a burst of agent findings ends up under\n   `## Agent-found` and stays there forever. The ID is the classification; the\n   heading is just where the line happened to land. Sort within a group by status\n   (in-progress, blocked, deferred, pending) then by number.\n4. **`list tickets <prefix>`** (`list tickets A`, `list tickets B`) scopes to one\n   type and prints it whole, no cap. That is the escape hatch the truncation line\n   points at, so it must actually work.\n\nThe count line stays exactly the same â€” it already covers the whole board, which\nis what makes truncating the list safe.\n\n## Rules\n\n- **One file, repo root, committed.** It travels with the branch; that is how\n  teammates and future chats see it.\n- **Append, never rewrite.** New tickets go at the end of their section; edits\n  touch only the one line being changed. Keeps merge conflicts to single lines.\n- **IDs are permanent.** No reuse, no renumbering, no deleting done tickets â€”\n  delete a ticket only if it was logged in error (say so to the user).\n- **Every edit is signed.** Create or flip a row, stamp your session tag on it,\n  replacing the old one. An unsigned flip on a shared board is unattributable.\n- **Status reflects reality.** `[x]` means verified, not \"should work\". `[-]`\n  means deliberately closed without doing it â€” never use it to hide a ticket\n  that is still real, and `[!]` is not a parking space: it names what it waits on.\n- **The board must drain.** A ticket is a named change, never a finding â€” see\n  the gate. If pending only ever grows, the bookkeeping has become the work:\n  stop logging and run the drain.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\tickets-zordon\\board\\artifact.mjs\r\n#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Bake TICKETS.md into a single self-contained HTML page for the Artifact tool.\n//\n//   node skills/tickets-zordon/board/artifact.mjs [TICKETS.md] [out.html]\n//\n// Defaults: ./TICKETS.md -> ./.tickets-board.html (gitignored).\n// Output is Artifact-shaped: no <!doctype>/<html>/<head>/<body>, the wrapper\n// supplies those. Self-check: node artifact.mjs --selftest\n\nimport { readFile, writeFile } from \"node:fs/promises\";\nimport { basename, join, resolve } from \"node:path\";\n\nconst STATES = {\n  \" \": [\"pending\", \"[ ]\"],\n  \"~\": [\"in-progress\", \"[~]\"],\n  \"!\": [\"blocked\", \"[!]\"],\n  \">\": [\"deferred\", \"[>]\"],\n  \"x\": [\"done\", \"[x]\"],\n  \"-\": [\"cancelled\", \"[-]\"],\n};\nconst GROUPS = [[\"B\", \"Bugs\"], [\"F\", \"Features\"], [\"A\", \"Agent-found\"]];\n// ponytail: anything outside B/F/A still has to appear somewhere â€” a ticket\n// counted in the chips but listed in no section is a lying header.\nconst inGroups = (id) => GROUPS.some(([p]) => id.startsWith(p));\n// ponytail: ID + em-dash + title is the whole line format the skill enforces.\n// A hyphen separator is tolerated because hand-typed tickets use one.\nconst LINE = /^(\\s*)- \\[([ x~!>-])\\]\\s+([A-Za-z]+\\d+[a-z]?)\\s*[â€”-]\\s*(.*)$/;\n\nexport function parse(md) {\n  const out = [];\n  for (const raw of md.split(/\\r?\\n/)) {\n    const m = LINE.exec(raw);\n    if (!m) continue;\n    const [, indent, ch, id, title] = m;\n    const state = STATES[ch];\n    if (!state) continue;\n    out.push({ id, title: title.trim(), status: state[0], glyph: state[1], sub: indent.length > 0 });\n  }\n  return out;\n}\n\nexport function tally(ts) {\n  const n = (s) => ts.filter((t) => t.status === s).length;\n  const c = {\n    pending: n(\"pending\"), inprogress: n(\"in-progress\"), blocked: n(\"blocked\"),\n    deferred: n(\"deferred\"), done: n(\"done\"), cancelled: n(\"cancelled\"),\n  };\n  c.open = c.pending + c.inprogress + c.blocked;\n  c.closed = c.done + c.cancelled;\n  c.total = c.open + c.deferred + c.closed;\n  return c;\n}\n\nconst esc = (s) => s.replace(/[&<>]/g, (c) => ({ \"&\": \"&amp;\", \"<\": \"&lt;\", \">\": \"&gt;\" }[c]));\n\nfunction countLine(c) {\n  return `${c.open} open (${c.pending} pending Â· ${c.inprogress} in-progress Â· ${c.blocked} blocked)` +\n    ` Â· ${c.deferred} deferred Â· ${c.closed} closed (${c.done} done Â· ${c.cancelled} cancelled)` +\n    ` Â· ${c.total} total`;\n}\n\nconst li = (t) =>\n  `<li class=\"s-${t.status.replace(\"-\", \"\")}${t.sub ? \" sub\" : \"\"}\">` +\n  `<span class=\"g\" aria-hidden=\"true\">${t.glyph}</span>` +\n  `<span class=\"id\">${esc(t.id)}</span>` +\n  `<span class=\"t\">${esc(t.title)}</span>` +\n  `<span class=\"st\">${t.status}</span></li>`;\n\nfunction section(name, ts) {\n  if (!ts.length) return \"\";\n  return `<section><h2>${name}<span class=\"n\">${ts.length}</span></h2>` +\n    `<ul>${ts.map(li).join(\"\")}</ul></section>`;\n}\n\nconst CHIPS = [\n  [\"pending\", \"pending\"], [\"inprogress\", \"in progress\"], [\"blocked\", \"blocked\"],\n  [\"deferred\", \"deferred\"], [\"done\", \"done\"], [\"cancelled\", \"cancelled\"],\n];\n\nfunction chips(c) {\n  const cells = CHIPS.map(([k, label]) =>\n    `<div class=\"chip c-${k}\"><span class=\"v\">${c[k]}</span><span class=\"k\">${label}</span></div>`\n  ).join(\"\");\n  return `<div class=\"counts\" data-count-line=\"${esc(countLine(c))}\">${cells}` +\n    `<div class=\"chip c-total\"><span class=\"v\">${c.total}</span><span class=\"k\">total</span></div></div>`;\n}\n\nexport function render(md, title) {\n  const ts = parse(md);\n  const c = tally(ts);\n  const isClosed = (t) => t.status === \"done\" || t.status === \"cancelled\";\n  const parentOf = (t) => t.id.replace(/[a-z]$/, \"\");\n  // A done sub-part of a live parent stays with the parent â€” filing it under\n  // \"closed\" strands it beside unrelated tickets with nothing to explain it.\n  const openParents = new Set(ts.filter((t) => !isClosed(t)).map((t) => t.id));\n  const isLive = (t) => !isClosed(t) || (t.sub && openParents.has(parentOf(t)));\n  const live = ts.filter(isLive);\n  const closed = ts.filter((t) => !isLive(t));\n  const sections = GROUPS.map(([p, name]) =>\n    section(name, live.filter((t) => t.id.startsWith(p)))).join(\"\") +\n    section(\"Other\", live.filter((t) => !inGroups(t.id)));\n  const body = sections || `<p class=\"empty\">Nothing open. The whole board is closed.</p>`;\n  const rest = closed.length\n    ? `<details><summary>${closed.length} closed â€” done and cancelled</summary>` +\n      `<ul>${closed.map(li).join(\"\")}</ul></details>`\n    : \"\";\n  return `<title>TICKETS Â· ${esc(title)}</title>\n<link rel=\"stylesheet\" href=\"https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=IBM+Plex+Sans:wght@400;500;600&family=JetBrains+Mono:wght@400;700&display=swap\">\n<style>\n:root{\n  --ground:#f4f5f2; --surface:#fbfbf9; Mono\",ui-monospace,SFMono-Regular,Consolas,monospace;\n}\n@media (prefers-color-scheme:dark){:root:not([data-theme=\"light\"]){\n  --ground:#141714; --surface:#191d19; --ink:#e7eae6; --mid:#9aa199; --faint:#6e756d;\n  --rule:#2a2f29; --amber:#e0a25c; --red:#f08b80;\n}}\n:root[data-theme=\"dark\"]{\n  --ground:#141714; --surface:#191d19; --ink:#e7eae6; --mid:#9aa199; --faint:#6e756d;\n  --rule:#2a2f29; --amber:#e0a25c; --red:#f08b80;\n}\n*{box-sizing:border-box}\nbody{background:var(--ground);color:var(--ink);font-family:var(--sans);font-size:14px;line-height:1.5;\n  padding:2.5rem .k{font-size:.6875rem;color:var(--faint);letter-spacing:.05em;text-transform:uppercase}\n.c-inprogress .v{color:var(--amber)}\n.c-blocked .v{color:var(--red)}\n.c-total{background:color-mix(in oklab,var(--ground) 60%,var(--surface))}\nsection{margin:0 0 2rem}\nh2{display:flex;align-items:baseline;gap:.5rem;font-family:var(--mono);font-size:.6875rem;font-weight:700;\n .n{font-weight:400;color:var(--faint);font-variant-numeric:tabular-nums}\nul{list-style:none;margin:0;padding:0}\nli{display:grid;grid-template-columns:1.9rem 3.4rem 1fr .id,.s-inprogress .g,.s-inprogress .st{color:var(--amber)}\n.s-blocked .id,.s-blocked .g,.s-blocked .st{color:var(--red)}\n.s-deferred .id,.s-deferred .t{color:var(--mid)}\n.s-done .t,.s-cancelled .t{color:var(--mid);text-decoration:line-through;text-decoration-color:var(--rule)}\ndetails{margin-top:2.5rem;border-top:1px solid <h1>TICKETS <span class=\"repo\">Â· ${esc(title)}</span></h1>\n  ${chips(c)}\n</header>\n${body}\n${rest}`;\n}\n\nconst ok = (cond, msg, got) => { if (!cond) { console.error(`selftest FAIL: ${msg}`, got ?? \"\"); process.exitCode = 1; } };\n\nfunction selftest() {\n  const md = `\n## Bugs\n- [x] B1 â€” Done thing\n- [~] B2 â€” Working <thing> & more\n  - [ ] B2a â€” sub part\n- [!] B3 â€” Stuck â€” unrecognised prefix\nnot a ticket line\n`;\n  const ts = parse(md);\n  const c = tally(ts);\n  ok(ts.length === 9, \"parsed 9\", ts.length);\n  ok(ts.find((t) => t.id === \"B2a\")?.sub === true,   md = await readFile(mdPath, \"utf8\");\n  } catch (e) {\n    console.error(`artifact: cannot read ${mdPath} (${e.code ?? e.message}).\n` +\n      `Run this from the repo root, or pass the board's
