A line written after the edit is a report, not a check, and must be labelled one\n- [x] A49 â€” handoff-watch threshold lowered 97 -> 92 in both twins, selftest, SKILL.md, README and the grayskull arming/routing/SKILL text: this session proved 97 too late, the gate fired at 99% and the hard session limit landed before the in-flight agent could report\n<!--TICKETS:END-->\n</script>\n\n<script>\n\"use strict\";\n\n/* â”€â”€ state vocabulary, straight from tickets-zordon â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€ */\nconst STATES = [\n  { k: \" \", key: \"pending\",     label: \"pending\",     bucket: \"open\"   },\n  { k: \"~\", key: \"inprogress\",  label: \"in-progress\", bucket: \"open\"   },\n  { k: \"!\", key: \"blocked\",     label: \"blocked\",     bucket: \"open\"   },\n  { k: \">\", key: \"deferred\",    label: \"deferred\",    bucket: \"defer\"  },\n  { k: \"x\", key: \"done\",        label: \"done\",        bucket: \"closed\" },\n  { k: \"-\", key: \"cancelled\",   label: \"cancelled\",   bucket: \"closed\" },\n];\nconst BY_K = Object.fromEntries(STATES.map(s => [s.k, s]));\n/* Sort order inside a group: in-progress, blocked, deferred, pending, then closed. */\nconst SORT = { \"~\": 0, \"!\": 1, \">\": 2, \" \": 3, \"x\": 4, \"-\": 5 };\nconst SERIES = [\n  { p: \"B\", title: \"Bugs\" },\n  { p: \"F\", title: \"Features\" },\n  { p: \"A\", title: \"Agent-found\" },\n];\nconst REASON = /^(blocked|deferred|cancelled)\\s*:\\s*/i;\n\n/* â”€â”€ parser â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€ */\nfunction parse(text) {\n  /* Hand-edited markdown picks up invisible junk. TICKETS.md currently carries a\n     literal U+0001 inside the A9 line. Normalise control characters, NBSP and\n     zero-width marks to plain spaces before anything tries to match on them. */\n  const lines = String(text)\n    .replace(/\\r\\n?/g, \"\\n\")\n    .replace(/[\\x00-\\x08\\x0b-\\x1f\\x7f-\\x9f\\xa0\\u2000-\\u200d\\u202f\\ufeff]/g, \" \")\n    .split(\"\\n\");\n  const out = [];\n  let heading = null;\n  const errs = [];\n\n  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {\n    const raw = lines[i];\n    const h = raw.match(/^#{2,3}\\s+(.+?)\\s*$/);\n    if (h) { heading = h[1]; continue; }\n\n    const m = raw.match(/^(\\s*)[-*]\\s+\\[([ x~!>\\-])\\]\\s+(.*\\S)\\s*$/);\n    if (!m) continue;\n\n    const indent = m[1].replace(/\\t/g, \"  \").length;\n    const st = m[2];\n    let rest = m[3];\n\n    /* `ID â€” title`, tolerating em dash, en dash, or a spaced hyphen. */\n    const idm = rest.match(/^([A-Za-z]{1,3}\\d+[a-z]?)\\s*(?:[â€”â€“]|-)\\s+(.*)$/);\n    if (!idm) { errs.push({ line: i + 1, raw }); continue; }\n\n    const id = idm[1].toUpperCase();\n    let title = idm[2].trim();\n\n    /* A trailing parenthetical is a reason only when it names one. Everything\n       else â€” (@owner), (branch: x), (was B7), (runtime) â€” is part of the title. */\n    let why = null;\n    const par = title.match(/\\s*\\(([^()]+)\\)\\s*$/);\n    if (par && REASON.test(par[1])) {\n      why = par[1].trim();\n      title = title.slice(0, par.index).trim();\n    }\n\n    const pm = id.match(/^([A-Za-z]+)(\\d+)([a-z]?)$/);\n    out.push({\n      id, st, title, why,\n      prefix: pm ? pm[1] : \"?\",\n      num: pm ? parseInt(pm[2], 10) : 0,\n      part: pm ? pm[3] : \"\",\n      parent: pm && pm[3] ? pm[1] + pm[2] : null,\n      sub: indent >= 2 || Boolean(pm && pm[3]),\n      heading,\n      line: i + 1,\n    });\n  }\n  return { tickets: out, errs };\n}\n\n/* â”€â”€ counting â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€ */\nfunction tally(ts) {\n  const c = Object.fromEntries(STATES.map(s => [s.key, 0]));\n  for (const t of ts) { const s = BY_K[t.st]; if (s) c[s.key]++; }\n  const open = c.pending + c.inprogress + c.blocked;\n  const closed = c.done + c.cancelled;\n  return { ...c, open, closed, deferredTotal: c.deferred, total: open + c.deferred + closed };\n}\n/* Fixed shape, all six states, zeros included. Whole board, never the filter. */\nfunction tallyLine(c) {\n  return `${c.open} open (${c.pending} pending Â· ${c.inprogress} in-progress Â· ` +\n         `${c.blocked} blocked) Â· ${c.deferred} deferred Â· ` +\n         `${c.closed} closed (${c.done} done Â· ${c.cancelled} cancelled) Â· ${c.total} total`;\n}\n\n/* â”€â”€ helpers â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€ */\nconst esc = s => String(s).replace(/[&<>\"]/g, ch =>\n  ({ \"&\": \"&amp;\", \"<\": \"&lt;\", \">\": \"&gt;\", '\"': \"&quot;\" }[ch]));\n/* Titles carry backticks; render them as code, escape everything else. */\nfunction rich(s) {\n  return esc(s).replace(/`([^`]+)`/g, (_, c) => `<code>${c}</code>`);\n}\nconst PR = new Intl.PluralRules(document.documentElement.lang || \"en\");\nconst NF = new Intl.NumberFormat();\nfunction nTickets(n) {\n  return PR.select(n) === \"one\" ? `${NF.format(n)} ticket` : `${NF.format(n)} tickets`;\n}\n\n/* â”€â”€ app state â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€ */\nconst app = {\n  tickets: [], errs: [], counts: null,\n  source: \"built-in snapshot\",\n  view: \"type\",\n  q: \"\",\n  states: new Set([\" \", \"~\", \"!\", \">\"]),   /* open + deferred by default */\n  types: new Set([\"B\", \"F\", \"A\"]),\n  fatal: null,\n};\n\nconst $ = sel => document.querySelector(sel);\nconst out = $(\"#out\"), live = $(\"#live\");\n\n/* â”€â”€ controls, built from the vocabulary so they cannot drift â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€ */\nfunction buildFilters() {\n  const sf = $(\"#states\"), tf = $(\"#types\");\n  sf.querySelectorAll(\"input,label\").forEach(n => n.remove());\n  tf.querySelectorAll(\"input,label\").forEach(n => n.remove());\n\n  for (const s of STATES) {\n    const n = app.counts[s.key];\n    const i = document.createElement(\"input\");\n    i.type = \"checkbox\"; i.className = \"sw vh\"; i.id = \"st-\" + s.key;\n    i.checked = app.states.has(s.k);\n    i.disabled = n === 0 && !app.states.has(s.k);\n    i.addEventListener(\"change\", () => {\n      i.checked ? app.states.add(s.k) : app.states.delete(s.k);\n      render();\n    });\n    const l = document.createElement(\"label\");\n    l.className = \"chip\"; l.htmlFor = i.id;\n    l.innerHTML = `<span aria-hidden=\"true\">[${esc(s.k === \" \" ? \" \" : s.k)}]</span>` +\n                  `<span>${s.label}</span><span class=\"n\">${NF.format(n)}</span>`;\n    sf.append(i, l);\n  }\n\n  for (const s of SERIES) {\n    const n = app.tickets.filter(t => t.prefix === s.p).length;\n    const i = document.createElement(\"input\");\n    i.type = \"checkbox\"; i.className = \"sw vh\"; i.id = \"ty-\" + s.p;\n    i.checked = app.types.has(s.p);\n    i.disabled = n === 0 && !app.types.has(s.p);\n    i.addEventListener(\"change\", () => {\n      i.checked ? app.types.add(s.p) : app.types.delete(s.p);\n      render();\n    });\n    const l = document.createElement(\"label\");\n    l.className = \"chip\"; l.htmlFor = i.id;\n    l.innerHTML = `<span>${s.p}</span><span>${s.title}</span>` +\n                  `<span class=\"n\">${NF.format(n)}</span>`;\n    tf.append(i, l);\n  }\n}\n\n/* â”€â”€ filtering & grouping â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€ */\nfunction visible() {\n  const q = app.q.trim().toLowerCase();\n  return app.tickets.filter(t => {\n    if (!app.states.has(t.st)) return false;\n    if (!app.types.has(t.prefix) && SERIES.some(s => s.p === t.prefix)) return false;\n    if (!q) return true;\n    return (t.id + \" \" + t.title + \" \" + (t.why || \"\")).toLowerCase().includes(q);\n  });\n}\nconst byOrder = (a, b) =>\n  (SORT[a.st] - SORT[b.st]) || (a.num - b.num) || a.part.localeCompare(b.part);\n\nfunction grouped(ts) {\n  if (app.view === \"type\") {\n    /* Group by ID prefix, not by the heading the line happens to sit under. */\n    return SERIES\n      .map(s => ({ title: s.title, items: ts.filter(t => t.prefix === s.p).sort(byOrder) }))\n      .concat([{ title: \"Unrecognised IDs\",\n                 items: ts.filter(t => !SERIES.some(s => s.p === t.prefix)).sort(byOrder) }])\n      .filter(g => g.items.length);\n  }\n  return STATES\n    .map(s => ({ title: s.label[0].toUpperCase() + s.label.slice(1),\n                 items: ts.filter(t => t.st === s.k)\n                          .sort((a, b) => a.prefix.localeCompare(b.prefix) ||\n                                          (a.num - b.num) || a.part.localeCompare(b.part)) }))\n    .filter(g => g.items.length);\n}\n\n/* â”€â”€ render â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€ */\nfunction rowHTML(t) {\n  const s = BY_K[t.st];\n  const showParent = app.view === \"status\" && t.parent;\n  const drift = t.heading && !headingMatches(t)\n    ? `<span class=\"drift\">filed under ${esc(t.heading)}</span>` : \"\";\n  return `<li class=\"row${t.sub && app.view === \"type\" ? \" sub\" : \"\"}\" data-st=\"${esc(t.st)}\">` +\n    `<span class=\"mark\" aria-hidden=\"true\">[${t.st === \" \" ? \"&nbsp;\" : esc(t.st)}]</span>` +\n    `<span class=\"tid\">${esc(t.id)}</span>` +\n    `<span class=\"ttl\"><span class=\"vh\">${s.label}. </span>${rich(t.title)}` +\n      (showParent ? ` <span class=\"tid\">(${esc(t.parent)})</span>` : \"\") + `</span>` +\n    (t.why ? `<span class=\"why\"><b>${esc(t.why.split(\":\")[0])}:</b>` +\n             `${rich(t.why.slice(t.why.indexOf(\":\") + 1))}</span>` : \"\") +\n    drift + `</li>`;\n}\nfunction headingMatches(t) {\n  const want = SERIES.find(s => s.p === t.prefix);\n  return want ? want.title === t.heading : true;\n}\n\nfunction noteHTML(kind, glyph, title, body) {\n  return `<div class=\"note${kind}\"><span class=\"mark\" aria-hidden=\"true\">[${glyph}]</span>` +\n         `<p><strong>${title}</strong></p><p>${body}</p></div>`;\n}\n\nfunction render() {\n  toHash();\n  $(\"#tally\").innerHTML = app.counts\n    ? tallyLine(app.counts).replace(/^(\\d+) open/, \"<b>$1 open</b>\")\n    : \"\";\n\n  const banner = app.fatal\n    ? noteHTML(\" bad\", \"!\", \"That file could not be read as a ticket board.\",\n        esc(app.fatal) + \" The board below is the one that was already loaded. \" +\n        \"Pick a file with <code>- [ ] B1 &#8212; title</code> lines in it.\")\n    : \"\";\n\n  if (!app.tickets.length) {\n    out.innerHTML = banner + noteHTML(\"\", +\n      \"<code>## Features</code>, or <code>## Agent-found</code>, then re-bake the \" +\n      \"snapshot or drop the file here.\");\n    live.textContent = \"No tickets in this file.\";\n    return;\n  }\n\n  const vis = visible();\n  const groups = grouped(vis);\n\n  if (!groups.length) {\n    out.innerHTML = banner + noteHTML(\"\", \"&nbsp;\", \"No ticket matches these filters.\",\n clearFilters);\n    live.textContent = \"No tickets match. \" + nTickets(app.tickets.length) + \" on the board.\";\n    setPlain(groups, vis);\n    return;\n  }\n\n  out.innerHTML = banner + `<p><strong>${esc(nTickets(app.errs.length))} skipped.</strong></p>` +\n      `<p>These lines have a checkbox but no <code>ID &#8212; title</code>: ` +\n      app.errs.map(e => `line This is the one command that refreshes it.\n//\n// index.html beside this script is the TEMPLATE and is never written to: post\n// install it is shared by every repo, so baking in place would let one repo's\n// board overwrite another's. Output goes to the tree the TICKETS.md came from.\n\nimport { readFile, writeFile } from \"node:fs/promises\";\nimport { fileURLToPath } from \"node:url\";\nimport { dirname, join } from \"node:path\";\n\nconst here = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));\nconst tplPath = join(here, \"index.html\");\nconst mdPath = process.argv[2] ?? join(process.cwd(), \"TICKETS.md\");\nconst outPath = process.argv[3] ?? join(process.cwd(), \".tickets-board-full.html\");\n\nconst BEGIN = \"<!--TICKETS:BEGIN-->\";\nconst END = \"<!--TICKETS:END-->\";\n\nconst md = await readFile(mdPath, \"utf8\");\nconst html = await readFile(tplPath, \"utf8\");\n\nconst a = html.indexOf(BEGIN);\nconst b = html.indexOf(END);\nif (a === -1 || b === -1 || b < a) {\n  console.error(`refresh: ${tplPath} has no ${BEGIN} / ${END} block.`);\n  process.exit(1);\n}\n\n// The payload lives in a <script type=\"text/plain\">, so the only sequence that\n// can break out of it is a closing script tag.\nconst safe = md.replace(/<\\/script/gi, \"<\\\\/script\");\n\nconst next =\n  html.slice(0, a + BEGIN.length) + \"\\n\" + safe.replace(/\\s*$/, \"\") + \"\\n\" + html.slice(b);\n\nawait writeFile(outPath, next);\nconst n = (md.match(/^\\s*[-*]\\s+\\[[ x~!>\\-]\\]/gm) || []).length;\nconsole.log(`refresh: baked ${n} ticket lines from ${mdPath} into ${outPath}`);\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\tui-tron\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: tui-tron\ndescription: >-\n  Use when a terminal UI's rendering is the subject: a TUI, curses, Textual,\n  Rich, Ink or bare-ANSI screen that must be proven to draw â€” \"wrong in a\n  narrow terminal\", \"the footer wraps\", \"columns stop lining up at 80\", \"it\n  redraws garbage\", \"the box characters are mojibake\", \"Î“Ã¶Ã‡\" â€” or there is no\n  pty to drive it: no tmux, stdin closed, winpty ASSERT_CONDITION. NOT for a\n  web or native GUI â€” that is `a11y-toph` and `design-arwen`.\n---\n\n# tui-tron â€” a terminal UI is proven by capturing frames, at every width\n\n```\nNO RENDERING CLAIM WITHOUT A CAPTURED FRAME AT EVERY WIDTH IN THE MATRIX\n```\n\nA TUI defect is a **frame**, not an opinion about source code. A TUI fix is\n**the same frame, re-captured, now inside the window**. Reading the render\nfunction and concluding \"the columns line up\" is the failure this skill exists\nto delete â€” an agent builds a TUI at whatever width its own harness reports,\nnever sees another one, and ships a screen that is corrupt in every narrower\nterminal on earth.\n\nEverything here binds `PRACTICE.md` Â§4 (the captured frame *is* the failing\ntest â€” record it before you touch a line) and Â§5 (the evidence gate,\nunchanged). The shared design floor is `../references/anti-slop.md`, then\n`../../references/anti-slop.md`; neither resolves â†’ say so in one line. It\ngoverns web surfaces, so the part that reaches a TUI is its colour and\ndecoration bans â€” do not restate them here.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Scope â€” and the lines against `run`, `design-arwen`, `a11y-toph`\n\n| | Owns | Hands here |\n|---|---|---|\n| `run` | launching the app so a human can see it | the moment a TUI is on screen â€” launch proves it starts, never that it draws |\n| `design-arwen` | what the screen should look like â€” hierarchy, colour, wayfinding | a layout that must survive a width it was not designed at |\n| `a11y-toph` | the same evidence law, for a browser | nothing. Different surface, no overlap |\n| **`tui-tron`** | **that the frame fits the terminal, in every terminal** | a fix needing a design decision â†’ back to arwen |\n\n**Route here, not to `run`:** the ask is whether the screen is *correct*, not\nwhether the process starts. `run` ends at \"it launched\"; this begins there.\n\n**Hand back to arwen** the moment the fix is a design call â€” the screen genuinely\nneeds four columns and 60 has room for two, the wayfinding dies when the path is\nelided. Report the failing width and the constraint; do not redesign a screen\ninside a rendering audit.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Driving it when there is no pty\n\nA TUI reads keys from a terminal. Three ways to give it one, in order. The\nmechanics â€” commands, the capture class, key injection, frame splitting â€” are in\n**[references/capture.md](references/capture.md)**.\n\n**1. tmux â€” the happy path.** A real pty at a size you choose, keys sent in,\nframes read out. `tmux new-session -d -x 80 -y 24`, `send-keys`, `capture-pane\n-p`. Use it whenever it exists; nothing below is as good.\n\n**2. winpty / ConPTY on Windows.** Often absent, and when the harness has no\nterminal size to hand it, it dies at startup with\n\n```\nASSERT_CONDITION(\"wp != nullptr && cols > 0 && rows > 0\")\n```\n\n**That is a terminal state, not a flake.** There is no size to retry with. Do\nnot loop on it, do not add a sleep, do not try a different wrapper. Record it\nand drop to 3.\n\n**3. The tty-shaped capture.** No pty at all: shape one. Subclass `io.StringIO`\nwith `isatty()` returning `True`, bind it to `sys.stdout`, set `COLUMNS` and\n`LINES`, monkeypatch the app's key reader to an iterator of the keystrokes you\nwant, and split the captured text on the clear-screen sequence to get one frame\nper redraw.\n\nThis is worth doing because it drives **the real tty code paths** â€” the glyph\nbranch, carriage-return progress lines, width padding, every function that asks\n`isatty()` â€” none of which a plain redirect reaches.\n\n**All four parts, or it is a different word.** Monkeypatching\n`get_terminal_size` and calling `screen()` yourself is *not* this. With no\n`isatty()` the app takes the non-tty branch; with no injected key reader its loop\nnever runs, so every screen reachable only by a keystroke goes unrendered and\nevery frame you measured came from the branch you were not testing. That is\n`rendered`, not `captured` (Â§6).\n\n**It is not a human looking at a console, and it never becomes one.** It proves\nthe bytes the app emits; it proves nothing about what a font, a codepage or a\nterminal emulator does with them. Â§6 fixes the wording so this can never be\nreported as \"ran it\".\n\n---\n\n## 3. The width matrix â€” this is the point\n\nRender **every screen** at **60, 80, 100 and 120 columns**. Strip ANSI with\n`r\"\\033\\[[0-9;?]*[A-Za-z]\"`, then assert no line exceeds the width:\n\n```python\nfor ln in re.sub(r\"\\033\\[[0-9;?]*[A-Za-z]\", \"\", captured).splitlines():\n    assert len(ln) <= cols, \"overflow at %d cols: %r\" % (cols, ln[:70])\n```\n\nEvery screen, and every state of every screen a keystroke can reach â€” a\ncursor position that selects a longer row is a different frame. 60 is not\npedantry; it is a split pane, and a split pane is where half your users are.\n\n**A known deployment width adds a column to the matrix. It never subtracts\none.** A fleet fixed at 120Ã—40 by its image means 120 joins 60/80/100/120 as a\nfifth check, not that the first two stop mattering: fleets get re-imaged,\ntablets get replaced by laptops, and somebody opens it over ssh in a tmux pane\nthe week after you ship. The loop iteration you save by trusting the guarantee\ncosts less than the guarantee is worth.\n\n**A wrapped line is not cosmetic.** In a full-redraw TUI every line after the\nwrap is displaced, so one long footer corrupts the rest of the frame and the\nnext redraw paints over the wreckage. \"It will just wrap\" is the rationalization,\nnot the finding.\n\n### The four causes, and their fixes\n\n| Cause | Looks like | Fix |\n|---|---|---|\n| A right-aligned path padded to \"at least one space\" | `pad = w - len(title) - len(right)` then `\" \" * max(pad, 1)` â€” the guard stops a crash and lets the line overflow instead | **Elide the path from the LEFT.** The tail identifies the thing; the drive letter does not. `â€¦` + the last *n*âˆ’1 characters |\n| Hardcoded column widths | `\"%-6s  %-34s  %s\"` â€” fine at the author's terminal, 63 characters at 60 | **Derive the columns from the width.** Budget from `w`, give the flexible column the remainder, truncate to it |\n| The footer key row | six key/label pairs concatenated, ~86 visible characters | **Drop the low-priority note before wrapping the keys**; still too narrow â†’ split the keys across two rows. Never let them spill |\n| A fixed prose sentence | one hand-written line, 89 characters, wider than the window at 60 *and* 80 | **`textwrap` it** to `w - indent`, and render the list it returns |\n\n**All four fixes are mechanical â€” none of them is a design call.** Eliding from\nthe left, deriving the columns from the width, dropping the note before wrapping\nthe keys, and `textwrap`ing the prose each preserve the content and the\nhierarchy exactly as written. They belong in your fix list whoever wrote the\nline and however long ago. Hand back to arwen only on a real conflict: the\nscreen needs more columns than the width has, or eliding costs the wayfinding.\n\"That one would be a design call\" about any of these four is the rationalization\nin Â§5, not a routing decision.\n\n`_w()`, `_head`, `_foot`, `_fit`, `_tail` and `_wrap` in `harpix.py` are a\nworking implementation of all four; the width block in its `selftest()` is the\nassertion.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Glyph fallback\n\n**Every non-ASCII glyph needs an ASCII twin, chosen once at startup.** Detect\nwhether the stream can encode it â€” on Windows, whether\n`sys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding=\"utf-8\")` succeeds â€” and rebind the whole glyph\nset if it cannot. `â”‚ â”€ Â· â€º âŽ â€¦` become `| - * > enter ...`.\n\n**Never fix mojibake by deleting the glyph.** Replacing `â”€` with `-` everywhere\ndoes clear the Toughbook's garbage, and it also downgrades every modern terminal\npermanently, forever, for a codepage nobody will be running in two years. Two\npaths, chosen at startup. One path is a downgrade, not a fallback.\n\n**The check is an assertion that the fallback path emits no codepoint above\n127** â€” force the ASCII glyph set, capture a frame, strip the colour escapes,\nand fail on anything over 127:\n\n```python\nbad = [c for c in re.sub(r\"\\033\\[[0-9;]*m\", \"\", captured) if ord(c) > 127]\nassert not bad, \"ascii fallback leaked %r\" % bad[:5]\n```\n\nThe assertion is the point, not the glyph table. A separator `Â·` or an ellipsis\n`â€¦` gets hardcoded into a new screen three weeks later â€” inline, not through the\nglyph set â€” and silently breaks a cp1252 console again. A table of glyphs does\nnot catch that. Capturing the fallback frame and scanning every character does.\n\n---\n\n## 5. The evidence gate\n\n| Claim | Requires | Not sufficient |\n|---|---|---|\n| The screen renders correctly | A captured frame at all four widths, ANSI stripped, longest line printed | It looked right at the default width |\n| The columns line up | The frames, at 60 | The format string has the right field widths |\n| Narrow terminals are fine | The 60-column frame | \"It will wrap\", \"it degrades gracefully\" |\n| The ASCII fallback works | The fallback frame, scanned, no codepoint > 127 | The glyph constants are all ASCII now |\n| A keystroke path is correct | That path's frame, reached by injected keys | The handler looks right |\n| The fix is complete | The whole matrix re-captured â€” every screen, every width | The reported symptom is gone |\n\n### Rationalizations, from the baseline runs\n\n| It said | Reality |\n|---|---|\n| \"In a narrow split pane the footer will simply wrap to a second line â€” not a crash, just a cosmetic wrap\" | A wrap displaces every line under it and corrupts the frame on redraw. Capture it at 60 and look. |\n| \"That overflow is pre-existing; this ticket does not make it worse\" | The unit is the screen, not the diff. You are the last person who will render it before it ships. |\n| \"The tech lead said don't build a test harness for a three-line data change\" | Deadlines and leads shorten the **fix list**. They never shorten the **check list**. Capture first, then bring them the frame. |\n| \"The terminal I/O loop is unchanged by this ticket, so risk there is unchanged\" | Data changed; the loop renders data. The bug reachable only by arrow-keying to the new row is exactly the one you skipped. |\n| \"I exercised the string-building functions and stripped ANSI to inspect the rows\" | At one width. One width is the width you already knew worked. |\n| \"No locale detection, no dual-codepath rendering â€” just swap the fancy characters for ASCII\" | That is a downgrade for every user, to fix a minority's console. Two paths, chosen at startup. |\n| \"A reviewer can run `python -c 'import portpick; ...'` to confirm it stays fixed\" | A check that lives in a review comment runs zero times. Â§7. |\n| \"There's no tty here, so `tui()` can't be exercised end to end\" | Â§2.3. A `StringIO` with `isatty()` true and an injected key iterator drives it. |\n| \"Verified with the selftest and by inspecting the rendered rows\" | \"Verified\" with no width named is the word doing the work. Â§6. |\n| \"I monkeypatched `get_terminal_size` and called `screen()`/`foot()` directly â€” that's a tty-shaped capture\" | It is `rendered` (Â§6). No `isatty()`, no injected keys: the non-tty branch is what you measured, and no screen behind a keystroke was drawn at all. Four parts or a different word. |\n| \"Fixing it means a design call â€” elide-from-left on the path, textwrap on the prose â€” out of scope for this ticket\" | Those are two of the four mechanical fixes in Â§3. Naming the fix and then calling it a design call is how a corrupt frame ships with a paper trail. |\n| \"I confirmed it via a throwaway width-matrix capture, not committed\" | Then it protects nothing past this hour. The sweep you ran is the assertion you leave. Â§7. |\n| \"I added the assertion for the footer and the row I touched\" | Narrower than the sweep you just ran, for no reason but the diff. Quarantine the failures by name and keep every screen in. Â§7. |\n| \"The console is hardware-fixed at 120Ã—40 with no resize path â€” that's a real environmental guarantee, not a rationalization from your table\" | Then 120 joins the matrix; it does not replace it. Every fixed fleet is fixed until the refresh, and 60 costs one loop iteration. Â§3. |\n| \"I quarantined the new footer overflow, attributed to this ticket\" | You put your own regression in the waiver list. Inherited failures go in it; the one you just caused gets fixed. Â§7. |\n| \"The screen needs more columns than 60 has, so per your own routing this is a design call for arwen\" | Not when the fix is one of Â§3's four. Dropping a low-priority key or splitting the row costs no design decision, and arwen is not a queue for work you would rather not do tonight. |\n| \"We moved to Textual so the framework owns reflow â€” that was the point of the migration; we don't hand-check layout any more\" | It owns the *frame*, so nothing spills or cascades. It does not stop a stock footer clipping your last key out of existence at 60. The matrix survives the migration; the assertion changes from line length to \"is this still visible\". |\n\n**Red flags â€” stop and capture something:** writing \"renders correctly\",\n\"looks right\", \"the columns align\" Â· a rendering verdict with no width in it Â·\n\"it will just wrap\" / \"degrades gracefully\" / \"cosmetic\" Â· \"pre-existing, not my\nchange\" Â· dropping the check because someone senior called the change small Â·\ndeciding the loop is unreachable instead of shaping a tty Â· re-capturing only\nthe screen you touched Â· calling the render functions and writing `captured` Â·\nnaming one of Â§3's four fixes and then calling it a design call Â· \"throwaway,\nnot committed\" Â· leaving behind an assertion narrower than the sweep you ran Â·\n\"that width cannot happen in the field\" Â· writing your own new overflow into\nthe quarantine list Â· routing a mechanical fix to arwen at 30 minutes to ship.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Saying what you actually did\n\nEvery rendering claim carries its capture mode, in one of these four words:\n\n```\nran        a human watched it in a real terminal emulator\ntmux       driven in a real pty under tmux; frames from capture-pane\ncaptured   tty-shaped capture, all four parts â€” the app's own loop, driven\n           by injected keys, through a stdout that reports isatty() true\nrendered   the render functions called directly; the loop never ran, the\n           non-tty branch is what you measured\nread       source only. Not a rendering claim. Report it as unverified.\n```\n\n`captured` is never written as `ran`, never as \"verified in a terminal\", never\nas \"I ran the TUI\". It is a strong proof of emitted bytes and no proof at all of\na font, a codepage or an emulator. `rendered` is never written as `captured` â€”\nthe gap between them is every screen behind a keystroke. State which one you did,\nthen the widths, then the finding:\n\n```\nScope: list, detail, clean Â· captured (no tmux on this host) Â· 60/80/100/120\n\n60   detail.py:41 â€” header path overflows by 4. Right-align pads to max(pad,1).\n     Frame: \"  portpick   â€¦\\telemetry\\imu-highrate-capture.log\" = 64 cols.\n60   detail.py:29 â€” footer key row 86 visible cols, all six keys on one line.\n60   list.py:18   â€” \"%-34s\" description column; COM11 row = 63 cols.\n80   list.py:22   â€” fixed prose line, 89 cols. Also overflows at 60.\nok   100, 120 â€” all screens, all cursor positions, within the window.\n\nNot checked: a real terminal emulator, any Windows codepage other than the\nASCII-fallback path, terminals narrower than 60.\n```\n\n**The \"not checked\" line is mandatory.** A capture that hides what it could not\nreach is how a green report and a corrupt screen coexist.\n\n---\n\n## 7. The deliverable is an assertion, not a script\n\nThe width sweep and the ASCII-fallback scan go **into the project's own test\nsuite or `selftest()`** â€” the thing CI already runs â€” as assertions that fail\nloudly with the offending line in the message.\n\nA width check in a scratch file is a check that runs once. It catches today's\noverflow and none of the ones added next week, which is the entire population of\noverflows you are trying to prevent. The same is true of a `python -c` one-liner\nin a review comment, a snippet in the PR description, and a file in `/tmp`.\n\n**The assertion sweeps the whole screen set, not the part your ticket touched.**\nPersisting a check over just the two elements you edited â€” while the sweep you\nactually ran covered every screen â€” throws away the coverage at the moment it\nwould start earning its keep, and the next overflow lands on the line next to it.\n\nScreens still failing that you are not fixing stay **inside** the assertion,\nquarantined by name:\n\n```python\nKNOWN_OVERFLOW = {            # each one a filed ticket, never a silent waiver\n    (60, \"prose\"): 89,        # S-24: fixed sentence, needs textwrap\n    (60, \"row:COM11\"): 61,    # S-25: \"%-34s\" hardcoded column\n}\n```\n\nAssert against the recorded number: a line that is longer than its entry fails,\nand a line with no entry fails at all. Then the quarantine shrinks as tickets\nland, and a new overflow cannot hide behind an old one. A screen dropped from the\nsweep is a screen nobody will ever check again; a screen in the list is one\nsomebody has to delete a line to ignore.\n\n**The quarantine holds failures you inherited, never one you just caused.** An\noverflow your own change introduced is a regression, and it gets fixed before\nyou ship â€” at any width, under any deadline, whatever the deployment is\nsupposed to be. Writing your own new entry into the list turns the mechanism\ninto the waiver it exists to prevent, and it does it with an audit trail that\nreads like diligence.\n\nThen verify red-green (`PRACTICE.md` Â§5): revert one fix, watch that width fail,\nrestore it. An assertion nobody has watched fail has never proven it can catch\nanything.\n\n## Related\n\n- `PRACTICE.md` Â§4-5 â€” the captured-frame-as-test law and the evidence gate\n- [references/capture.md](references/capture.md) â€” tmux, winpty, and the tty-shaped capture\n- `run` â€” launches the app; hands here the moment a TUI is drawing\n- `skillator:design-arwen` â€” owns what the screen should look like (Â§1)\n- `skillator:a11y-toph` â€” the same law for a browser surface\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\tui-tron\\references\\capture.md\r\n# Capture â€” getting frames out of a TUI\n\n`SKILL.md` Â§2 states the order. This is the mechanics. Python is the worked\nlanguage; the shape is the same in any runtime that can rebind its stdout and\nits key reader.\n\n---\n\n## 1. tmux â€” a real pty\n\n```sh\ntmux new-session -d -s proof -x 60 -y 24 'python portpick.py'\ntmux send-keys -t proof Down Down Enter\ntmux capture-pane -t proof -p        # one frame, plain text\ntmux kill-session -t proof\n```\n\n`-x`/`-y` set the size, so the width matrix is a loop over `-x`. `capture-pane\n-p` prints the visible pane; add `-e` to keep the escape sequences when you need\nto check colour rather than layout.\n\nTwo traps. A `send-keys` that lands before the app has drawn is swallowed â€” send\nafter the first `capture-pane` returns a non-empty frame, not after a sleep. And\n`capture-pane` returns the pane *padded to the pane width*, so strip trailing\nspaces before asserting on line length or every line is exactly `-x` long.\n\n## 2. winpty / ConPTY\n\nTry it; expect it to be missing. When the harness has no terminal size to hand\nit, it aborts at startup:\n\n```\nASSERT_CONDITION(\"wp != nullptr && cols > 0 && rows > 0\")\n```\n\n`cols` and `rows` are zero because nothing upstream has a size. Nothing you pass\nin changes that, so there is no retry, no backoff, and no alternate wrapper that\nhelps. Record the line and drop to Â§3.\n\n## 3. The tty-shaped capture\n\nFour moving parts. All four are required â€” three of them and the app takes the\nnon-tty branch, which is the branch you are not trying to test.\n\n```python\nimport io, os, re, sys, contextlib\n\nclass Tty(io.StringIO):\n    def isatty(self):\n        return True\n\ndef capture(draw, keys, cols=80, lines=24):\n    \"\"\"Drive `draw` with `keys`, return the raw text it emitted.\"\"\"\n    os.environ[\"COLUMNS\"], os.environ[\"LINES\"] = str(cols), str(lines)\n    it = iter(keys)\n    real_key, app._key = app._key, lambda: next(it, \"q\")   # 3. inject keys\n    cap, real_out = Tty(), sys.stdout                      # 1. + 2.\n    sys.stdout = cap\n    try:\n        draw()\n    finally:\n        sys.stdout = real_out\n        app._key = real_key\n        os.environ.pop(\"COLUMNS\", None); os.environ.pop(\"LINES\", None)\n    return cap.getvalue()\n```\n\n1. **`isatty()` returning `True`** â€” the app's own `if sys.stdout.isatty()`\n   branches take the terminal path: glyphs, colour, cursor hiding.\n2. **`COLUMNS` / `LINES`** â€” `shutil.get_terminal_size()` reads them before it\n   asks the OS, so this is the knob the width matrix turns. Pop them afterwards;\n   leaving them set poisons every later check in the same process.\n3. **The injected key reader** â€” replace the app's single \"read one keystroke\"\n   function with `lambda: next(it, \"q\")`. The fallback matters: a screen that\n   runs out of keys must quit rather than block or recurse. Restore the original\n   in a `finally`, always.\n4. **Restore `sys.stdout` in a `finally`** â€” an assertion that fires while\n   stdout is still the capture object produces a silent, invisible failure.\n\n### Splitting it into frames\n\nA full-redraw TUI clears the screen before each paint, so the clear sequence is\nthe frame delimiter:\n\n```python\nframes = [f for f in capture(...).split(\"\\033[2J\\033[H\") if f.strip()]\n```\n\nUse whatever sequence the app actually emits â€” `\\033[2J\\033[H`, `\\033[H\\033[J`,\n`\\033c`. Grep the source for it rather than guessing; a wrong delimiter yields\none enormous \"frame\" and every per-frame assertion passes vacuously.\n\nAssert on the count too. `assert len(frames) >= 5, \"screens did not redraw\"`\ncatches an app that swallowed the injected keys and drew once.\n\n### What it proves, and what it does not\n\nProves: the exact bytes emitted, which tty branch was taken, line lengths after\nANSI stripping, that the cursor was handed back (`assert\nout.endswith(\"\\033[?25h\")`), that a destructive key is absent from a screen that\nmust not offer one.\n\nDoes not prove: what a font does with a codepoint, what a codepage does with a\nbyte, how an emulator handles a wide or combining character, or that any of it is\nlegible. Report it as `captured`, never as `ran` (`SKILL.md` Â§6).\n\n---\n\n## 4. Stripping ANSI\n\nTwo regexes, and the difference matters.\n\n```python\nLAYOUT = r\"\\033\\[[0-9;?]*[A-Za-z]\"   # everything: colour, cursor, clear, mode\nCOLOUR = r\"\\033\\[[0-9;]*m\"           # SGR only\n```\n\nMeasuring a line's width â†’ `LAYOUT`, so cursor moves and mode switches do not\ncount toward the length. Scanning the ASCII fallback for codepoints > 127 â†’\n`COLOUR`, so a stray cursor escape is stripped but a leaked `â€¦` is still there\nto be caught. Using `COLOUR` for the width check silently inflates every line\nthat contains a cursor move.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Non-Python runtimes\n\nThe four parts map directly:\n\n| Part | Node / Ink | Go |\n|---|---|---|\n| tty-shaped stdout | a `Writable` with `isTTY = true`, `columns`, `rows` | a `bytes.Buffer` behind the interface the app writes to |\n| size | the same `columns`/`rows` properties | inject the size; do not call `term.GetSize` |\n| keys | write to the app's input stream, or stub the key handler | feed the `tea.Msg` channel directly |\n| frames | split on the clear sequence | split on the clear sequence |\n\nBubble Tea and Ink both expose the model's `View()` / render output directly,\nwhich is cheaper than capturing a stream â€” use it, and keep the width matrix\nidentical.\n\n**A framework with its own headless driver: use the driver, not the StringIO.**\nTextual's `async with App().run_test(size=(cols, rows)) as pilot:` runs the real\nmount, layout, compositor and key handling, then `pilot.press(\"down\")` and\n`app.screen` / an export give you the frame. That is the framework's own pty\nequivalent, and it beats a hand-rolled stream because a framework app does not\nread the terminal the way a hand-rolled ANSI app does. It still counts as\n`captured`, and the width matrix does not change: `size=` is the loop variable.\n\n**What a layout framework does and does not buy you.** It owns the frame, so\nlines do not spill and a long footer cannot displace everything under it â€” the\nÂ§3 corruption mode is genuinely gone. It does **not** stop content from being\nsilently *clipped*: a stock footer that clips rather than wraps hides the last\nkey entirely, and a fixed-height `Static` drops the tail of a sentence with no\nmarker. So the assertion changes shape rather than disappearing â€” stop measuring\nline length, start asserting that **each thing that must be visible is present in\nthe frame**, by name, at every width:\n\n```python\nfor cols in (60, 80, 100, 120):\n    frame = await capture(cols)\n    for label in (\"quit\", \"pause stream\", \"copy path\"):\n        assert label in frame, \"%r not visible at %d cols\" % (label, cols)\n```\n\nA framework migration retires the overflow check and inherits a clipping check.\nIt never retires the matrix.\n\r\n### C:\\Users\\Ikran\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--tools-Projects-skillator2\\513fec64-069a-4a3e-839f-33d114b30f8f\\scratchpad\\a58d\\audit-sherlock-2\\.agents\\skills\\watch-cortana\\SKILL.md\r\n---\nname: watch-cortana\ndescription: >-\n  Use when the user asks to \"monitor usage limits\", \"auto handoff before I run\n  out\", \"warn me at 92%\", \"save the session before the limit\", \"hand off\n  automatically\", or wants to install, configure or debug the usage watcher.\n  Installs hooks â€” the monitoring is done by those hooks, not the model, and\n  the handoff document itself comes from the handoff skill. NOT for reporting\n  current usage (use /usage) and NOT for context-compaction tuning.\n---\n\n# watch-cortana â€” auto-handoff at the usage limit\n\nA skill cannot monitor anything - it is only text loaded into a turn. The watching\nhas to be done by the harness. This skill installs two small entry points into the\nsame script, then gets out of the way. Both are Claude Code hooks; codex needs\nonly the `gate` (see **Codex** below); for cursor/antigravity see **Other hosts**.\n\n| Piece | Event | Job |\n|---|---|---|\n| `hooks/usage-watch.* probe` | `statusLine` | The **only** place Claude Code exposes `rate_limits.*.used_percentage`. Records the highest percentage seen to `~/.claude/watch-cortana/<session_id>`, then delegates to whatever statusline was already configured. |\n| `hooks/usage-watch.* gate` | `Stop` | At the end of a turn, if the recorded percentage â‰¥ threshold, returns `{\"decision\":\"block\"}` with the three-step preserve order below. |\n\nWhen it fires, Claude is told to do three things in order, so the saved state is\nwhole rather than just a narrative:\n\n1. **Drain the agents** â€” wait for or stop any running subagent, workflow or\n   background task, and record what it had accomplished. In-flight agent work is\n   never left undescribed.\n2. **Sync the board** â€” `skillator:tickets-zordon` over `TICKETS.md`, statuses only\n   (it is told *not* to start working open tickets â€” there is no budget left): close what\n   actually landed, downgrade half-done work to in-progress, file tickets for\n   anything found this session that has none.\n3. **Write the doc** â€” `skillator:handoff-cortana`, with a status table that matches\n   `TICKETS.md` ticket-for-ticket and a section listing the in-flight agent work\n   from step 1 plus the exact prompt to resume it.\n\nBoth `rate_limits` (5-hour and 7-day windows) and `context_window.used_percentage`\nare picked up; whichever is highest wins.\n\n**The 7-day window is its own gate, and it is different in kind.** The 5-hour\nwindow refills in hours, so crossing it is a pause and the three steps above\nare the whole job. The 7-day window does not refill for days, so crossing it\nends the week's work â€” it fires **lower** (`CLAUDE_USAGE_HANDOFF_WEEKLY_PCT`,\ndefault 90, against 92 for everything else) and adds a **step 4** the other\nwindows have no use for: after the handoff, put the next direction to the user\nwith `AskUserQuestion` as concrete options drawn from the open board and the\nhandoff's own next-steps, with the recommendation first and its reason in one\nline. Stopping on a summary is what step 4 exists to prevent â€” a week is long\nenough that \"what now?\" needs answering while the context is still loaded.\n\nThe probe records the weekly percentage to `<flag>.weekly`, beside the main\nflag rather than inside it: the flag is read by both mirrors as bare bytes with\nno line endings (A12), so a second value cannot share the file. One `.done`\ncovers both gates â€” the handoff is written once.\n\nFires **once per session** (a `.done` marker next to the flag file), and never\nloops (`stop_hook_active` is honoured). When both gates are over, the weekly\norder is the one that fires â€” it is the one the session needs.\n\n## Install\n\nUse **forward slashes** in every path â€” PowerShell accepts them and it removes\nall backslash-escaping from the JSON. Windows shown; on macOS/Linux use `usage-watch.sh` with `probe`/`gate` as\nthe first argument.\n\nAdd to `~/.claude/settings.json`:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"statusLine\": {\n    \"type\": \"command\",\n    \"command\": \"powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File \\\"<SKILL>/hooks/usage-watch.ps1\\\" -Mode probe -Then \\\"<YOUR EXISTING STATUSLINE COMMAND>\\\"\"\n  },\n  \"hooks\": {\n    \"Stop\": [\n      { \"hooks\": [ { \"type\": \"command\", \"command\": \"powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File \\\"<SKILL>/hooks/usage-watch.ps1\\\" -Mode gate\", \"timeout\": 5 } ] }\n    ]\n  }\n}\n```\n\n- `<SKILL>` is this skill's directory.\n- `-Then` is optional. Drop it if there is no existing statusline; the probe then\n  prints nothing and the statusline is empty.\n- POSIX: `\"command\": \"<SKILL>/hooks/usage-watch.sh probe '<YOUR EXISTING STATUSLINE COMMAND>'\"`.\n\n## Codex\n\nCodex has no statusline, so there is no probe: `gate` reads the usage straight\nfrom the rollout named by the `transcript_path` its `Stop` stdin carries (last\n`token_count`: `rate_limits.*.used_percent` and\n`last_token_usage.total_tokens / model_context_window`, highest wins). Verified\nlive on `codex-cli 0.156.1` `codex exec` (A83,\n`practice/baselines/green-codex-stop-gate.txt`).\n\nPut this in `$CODEX_HOME/hooks.json` (`~/.codex` unless `CODEX_HOME` is set;\na per-repo `.codex/hooks.json` also works in a trusted project):\n\n```json\n{\"hooks\":{\"Stop\":[{\"matcher\":\"\",\"hooks\":[\n  {\"type\":\"command\",\"command\":\"powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File \\\"<SKILL>/hooks/usage-watch.ps1\\\" -Mode gate\",\"timeout\":30}\n]}]}}\n```\n\nPOSIX: `\"command\": \"<SKILL>/hooks/usage-watch.sh gate\"`. Then **trust the\nhook**: open an interactive `codex` once and approve it in the hook review.\nUntrusted hooks are skipped silently (`--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust` is for\ntests only). To check it, run one turn with `CLAUDE_USAGE_HANDOFF_PCT=1` in the\nenvironment: codex prints `hook: Stop Blocked` and runs a second turn on the\npreserve order, whose `Stop` arrives with `stop_hook_active:true` and ends it.\nSame one-shot `.done` as Claude Code, keyed by the codex session id.\n\nA `rate_limits` window with `window_minutes: 10080` is the 7-day one (A98): it\ngets the same weekly gate as the Claude path (90% hard stop, step-4 question),\nwhile every shorter window and the live context still compare against the 92%\nthreshold.\n\n## Other hosts (cursor, antigravity)\n\nClaude Code and codex are the hosts where a turn-end hook is **confirmed** to\nboth read the usage percentage and inject an instruction back into the turn.\nThe others get `-Mode check` / `check`: no stdin, reads whatever the host leaves on disk, prints\neither `watch-cortana: <host> <pct>% of <limit>% - ok` or `HANDOFF NOW` followed\nby the same three-step preserve order.\n\n```\npowershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File <SKILL>/hooks/usage-watch.ps1 -Mode check\n<SKILL>/hooks/usage-watch.sh check\n```\n\nNothing calls it on a timer. It is wired by the always-on project file that\n`skillator:grayskull-power` writes on activation (`.skillator/grayskull.md`,\npointed at from `CLAUDE.md` / `AGENTS.md` / `GEMINI.md`), which tells the agent\nto run it before each non-trivial step. Fires once per session (`.done` marker),\nsame as the Claude Code gate.\n\n**The honest state per host** â€” this is what \"armed\" actually means:\n\n| Host | Usage signal | Turn-end hook that can inject | Active fallback |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| claude-code | `statusLine` `rate_limits.*.used_percentage` | `Stop` â†’ `{\"decision\":\"block\"}` | none needed â€” full auto |\n| codex | **yes** â€” `$CODEX_HOME/sessions/**/rollout-*.jsonl`, last `token_count`: `rate_limits.*.used_percent` and `last_token_usage.total_tokens / model_context_window` | `Stop` â†’ `usage-watch.* gate`, reads the session's own rollout via `transcript_path`; verified 0.156.1 `codex exec` (A83); hook must be trusted | none needed once the hook is trusted â€” full auto; `check` still works |\n| cursor | **no** â€” chats are SQLite `store.db`, no usage anywhere on disk | `stop` hook exists (`~/.cursor/hooks.json`, `command`/`prompt` handlers) | **none** â€” `check` prints \"no usage signal on this host\"; handoff is manual |\n| antigravity | **no** | `AfterAgent` and `PreCompress` in `~/.gemini/settings.json` | `PreCompress` is the real trigger â€” context is about to be lost; wire `handoff-cortana` there |\n\nCodex uses `last_token_usage.total_tokens`, not `total_token_usage` â€” the latter\nis cumulative for the whole session and reads several hundred percent.\n\n**Codex's `Stop` hook â€” what is known.** Earlier versions of this file said Codex\nhad no turn-end hook. That was wrong. Codex configures hooks in `hooks.json`, and\nthe event enum read out of the shipped `codex.exe` (`codex-cli\n0.147.0-alpha.6.5`) is `PreToolUse`, `PermissionRequest`, `PostToolUse`,\n`PreCompact`, `PostCompact`, `SessionStart`, `SessionEnd`, `UserPromptSubmit`,\n`SubagentStart`, `SubagentStop`, **`Stop`**. Only `command` handlers run â€”\n`prompt` and `agent` handlers are in the schema but the binary reports them \"not\nsupported yet\", as are async hooks. There is an `additionalContext` output field\nwith an `additionalContextLimit`, and a warning that some events cannot emit it;\nthe binary does not say which.\n\n**Live test, 2026-09-23, `codex-cli 0.155.1`, `codex exec`: the hook fires.** A\n`command` handler in\n`{\"hooks\":{\"Stop\":[{\"matcher\":\"\",\"hooks\":[{\"type\":\"command\",\"command\":â€¦,\"timeout\":30}]}]}}`\nran from both `$CODEX_HOME/hooks.json` and a per-repo `.codex/hooks.json`. The\nper-repo file needs a trusted project. Both need hook trust:\n`--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust`, or trust persisted by the interactive review.\nUntrusted hooks are skipped silently. Stdin carries `stop_hook_active` and\n`last_assistant_message`. **Exit 2 + stderr does not continue the turn**\n(reported `Stop Failed`). **stdout `{\"decision\":\"block\",\"reason\":â€¦}` does:** the\nreason re-enters as a `<hook_prompt>` and the model runs again with\n`stop_hook_active:true`. The 0.153.2 failure (see `PLATFORMS.md`) is superseded.\n`usage-watch.* gate` is now wired and verified as that handler â€” see **Codex**\nabove. Do not remove `check`: it remains the only mechanism cursor and\nantigravity have.\n\nDon't claim cursor is armed. It isn't, and no amount of scripting makes it so\nuntil Cursor writes a usage number somewhere readable.\n\n## Configure\n\n- **Threshold** â€” `CLAUDE_USAGE_HANDOFF_PCT` env var (default `92`). Set it in\n  `settings.json` under `env` to make it stick.\n- **Where the handoff lands** â€” decided by the `handoff-cortana` skill (`docs/handoffs/`\n  by default), not here.\n\n## Verify\n\n```\npowershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File <SKILL>/hooks/selftest.ps1\n```\nPrints `ok`. It feeds fake statusline JSON through `probe`/`gate` and asserts:\nthe max percentage wins, no fire below threshold, a block with the percentage\nabove it, no second fire, and no fire when `stop_hook_active`. It then runs\n`check` for real and asserts it exits 0, says one of its three allowed lines,\nand never reports over 100% (the guard against reading Codex's cumulative\n`total_token_usage` instead of `last_token_usage`).\n\nTo check it live, temporarily set `CLAUDE_USAGE_HANDOFF_PCT=1` and end a turn -\nthe handoff should be written immediately.\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n- **Never fires in one profile but works in another** â€” the wiring lives in\n  *that config dir's* `settings.json`. A `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` profile\n  (`~/.claude-work`, `~/.claude-bodish`, â€¦) is a separate install: arming\n  `~/.claude` arms nothing else. Wire every profile you actually code in, and\n  point `-Then` at that profile's own statusline command â€” the flag files are\n  shared (`~/.claude/watch-cortana/`) but the hooks are not.\n- **Never fires** â€” the statusline is where the percentage comes from. If\n  `~/.claude/watch-cortana/<session_id>` does not exist, the probe is not wired in\n  as `statusLine`, or this Claude Code build predates the `rate_limits` statusline\n  field (2.0.44+).\n- **Statusline went blank** â€” the `-Then` command is wrong or missing.\n- **Fired once, want it again** â€” delete `~/.claude/watch-cortana/<session_id>.done`.\n- **Stale flag files** â€” plain text, a few bytes each; delete\n  `~/.claude/watch-cortana/` whenever.\n\n## Related\n\n- `skillator:handoff-cortana` â€” writes the document this skill triggers.\n- `skillator:tickets-zordon` â€” the `TICKETS.md` board the handoff is reconciled against.\n- `skillator:resume-cortana` â€” executes it in the next session.\n\r\n### Runnable check for usage-watch.ps1 (and its sh twin, when sh is on PATH).\n# Run: powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File selftest.ps1\n$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'\n$ps  = Join-Path $PSScriptRoot 'usage-watch.ps1'\n$sh  = Join-Path $PSScriptRoot 'usage-watch.sh'\n$sid = \"selftest-$PID\"\n$flag = Join-Path $HOME \".claude\\handoff-watch\\$sid\"\n# check-mode fixtures live here, never in the user's real HOME: `check` writes a\n# genuine one-shot .done when it fires, so running it against the live\n# ~/.codex/sessions would burn a real session's handoff (and be non-deterministic).\n$tmp = Join-Path ([IO.Path]::GetTempPath()) \"watch-cortana-selftest-$PID\"\n$shExe = (Get-Command sh -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)\n\n# --- helpers ----------------------------------------------------------------\n# A crashed script must fail the test: capture the output AND the exit code, so\n# \"produced no output\" can never be mistaken for \"correctly did not fire\".\nfunction Run($mode, $json) {\n  $out = if ($null -eq $json) { (& powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File $ps -Mode $mode) -join \"`n\" }\n         else { ($json | & powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File $ps -Mode $mode) -join \"`n\" }\n  if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw \"ps $mode`: exit $LASTEXITCODE, output '$out'\" }\n  $out\n}\nfunction Probe($json) { Run 'probe' $json | Out-Null }\nfunction Gate($json)  { Run 'gate'  $json }\n\nfunction RunSh($mode, $hm, $json) {\n  $posix = ($hm -replace '\\\\', '/')\n  $old = $env:HOME; $env:HOME = $posix\n  try {\n    $out = if ($null -eq $json) { (& $shExe.Source $sh $mode) -join \"`n\" }\n           else { ($json | & $shExe.Source $sh $mode) -join \"`n\" }\n  } finally { $env:HOME = $old }\n  if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw \"sh $mode`: exit $LASTEXITCODE, output '$out'\" }\n  $out\n}\n# $HOME in a child PowerShell follows USERPROFILE on Windows.\nfunction RunCheck($hm) {\n  $oldU = $env:USERPROFILE; $oldH = $env:HOME\n  $env:USERPROFILE = $hm; $env:HOME = $hm\n  try { $out = (& powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File $ps -Mode check) -join \"`n\" }\n  finally { $env:USERPROFILE = $oldU; $env:HOME = $oldH }\n  if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw \"ps check: exit $LASTEXITCODE, output '$out'\" }\n  $out\n}\n\nfunction NewHome($name) {\n  $h = Join-Path $tmp $name\n  New-Item (Join-Path $h '.claude\\handoff-watch') -ItemType Directory -Force | Out-Null\n  $h\n}\n# $ageHours backdates the rollout's mtime (A32 freshness window); $sub varies the\n# nesting depth (A32 recursion - the sh twin used to glob exactly */*/*/).\n# A99: $windowMinutes tags the primary window (10080 = weekly); -NoSecondary\n# drops the secondary window entirely, leaving a single used_percent (the ps1\n# scalar `+=` bug, A83).\nfunction AddCodex($hm, $usedPercent, $ctxTokens, $ctxWindow, $ageHours = 0, $sub = '.codex\\sessions\\2026\\09\\04', $windowMinutes = $null, [switch]$NoSecondary) {\n  $d = Join-Path $hm $sub\n  New-Item $d -ItemType Directory -Force | Out-Null\n  $primary = if ($windowMinutes) { \"`\"primary`\":{`\"used_percent`\":$usedPercent,`\"window_minutes`\":$windowMinutes}\" }\n             else { \"`\"primary`\":{`\"used_percent`\":$usedPercent}\" }\n  $secondary = if ($NoSecondary) { '' } else { ',\"secondary\":{\"used_percent\":1.0}' }\n  $line = '{\"type\":\"event_msg\",\"payload\":{\"type\":\"token_count\",\"info\":' +\n          '{\"total_token_usage\":{\"total_tokens\":9999999},' +\n          \"`\"last_token_usage`\":{`\"total_tokens`\":$ctxTokens},\" +\n          \"`\"model_context_window`\":$ctxWindow},\" +\n          \"`\"rate_limits`\":{$primary$secondary}}}\"\n  $p = [IO.Path]::Combine((Convert-Path $d), 'rollout-test.jsonl')\n  [IO.File]::WriteAllText($p, \"$line`n\")\n  if ($ageHours) { (Get-Item $p).LastWriteTime = (Get-Date).AddHours(-$ageHours) }\n  $p\n}\nfunction AddFlag($hm, $name, $value, [switch]$Bom) {\n  $p = Join-Path $hm \".claude\\handoff-watch\\$name\"\n  if ($Bom) { Set-Content $p $value -Encoding utf8 }          # the pre-fix writer: BOM + CRLF\n  else      { [IO.File]::WriteAllText((Join-Path (Convert-Path (Split-Path $p -Parent)) $name), $value) }\n  $p\n}\nfunction NoDoneFiles($hm) {\n  -not (Get-ChildItem (Join-Path $hm '.claude\\handoff-watch') -Filter '*.done' -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)\n}\n\nRemove-Item \"$flag\", \"$flag.weekly\", \"$flag.done\", \"$flag.weekly.done\" -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue\n\n# The runtime state dir is storage, not a skill reference, and must survive a\n# skill rename: a session that already fired wrote its .done under the old path\n# and would fire a SECOND time if the dir moved. A rename sweep has already\n# moved it once, taking these selftest paths with it, so the test agreed with\n# the regression instead of catching it. Assert the literal path.\nforeach ($f in @($ps, $sh)) {\n  if (-not (Select-String -Path $f -Pattern 'handoff-watch' -Quiet)) {\n    throw \"state dir moved in $(Split-Path $f -Leaf) - it must stay ~/.claude/handoff-watch\"\n  }\n}\n\ntry {\n  # --- probe + gate against the real HOME (a scratch session id) -------------\n  $sl = { param($a, $b, $c) \"{`\"session_id`\":`\"$sid`\",`\"rate_limits`\":{`\"five_hour`\":{`\"used_percentage`\":$a},`\"seven_day`\":{`\"used_percentage`\":$b}},`\"context_window`\":{`\"used_percentage`\":$c}}\" }\n  $stop = \"{`\"session_id`\":`\"$sid`\",`\"stop_hook_active`\":false}\"\n\n  Probe (& $sl 40 12 55)\n  if ((Get-Content $flag -Raw).Trim() -ne '55') { throw 'probe: max not taken' }\n  # A12: the flag is read by the sh twin - it must be bare bytes, no BOM, no CRLF.\n  $bytes = [IO.File]::ReadAllBytes((Convert-Path $flag))\n  if ($bytes[0] -eq 0xEF -and $bytes[1] -eq 0xBB -and $bytes[2] -eq 0xBF) { throw 'probe: flag has a UTF-8 BOM' }\n  if ($bytes -contains 0x0D -or $bytes -contains 0x0A) { throw 'probe: flag has a line ending' }\n  if (Gate $stop) { throw 'gate: fired below threshold' }\n\n  Probe (& $sl 98.2 12 55)\n  if ((Get-Content $flag -Raw).Trim() -ne '98.2') { throw 'probe: not updated' }\n  $r = Gate $stop\n  if ($r -notmatch '\"decision\":\"block\"' -or $r -notmatch '98.2') { throw \"gate: no block, got '$r'\" }\n  if (Gate $stop) { throw 'gate: fired twice (done-marker ignored)' }\n  if (Gate \"{`\"session_id`\":`\"$sid`\",`\"stop_hook_active`\":true}\") { throw 'gate: ignored stop_hook_active' }\n\n  # --- F18: the 7-day window is its own gate ---------------------------------\n  # The 5-hour window refills in hours, so crossing it is a pause. The 7-day\n  # window does not, so crossing it ends the week's work - which is why it\n  # fires at a lower threshold and adds a fourth step the other windows have no\n  # use for: ask the user where to go next, with recommendations.\n  Remove-Item \"$flag\", \"$flag.weekly\", \"$flag.done\", \"$flag.weekly.done\" -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue\n\n  # A 5-hour spike must NOT fire the weekly order, even though it is the max.\n  Probe (& $sl 98.2 12 40)\n  if ((Get-Content \"$flag.weekly\" -Raw).Trim() -ne '12') { throw 'probe: weekly value not recorded' }\n  $r = Gate $stop\n  if ($r -notmatch '\"decision\":\"block\"') { throw 'gate: 5-hour spike did not fire at all' }\n  if ($r -match 'AskUserQuestion') { throw 'gate: 5-hour spike fired the weekly order' }\n  Remove-Item \"$flag\", \"$flag.weekly\", \"$flag.done\", \"$flag.weekly.done\" -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue\n\n  # The weekly window alone, over 90 but under the 92 the other windows use.\n  Probe (& $sl 40 91 55)\n  if ((Get-Content \"$flag.weekly\" -Raw).Trim() -ne '91') { throw 'probe: weekly value not recorded' }\n  # Same bare-bytes contract as the main flag - the sh twin reads it too (A12).\n  $wb = [IO.File]::ReadAllBytes((Convert-Path \"$flag.weekly\"))\n  if ($wb[0] -eq 0xEF) { throw 'probe: weekly flag has a UTF-8 BOM' }\n  if ($wb -contains 0x0D -or $wb -contains 0x0A) { throw 'probe: weekly flag has a line ending' }\n  $r = Gate $stop\n  if ($r -notmatch '\"decision\":\"block\"') { throw 'gate: weekly 91% did not fire' }\n  if ($r -notmatch '91') { throw 'gate: weekly block does not name the percentage' }\n  if ($r -notmatch 'AskUserQuestion') { throw 'gate: weekly block has no step 4' }\n  if ($r -notmatch 'recommend') { throw 'gate: step 4 does not ask for a recommendation' }\n  Remove-Item \"$flag\", \"$flag.weekly\", \"$flag.done\", \"$flag.weekly.done\" -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue\n\n  # Weekly under its own threshold and nothing else over: silence.\n  Probe (& $sl 40 89 55)\n  if (Gate $stop) { throw 'gate: weekly fired below 90' }\n  Remove-Item \"$flag\", \"$flag.weekly\", \"$flag.done\", \"$flag.weekly.done\" -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue\n\n  # F18/finding 4: the two gates fire at different times in one session. A\n  # 5-hour spike at 10:00 must not eat the weekly order when the 7-day window\n  # crosses at 14:00 - that later crossing is the one the user has to answer.\n  Probe (& $sl 98.2 40 55)\n  $r = Gate $stop\n  if ($r -notmatch '\"decision\":\"block\"') { throw 'gate sequence: the 5-hour spike did not fire' }\n  Probe (& $sl 98.2 91 55)\n  $r = Gate $stop\n  if ($r -notmatch 'AskUserQuestion') { throw 'gate sequence: the weekly order was eaten by the earlier .done' }\n  Remove-Item \"$flag\", \"$flag.weekly\", \"$flag.done\", \"$flag.weekly.done\" -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue\n\n  # F18 review: step 4 must come BEFORE \"Then tell the user ... and stop.\".\n  # Appending it after put the stop instruction ahead of the one step whose\n  # point is that the session must NOT stop on a summary. Asserting only that\n  # the string is present, as the first version did, misses the ordering.\n  Probe (& $sl 40 91 55)\n  $r = Gate $stop\n  $iAsk = $r.IndexOf('AskUserQuestion'); $iStop = $r.IndexOf('Then tell the user')\n  if ($iAsk -lt 0 -or $iStop -lt 0) { throw 'gate: weekly order is missing a piece' }\n  if ($iAsk -gt $iStop) { throw 'gate: step 4 comes after the stop instruction' }\n  Remove-Item \"$flag\", \"$flag.weekly\", \"$flag.done\", \"$flag.weekly.done\" -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue\n\n  # F18 review: a weekly flag with NO main flag beside it is reachable - it is\n  # exactly what a probe that failed halfway leaves behind - and the gate used\n  # to return before ever reading it, making the 7-day hard stop unreachable in\n  # the one case it mattered most.\n  [IO.File]::WriteAllText((Join-Path (Convert-Path (Split-Path $flag -Parent)) \"$sid.weekly\"), '91')\n  $r = Gate $stop\n  if ($r -notmatch 'AskUserQuestion') { throw 'gate: weekly-only flag did not fire' }\n  Remove-Item \"$flag\", \"$flag.weekly\", \"$flag.done\", \"$flag.weekly.done\" -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue\n\n  # A45/F18: `sort` is a name Windows steals. The sh probe used to pipe the\n  # percentages through `sort -g`, which from a PowerShell parent resolves to\n  # sort.exe, prints \"The system cannot find the file specified\", exits 0, and\n  # writes the weekly flag with NO main flag. This block is that regression:\n  # it drives the sh probe from here, which is a PowerShell process.\n  if ($shExe) {\n    $h = NewHome 'sh-probe-path'\n    RunSh 'probe' $h (& $sl 98.2 12 55) | Out-Null\n    $mf = Join-Path $h \".claude\\handoff-watch\\$sid\"\n    if (-not (Test-Path $mf)) { throw 'sh probe: no main flag (a stolen Windows binary ate the pipeline)' }\n    if ((Get-Content $mf -Raw).Trim() -ne '98.2') { throw \"sh probe: main flag is '$((Get-Content $mf -Raw).Trim())', wanted 98.2\" }\n  }\n\n  # The sh twin has to agree - PLATFORMS.md requires the pair to move together.\n  if ($shExe) {\n    $h = NewHome 'weekly-sh'\n    RunSh 'probe' $h (& $sl 40 91 55) | Out-Null\n    $wf = Join-Path $h \".claude\\handoff-watch\\$sid.weekly\"\n    if (-not (Test-Path $wf)) { throw 'sh probe: no weekly flag' }\n    if ((Get-Content $wf -Raw).Trim() -ne '91') { throw 'sh probe: weekly value wrong' }\n    $r = RunSh 'gate' $h $stop\n    if ($r -notmatch 'AskUserQuestion') { throw 'sh gate: weekly block has no step 4' }\n  }\n\n  # A12 regression, gate side: a BOM'd flag below threshold must not fire.\n  Remove-Item \"$flag\", \"$flag.weekly\", \"$flag.done\", \"$flag.weekly.done\" -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue\n  Set-Content $flag '12.0' -Encoding utf8\n  if (Gate $stop) { throw 'gate: BOM flag fired below threshold (A12)' }\n  if (Test-Path \"$flag.done\") { throw 'gate: BOM flag burned the one-shot (A12)' }\n  Remove-Item \"$flag\", \"$flag.weekly\", \"$flag.done\", \"$flag.weekly.done\" -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue\n\n  # --- check mode, against fixture HOMEs only --------------------------------\n  # codex rollout below threshold: report, do not fire, leave no .done behind.\n  $h = NewHome 'codex-low'; AddCodex $h 98.4 12000 200000 | Out-Null\n  $c = RunCheck $h\n  if ($c -notmatch '^HANDOFF NOW \\(codex 98\\.4%\\)') { throw \"check codex-high: got '$c'\" }\n  if ($c -notmatch 'skillator:handoff-cortana') { throw 'check codex-high: no handoff order' }\n  if (NoDoneFiles $h) { throw 'check codex-high: no one-shot .done written' }\n  $c = RunCheck $h\n  if ($c -match 'HANDOFF NOW') { throw 'check codex-high: fired twice' }\n\n  # context-window fallback: no used_percent worth reading, 170k/200k = 85%.\n  $h = NewHome 'codex-ctx'; These two pin the threshold retune itself - at the old\n  # default of 97 the first would print \"ok\" and the second would not fire, so\n  # anyone raising the default back without meaning to breaks this test.\n  $h = NewHome 'cc-band'; AddFlag $h 'sess-a' '95.0' | Out-Null\n  $c = RunCheck $h\n  if ($c -notmatch '^HANDOFF NOW \\(claude-code 95(\\.0)?%\\)') { throw \"check cc-band: got '$c'\" }\n  if (NoDoneFiles $h) { throw 'check cc-band: fired without writing the one-shot .done' }\n\n  $h = NewHome 'cc-under'; AddFlag $h 'sess-a' '91.0' | Out-Null\n  $c = RunCheck $h\n  if ($c -notmatch '^watch-cortana: claude-code 91(\\.0)?% of 92% - ok$') { throw \"check cc-under: got '$c'\" }\n  if (-not (NoDoneFiles $h)) { throw 'check cc-under: wrote a .done just below threshold' }\n\n  # F18/finding 1: a `.weekly` file is written AFTER the main flag, so it is\n  # always the newest in the directory. Picking by mtime reported the 7-day\n  # number as the max and compared it against the wrong threshold - a session\n  # at 98.2% read \"ok\", on the hosts where `check` is the ONLY signal.\n  $h = NewHome 'cc-weekly-shadow'; Just outside the 3h window - the boundary, not just the obvious week.\n    $h = NewHome \"a32-edge-$($r.n)\"; AddCodex $h 98.4 12000 200000 4 | Out-Null\n    $c = & $r.f $h\n    if ($c -match 'HANDOFF NOW') { throw \"$($r.n) check: fired on a 4h-old rollout (A32), got '$c'\" }\n\n    # --- the sh twin must agree, where sh exists -------------------------------\n  if ($shExe) {\n    $h = NewHome 'sh-codex-low'; AddCodex $h 12.0 12000 200000 | Out-Null\n    $c = RunSh 'check' $h $null\n    if ($c -notmatch '^watch-cortana: codex 12\\.0% of 92% - ok$') { throw \"sh check codex-low: got '$c'\" }\n    if (-not (NoDoneFiles $h)) { throw 'sh check codex-low: wrote a .done below threshold' }\n\n    $h = NewHome 'sh-codex-high'; AddCodex $h 98.4 12000 200000 | Out-Null\n    $c = RunSh 'check' $h $null\n    if ($c -notmatch '^HANDOFF NOW \\(codex 98\\.4%\\)') { throw \"sh check codex-high: got '$c'\" }\n    if (NoDoneFiles $h) { throw 'sh check codex-high: no one-shot .done written' }\n\n    #
